Measure rigidity for generalized u-Gibbs states and stationary measures via the factorization method Thanks: Revised August 24, 2026
Aaron Brown, Alex Eskin, Simion Filip, Federico Rodriguez Hertz

Abstract
. We obtain measure rigidity results for stationary measures of random walks generated by diffeomorphisms, and for actions of {SL} ⁡ ( 2 , ℝ ) \SL(2,\mathbb{R}) on smooth manifolds. Our main technical result, from which the rest of the theorems are derived, applies also to the case of a single diffeomorphism or 1 1 -parameter flow and establishes extra invariance of a class of measures that we call “generalized u-Gibbs states”.



[title = Index of Notation, columns = 3]

1.
Introduction

In this paper, we study random dynamics and actions of
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
on
smooth manifolds.
Our main results are stated in
§
4.1
. We note that our main technical
result,
Theorem 4.1.2
, from which the rest of
the theorems are derived, applies also to the
situation where we are working with a single diffeomorphism or flow,
and may be of independent
interest.
Theorem 4.1.2
may be viewed as a
generalization of the main theorem of

[
Kat23
]
.

As a special case, we prove a generalization of the results of

[
EM18
]

and
[
EMM15
]
to actions on products
of moduli spaces of translation surfaces.

In this introductory section, we list some
easy to state consequences of the results of
§
4.1
.

1.1.
Random Dynamics

Let
Q
be a connected smooth manifold.
Let
\Diff_{\infty}(Q)
denote the group of
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphisms of
Q
.

1.1.1 Definition
.

Given a probability measure
\mu
on
\Diff_{\infty}(Q)
, a probability measure
\nu
on
Q
is said to be

(i)

\mu
-stationary
if
\int f_{*}\nu\,d\mu(f)=\nu
, and

(ii)

\mu
-invariant
if
f_{*}\nu=\nu
for
\mu
-a.e.
f\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)
.

We observe the collection of
\mu
-stationary measures is a closed convex set.

(iii)

A
\mu
-stationary probability measure is said to be
ergodic
if it is an extreme point of this collection.

Finally,

(iv)

we say that
\mu
is volume-preserving
if there exists a smooth volume form on
Q
preserved that is
\mu
-invariant.

In this
version of the paper, we will be assuming that
\mu
is finitely
supported. This assumption will be removed in a later version.

For
x\in Q
let
TQ(x)
denote the tangent space to
Q
.
Let
\Gr_{d}(x)
denote the Grassmannian of
d
-dimensional subspaces of
TQ(x)
.
Suppose that for each
x\in Q
we are given a norm

\|\cdot\|_{x}
on the exterior power
\bigwedge^{d}(TQ(x))
. We then have a cocycle
\sigma_{d}\colon\Diff_{\infty}(Q)\times TQ\to\mathbb{R}
, given by

(1.1.2)

\sigma_{d}(f,L)=\log\frac{\|Df(l_{1}\wedge\dots l_{d})\|_{f(x)}}{\|l_{1}\wedge\dots l_{d}\|_{x}}

where
\{l_{1},\dots,l_{d}\}
is any basis for
L
. Then
\sigma_{d}(f,L)
measures the change in the volume of the subspace
L
under the diffeomorphism
f
.

1.1.3 Definition

(Uniform expansion)
.

The measure
\mu
is
uniformly expanding in dimension
d
if
if there exist
C_{d}>0
and
N_{d}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
x\in Q
and all
L\in\Gr_{d}(x)
,

(1.1.4)

\int_{\Diff_{\infty}(Q)}\sigma_{d}(f,L)\,d\mu^{(N_{d})}(f)>C_{d}>0

where
\mu^{(N_{d})}
denotes the
N_{d}
-fold convolution of
\mu
with itself.

1.1.5 Theorem
.

Suppose
\mu
is volume preserving and satisfies uniform expansion in dimension
d
for all
1\leq d<\dim(Q)
. Suppose also that either
Q
is compact, or there exists an admissible Margulis function on
Q
(see
Definition 2.4.2
). Then, any
\mu
-stationary measure is
\mu
-invariant.

A more technical result, but without assuming that
\mu
is volume-preserving, is contained in
Theorem 4.1.4
.

1.1.6 Definition

(Uniform expansion gaps)
.

The measure
\mu
has a
uniform expansion gap in dimension
d
if
if there exist
C_{d}>0,\delta_{d}\in\{\pm 1\}
, and
N_{d}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
x\in Q
and for all
L_{0}\subset L_{1}
with
L_{i}\in\Gr_{d+i}(x)
, we have

(1.1.7)

\delta_{d}\cdot{\int_{\Diff_{\infty}(Q)}\big[\sigma_{d+1}(f,L_{1})-\sigma_{d}(f,L_{0})\big]\,d\mu^{(N_{d})}(f)}>C_{d}>0

where
\mu^{(N_{d})}
denotes the
N_{d}
-fold convolution of
\mu
with itself.

1.1.8 Remark

(On uniform expansion and gaps)
.

Suppose that
\nu
is an ergodic
\mu
-stationary measure with Lyapunov spectrum
\lambda_{1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{\dim Q}
on
TQ
.
If
\mu
is uniformly expanding in dimension
d
, then it follows from the same arguments as in the proof of
[
Chu20
, Thm. C]
, that the Lyapunov exponents satisfy
\lambda_{1}+\dots+\lambda_{d}>C_{d}/N_{d}
.
Analogously, if
\mu
has uniform expansion gap in dimension
d
then
\delta_{d}\lambda_{d}>C_{d}/N_{d}
where
\delta_{d}\in\{\pm 1\}
.

Note also that both
Definition 1.1.3
and
Definition 1.1.6
are properties that are open upon a
C^{1}
-perturbation.

1.1.9 Theorem
.

Suppose
\mu
is volume preserving and satisfies uniform expansion in dimension
d
for all
1\leq d<\dim(Q)
.
Suppose
\nu
is an ergodic
\mu
-invariant measure on
Q
, and suppose that
\nu
has no zero Lyapunov exponents.
Furthermore, suppose that either
Q
is compact, or that there exist admissible Margulis functions on
Q
and
Q\times Q
(see
Definition 2.4.2
and
Definition 2.4.5
).

Then
\nu
is either absolutely continuous with respect to volume or is finitely supported.
If, in addition,
\mu
has uniform expansion gap in dimension
d
for all
1\leq d<\dim Q
, then the volume measure is
\mu
-ergodic and therefore
\nu
is either volume or finitely supported.

1.1.10 Definition

(
\mu
-invariant subbundle)
.

Suppose
\mu
is a probability measure on
\Diff_{\infty}(Q)
and
\nu
is a
\mu
-stationary ergodic measure.
A measurable subbundle
S\subset TQ
, defined
\nu
-a.e., is called
\mu
-invariant
if for
\mu
-a.e.
g
and
\nu
-a.e.
q
we have that
Dg(S(q))=S(gq)
.

We emphasize that
S
is defined on
Q
and does not depend on the trajectory of the random walk.

Theorem 1.1.5
is derived from
the following:

1.1.11 Theorem
.

Let
\mu
be a finitely supported probability measure on
\Diff_{\infty}(Q)
.
Let
\nu
be an ergodic
\mu
-stationary measure on
Q
.
Suppose there is no

\mu
-invariant
\nu
-measurable subbundle of
TQ
on which the sum of
the Lyapunov exponents (see
§2.3
) is
negative. Then,
\nu
is
\mu
-invariant.

Our main result for random actions is

Theorem 4.1.9
, from which

Theorem 1.1.9
is derived.
The assumptions in
Theorem 4.1.9

are the same as in

Theorem 1.1.11
, and the
conclusion is a structure theorem for the stationary (and thus
invariant) measures, including a precise description of the
conditional measures along the stables and unstables.

We also state the following:

1.1.12 Conjecture
.

Suppose
Q
is a compact symplectic manifold, and suppose
\mu
is a probability measure on the group of symplectic diffeomorphisms of
Q
which satisfies uniform expansion on all the isotropic subspaces of
TQ
.
Suppose
\nu
is a
\mu
-stationary measure on
Q
which has no
0

Lyapunov exponents. Then
\nu
is a
\mu
-invariant measure on a smooth submanifold
M
of
Q
. (The case
M=Q
is allowed).

Our progress towards Conjecture
1.1.12
is given in

Theorem 4.1.10
.

1.2.
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
actions

Suppose that
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
acts smoothly and locally freely on
Q
.
Let
g_{t}=\begin{pmatrix}e^{t}&0\\
0&e^{-t}\end{pmatrix}
, and let

A=\{g_{t}\in\mathbb{R}\},\qquad N=\begin{pmatrix}1&\ast\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bar{N}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\ast&1\end{pmatrix},

and let
P=AN
.

Let
SO(2)
be a maximal compact subgroup of
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
.
Recall that the quotient
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})/SO(2)
is identified with the hyperbolic plane
\mathbb{H}^{2}
.
Let
\Delta
denote the Laplace operator for the hyperbolic metric.

The analogue of uniform expansion is the following:

1.2.1 Definition

(Nonnegative Laplacian)
.

Suppose
E\to Q
is a cocycle over a smooth
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-action.
Given
d\in\{1,\dots,\dim E\}
, we say that
E
satisfies the
non-negative Laplacian condition in rank
d
if there exists a family of
SO(2)
-invariant norms

\|\cdot\|_{q}
on
\bigwedge^{d}E
(for all
q\in Q
)
such that the following holds:

For any
q\in Q
, fix
\{v_{1},\dots,v_{d}\}
a linearly independent set in
E
.
Let
f\colon\SL(2,\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
be defined by

f(g)=\log\|gv_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge gv_{d}\|_{gq}.

Since
\|\cdot\|_{q}
is
SO(2)
-invariant,
f
descends to a function
h\colon\mathbb{H}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
. Then,

(1.2.2)

\Delta h\geq 0.

The following is an analogue of
Theorem 1.1.5
:

1.2.3 Theorem
.

Suppose
SL(2,\mathbb{R})
acts on
Q
and satisfies the non-negative Laplacian condition on
TQ
, for all ranks
d\in\{1,\dots,\dim Q\}
(
Definition 1.2.1
).
Suppose
\nu
is a
P
-invariant ergodic measure on
Q
.

Then,
\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant.

Theorem 1.2.3
is
derived from the following:

1.2.4 Theorem
.

Suppose
SL(2,\mathbb{R})
acts on
Q
and
let
\nu
be an ergodic
P
-invariant measure on
Q
.
Suppose there is no
P
-invariant
\nu
-measurable subbundle of
TQ

on which the sum of the Lyapunov exponents of
g_{1}
is negative. Then,
\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant.

Our main result for general
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-actions is

Theorem 4.1.7
.
The assumptions in
Theorem 4.1.7

are the same as in

Theorem 1.2.4
, and the
conclusion is a structure theorem for the
P
-invariant (and thus

\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant) measures, including a precise description of the
conditional measures along the stable and unstable manifolds of
g_{t}
.

1.3.
Teichmüller dynamics

Suppose
g\geq 1
, and
let
\alpha=\alpha_{1}+\dots+\alpha_{n}

be a partition of
2g-2
, and
let
\mathcal{H}(\alpha)
be a stratum of Abelian differentials,
i.e. the space of pairs
(M,\omega)
where
M
is a Riemann surface
and
\omega
is a holomorphic
1
-form on
M
whose
zeroes have
multiplicities
\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{n}
. The form
\omega
defines a
canonical flat metric on
M
with conical singularities at the zeros
of
\omega
. Thus we refer to points of
\mathcal{H}(\alpha)
as

flat surfaces
or
translation surfaces
. For an introduction
to this subject, see the surveys
[
Zor06
,
Fil24
]
.

The space
\mathcal{H}(\alpha)

admits an action of the group
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
which generalizes the
action of
SL(2,\mathbb{R})
on the space
GL(2,\mathbb{R})/SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
of flat
tori. In

[
EM18
]

and
[
EMM15
]

some ergodic-theoretic rigidity properties of this action were proved. In this paper, we generalize the results to the diagonal action of
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
on products of the
\mathcal{H}(\alpha)
.

As in

[
EM18
]
,
we always replace
\mathcal{H}(\alpha)
by a finite cover which is a
manifold (for example the congruence cover with
\mathbb{Z}/3
-homology trivialized).
However,
we suppress this from the notation.

Products of strata

Let
\vec{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})
. Let
\mathcal{H}(\vec{\alpha})=\mathcal{H}(\alpha_{1})\times\mathcal{H}(\alpha_{n})
. The moduli space

\mathcal{H}(\vec{\alpha})
parametrizes a holomorphic
1
-form on a
disconnected surface
M=M_{1}\cup\dots\cup M_{n}
, which is an

n
-tuple
\omega=(\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{n})
where
\omega_{i}
is a
holomorphic
1
-form on
M_{i}
.

Let
\Sigma_{i}
denote the set of zeroes of
\omega_{i}
, and let
\Sigma=\bigcup_{i}\Sigma_{i}
. We have

H^{1}(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{C})=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}H^{1}(M_{i},\Sigma_{i},\mathbb{C}).

Let
\Phi:\mathcal{H}^{1}(\vec{\alpha})\to H^{1}(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{C})
be the
period coordinate map. Then,
\Phi=(\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{k})
where

\Phi_{j}:\mathcal{H}(\alpha_{j})\to H^{1}(M_{j},\Sigma_{j},\mathbb{C})
is period map of

\mathcal{H}(\alpha_{j})
which takes
(X_{j},\omega_{j})\in\mathcal{H}(\alpha_{j})
to the
cohomology class of
\omega_{j}
in
H^{1}(M_{j},\Sigma_{j},\mathbb{C})
.

The area of a translation surface is given by

a_{j}(M_{j},\omega_{j})=\frac{i}{2}\int_{M_{j}}\omega_{j}\wedge\bar{\omega}_{j}.

A “unit hyperboloid”
\mathcal{H}_{1}(\alpha)

is defined as a subset of translation surfaces in
\mathcal{H}(\alpha)
of
area one. We then define

\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})=\mathcal{H}_{1}(\alpha_{1})\times\dots\times\mathcal{H}_{1}(\alpha_{n}).

Let
\tilde{\lambda}_{j}
denote the Masur-Veech measure on
\mathcal{H}(\alpha_{j})
,
and let
\lambda_{j}
denote the Masur-Veech measure on

\mathcal{H}_{1}(\alpha_{j})
which is the restriction of
\tilde{\lambda}_{j}
,
so that
d\tilde{\lambda}_{j}=cd\lambda_{j}\,da_{j}

where
c
is a constant. We normalize
\lambda_{j}
so that

\lambda_{j}(\mathcal{H}_{1}(\alpha_{j}))=1
. Let
\tilde{\lambda}=\tilde{\lambda}_{1}\times\dots\times\dots\tilde{\lambda}_{n}
and let

\lambda=\lambda_{1}\times\dots\lambda_{n}
, so that
\lambda
is a
probability measure on
\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
.

For a subset
\mathcal{M}_{1}\subset\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
we write

\mathbb{R}^{n}\mathcal{M}_{1}=\{((M_{1},t_{1}\omega_{1}),\dots,(M_{n},t\omega_{n}))\;|\;((M_{1},\omega_{1}),\dots,(M_{n},\omega_{n})\in\mathcal{M}_{1},\\
t_{j}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\}\subset\mathcal{H}(\vec{\alpha}).

1.3.1 Definition
.

An ergodic
SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant probability measure
\nu
on

\mathcal{H}_{1}(\alpha)
is called
affine
if the following conditions hold:

(i)

The support
\mathcal{M}_{1}
of
\nu
is an

immersed submanifold
of

\mathcal{H}_{1}(\alpha)
, i.e. there exists a manifold
\mathcal{N}
and a proper continuous
map
f:\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{H}_{1}(\alpha)
so that
\mathcal{M}_{1}=f(\mathcal{N})
. The self-intersection set of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
, i.e. the set of
points of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
which do not have a
unique preimage under
f
, is a closed subset of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
of

\nu
-measure
0
. Furthermore, each point in
\mathcal{N}
has a
neighborhood
U
such
that locally
\mathbb{R}^{n}f(U)
is given by a complex linear subspace defined
over
\mathbb{R}
in the period coordinates.

(ii)

Let
\tilde{\nu}
be the measure supported on
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}^{n}\mathcal{M}_{1}
so that
d\tilde{\nu}=d\nu_{1}da_{1}\dots da_{n}
. Then each point in
\mathcal{N}
has a
neighborhood
U
such that the restriction of
\tilde{\nu}
to
\mathbb{R}^{n}f(U)
is an affine linear measure in the period
coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{n}f(U)
, i.e. it is (up to normalization) the
induced measure of the Masur-Veech measure
\tilde{\lambda}
to the subspace

\mathbb{R}^{n}f(U)
.

1.3.2 Definition
.

We say that any suborbifold
\mathcal{M}_{1}
for which there exists a measure

\nu
such that the pair
(\mathcal{M}_{1},\nu)

satisfies (i) and (ii) is an
affine invariant submanifold
.

We also consider all of
\mathcal{H}(\vec{\alpha})
to be an
(improper) affine invariant submanifold.
It follows from the analogue of

[
EMM15
, Theorem 2.2]
that
the self-intersection set of an affine invariant manifold is itself a
finite union of
affine invariant manifolds of lower dimension.

Let

N=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},t\in\mathbb{R}\right\},\quad A=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}e^{t}&0\\
0&e^{-t}\end{pmatrix},t\in\mathbb{R}\right\},\quad\bar{N}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
t&1\end{pmatrix},t\in\mathbb{R}\right\}

Let
r_{\theta}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta&\sin\theta\\
-\sin\theta&\cos\theta\end{pmatrix}
,
and let
SO(2)=\{r_{\theta}\mid\theta\in[0,2\pi)\}
.
Then
N
,
\bar{N}
,
A
and
SO(2)

are subgroups of
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
. Let
P=AN

denote the set of upper triangular matrices of determinant

1
, which is a subgroup of
SL(2,\mathbb{R})
.

The following generalizes
[
EM18
, Theorem 1.4]
:

1.3.3 Theorem
.

Let
\nu
be any ergodic
P
-invariant probability measure on

\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
. Then
\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant and affine.

The following generalizes

[
EMM15
, Theorem 2.1]
:

1.3.4 Theorem
.

Suppose
x\in\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
. Then the orbit closure

\overline{Px}
is equal to
\overline{\SL(2,\mathbb{R})x}
and
is an affine invariant
submanifold of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
.

We note that the other statements from Sections 2 and 3 of

[
EMM15
]

also hold in this setting.

1.4.
Outline and General Remarks

All the main results are deduced from
Theorem 4.1.2
, whose proof is outlined in
§4.5
.

1.4.1.
Previous results

The work on measure and topological rigidity questions in dynamics is vast and to survey it is beyond our capacity.

For homogeneous spaces, some landmark contributions in the rigidity of unipotent flows have been made by
Furstenberg
[
Fur73
]
,
Margulis
[
Mar87
]

and Ratner
[
Rat91
]
.
Rigidity of measures invariant under abelian groups, under a positive entropy assumption, has been established in the work of Einsiedler, Katok, and Lindenstrauss
[
EKL06
]
.
Still in the setting of Lie groups and homogeneous spaces, Benoist–Quint
[
BQ11
]
obtained the first rigidity results for stationary measures (see also the work of Bourgain–Furman–Lindenstrauss–Moses
[
BFLM11
]
for a precursor in the case of tori, and Eskin–Lindenstrauss
[
EL18
]
for a view on this setting closer to the spirit of this work).

Beyond the homogeneous setting, in the setting of Teichmüller dynamics rigidity of measures invariant under the upper-triangular subgroup of
\SL_{2}(\mathbb{R})
was obtained by Eskin, Mirzakhani, and Mohammadi
[
EM18
,
EMM15
]
.
For random walks generated by diffeomorphisms of real
2
-dimensional surfaces, Brown and Rodriguez Hertz obtained a measure rigidity result

[
BRH17
]
that was used and extended to the case of complex
2
-dimensional surfaces by Cantat and Dujardin

[
CD23
]
(see also the work of Roda
[
Rod24
]
).

The QNI condition (see
§1.4.4
) appears in the work of Katz
[
Kat23
]
, as well as Eskin–Potrie–Zhang

[
EPZ23
]
.
Uniqueness of u-Gibbs states has been recently considered in the works of Alvarez, Leguil, Obata, Santiago, and Cantarino
[
ALOS22
,
CS24
]
.

1.4.2.
The role of normal forms

The unstable manifolds of the flow are equipped with normal form coordinates, as we explain in
Appendix D
.
We view these as a class of charts on the unstable manifolds, with transition maps in the group of subresonant maps.
Among the consequences of this structure are several additional properties and constructions.
The first is that the unstable manifold acquires the action of the group of subresonant, and strictly subresonant maps.
The second is that we can linearize the action on the unstable manifold by polynomially embedding the manifold into a linear cocycle.

1.4.3.
Subgroups compatible with the measure

The subresonant structure on the unstable manifolds allows us to define generalized u-Gibbs states, see
Definition 3.1.7
.
These are
g_{t}
-invariant measures whose conditionals along unstables are invariant under a family of subgroups
U^{+}(x)
of the strictly subresonant maps.

1.4.4.
QNI condition

The crucial extra invariance of
Theorem 4.1.2
is deduced from the assumption that the family
U^{+}
and the center-stable manifolds satisfy a non-integrability condition, as explicated in
Definition 3.2.2
.

1.4.5.
Some notational conventions

We will adopt the convention from

[
EM18
]

that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]

is the stable manifold

\displaystyle W^{s}(q)

is the stable tangent space

and more generally, we’ll use
E(q)
for the vector space which is a fiber of a vector bundle at
q
and
E[q]
for an actual manifold, or “something inside the manifold”.

2.
Technical preliminaries

2.1.
Assumptions on the total space

2.1.1.
Notation

We work on a manifold
Q
, denote its tangent bundle by
TQ
, and assume that
Q
is equipped with a smooth Riemannian metric.
The tangent space at
q\in Q
is denoted
TQ(q)
and the ball of radius
r>0
at
0\in TQ(q)
is denoted
B(q;r)
.
Later on, we will introduce other norms on
TQ(q)
and such balls will be denoted
B_{\bullet}(q;r)
where
\bullet
indicates the flavor of norm.

2.1.2.
Norms on jet bundles

Denote by
J_{k}Q
the bundle of
k
-jets of functions on
Q
and its dual
J_{k}^{\vee\mkern-3.0mu}Q
.
For example
J_{0}Q
is the trivial
1
-dimensional bundle, and
T^{\vee\mkern-3.0mu}Q=\ker\left(J_{1}Q\twoheadrightarrow J_{0}Q\right)
.

The Riemannian metric on
Q
yields:

(i)

Norms on the
k
-jets
J_{k}Q
, and induced norms on the duals
J_{k}^{\vee\mkern-3.0mu}Q
.
The norms are compatible, i.e. the embedding
J_{k}^{\vee\mkern-3.0mu}Q\hookrightarrow J_{k+1}^{\vee\mkern-3.0mu}Q
is an isometry.

(ii)

The norm on
TQ
gives a distance function on
Q
, by minimization along paths.

We will denote these “ambient” norms by
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bullet\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}
, and if necessary indicate with a subscript the order of the jets.

2.1.3.
Charts with bounds on jets

We assume also that there exists an “ambient radius” function
r_{a}\colon Q\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, such that for every
q\in Q
there exist charts, which we will call
“exponential maps”,

\exp_{q}\colon B(q;r_{a}(q))\to Q\text{ where }B(q;r_{a}(q))\text{ is the ${\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bullet\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}$-ball of radius }r_{a}(q).

We assume the following compatibility between these charts, and the background splittings of higher order jets:
There exists a function
C_{k}(q)
, such that for any
v\in B(q;r_{a}(q))
and
q^{\prime}:=\exp_{q}(v)
the induced map between higher order jets

J_{k}(TQ(q))(v)\xleftarrow{J_{k}\exp_{q}}J_{k}Q(q^{\prime})

has norm bounded by
C_{k}(q)
.
Note that the jet bundles over a vector space equipped with a norm are naturally split and have natural metrics induced from the metric of the vector space.

We will put further restrictions on
r_{a}(q)
and
C_{k}(q)
in
§2.1.6
below.

2.1.4 Remark

(On distances and norms)
.

If we have a Riemannian metric on
Q
, its Levi-Civita connection provides the above splittings.

For every
n
, there exists a constant
c_{n}
such that for every Finsler norm
\left\|-\right\|_{F}
on an
n
-dimensional vector space, there exists a positive-definite inner product
\left\|-\right\|_{J}
(provided by the “John ellipsoid” of the unit ball of the Finsler metric) such that
\left\|-\right\|_{F}\leq\left\|-\right\|_{J}\leq c_{n}\left\|-\right\|_{F}
.
So on tangent spaces, we can freely pass from a Finsler metric to a positive-definite quadratic form.

2.1.5.
The flow
g_{t}
.

Let
g_{t}
be a smooth flow on the manifold
Q
.
If we are considering an
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-action, then
g_{t}
denotes the action of

\begin{pmatrix}e^{t}&0\\
0&e^{-t}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
.

2.1.6.
Systole function for flows

We assume there exists an (ambient)
inverse systole function

\phi_{a}(q)\geq 2
, that we think of as
y
on the upper half-plane, or the inverse of the distance to a singular set of a subvariety, such that
\log\phi_{a}
is uniformly continuous.
This holds if, for instance,
\log\phi_{a}
is Lipschitz.
In particular, we assume
\phi_{a}
is continuous and therefore bounded on compact sets.

We also require
\phi_{a}
to control the dynamics in the following manner:

Bounded growth:

there exists
C>0
such that

\phi_{a}(g_{t}q)\leq e^{C|t|}\phi_{a}(q)

which follows from the infinitesimal bound:
|\mathcal{L}_{g_{t}}\phi_{a}|\leq C\phi_{a}
, abusing notation for the Lie derivative along the vector field generating
g_{t}
.

Bounds on
k
-jet dynamics:

There exists a constant
e>0
, and a “dynamical radius”
r_{d}(q):=\phi_{a}(q)^{-e}r_{a}(q)
, such that for all
t\in[-1,1]
, the transformation
g_{t}
takes
\exp_{q}(B(q;r_{d}(q)))
into the image of
\exp_{g_{t}q}(B(q;r_{a}(g_{t}q)))
.
Conjugating
g_{t}
by these charts to measure the
C^{k}
-jets, we have the inequality:

\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}\left\|g_{t}\right\|_{C^{k}\left(B(q;r_{d}(q))\right)}\leq\phi_{a}(q)^{a_{k}}

for some fixed constant
a_{k}>0
.

Bounds on
k
-jet transitions:

: We assume that the size of charts
r_{a}(q)
from the previous part is bounded below by
\phi_{a}(q)^{-e_{0}}
.
Similarly, the constants
C_{k}(q)
satisfy the bound
|C_{k}(q)|\leq\phi_{a}(q)^{e_{k}}
for some
e_{k}>0
.

We will denote by

(2.1.7)

\displaystyle G_{t,q}:=\exp_{g_{t}q}^{-1}\circ g_{t}\circ\exp_{q}

the map between subsets of
TQ(q)
and
TQ(g_{t}q)
which are to be specified depending on the context.

2.1.8.
Some special cases

For example, if
Q
is compact we can take
\phi_{a}\equiv 10
, i.e. any constant larger than
1
.
Then the constants
C,e,a_{k},e_{k}
in
§2.1.6
can be chosen sufficiently large depending on the smooth vector field generating
g_{t}
.

2.1.9.
An elementary bound

We record, for future use, the following elementary fact.
For any compact set
K\subset Q
and any
t_{0},\varepsilon_{0}>0
there exists
C:=C(K,t_{0},\varepsilon_{0})
such that if
q\in K
and
d^{Q}(q,q^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon_{0}
then

(2.1.10)

\displaystyle d^{Q}(g_{t}q,g_{t}q^{\prime})\leq C\cdot d^{Q}(q,q^{\prime})\quad\forall t\in[0,t_{0}].

2.1.11.
Systole function for random dynamics

Let
\mathcal{S}_{\mu}
denote the support of
\mu
. Recall that we are assuming
that
\mathcal{S}_{\mu}
is a finite set.
We assume that there exists a function
\phi_{a}:Q\to\mathbb{R}
such that all of the conditions of

§2.1.6
are satisfied, where in place of

g_{t}
we consider any word in
\mathcal{S}_{\mu}\cup\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{-1}
of length at
most
t
.

2.2.
Assumptions on the measure and consequences

Suppose that
(Q,g_{t})
satisfies the conditions from
§2.1.6
with a systole function
q\mapsto\phi_{a}(q)
.

2.2.1 Assumption
.

Throughout,
\nu
will be an ergodic,
g_{t}
-invariant
Borel probability measure satisfying the integrability assumption:

(2.2.2)

\int\log^{+}|\phi_{a}(q)|d\nu(q)<+\infty.

2.2.3.
Lyapunov exponents

As the systole function
\phi_{a}(\cdot)
controls the norm of the derivative cocycle, we may apply the Oseledets theorem.
We enumerate the Lyapunov exponents of the derivative cocycle as

\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}>\dots

including possible zeros.

2.2.4 Proposition

(Adapted Lyapunov norms)
.

For any
k\geq 1,\varepsilon>0
, there exists a measurable “dynamical systole function”
\phi_{k,\varepsilon}(q)\geq 1
, and a measurable family of
\varepsilon
-Lyapunov metrics
\left\|\bullet\right\|_{k,\varepsilon}
on the tangent spaces, both defined
\nu
-a.e., with the following properties:

(i)

Slow variation:

|\phi_{k,\varepsilon}(g_{t}q)|\leq e^{|t|\varepsilon}|\phi_{k,\varepsilon}(q)|

(ii)

Comparison to ambient metric: we have
\nu
-a.e. that

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bullet\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\leq\left\|\bullet\right\|_{k,\varepsilon}\leq\phi_{k,\varepsilon}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\bullet\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}

(iii)

Uniform hyperbolicity: the Lyapunov decomposition of the tangent cocycle
TQ

TQ=\oplus_{\lambda_{i}}TQ^{\lambda_{i}}

is orthogonal for
\left\|\bullet\right\|_{k,\varepsilon}
and satisfies on a set of full measure:

e^{-t\lambda_{i}}\left\|Dg_{t}|_{TQ^{\lambda_{i}}}\right\|_{k,\varepsilon}\leq e^{|t|\varepsilon}\quad\text{ for all }t\in\mathbb{R}.

(iv)

Dynamics bounds: for a constant
r_{0}>0
that only depends on the Lyapunov spectrum, we have that (see
Eqn. (2.1.7)
)

G_{t,q}:=\exp_{g_{t}q}^{-1}\circ g_{t}\circ\exp_{q}\colon B_{k,\varepsilon}(q;r_{0})\to B_{k,\varepsilon}(g_{t}q;1)

is well-defined for
t\in[-1,1]
, where
B_{k,\varepsilon}(q^{\prime};r)
denotes the ball of radius
r
in the metric
\left\|\bullet\right\|_{k,\varepsilon}
centered at
0
in
T_{q^{\prime}}Q
.
Furthermore we have the bound on derivatives:

\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}\left\|G_{t,q}-Dg_{t}\right\|_{C^{k}(B_{k,\varepsilon}(q;r_{0}))}\leq\varepsilon

and the
C^{k}
-norm is for the norms
\left\|\bullet\right\|_{k,\varepsilon}
on the respective vector spaces.

The proof is given in
§A.2.2
.

2.2.5 Remark

(Basic
C^{k}
bounds)
.

From (iii) and (iv) we obtain the following: for every
k
and
\epsilon>0
there is
C_{k}>1
and
\kappa_{k}>0
such that for all
T
and
\mu
-a.e.
x
,
the map

\exp_{g_{t}q}^{-1}\circ g_{t}\circ\exp_{q}\colon B_{k,\varepsilon}(q;C_{k}^{-1}e^{-\kappa_{k}T})\to B_{k,\varepsilon}(g_{t}q;1)

is well-defined and

\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}\left\|\exp_{g_{t}q}^{-1}\circ g_{t}\circ\exp_{q}\right\|_{C^{k}(B_{k,\varepsilon}(q;C^{-1}e^{-\kappa_{k}T}))}\leq C_{k}e^{\kappa_{k}T}.

Furthermore, there exists a tempered function
\widetilde{C}_{k}
such that

\sup_{t\in[-T,T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\exp_{g_{t}q}^{-1}\circ g_{t}\circ\exp_{q}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{C^{k}(B_{k,\varepsilon}(q;C^{-1}e^{-\kappa_{k}T}))}\leq\widetilde{C}_{k}(q)e^{\kappa_{k}T}.

2.2.6 Definition

(Measurably good smooth dynamics)
.

A system satisfying the conclusions of
Proposition 2.2.4
will be said to have
measurable good smooth dynamics
, or we’ll say it is
measurably good
.

General properties of measurably good dynamical systems, such as the
existence of stable and unstable manifolds are developed in

§A.3
. We denote the
stable manifold through
q\in Q
by
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
, and its tangent space
by
W^{s}(q)
. Similarly,
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
and
W^{u}(q)
denote the
unstable manifold and its tangent space.

Normal forms coordinates on
stables and unstables are developed in

Appendix D
.

2.2.7.
Lyapunov charts

We define

\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[q]:=\exp_{q}(B_{k,\varepsilon}(q;r_{0}))\subset Q

where
B_{k,\varepsilon}(q;r_{0})
is as in
Proposition 2.2.4
.

For many purposes, it will be enough to take
k=2
and
\varepsilon(2)>0
sufficiently small and work in such charts.
Later on, in
§6.3.9
, we will fix once and for all
k,\varepsilon
(which will depend on the QNI condition).
Until then
\mathcal{L}[q]
will refer to
\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[q]
for
(k,\varepsilon)
to be specified.

2.2.8 Remark

(On Lyapunov radius)
.

From the construction of Lyapunov charts, it follows that there exists a measurable,
\varepsilon
-slowly varying, a.e. nonzero function
r_{k,\varepsilon}(x)
such that if
d^{Q}(x^{\prime},x)\leq r_{k,\varepsilon}(x)
then
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[x]
.
Indeed, we take
r_{k,\varepsilon}^{\prime}\colon Q\to(0,1)
to be
r_{k,\varepsilon}^{\prime}(q)=\frac{1}{\phi_{k,\varepsilon}(q)}{r_{0}}.

Then

B(q,r_{k,\varepsilon}^{\prime}(q))\subset\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[q].

Since
r_{k,\varepsilon}^{\prime}
is tempered, it can be bounded from below by an
\varepsilon
-slowly varying
r_{k,\varepsilon}
.
Recall that we typically drop
(k,\varepsilon)
from the notation of Lyapunov charts.

2.3.
Random Dynamics: The Skew Product Construction

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\Diff_{\infty}(Q)
denote the support of
\mu
.
We consider the two sided shift space
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}
. For
\omega\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

\omega=(\dots,\omega_{-1},\omega_{0},\omega_{1},\dots)
.
We write
\omega=(\omega^{-},\omega^{+})
where
\omega^{-}=(\dots,\omega_{-1})
is the
“past”, and
\omega^{+}=(\omega_{0},\omega_{1},\dots)
is the “future”.
Let
T:\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}
denote the left shift

(T\omega)_{i}=\omega_{i+1}
(which we are
thinking of as “taking one step into the future”).
We use
\hat{T}
to denote the
“skew product” map
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}\times Q\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}\times Q
given by

(2.3.1)

\hat{T}(\omega,q)=(T\omega,\omega_{0}\cdot q).

Let
\hat{Q}=(\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}\times Q)\times[0,1]
.
Let
\hat{T}^{t}

denote the suspension flow on
on
\hat{Q}
, i.e.
\hat{T}^{t}
is obtained as a quotient of the flow
(x,s)\to(x,t+s)
on
(\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}\times Q)\times\mathbb{R}
by the equivalence relation

(x,s+1)\sim(\hat{T}x,s)
, where
\hat{T}
is as in (
2.3.1
).

2.3.2.
Measures on skew-products.

Suppose we are given an ergodic
\mu
-stationary measure
\nu
on
Q
.
As in
[
BQ11
]
,
for

\omega=(\dots,\omega_{-1},\omega_{0},\omega_{1},\dots),

let

\nu_{\omega^{-}}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\omega_{-1}\dots\omega_{-n}\,\nu.

The fact that the limit exists follows from the Martingale
convergence theorem.
Then
\nu_{\omega^{-}}
is a measure on
Q
.

Basic Fact:
Given a
\mu
-stationary measure
\nu
on

Q
, we get a
\hat{T}
-invariant measure
\hat{\nu}

on
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}\times Q
given by

(2.3.3)

d\hat{\nu}(\omega^{-},\omega^{+},q)=d\mu^{\mathbb{Z}}(\omega^{-},\omega^{+})\,d\nu_{\omega^{-}}(q).

It is important that the measure
\hat{\nu}
defined in (
2.3.3
) is a product of a
measure depending on
(\omega^{-},q)
and a measure depending on
\omega^{+}
.
(If instead of the two-sided shift space we use the
one-sided shift
\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{N}}\times Q
, then
\mu^{\mathbb{Z}}\times\nu
would be an invariant measure for
\hat{T}
.)

We also use
\hat{\nu}
to denote the
\hat{T}^{t}
-invariant measure on

\hat{Q}=\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{Z}}\times Q\times[0,1]
given by

d\hat{\nu}(\omega^{-},\omega^{+},q,s)=d\mu^{\mathbb{Z}}(\omega^{-},\omega^{+})\,d\nu_{\omega^{-}}(q)\,dm(s),

where
m(\cdot)
denotes the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}
.

2.3.4 Proposition
.

If
\nu
is an ergodic stationary measure on
Q
, then
the
\hat{T}
-invariant measure
\hat{\nu}
on
\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}\times Q
is
\hat{T}
-ergodic. Also the
\hat{T}^{t}
-invariant measure

\hat{\nu}
on
\hat{Q}
is
\hat{T}^{t}
-ergodic.

Proof.

This
follows from
[
Kif86
, Lemma I.2.4, Theorem I.2.1]

∎

Stable and unstable manifolds.

For
x=(\omega,s)\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}\times[0,1]
, let

W^{-}[x]=\{(\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}\times[0,1]\;\>:\;\>\\
\text{ $s^{\prime}=s$ and for $n\in\mathbb{N}$
sufficiently large, $\omega^{\prime}_{n}=\omega_{n}$}\}.

Then
W^{-}[x]
consists of sequences
y
which eventually agree with

x
. We call
W^{-}[x]
the “stable leaf through
x
”.
We also have the subset

W^{-}_{1}[x]=\{(\omega^{\prime},m^{\prime})\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}\times[0,1]\;\>:\;\>m^{\prime}=m\text{ and
}(\omega^{\prime})^{+}=\omega^{+}\}\subset W^{-}[x].

Similarly, we define

W^{+}[x]=\{(\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}\times[0,1]\;\>:\;\>\\
\text{ $s^{\prime}=s$ and for
$n\in\mathbb{N}$ sufficiently large, $\omega^{\prime}_{-n}=\omega_{-n}$}\},

and we also have the subset

W^{+}_{1}[x]=\{(\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}\times[0,1]\;\>:\;\>(\omega^{\prime})^{-}=\omega^{-}\}\subset W^{+}[x].

For
x=(\omega,s)\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}\times[0,1]
, we define

x^{+}=(\omega^{+},s)
and
x^{-}=(\omega^{-},s)
.
Then
W_{1}^{+}[x]
depends only on
x^{-}
and
W_{1}^{-}[x]
depends only on

x^{+}
.

We now consider the skew-product map.
The base dynamics is the flow
\hat{T}^{t}
on
(\hat{Q},\hat{\nu})
.
Associated to almost every
\hat{q}=(q,\omega,s)\in\hat{Q}

is an unstable manifold
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{u}[\hat{q}]
. It takes the form

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{u}[q,\omega,s]=W^{+}[\omega,s]\times\mathcal{W}^{u}[q,\omega,s],

where
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q,\omega,s]\subset Q
.

We write
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
for the measurable partition whose atom
\mathfrak{B}_{0}[\hat{q}]
is contained in the (fiber) unstable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{u}[\hat{q}]
and satisfies
\hat{T}^{t}\mathfrak{B}_{0}\prec\mathfrak{B}_{0}
for all
t\geq 0
as constructed in
Definition A.5.2
.
Given
\hat{q}=(q,\omega,s)
, we write
\widehat{\mathfrak{B}}_{0}=W^{+}_{1}[\omega,s]\times\mathfrak{B}_{0}[\hat{q}]
. Then
\widehat{\mathfrak{B}}_{0}
is a measurable partition of
\hat{Q}
and also satisfies

\hat{T}^{t}\widehat{\mathfrak{B}}_{0}\prec\widehat{\mathfrak{B}}_{0}
for all
t\geq 0
.
We similarly define
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{B}}_{0}^{-}
.

We use the notations

\mathcal{W}^{u}[q,\omega,s]
,
\mathbf{G}^{sr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q,\omega,s])
,

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q,\omega,s])
,
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q,\omega,s)
,

\mathbf{G}^{sr}(q,\omega,s)\subset\GL(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q,\omega,s))

as in

§3.1.1
.

The flow
\hat{T}^{t}
plays a role analogous to
g_{t}
in the case of

\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-actions. In particular, by “the Lyapunov exponents of the
random dynamical system” we mean the Lyapunov exponents of

\hat{T}^{t}
.

2.4.
Margulis functions

\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
actions

Let

g_{t}=\begin{pmatrix}e^{t}&0\\
0&e^{-t}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL(2,\mathbb{R}),\qquad r_{\theta}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta&\sin\theta\\
-\sin\theta&\cos\theta\end{pmatrix}\in\SL(2,\mathbb{R}).

Let

(A_{\tau}f)(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f(g_{\tau}r_{\theta}x)\,d\theta.

2.4.1 Definition
.

A continuous function
h:Q\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is called an admissible
Margulis function on
Q
if there exist
\tau>0
,
c<1
,
b>0

and
C>0
and
\delta>0
such that the following hold:

(a)

For all
q\in Q
,

(A_{\tau}h)(q)\leq c\;h(q)+b.

(b)

For all
q\in Q
,

h(q)\geq C\phi_{a}(q)^{\delta},

where
\phi_{a}
is the systole function.

Random dynamics

2.4.2 Definition
.

A continuous function
h:Q\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is called an admissible
Margulis function on
Q
if there exist
c<1
,
b>0

and
C>0
and
\delta>0
such that the following hold:

(a)

For all
q\in Q
,

(2.4.3)

\int_{\Diff_{\infty}(Q)}h(f(q))\,d\mu(f)\leq c\,h(q)+b.

(b)

For all
q\in Q
,

h(q)>C\phi_{a}(q)^{\delta},

where
\phi_{a}
is the systole function.

2.4.4 Proposition
.

Suppose that either
Q
is compact or there exists an admissible
Margulis function on
Q
. Let
\nu
be an ergodic
P
-invariant (or in the
random dynamics setting
\mu
-stationary) measure. Also assume in the
random dynamics setting that
\mu
is finitely supported.
Then, (
2.2.2
) holds.

Proof.

Suppose
q\in Q
. Then, iterating (
2.4.3
) we get for any

n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\int_{\Diff_{\infty}(Q)}h(f(q))\,d\mu^{(n)}(f)\leq c^{n}\,h(q)+\frac{b}{1-c}.

where
\mu^{(n)}
is the
n
-fold convolution of
\mu
with itself.
Then, by the random ergodic theorem

[
Kif86
, Corollary I.2.2]
,
for
\nu
-a.e.
q\in Q
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\int_{\Diff_{\infty}(Q)}h(f(q))\,d\mu^{(k)}(f)\to\int_{Q}h\,d\nu.

Thus,

\int_{Q}h\,d\nu\leq\frac{b}{1-c}.

Since
f
is admissible, this implies that for some
\delta>0
,

\int_{Q}\phi_{a}(q)^{\delta}\,d\nu(q)\leq\frac{Cb}{1-c}.

This implies the statement of the lemma.
∎

Avoiding the diagonal

2.4.5 Definition
.

A continuous function
h:Q\times Q\to\mathbb{R}^{+}\cup\{\infty\}
is
called an admissible
Margulis function on
Q\times Q
if there exist
c<1
,
b>0

and
C>0
and
\delta>0
such that the following hold:

(a)

h(x,y)<\infty
unless
x=y
.

(b)

For all
x,y\in Q\times Q
,

(2.4.6)

\int_{\Diff_{\infty}(Q)}h(f(x),f(y)))\,d\mu(f)\leq c\,h(x,y)+b.

(c)

For all
(x,y)\in Q\times Q
,

h(x,y)\geq Cd(x,y)^{-\delta}+C\phi_{a}(x)^{\delta}+C\phi_{a}(y)^{\delta}.

where
\phi_{a}
is the systole function.

2.4.7 Lemma
.

Suppose
Q
is compact and suppose
\mu
satisfies uniform expansion
in dimension
1
(see
Definition 1.1.3
). Then,
there exists a Margulis function on
Q\times Q
.

Proof.

We define the length of a path
\gamma:[0,1]\to Q
to be

\int_{0}^{1}\|\gamma^{\prime}(t)\|\,dt,

where the norm
\|\cdot\|
is as in (
1.1.2
).
Given
x,y\in Q
, let
d(x,y)
be the infimum of the lengths of paths
conecting
x
and
y
. For
\delta>0
, let
h(x,y)=d(x,y)^{-\delta}
. Then, arguing is in the proof of
[
EM04
, Lemma 4.2]
we see that
h
is a Margulis function on
Q\times Q
.
∎

2.4.8.
Dimension bounds

We define, for
x\in Q
, the “lower local dimension”

\underline{\dim}(\nu,x)=\liminf_{r\to 0}\frac{\log\nu(B(x,r)}{\log r},

where

B(x,r)=\{y\in Q\;\>:\;\>d(x,y)<r\}.

2.4.9 Proposition
.

Suppose there exists an admissible
Margulis function on
Q\times Q
. Let
\nu
be ergodic

\mu
-stationary measure. Then, either
\nu
is finitely supported,
or for almost all
x\in Q
,
\underline{\dim}(\nu,x)>0
.

Proof.

This argument is taken from

[
EL18
]
. Suppose
\nu
is not finitely
supported. We note that in view of the
ergodicity of
\nu
,
\nu
has no atoms. Therefore the
\nu\times\nu

measure of the diagonal is
0
.

By iterating (
2.4.6
), for any

x,y\in Q
with
x\neq y
,

(2.4.10)

\limsup_{k\to\infty}\int_{G}h(f(x),f(y)))\,d\mu^{(k)}(f)\leq\frac{b}{1-c}.

By the random ergodic theorem
[
Kif86
, Corollary I.2.2]
, there
exists a function
\phi:Q\times Q\to\mathbb{R}
such
that

\int_{Q\times Q}\phi\,d(\nu\times\nu)=\int_{Q\times Q}f\,d(\nu\times\nu),

and for
\mu^{\mathbb{N}}
a.e.
(f_{1},\dots,f_{n},\dots)\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{N}}
and
\nu\times\nu
a.e.
x
,
y\in Q
,

(2.4.11)

\phi(x,y)=\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}h((f_{j}\dots f_{1})x,(f_{j}\dots f_{1})y).

Then, integrating both sides of (
2.4.11
) over

\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{N}}\times Q\times Q
, using Fatou’s lemma to take
the limsup outside the integral, and then using
(
2.4.10
), we get

\int_{Q\times Q}h\,d(\nu\times\nu)\leq\frac{b}{1-c}.

Therefore, for any
\eta>0
there exists
K^{\prime\prime}\subset Q
with

\nu(K^{\prime\prime})>1-c(\eta)
where
c(\eta)\to 0
as
\eta\to 0
and a constant
C=C(\eta)

such that for any
x\in K^{\prime\prime}
,

(2.4.12)

\int_{Q}h(x,y)\,d\nu(y)<C.

It follows from (
2.4.12
)
that for all
\epsilon>0
and all
x\in K^{\prime\prime}
,

\nu(B(x,\epsilon))\leq C(\eta)\epsilon^{\delta},

hence

\frac{\log\nu(B(x,\epsilon))}{\log\epsilon}\geq\delta-\frac{|\log C(\eta)|}{|\log\epsilon|}.

This implies
\underline{\dim}(\nu,x)\geq\delta
for
x\in K^{\prime\prime}
.
Then, in view of the ergodicity of
\nu
,
the same holds for a.e.
x\in Q
.
∎

2.5.
The finite measurable cover

2.5.1.
Basic setup

The base dynamics is the flow
g_{t}
on
(Q,\nu)
. We assume the
g_{t}
-action on
(Q,\nu)
is ergodic.
Associated to almost every
q\in Q
is an unstable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
.
We have
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
as a subgroup of
\GL(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q))
.
Recall for almost every
q\in Q
and every
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
, we have a well defined
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q^{\prime})
and
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q^{\prime})
and identifications:

(2.5.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}P^{ssr}_{GM}(q,q^{\prime})\colon\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)&\tilde{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q^{\prime})\\
g&\mapsto P^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{GM}(q,q^{\prime})\circ g\circ P^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{GM}(q^{\prime},q)\end{split}

where
P^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{GM}(q,q^{\prime})\colon L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q^{\prime})
is as in

Theorem D.1.2
(see also
§3.1.2
).

If needed, we also write
\mathbf{G}^{sr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q])
and

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q])
to denote the groups of self-maps of

\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
. Thus with this notation, for
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
we
have

\mathbf{G}^{*}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q])=\mathbf{G}^{*}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}])

for
*\in\{sr,ssr\}
.

Let
\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})(q)
denote the Lie algebra of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
; we have

\mathrm{Ad}\left(P^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{GM}(q,q^{\prime})\right)\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})(q)=\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})(q^{\prime}).

Note that this map is the differential of the one defined in
Eqn. (2.5.2)
.
The
g_{t}
-action intertwining
\mathcal{W}^{u}
-manifolds induces an action intertwining the groups
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}
which induces a linear cocycle

g_{t}\colon\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})(q)\to\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})(g_{t}q).

2.5.3.
The measurable cover

Let
L=\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})
. We apply

Theorem B.3.5
to the cocycle
L
and the backwards
dynamics
g_{-t}
to obtain a finite measurable cover
X=Q\times F
. Let
\sigma\colon X\to Q
be the finite-to-one map. We let
g_{t}
denote the
induced cocycle on
X
. We abuse notation and denote the measure on
X
by
\nu
.

We view a point
x\in X
as a pair
(q,\mathfrak{F})
where
q=\sigma(x)

and
\mathfrak{F}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\bullet}L(q)
is a possible flag as in
Theorem B.3.5
.
We emphasize, that since we used the backwards dynamics to construct

X
,
\mathfrak{F}
is a backwards flag.

The cocycles that will be key for our arguments, such as
\mathbf{H}
from
§5.1.9
, can be put in Jordan normal form on
X
(see for instance
Proposition 5.1.6
).

2.5.4.
Stable and unstable manifolds on the measurable cover

Consider a biregular point
q\in Q
and
\mathfrak{F}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\bullet}L(q)
. Let
x=(q,\mathfrak{F})\in\sigma^{-1}(q)
.

We use
Theorem B.3.5
(iii)
to define the measurable stable set
\mathcal{W}^{s}[x]=\mathcal{W}^{s}[q,\mathfrak{F}]
of
x
and
Theorem B.3.5
(iv)
to define the (smooth) unstable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]=\mathcal{W}^{u}[q,\mathfrak{F}]
of
x
in
X
.

Recall from
§A.6

the leaf-wise measure
\nu_{q}^{s}
and
\nu_{q}^{u}
defined on almost every
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
and
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
, respectively.
Given
q
and
x\in\sigma^{-1}(q),
we associate the leaf-wise measures
\nu_{x}^{s}
and
\nu_{x}^{u}
on
\mathcal{W}^{s}[x]
and
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
, respectively via pull-back under the 1-1 map
\sigma\colon\mathcal{W}^{s}[x]\to\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
and
\sigma\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
.

We note that
g_{t}
intertwines stable sets and unstable manifolds on
X
. Moreover, for a.e.
x
and every
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the map
g_{t}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{t}x]
is a diffeomorphism.

2.5.5.
Measurable connections on finite covers

Recall from
§B.2.6
that for any admissible cocycles
E
on
Q
we have measurable connections
P^{\pm}(q,q^{\prime})
for
q,q^{\prime}
on the same stable/unstable manifolds.
For such a cocycle
E
pulled back to
X
, we will use the same notation and define by pullback the measurable connections
P^{\pm}(x,x^{\prime})
for
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}[x]
or
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
(with these manifolds defined as above).
We observe that on the finite cover
X
, for the subcocycle
\mathfrak{u}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{ssr}
, the measurable connections preserve it, by the Ledrappier invariance principle
Corollary B.2.7
.

2.5.6.
Pulling back Markov Partitions

Given a Markov partition
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
of
Q
subordinate to the unstables (see

§A.5
)
we can pull it back to
X
. More precisely, we set

\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]=\sigma^{-1}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}[\sigma(x)])\cap\mathcal{W}^{u}[x].

Similarly, for the partition
\mathfrak{B}^{-}
of
Q
subordinate to the
stables, we set

\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[x]=\sigma^{-1}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[\sigma(x)])\cap\mathcal{W}^{s}[x].

We then use all the notation of
§A.5
.

2.5.7.
Distances and charts in
Q

In addition to standard constructions of stable and unstable manifolds, we will make use of the following distances adapted to the dynamics:

Ambient distance:

d^{Q}
coming from the Riemannian metric on
Q
.

Lyapunov distance:

d^{\mathcal{L}}_{q}(q^{\prime},q^{\prime\prime})
will only be defined for
q^{\prime},q^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[q]
(see
§A.2.9
); the parameters
k,\varepsilon
will be suppressed from the notation.

Unstable distance:

d^{u}(q,q^{\prime})
will only be defined for
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
and will have a uniform contraction property under
g_{-t}
for
t\geq 0
(see
§A.2.10
); the parameter
\varepsilon>0
is suppressed from the notation.

Induced ambient distance:

d^{Q,u}
on
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
induced by the path metric from the ambient distance.

We will also construct the following types of charts adapted to the dynamics:

Lyapunov charts:

\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[q]\subset Q
will denote the
(k,\varepsilon)
-Lyapunov chart at
q
(see
§A.2.9
).

Local unstable manifold:

(see
§A.2.10
)

\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[q]:=\{q^{\prime}\colon d^{u}(q,q^{\prime})<1\}\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[q].

2.5.8.
Distances in
X

For
x_{1},x_{2}\in X
, we will
occasionally abuse notation and write
d^{Q}(x_{1},x_{2})
in place of

d^{Q}(\sigma(x_{1}),\sigma(x_{2}))
.
We equip
X
with the (non-Hausdorff) topology induced by this metric. All notions of convergence in
X
will be with respect to this topology.

For two sets
A,B\subset X
we define

d^{Q}_{min}(A,B):=\inf_{a\in A,b\in B}d^{Q}(a,b).

On the finite cover
X
, the distance
d^{u}
for points in the same unstable is defined such that the map
\sigma\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[\sigma(x)]
is
is a diffeomorphism and an isometry for the
d^{u}
-distance.
Note that
d^{\mathcal{L}}
is only defined for points on
Q
.

2.5.9 Lemma
.

There exists a measurable function
r\colon Q\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that the following holds:
Suppose
x_{1},x_{2},y\in X
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[y]
.
Then,

r(y)d^{Q}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq d^{u}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq r(y)^{-1}d^{Q}(x_{1},x_{2}).

Proof.

This follows from the construction of
d^{u}
in
§A.3
.
∎

2.5.10 Lemma

(Staying in the local unstable)
.

Fix
\beta>0
.
For any
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K\subset Q
of measure at least
1-\delta
with the following property.
For
x_{1},x_{2},y\in X
and
\tau>0
, suppose that
y,g_{-\tau}y\in\sigma^{-1}(K)
,
x_{1}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[y]
, and that
g_{-\tau}x_{1},g_{-\tau}x_{2},\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{-\tau}y]
.
Suppose furthermore that
d^{Q}\left(g_{-t}x_{1},g_{-t}x_{2}\right)\leq e^{-\beta\tau}
for
t\in[0,\tau]
.

Then, provided
\tau
is sufficiently large, we have
x_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[x_{3-i}]

and
d^{u}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq C(\delta)e^{-\beta\tau/2}
.

Proof.

By the definition of
d^{Q}(\cdot,\cdot)
,
\mathcal{W}^{u}[y]
, and
d^{u}(\cdot,\cdot)
on
X
, it suffices to prove the analogous statements for
\sigma(y),\sigma(x_{1}),\sigma(x_{2})
.
Indeed, the projection map
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[\sigma(x)]
is a
d^{u}
-isometry, and the hypotheses already imply that
x_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}\left[x_{3-i}\right]
, so it only remains to establish the distance bounds.
We thus assume for the remainder that
y,x_{1},x_{2}\in Q
.

Fix
\beta>0
. There is
\lambda_{0}>0
and a measurable function
C\colon Q\to[1,\infty)
such that for
\nu
-a.e.
z
,
d^{Q}(g_{-t}z,g_{-t}x)\leq C(z)e^{-\lambda_{0}t}
for all
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[z]
and
t\geq 0
(see
Proposition A.3.2
and
Remark A.3.3
).

Fix a choice of
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{10}\min\{\lambda_{0},\beta\}
and let
\mathcal{L}
be a choice of
(\varepsilon,1)
-Lyapunov charts.
Given such a choice of Lyapunov charts
\mathcal{L}
, we write
\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\mathcal{L}}[y]
for the path component of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[y]\cap\mathcal{L}[y]
containing
y
. For
t\in[0,1]
we have (see
Proposition A.3.2
)

(2.5.11)

g_{t}(\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\mathcal{L}}[y])\cap\mathcal{L}[g_{t}y]=\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\mathcal{L}}[g_{t}y].

Fix
L\gg 1
sufficiently large. Let
K_{0}
be a compact subset of measure at least
1-\delta/5
such that for
z\in K_{0}

(i)

the identity map
(\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[z],d^{Q})\to(\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[z],d^{u})
is
L
-Lipschitz,

(ii)

d^{Q}(g_{-t}z,g_{-t}x)\leq Le^{-\lambda_{0}t}
for all
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[z]
and
t\geq 0
,

(iii)

B^{u}(z,L^{-1}):=\{x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[z]:d^{u}(x,z)<L^{-1}\}\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\mathcal{L}}[z]
, and

(iv)

r(z)\geq L^{-1}
(where
r(\cdot)
is the function that
\varepsilon
-slowly decreases along 2-sided orbits from
Remark 2.2.8
.)

Fix
\tau_{0}>0
so that for all
t\geq\tau_{0}
,

L^{2}e^{-t(\lambda_{0}-\varepsilon)}+Le^{-t(\beta-\varepsilon)}\leq 1.

Fix
L^{\prime}\gg L
sufficiently large and take
K_{1}\subset K_{0}
to be a compact subset of measure at least
1-2/5\delta
such that for
z\in K_{1}

(v)

d^{u}(g_{\tau_{0}}z_{1},g_{\tau_{0}}z_{2})\leq L^{\prime}d^{u}(z_{1},z_{2})
for all
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[z].

Let
K\subset K_{0}\cap g_{\tau_{0}}K_{1}
be a compact subset of measure at least
1-\delta
.
Finally, fix
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
sufficiently large so that

L^{4}e^{-\lambda_{0}\tau}+L^{3}e^{-\tau\beta}\leq 1.

We claim the proposition holds with the above choices.
Indeed,
suppose
y\in K
. It follows for all
t\in[\tau_{0},\tau]
and
i=1,2
that

d^{Q}(g_{-t}y,g_{-t}x_{i})\leq Le^{-t\lambda_{0}}+e^{-t\beta}\leq L^{-1}e^{-\varepsilon t}\leq r(y)e^{-\varepsilon t}\leq r(g_{-t}(y)).

It follows that
g_{-t}x_{i}\in\mathcal{L}[(g_{-t}y)]
for both
i=1,2
and all
t\in[\tau_{0},\tau]
.
Also, supposing
g_{-\tau}y\in K\subset K_{0}
and
g_{-\tau}x_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{-\tau}y]
,
we have

d^{u}(g_{-\tau}y,g_{-\tau}x_{i})\leq Ld^{Q}(g_{-\tau}y,g_{-\tau}x_{i})\leq L^{3}e^{-\tau\lambda_{0}}+L^{2}e^{-\tau\beta}\leq L^{-1}

and so
g_{-\tau}x_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\mathcal{L}}[g_{-\tau}y]
for both
i=1,2
.
By (
2.5.11
),
we have

g_{-t}x_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\mathcal{L}}[g_{-t}y]

for all
\tau_{0}\leq t\leq\tau
and
i=1,2
. In particular,

g_{-\tau_{0}}x_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\mathcal{L}}[g_{-\tau_{0}}y]

for both
i=1,2
. Also since
g_{-\tau_{0}}y\subset K_{1}\subset K_{0}
, we have

d^{u}(g_{-\tau_{0}}y,g_{-\tau_{0}}x_{i})\leq Ld^{Q}(g_{-\tau_{0}}y,g_{-\tau_{0}}x_{i})\leq L^{2}e^{-\tau_{0}\lambda_{0}}+Le^{-\tau_{0}\beta}\leq 1

whence
g_{-\tau_{0}}x_{i}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{-\tau_{0}}y]
for both
i=1,2
.
Since
g_{-\tau_{0}}y\in K_{1}\subset g_{-\tau_{0}}(K)
we have

d^{u}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq L^{\prime}d^{u}(g_{-\tau_{0}}x_{1},g_{-\tau_{0}}x_{2})\leq(L^{\prime}L)d^{Q}(g_{-\tau_{0}}x_{1},g_{-\tau_{0}}x_{2})\leq(L^{\prime}L)e^{-\beta\tau}

and the proposition follows.
∎

2.6.
The skew product and the measurable cover for random dynamics

Let
\hat{X}
be the measurable cover of
\hat{Q}

built as in
§2.5
.
Then, the flow

\hat{T}^{t}
on
\hat{Q}
lifts to a flow (also denoted
\hat{T}^{t}
) on

\hat{X}
. All the constructions of
§2.5

carry over with no modifications.

3.
Generalized u-Gibbs states and QNI

3.1.
Subgroups compatible with the measure

3.1.1.
Setup

Recall that unstable manifolds
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
admits a subresonant structure, see
Appendix C
for the formalism.
We also have the family of subresonant, and strictly subresonant, transformations of the unstable manifold denoted
\mathbf{G}^{sr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]),\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q])
(see
Corollary C.2.8
for the definition of strictly subresonant maps of an unstable manifold).
Note that if
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
then we have a natural identification
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]=\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}]
and hence an identification of the associated groups.
To study the dynamics on unstable manifolds in more detail, we introduce the following notation.

Recall that
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)
denotes the fiber of the linearization cocycle, and we have
g_{t}
-equivariant embeddings
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]\hookrightarrow L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)
such that the linear cocycle maps on fibers of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
induce subresonant maps between unstable manifolds (see
Theorem D.1.2
).
For
q\in Q
, let
\mathbf{G}^{sr}(q)\subset\GL(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q))
denote the group of linear maps induced by subresonant maps of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
.

3.1.2.
Holonomies and relating different groups

Recall that the cocycle
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
admits unstable holonomies
P^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{GM}
, which we will call the “Gauss–Manin” connection.
So for
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[q]
the isomorphism
P^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{GM}(q,q^{\prime})
identifies
\mathbf{G}^{sr}(q)
with
\mathbf{G}^{sr}(q^{\prime})
via conjugation, and similarly for
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}
.
Equivalently, all these natural isomorphisms can be seen via the identification of
\mathbf{G}^{sr}(q)
with the subresonant automorphisms of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
, and then the set-theoretic identification of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
with
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}]
inside
Q
.
We will write:

P_{GM}^{\bullet}(q,q^{\prime})\colon\mathbf{G}^{\bullet}(q)\to\mathbf{G}^{\bullet}(q^{\prime})\quad\text{ for }q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]

where
\bullet\in\{sr,ssr\}
for the corresponding identifications (see also
Eqn. (2.5.2)
).

3.1.3.
Lifting to
X
.

All the constructions of

§3.1.1
and

§3.1.2
naturally
lift from
Q
to
X
. We will thus use notation such as

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
for
x\in X
to denote the lift.

3.1.4.
Stabilizers and subgroups

For
x\in X
, let also
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}_{x}(x)
denote the
stabilizer
of
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
inside
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
.
Note that this family of subgroups is
not
preserved by the holonomy identifications.

Recall that the strictly subresonant maps
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
are a unipotent algebraic group, and we will study unipotent algebraic subgroups
U^{+}(x)\subseteq\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
, which we identify with their Lie algebras
\mathfrak{u}^{+}(x)
.
For such subgroups, we will also denote by
U^{+}_{x}(x)
and
\mathfrak{u}^{+}_{x}(x)
the group and Lie algebra of the stabilizer of
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
.

Additionally, for a subgroup
U^{+}(x)
we will denote by
U^{+}[x]:=U^{+}(x)\cdot x\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
the corresponding orbit of
U^{+}
on the unstable manifold.
As a
U^{+}(x)
-homogeneous space, it is isomorphic to
U^{+}(x)/U^{+}_{x}(x)
.
Note that since both groups are unipotent (as subgroups of the unipotent
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}
), they are unimodular and hence there is a unique up to scaling Haar measure on the quotient.

An important property of
U^{+}[x]
is that it is an algebraic subset of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
(and not just a connected component of an algebraic subset), when
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
is viewed with its subresonant structure.
Indeed, by
[
Ser02
, III§2, Prop. 6]
the Galois cohomology group
H^{1}(\mathbb{R},\mathbf{U})
vanishes for any unipotent algebraic group
\mathbf{U}
, therefore
U^{+}[x]
coincides with the real points of the algebraic variety corresponding to the orbit
U^{+}(x)\cdot x
(see also
[
Zim84
, Thm. 3.1.1]
).

The following is one of the fundamental definitions we will use, following
[
EM18
, Def. 6.2]
.
For notation on measurable partitions, see
§A.5
.

3.1.5 Definition

(Compatible family of subgroups)
.

Let
\nu
be an ergodic,
g_{t}
-invariant probability measure
on
X
which is the lift of an ergodic,
g_{t}
-invariant
probability measure on
Q.

We say that a measurable family of connected algebraic subgroups
x\mapsto U^{+}(x)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
is
compatible with
\nu
if the following hold:

(i)

The subgroups are
g_{t}
-equivariant.

(ii)

Let
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
be the partition of
X
defined in

§A.5
and
§
2.5.6
.
Then the sets of the form
U^{+}[x]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]
form a measurable partition of
X
.

Furthermore, the conditional measures along
U^{+}[x]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]
are proportional to the restriction of the unique
U^{+}(x)
-invariant measure on
U^{+}[x]
, which will be called the Haar measure on
U^{+}[x]
.

(iii)

Given
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
, we have natural identification of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
and
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x^{\prime})
, viewing both as acting on
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]=\mathcal{W}^{u}[x^{\prime}]
.
By further restricting to a set of full measure, if
x^{\prime},x\in X_{0}
and
x^{\prime}\in U^{+}[x]
, then
U^{+}(x)=U^{+}(x^{\prime})
.

Equivalently, viewed inside
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
and
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x^{\prime})
, the groups
U^{+}(x)
and
U^{+}(x^{\prime})
are identified by the holonomy
P_{GM}^{ssr}(x,x^{\prime})
as in
§3.1.2
.

(iv)

U^{+}(x)
is maximal in the sense that it is the largest connected subgroup that preserves the Haar measure on
U^{+}[x]
.

We say that the family
x\to U^{+}(x)
is
non-trivial
if
\dim U^{+}[x]>0
for
\nu
-a.e.
x
.

3.1.6 Remark

(On measures with local invariance)
.

The above
Definition 3.1.5
is meant to formalize the following situation.
Suppose
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
is equipped with its leafwise (conditional) measure
\nu^{u}_{x}
.
We would like to consider the possibility that
\nu^{u}_{x}
-a.e. point
y
has associated to it a group
U^{+}(y)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])
, but the groups
U^{+}(y)
need not be the same.
Nonetheless, the above definition expresses the possibility that the measure
\nu^{u}_{x}
is “Haar along
U^{+}
-orbits”.

3.1.7 Definition

(Generalized
u
-Gibbs state)
.

Let
\nu
be an ergodic,
g_{t}
-invariant probability measure on
X

which is the lift of an ergodic,
g_{t}
-invariant probability measure
on
Q
. Suppose there exists a non-trivial family
x\to U^{+}(x)
of
subgroups compatible with
\nu
. Then, we call
\nu
a “generalized

u
-Gibbs state”.

3.2.
The QNI condition for extra invariance

Let
x\mapsto U^{+}(x)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
be
a non-trivial measurable family of connected algebraic subgroups which is
compatible with
\nu
in the sense of

§3.1
.

Our strategy is to show that under some conditions on
U^{+}(\cdot)
,
there
exists a family of subgroups

U_{new}^{+}(x)
of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
compatible with
\nu
in the sense of

Definition 3.1.5
such that for almost all

x
we have
U^{+}(x)\subset U_{new}^{+}(x)
and
U^{+}(x)\neq U_{new}^{+}(x)
.
(See
Theorem 4.1.2
for the precise
statement).

3.2.1.
The QNI Condition

We start with a technical condition which encapsulates what is
needed for our proof to go through. It was introduced in

[
Kat23
]

in a somewhat different situation.

3.2.2 Definition

(QNI)
.

We say that
U^{+}(\cdot)
satisfies QNI (quantitative
non-integrability) if the following holds. There exists

•

\alpha_{0}>0
and,

•

For every
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
with

\nu(K)>1-\delta
, and constants
C=C(\delta)>0
and
\ell_{0}=\ell_{0}(\delta)>0
such that:

If
\ell>\ell_{0}
and
q_{1/2}\in K
,
q_{1}\equiv g_{\ell/2}q_{1/2}\in K
and
q\equiv g_{-\ell/2}q_{1/2}\in K
then

•

There is a subset
S=S(q_{1/2},\ell)\subset\mathcal{B}_{\ell/2}[q_{1/2}]
with
|S|\geq(1-\delta)|\mathcal{B}_{\ell/2}[q_{1/2}|]
(recall that
|\bullet|
is the Haar measure on
U^{+}[q_{1}/2]
).
Furthermore for each
y_{1/2}\in S
:

•

There is a subset
S^{\prime}=S^{\prime}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2},\ell)\subset\mathfrak{B}^{-}_{\ell/2}[q_{1/2}]
with
\nu^{s}_{q_{1/2}}(S^{\prime})\geq(1-\delta)\nu^{s}_{q_{1/2}}(\mathfrak{B}^{-}_{\ell/2}[q_{1/2}])
so that if
q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in S^{\prime}

(3.2.3)

d^{Q}_{min}\left(\mathcal{B}_{0}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}],\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{loc}[y_{1/2}]\right)\geq Ce^{-\alpha_{0}\ell},

where
d^{Q}_{min}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in §
2.5.8
.

3.2.4 Remark
.

The center-stable manifolds in
Eqn. (3.2.3)
are defined in

§A.4.5
. It is easy to
see that for
\ell
large enough and on a set of
y_{1/2}
of measure
arbitrarily close to
1
, the validity of
Eqn. (3.2.3)
depends
on the center-stable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{loc}[y_{1/2}]
only via its
its
k
-jet for some
k\gg\alpha_{0}
. Thus the arbitrary choice in

§A.4.5
does not affect
the validity of the QNI condition.

3.2.5.
Choice of constants

The QNI condition in
Definition 3.2.2
provides a constant
\alpha_{0}>0
.
We will then choose constants
\alpha_{2}
in
Lemma 8.1.18
and
\alpha_{3}
in
§8.1.22
.
Finally
§8.4.11
provides an integer
N>0
which will give a degree of approximation.

3.2.6 Remark
.

The QNI condition is used only in the proof of

Lemma 8.1.18
.

3.3.
Random Dynamics: subgroups compatible with the measure and the QNI Condition

In this section we restate the definitions of
§3.1
and
§3.2
for the case of random dynamics.
Let
\hat{T}^{t}:\hat{X}\to\hat{X}
be as in
§
2.6
.
As in
§3.1.4
, let

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}_{x}(x,\omega,s)
denote the stabilizer of
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x,\omega.s]
inside
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x,\omega,s)
.

As above the strictly subresonant maps
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x,\omega,s)
are a unipotent algebraic group, and we will study unipotent algebraic subgroups
U^{+}(x,\omega,s)\subseteq\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x,\omega,s)
, which we identify with their Lie algebras
\mathfrak{u}^{+}(x,\omega,s)
.
For such subgroups, we will also denote by
U^{+}_{x}(x,\omega,s)
and
\mathfrak{u}^{+}_{x}(x,\omega,s)
the group and Lie algebra of the stabilizer of
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x,\omega,s]
.

Additionally, for a subgroup
U^{+}(x,\omega,s)
we will denote by
U^{+}[q,\omega,s]:=U^{+}(x,\omega,s)\cdot q\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[x,\omega,s]
the corresponding orbit of
U^{+}
on the unstable manifold.
As a
U^{+}(x,\omega,s)
-homogeneous space, it is isomorphic to
U^{+}(x,\omega,s)/U^{+}_{x}(x,\omega,s)
.
Note that since both groups are unipotent (as subgroups of the
unipotent
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}
), they are unimodular and hence there is a
unique up to scaling Haar measure on the quotient.

The following is the analogue of

Definition 3.1.5
(which in turn follows

[
EM18
, Def. 6.2]
):

3.3.1 Definition

(Random Compatible family of subgroups)
.

Let
\nu
be a ergodic
\mu
-stationary measure on
Q
, and let

\hat{\nu}
be ergodic,
\hat{T}^{t}
-invariant probability measure on
\hat{X}

given by (
2.3.3
).
We say that a measurable family of connected algebraic subgroups

(x,\omega,s)\mapsto U^{+}(x,\omega,s)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x,\omega,s)
is
compatible with
\hat{\nu}
if the following hold:

(i)

The subgroups are
\hat{T}^{t}
-equivariant.

(ii)

Let
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
be the partition of
X
defined in

§A.5
and
§
2.5.6
.
Then for almost all
\omega
,
the sets of the form
U^{+}[x,\omega,s]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x,\omega,s]
form a partition of
Q
.

Furthermore, the conditional measures along
U^{+}[x,\omega,s]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x,\omega,s]
are proportional to the restriction of the unique
U^{+}(x,\omega,s)
-invariant measure on
U^{+}[x,\omega,s]
, which will be called the Haar measure on
U^{+}[x,\omega,s]
.

(iii)

Given
(x^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{u}[x,\omega,s]
, we have natural
identification of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x,\omega,s)
and
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})
, viewing both as acting on
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x,\omega,s]=\mathcal{W}^{u}[x^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime}]
.
By further restricting to a set
\hat{X}_{0}\subset\hat{X}
of full measure, if
(x^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})
and

(x,\omega,s)\in\hat{X}_{0}
and
(x^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})\in U^{+}[x,\omega,s]
, then

U^{+}(x^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},s)=U^{+}(x,\omega,s)
.

(iv)

U^{+}(x,\omega,s)
is maximal in the sense that it is the largest
(connected) subgroup of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x,\omega,s)
that preserves the Haar measure on
U^{+}[x,\omega,s]
.

3.3.2 Remark
.

In the random dynamics setting,
U^{+}(x,\omega,s)=\{e\}
is allowed.

As in the
SL(2,\mathbb{R})
action setting, this is meant to formalize
the following:

3.3.3 Remark

(On measures with local invariance)
.

The above
Definition 3.3.1
is meant to formalize the following situation.
Suppose
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x,\omega,s]
is equipped with its leafwise (conditional) measure
\hat{\nu}^{u}_{(x,\omega,s)}
.
We would like to consider the possibility that
\hat{\nu}^{u}_{(x,\omega,s)}
-a.e. point
(y,\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})
has associated to it a group
U^{+}(y,\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x,\omega,s])
, but the groups
U^{+}(y,\omega^{\prime},s^{\prime})
need not be the same.
Nonetheless, the above definition expresses the possibility that the measure
\hat{\nu}^{u}_{(x,\omega,s)}
is “Haar along
U^{+}
-orbits”.

Let
(x,\omega,s)\mapsto U^{+}(x,\omega,s)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x,\omega,s)
be
a measurable family of connected algebraic subgroups which is
compatible with
\hat{\nu}
in the sense of

§3.3
.

3.3.4 Definition

(Random QNI)
.

For
(q,\omega,s)\in\hat{X}
, let

\hat{U}^{+}[q,\omega,s]=W_{1}^{+}[\omega,s]\times U^{+}[q,\omega,s]\subset\hat{\mathcal{W}}^{u}[q,\omega,s].

We say that
U^{+}(\cdot,\cdot)
satisfies Random QNI
if the following holds. There exists

•

\alpha_{0}>0
and,

•

For every
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
with

\hat{\nu}(K)>1-\delta
, and constants
C=C(\delta)>0
and
\ell_{0}=\ell_{0}(\delta)>0
such that:

If
\ell>\ell_{0}
and
\hat{q}_{1/2}=(q_{1/2},\omega_{1/2},s_{1/2})\in K
,
\hat{q}_{1}=\hat{T}^{\ell/2}\hat{q}_{1/2}\in K
and
\hat{q}\equiv\hat{T}^{-\ell/2}\hat{q}_{1/2}\in K
then

•

There is a subset
S=S(\hat{q}_{1/2},\ell)\subset\mathcal{B}_{\ell/2}[\hat{q}_{1/2}]

with
|S|\geq(1-\delta)|\mathcal{B}_{\ell/2}[\hat{q}_{1/2}]|
(where
|\bullet|
is Haar measure on
U^{+}[\hat{q}_{1/2}]
).
And for each
\hat{y}_{1/2}\in S
:

•

There is a subset
S^{\prime}=S^{\prime}(\hat{y}_{1/2},\ell)\subset\mathfrak{B}^{-}_{\ell/2}[\hat{q}_{1/2}]
with
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{q}_{1/2}}(S^{\prime})\geq(1-\delta)\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{q}_{1/2}}(\mathfrak{B}^{-}_{\ell/2}[\hat{q}_{1/2}])
so that if
\hat{q}^{\prime}_{1/2}\in S^{\prime}

(3.3.5)

d^{Q}_{min}(\mathcal{B}_{\ell/2}[\hat{q}^{\prime}_{1/2}],\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{loc}[\hat{y}_{1/2}])\geq Ce^{-\alpha_{0}\ell},

where
d^{Q}_{min}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in §
2.5.8
.

4.
Main Theorems and Proofs

4.1.
The Main Theorems

4.1.1.
Extra Invariance

Our main result (which applies even if we have a single diffeomorphism or a flow) is the following:

4.1.2 Theorem
.

Suppose
\nu
is an ergodic,
g_{t}
-invariant measure on
Q
.
Suppose
x\mapsto U^{+}(x)
is a nontrivial
family of subgroups of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
compatible
with
\nu
in the sense of
Definition 3.1.5
.

Suppose the family
x\to U^{+}(x)
satisfies the QNI condition (see

Definition 3.2.2
). Then, there
exists a family of subgroups

U_{new}^{+}(x)
of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
compatible with
\nu
in the sense of

Definition 3.1.5
such that for almost all

x
we have
U^{+}(x)\subset U_{new}^{+}(x)
and
U^{+}(x)\neq U_{new}^{+}(x)
.

All of our other results are derived from
Theorem 4.1.2
(and its version for skew-products
Theorem 4.1.4
).

We note the following immediate:

4.1.3 Corollary
.

Suppose
\nu
is an ergodic,
g_{t}
-invariant measure on
Q
.
Suppose there exists
a family of subgroups of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
compatible
with
\nu
in the sense of
Definition 3.1.5
.

Then there exists (another) family
x\to U^{+}(x)
of subgroups compatible with
\nu
such that the
the QNI condition
Definition 3.2.2
does not hold for
U^{+}
.

Proof.

This is obtained by repeatedly applying
Theorem 4.1.2
.
∎

Theorem 4.1.2
also applies to skew-products. More precisely, we have the following:

4.1.4 Theorem
.

Suppose
\nu
is an ergodic,
\mu
-stationary measure on
Q
, and let
\hat{\nu}
be as in (
2.3.3
).
Suppose
\hat{x}\to U^{+}(\hat{x})
is a family of subgroups of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
compatible
with
\hat{\nu}
in the sense of
Definition 3.3.1
.
(Note that
U^{+}=\{e\}
is allowed). Suppose the family
q\to U^{+}(q)
satisfies the Random QNI condition (see

Definition 3.3.4
). Then, there
exists a family of subgroups

U_{new}^{+}(x)
of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
compatible with
\hat{\nu}
in the sense of

Definition 3.3.1
such that for almost all

x
we have
U^{+}(x)\subset U_{new}^{+}(x)
and
U^{+}(x)\neq U_{new}^{+}(x)
.

As above, we have the following immediate corollary:

4.1.5 Corollary
.

Suppose
\nu
is an ergodic,
\mu
-stationary measure on
Q
, and let
\hat{\nu}
be as in (
2.3.3
).
Then there exists a family
x\to U^{+}(x)
of subgroups compatible with
\nu
such that the
the Random QNI condition

Definition 3.3.4
) does not hold for
U^{+}
.

4.1.6.
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-actions

Our main results for general
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
actions are

Theorem 1.2.4
and
the following theorem:

4.1.7 Theorem
.

Suppose
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
acts on
Q
, and suppose
\nu
is an
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant measure on
Q
. Suppose there is no
P
-invariant
\nu
-measurable subbundle of
TQ
on which the sum of the Lyapunov exponents is negative. Then

(i)

For almost all
q\in Q
,
there exists a subgroup
U^{+}(q)
of

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q])
such that the conditional of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
is the Haar measure along the orbit
U^{+}[q]\equiv U^{+}(q)q
.

(ii)

For almost all
q\in Q
there exists a subgroup
U^{-}(q)
of

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{s}[q])
such that the conditional of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
is the Haar measure along the orbit
U^{-}[q]\equiv U^{-}(q)q
.

(iii)

The sum of the Lyapunov exponents of the bundle
T_{q}U^{+}[q]\oplus T_{q}U^{-}[q]
is
0
.

(iv)

The family of subgroups
q\to U^{+}(q)
is compatible
with the measure (see

Definition 3.1.5
) and the
QNI condition
Definition 3.2.2
does not
hold.

4.1.8.
Random Dynamics

Our main results for random dynamics are

Theorem 1.1.11
and the following:

4.1.9 Theorem
.

Suppose
\mu
is as in
Theorem 1.1.11
.
Suppose
\nu
is an ergodic
\mu
-invariant measure on
Q
, and suppose that
there is no
\mu
-invariant
\nu
-measurable subbundle of
TQ
(see
Definition 1.1.10
) on which the sum of the Lyapunov exponents is negative. Then,

(i)

For almost all
(q,\omega,s)\in\hat{Q}
,
there exists a subgroup
U^{+}(q,\omega,s)
of

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q,\omega,s])
such that the conditional of
\hat{\nu}

along
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q,\omega,s]
is the Haar measure along the orbit

U^{+}[q,\omega,s]\equiv U^{+}(q,\omega,s)q
.

(ii)

For almost all
(q,\omega,s)\in\hat{Q}
,
there exists a subgroup
U^{-}(q,\omega,s)
of

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{s}[q,\omega,s])
such that the conditional of
\hat{\nu}

along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q,\omega,s]
is the Haar measure along the orbit

U^{-}[q,\omega,s]\equiv U^{-}(q,\omega,s)q
.

(iii)

The sum of the Lyapunov exponents of the bundle
T_{q}U^{+}[q,\omega,s]\oplus T_{q}U^{-}[q,\omega,s]
is
0
.

(iv)

The family of subgroups
(q,\omega,s)\to U^{+}(q,\omega,s)
is compatible
with the measure (see

Definition 3.3.1
) and the
Random QNI condition
Definition 3.3.4
does not
hold.

The following theorem summarizes our progress towards Conjecture
1.1.12
.

4.1.10 Theorem
.

Suppose
Q
is a symplectic manifold, and suppose
\mu
is a finitely
supported probability measure on the group of symplectic diffeomorphisms of
Q
,
which satisfies uniform expansion on all the isotropic subspaces of
TQ
. Suppose
\nu
is a
\mu
-stationary measure on
Q
. Then
\nu
is
\mu
-invariant. Furthermore,
T_{q}U^{+}[q]\oplus T_{q}U^{-}[q]
is symplectic and also
conclusions (i)-(iv) of
Theorem 4.1.9
hold for
\nu
.

4.2.
Proof of the main theorems for
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
actions assuming
Theorem 4.1.2
.

4.2.1.
Stable support

Recall that
\nu^{s}_{q}
denotes the conditional measure
of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
.
For a.e.
q\in Q
,
let
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\subset\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
denote the smallest real-algebraic subset
for which there exists
\epsilon(q)>0
such that the intersection of the
ball of radius
\epsilon(q)
with support of
\nu^{s}_{q}
is contained in
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
.
Then,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is
g_{t}
-equivariant.
Since
the action of
g_{-t}
is expanding along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
, we see that for
almost all
q
and any
\epsilon>0
,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is the smallest
real-algebraic subset of
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
such that
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
contains

\operatorname{support}(\nu^{s}_{q})\cap B^{Q}(q,\epsilon)
.

4.2.2.
Smoothness and irreducibility

Let
T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\subset T_{x}\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
be the tangent space at
q
to
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
.
Note that for
\nu
-a.e.
q
the set
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is smooth at
q
, since for an algebraic set, the subset of singular points is a proper algebraic subset.
Note that
T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is
g_{t}
-invariant by construction.
Furthermore, we note that
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is irreducible.
Indeed, note that a.e.
q
sits on a unique irreducible component of
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
(otherwise, we can replace
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
by the union of the intersection loci of any pair of irreducible components).
Therefore,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
has to agree with that irreducible component for a.e.
q
.

4.2.3.
The bundle
\mathcal{Z}

Given
q\in Q
, let
T_{q}Q=E^{s}(q)\oplus E^{c}(q)\oplus E^{u}(q)
be the splitting into negative, zero, and positive Lyapunov exponents.
Let
\pi^{cs}_{q}\colon T_{q}Q\to E^{u}(q)
be the canonical projection.

We let

(4.2.4)

\mathcal{Z}(q)=\mathrm{ess\,span}\{n^{-1}T_{n\cdot q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[n\cdot q]:{n\in N}\}.

(Here
\mathrm{ess\,span}
denotes the essential span).

Note that the bundle
\mathcal{Z}
is
P
-invariant.
Let
\bar{\mathfrak{n}}(q)\subset T_{x}Q
denote the tangent space to the
orbit of
\bar{N}\subset\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
, and let

(4.2.5)

\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(q)=\mathcal{Z}(q)+\bar{\mathfrak{n}}(q).

Then the bundle
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is also
P
-invariant.

4.2.6.
Lifting to
X

Recall that the measure
\nu
lifts to
X
and we continue to denote it by
\nu
.
For
x\in X
declare
\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]

to be
\mathcal{W}^{s}[x]\cap\sigma^{-1}(\mathcal{L}^{s}[\sigma(x)])
, where
\sigma\colon X\to Q
is the projection map.
Similarly, we let
T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]:=T_{\sigma(x)}\mathcal{L}^{s}[\sigma(x)]
and similarly
\mathcal{Z}(x):=\mathcal{Z}(\sigma(x))
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x):=\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(\sigma(x))
.
The center-stable projection
\pi^{cs}_{x}
is defined analogously.

Let
U^{+}(x)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
be as in
Definition 3.1.5
and denote by
U^{+}[x]=U^{+}(x)\cdot x
the
U^{+}(x)
-orbit of
x
.

4.2.7 Lemma
.

The following are equivalent:

(1)

For a positive (and thus full)
\nu
-measure subset
of
x\in X
we have

(4.2.8)

\pi^{cs}_{x}\left(\mathcal{Z}(x)\right)\subset T_{x}U^{+}[x].

(2)

For a positive (and thus full)
\nu
-measure subset of
x\in X
there exists a positive (and thus full) Lebesgue measure subset
E_{x}\subset N
such that for
n\in E_{x}
we have

\pi^{cs}_{nx}\left(n\cdot T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}\left(x\right)\right)\subset T_{nx}U^{+}[nx].

We remark that to go from a positive to a full measure subset of
x\in X
in (1) and (2) we use the ergodicity of
g_{t}
. To go from a
positive to a full measure subset of
n\in N
in (2) we use

[
EM18
, Lemma 8.3]

(with
U^{+}
replaced by
N
).

Proof.

Suppose (1) holds. Then, in view of the definition of
\mathcal{Z}(x)
, for a.e.
x\in X
and a.e.
n\in N
,
n^{-1}\cdot T_{nx}\mathcal{L}^{s}[nx]\subset\mathcal{Z}(x)
. Then,
letting
y=nx
, we have, for a.e.
y\in X
and a.e.
n\in N
,

n^{-1}\cdot T_{y}\mathcal{L}^{s}[y]\subset\mathcal{Z}(n^{-1}y)
. Replacing
y
by
x
and
n
by
n^{-1}
, we get for a.e.
x\in X
and a.e.
n\in N
,

n\cdot T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]\subset\mathcal{Z}(nx).

Thus, for a.e.
x\in X
and a.e.
n\in N
,

\pi^{cs}_{nx}(n\cdot T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x])\subset\pi_{nx}^{cs}(\mathcal{Z}(nx))\subset T_{nx}U^{+}[nx],

where we have used the condition (1) for the final inclusion. Thus (2) holds.

Now suppose (2) holds. Then,
for a.e.
y\in X
, for a.e.
n\in N
,

\pi^{cs}_{ny}(n\cdot T_{y}\mathcal{L}^{s}[y])\subset T_{ny}U^{+}[ny].

Using Fubini’s theorem
we can let
ny=x
. Then, for a.e.
x\in X
, and a.e.
n\in N
,

\pi^{cs}_{x}(n\cdot T_{n^{-1}x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[n^{-1}x])\subset T_{x}U^{+}[x].

Replacing
n
by
n^{-1}
, we get for a.e.
x\in X
, for a.e.
n\in N
,

\pi^{cs}_{x}(n^{-1}\cdot T_{nx}\mathcal{L}^{s}[nx])\subset T_{x}U^{+}[x].

Since this is true for a fixed
x
for a.e.
n\in N
, we get using the definition of
\mathcal{Z}(x)
,

\pi^{cs}_{x}(\mathcal{Z}(x))\subset T_{x}U^{+}[x].

Thus (1) holds.
∎

4.2.9 Lemma
.

Suppose one of the two equivalent conditions of
Lemma 4.2.7
is not satisfied.
Then the family
x\to U^{+}(x)
satisfies the QNI condition (see
Definition 3.2.2
).

Proof.

See
[
ES25
]
.
∎

4.2.10.
Facts from entropy theory for flows

Let
g_{1}
denote
g_{t}
for
t=1
; similarly, let

g_{-1}
denote
g_{t}
for
t=-1
. Let

h_{\nu}(g_{t})

denote the entropy of the transformation
g_{t}
with respect to the
invariant measure
\nu
. For almost all
q\in Q
we have the Lyapunov
splitting for
g_{t}
(see
Theorem A.1.5
)

T_{q}Q=\oplus_{i}E^{\lambda_{i}}(q),

where the Lyapunov exponents
\lambda_{i}
satisfy
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}>\dots
. For
x\in\sigma^{-1}(q)
, we define

E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)=E^{\lambda_{i}}(q)
.

4.2.11 Remark

(Entropy and finite covers)
.

Recall that
X\to Q
is a finite cover, with measures and flows denoted
\nu,g_{t}
on both spaces.
Given a measurable partition
\xi
of
Q
whose atoms are contained in
\mathcal{W}^{u}
-manifolds, we write
\tilde{\xi}
for the associated measurable partition of
X
such that
\tilde{\xi}[x]\to\xi[x]
is a bijection for every
x\in Q
.
Then
\tilde{\xi}
is a finite refinement of
\sigma^{-1}(\xi)
.

Suppose now that
\xi
is
g_{t}
-invariant.
Then it follows that
h_{\nu}(g_{t},\tilde{\xi})=h_{\nu}(g_{t},\xi)
. Indeed, we have

h_{\nu}(g_{t},\tilde{\xi})\geq h_{\nu}(g_{t},\xi)
since
\tilde{\xi}
is a finite refinement of
\sigma^{-1}\xi
; the reverse inequality follows from
[
KRH10
, Lem. 6.1]
.

In particular,
h_{\nu}(g_{t})
is independent of whether we work on
X
or
Q
.

4.2.12 Proposition
.

For
\nu
-a.e.
x\in X
, the following hold:

(i)

h_{\nu}(g_{1})\geq\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap T_{x}U^{+}[{x}]
.

(ii)

h_{\nu}(g_{-1})\leq\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}({x})\cap T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x].

(iii)

We have
h_{\nu}(g_{-1})=h_{\nu}(g_{-1},\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-})
.

Note that the last two assertions also make sense on
Q
, and follow on
X
from the corresponding statement on
Q
, see
Remark 4.2.11
.

Proof.

We note that
(iii)
follows from
Proposition A.5.3
(applied to time-reversed flow
g_{-t}
).
Next,
(ii)
follows from
[
BRHW23
, Thm. 7.2]
combined with
(iii)
.

Finally
(i)
follows from the same
[
BRHW23
, Thm. 7.2]
by noting that
h_{\nu}(g_{1})\geq h_{\nu}(g_{1};\mathfrak{U})
where
\mathfrak{U}
is the measurable partition obtained by refining the
U^{+}[x]
-orbits by
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
.
We note that the proof of
[
BRHW23
, Thm. 7.2]
holds in our setting, for the lift of the
\mathcal{W}^{u}
-foliation to the measurable finite cover
X
.
∎

4.2.13 Proposition
.

Suppose for
\nu
-a.e.
q\in Q
, that
\nu^{u}_{q}
is supported on an embedded submanifold
M_{q}\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
. Then

h_{\nu}(g_{1})\leq\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}({q})\cap T_{q}M_{{q}}.
