Moreover, equality holds if and only if

\nu^{u}_{q}
equivalent to the Lebesgue measure on
M_{q}
for a.e.
q\in Q
.

Proof.

This follows again from
[
BRHW23
, Thm. 7.2]
.
∎

4.2.14 Proposition
.

Suppose that
\nu
is an ergodic
g_{t}
-invariant probability measure on
Q
.
Suppose for a.e.
q\in Q
that the conditional measure
\nu^{\overline{N}}_{q}
along the
\overline{N}
-orbit
\overline{N}\cdot q
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Haar measure along the orbit
\overline{N}\cdot q
. Then
\nu
is
\overline{N}
-invariant.

Proof.

Indeed, it follows exactly as in the proof of
[
Led84
, Thm. 3.4]
that conditional measure
\nu^{\overline{N}}_{q}
along a.e.
\overline{N}
-orbit is equivalent to the Haar measure with a constant density. This implies
\overline{N}
-invariance. ∎

4.2.15 Proposition

(Entropy balancing)
.

Suppose that

(4.2.16)

the sum of the Lyapunov exponents along
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is non-negative,

where
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is as in
Eqn. (4.2.5)
.
Furthermore, suppose that for
\nu
-a.e.
x\in X
,

Eqn. (4.2.8)
holds.

Then
\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant.
Furthermore,

(i)

U^{+}(x)
depends only on
\sigma(x)
, where
\sigma:X\to Q
is the covering map of
§2.5
.

(ii)

For almost all
q\in Q
, the conditional of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
is the Haar measure along the orbit
U^{+}[q]
.

(iii)

For almost all
q\in Q
there exists a subgroup
U^{-}(q)
of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{s}[q])
such that the conditional of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
is the Haar measure along the orbit
U^{-}[q]\equiv U^{-}(q)q
. In particular,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]=U^{-}[q]
.

(iv)

The sum of the Lyapunov exponents of the bundle
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is
0
.

Proof.

Since for
t>0
the action of
g_{t}
on
\overline{N}
is contracting,
\pi^{cs}_{x}\left(\mathcal{Z}(x)\right)=\pi^{cs}_{x}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)\right)
. Therefore, in view of (
4.2.8
)

(4.2.17)

\pi^{cs}_{x}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)\right)=\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)\cap T_{x}\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]\subset T_{x}U^{+}[x].

Let
g_{1}
denote
g_{t}
for
t=1
.
From
Proposition 4.2.12
(i)
, we have a lower bound

\displaystyle h_{\nu}(g_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap T_{x}U^{+}[x]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x).

where the final inequality follows from
Eqn. (4.2.17)
.

Similarly, by the definition of
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)

we have
T_{x}(\overline{N}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x])\subset\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)
whence by
Proposition 4.2.12
(ii)
,

\displaystyle h_{\nu}(g_{-1})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap T_{x}(\overline{N}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x])

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x).

In view of (
4.2.16
),

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\lambda_{i}}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)+\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle\leq-h_{\nu}(g_{-1})+h_{\nu}(g_{1})

Since
h_{\nu}(g_{-1})=h_{\nu}(g_{1})
, we conclude that the sum of the Lyapunov exponents along
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is
0
. Also,

(4.2.18)

\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap T_{x}(\overline{N}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x])=h_{\nu}(g_{-1})=\\
=h_{\nu}(g_{1})=\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap T_{x}U^{+}[x].

From
Proposition 4.2.13
(for
g_{-1}
), we conclude that stable conditionals measure
\nu^{s}_{x}
is absolutely continuous along
\overline{N}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]
; since the foliation by
\overline{N}
-orbits is smooth, we conclude the conditionals measure
\nu^{\overline{N}}_{x}
is absolutely continuous along the orbit
\overline{N}\cdot x
. By
Proposition 4.2.14
, we conclude that
\nu
is
\overline{N}
-invariant; this in particular, implies
\overline{N}\cdot\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]=\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]
.

Let
\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
denote the smallest algebraic subset of full
\nu^{u}_{x}
measure. Note that since the measure on
X
is a lift of the measure on
Q
,
\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]
depends only on
\sigma(x)
where
\sigma:X\to Q
is the projection map.

There exists
w\in\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
such that
wg_{1}w^{-1}=g_{-1}
. Since
\nu
is now
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant, we get, for a.e.
x\in X
,
\nu^{u}_{x}=w\nu^{s}_{w^{-1}x}
. In particular,
\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]=w\mathcal{L}^{s}[w^{-1}x]
. Thus, for a.e.
x\in X
,
\nu^{u}_{x}
is absolutely continuous on
\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]
. Note that
U^{+}[x]\subset\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]
.
By
Proposition 4.2.13
, we have

h_{\nu}(g_{1})=\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]\geq\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap T_{x}U^{+}[x].

Eqn. (4.2.18)
then implies that

\dim U^{+}[x]=\dim\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]
. Thus,
U^{+}[x]=\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]
since
U^{+}[x]
is an algebraic subset of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
(see
§3.1.4
) and since
\mathcal{L}^{u}[x]
is irreducible (see
§4.2.2
).
In particular
U^{+}[x]=U^{+}[\sigma(x)]
.

Let
U^{-}(x)=wU^{+}(w^{-1}x)w^{-1}
.
Since
w\in\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
,
\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant, and since
wg_{t}w^{-1}=g_{-t}
, the action by
w
intertwines stable and unstable manifolds and thus also intertwines the subresonant structures on stable and unstable manifolds. In particular,
U^{-}(x)
is a subgroup of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{s}[x])
and
\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]=U^{-}(x)x\equiv U^{-}[x]
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 1.2.4
and
Theorem 4.1.7
.

In view of
Corollary 4.1.3
, we may assume that

x\to U^{+}(x)
is a family of subgroups compatible with the measure
(see
Definition 3.1.5
) such that the QNI condition does not hold. Then, in view of

Lemma 4.3.3
, (
4.2.8
) holds.
Let
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
be as in (
4.2.5
). Then,
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is
P
-invariant.
Therefore, in view of the assumption
in
Theorem 1.2.4
or
Theorem 4.1.7
, (
4.2.16
) holds.
Now all the conclusions follow from
Proposition 4.2.15
.
∎

4.2.19.
The non-negative Laplacian condition

4.2.20 Proposition
.

Suppose
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
acts smoothly on
Q
, let
\nu
be a
P
-invariant measure, and suppose
Definition 1.2.1
holds.
Then,
there is no
P
-invariant
\nu
-measurable subbundle of
TQ
on which the sum of the Lyapunov exponents is negative.

Proof.

This is essentially
[
EM18
, Theorem A.3]
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 1.2.3
.

This follows immediately from
Theorem 1.2.4
and
Proposition 4.2.20
.
∎

4.3.
Proof of the main theorems for Random Dynamics assuming

Theorem 4.1.4
.

4.3.1.
Auxiliary bundles in the endgame

For a.e.
\hat{q}\in\hat{Q}
,
let
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{q}]\subset\mathcal{W}^{s}[\hat{q}]
denote the smallest real-algebraic subset
containing, for some
\epsilon>0
, the intersection of the
ball of radius
\epsilon
with
the support of the measure
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{q}}
, which is the conditional measure
of
\hat{\nu}
along
W^{s}[\hat{q}]
. Then,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{q}]
is

\hat{T}^{t}
-equivariant. Since
the action of
\hat{T}^{-t}
is expanding along
W^{s}[\hat{q}]
, we see that for
almost all
\hat{q}
and any
\epsilon>0
,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{q}]
is the smallest
real-algebraic subset of
\mathcal{W}^{s}(\hat{q})
such that
L^{s}[\hat{q}]
contains

\operatorname{support}(\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{q}})\cap B^{Q}(\hat{q},\epsilon)
.

We lift the measure
\hat{\nu}
to
\hat{X}
,
and for
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}
declare
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]

to be
\mathcal{W}^{s}[\hat{x}]\cap\sigma^{-1}(\mathcal{L}^{s}[\sigma(\hat{x})])
, where
\sigma:\hat{X}\to\hat{Q}
is the projection map given by
\sigma(x,\omega,s)=(\sigma(x),\omega,s)
.

For
\hat{x}=(x,\omega,s)\in\hat{X}
,
let
T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]\subset T_{x}\mathcal{W}^{s}[\hat{x}]
be the tangent space at
x
to
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]
. Note that
T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]
is
\hat{T}^{t}
-invariant by construction.

Let
U^{+}(\hat{x})\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\hat{x})
be as in
Definition 3.3.1
. Let
U^{+}[\hat{x}]=U^{+}(\hat{x})\cdot x\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[\hat{x}]
denote the

U^{+}(\hat{x})
-orbit of
x
.
Given
\hat{q}=(q,\omega,s)\in\hat{Q}
, let
T_{q}Q=E^{s}(\hat{q})\oplus E^{c}(\hat{q})\oplus E^{u}(\hat{q})
be the splitting into negative,
zero, and positive Lyapunov exponents. We then declare for
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}
,

E^{s}(\hat{x})=E^{s}(\sigma(\hat{x})),\qquad E^{c}(\hat{x})=E^{c}(\sigma(\hat{x})),\qquad E^{u}(\hat{x})=E^{u}(\sigma(\hat{x})).

Let
\pi^{cs}_{\hat{x}}\colon T_{x}Q\to E^{u}(\hat{x})
be the canonical projection.

For
x\in X
, let

(4.3.2)

\mathcal{Z}(x)=\mathrm{ess\,span}\{T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x,\omega,0]\;\>:\;\>{\omega\in\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}}\},

where the measure on
\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{Z}}
is
\mu^{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Note that
\mathcal{Z}(x)
is
\mu
-invariant (as in
Definition 1.1.10
)
since
\mathcal{L}^{s}
is
\hat{T}^{t}
-invariant.

The following is the random dynamics version of
Lemma 4.2.9
:

4.3.3 Lemma
.

Suppose for a positive (and thus full)
\nu
-measure subset of
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}
we have:

(4.3.4)

\pi^{cs}_{\hat{x}}\left(\mathcal{Z}(x)\right)\not\subset T_{\hat{x}}U^{+}[\hat{x}].

Then the family
\hat{x}\to U^{+}(\hat{x})
satisfies the Random QNI condition (see
Definition 3.3.4
).

Proof.

See
[
ES25
]
.
∎

The following is the analogue of

Lemma 4.2.7
:

4.3.5 Lemma
.

The following are equivalent:

(1)

For a positive (and thus full)
\hat{\nu}
-measure subset
of
\hat{x}=(x,\omega,s)\in\hat{X}
we have

(4.3.6)

\pi^{cs}_{(x,\omega,0)}\left(\mathcal{Z}(x)\right)\subset T_{x}U^{+}[x,\omega,0].

(2)

For a positive (and thus full)
\hat{\nu}
-measure
subset of
\hat{x}=(x,\omega,s)\in\hat{X}
there exists a positive (and thus full) Lebesgue measure subset
E_{x}\subset\Diff_{\infty}(Q)^{\mathbb{N}}
such that for
\omega^{\prime}_{-}\in E_{x}
we have

\pi^{cs}_{(x,\omega,0)}\left(T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x,\omega^{\prime}_{-},0)]\right)\subset T_{x}U^{+}[x,\omega,0].

4.3.7.
Entropy theory for the skew extensions

Recall that we assume
\mu
is finitely supported.

We work in the random dynamics setting. We have
\hat{T}^{t}\colon(\hat{X},\hat{\nu})\to(\hat{X},\hat{\nu})
which is a finite measurable extension of
\hat{T}^{t}\colon(\hat{Q},\nu)\to(\hat{Q},\hat{\nu})
. Moreover,
(\hat{Q},\hat{\nu})
is an extension over
(S^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{\mathbb{Z}})\times[0,1]
with fibers diffeomorphic to
Q
.

We note
U^{+}[{x},\omega,s]=U^{+}[{x},\omega^{\prime},s]
for all
\omega^{\prime}
with
(\omega^{\prime})^{+}=\omega^{+}
and for
\lambda_{i}>0
, the quantity
\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap T_{x}U^{+}[\hat{x}]
is
\hat{T}
-invariant and thus constant a.s.
We also note that
\mathcal{L}^{s}[x,\omega,s]=\mathcal{L}^{s}[x,\omega^{\prime},s]
for
\omega^{\prime}
with
(\omega^{\prime})^{-}=\omega^{-}
.

We recall that if
\mu=\sum{c_{i}}\delta_{f_{i}}
is a discrete probability measure on
\Diff^{\infty}(Q)
, we define the entropy of
\mu
to be

H(\mu)=-\sum c_{i}\log c_{i}.

We also write
\hat{T}=\hat{T}^{1}
.
If
\zeta
is a measurable partition of
\hat{Q}
with
\hat{T}\zeta\prec\zeta
we define

h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},\zeta):=H_{\hat{\nu}}(T^{-1}\zeta\mid\zeta)=-\int\log\hat{\nu}|_{\zeta[\hat{x}]}(T^{-1}\zeta(\hat{x}))\,d\hat{\nu}(\hat{x}).

Here,
\nu|_{\zeta[\hat{x}]}
is the conditional measure at
\hat{x}
relative to the measurable partition
\zeta
.

Given
\hat{x}=(x,\omega,s)\in\hat{Q}
, we write
W_{1}^{+}[\hat{x}]=\{x\}\times\{\omega^{\prime}:(\omega^{\prime})^{+}=\omega^{+}\}\times\{s\}
.
Let
\zeta
denote the partition of
\hat{Q}
whose atom through
\hat{x}
is
\zeta(x)=W_{1}^{+}(\hat{x})
. Then we define

h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},W_{1}^{+}):=h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},\zeta).

We similarly define
W_{1}^{-}[\hat{x}]
and
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})
.

4.3.8 Proposition
.

For
\hat{\nu}
-a.e.
\hat{x}=(x,\omega,s)\in\hat{X}
, the following hold:

(i)

h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T})\geq h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},W_{1}^{+})+\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap T_{x}U^{+}[\hat{x}]
.

(ii)

h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},W_{1}^{+})=h_{\mu^{\mathbb{Z}}\times ds}(T)=H(\mu)
.

(iii)

h_{\hat{\nu}}(T^{-1})\leq h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})+\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]
.

(iv)

h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})\leq H(\mu)
with equality if and only if
\nu
is
\mu
-invariant (invariance principle).

(v)

We have
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1})=h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},\widehat{\mathfrak{B}}_{0}^{-})
. Moreover, if
\nu
is
\mu
-invariant then

h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1})=H(\mu)+h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}).

(vi)

We have
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T})=h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},W_{1}^{+})
if and only if the leafwise measure
\hat{\nu}^{u}_{x}
is supported at a single point for a.e.
x
.

Proof.

The first three statements are analogous to
Proposition 4.2.12
.

Next,
(iv)
follows in the same way as the proof of
[
Led86
, §2.1, Prop. 2]
.

The first identity of
(v)
follows as in
Proposition A.5.3
from
[
LY85b
]
.
The second identity follows by using
(iv)
, which implies that
\hat{\nu}
has a local product structure on
\hat{\mathfrak{B}}_{0}^{-}
and hence the entropy adds up from each factor.

To prove
(vi)
, we note that applying the previous two parts to
\hat{T}
instead of
\hat{T}^{-1}
we find that
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},\mathfrak{B}_{0})=0
.
It follows that the relative entropy vanishes
H_{\hat{\nu}}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}|\hat{T}^{-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0})=0
from which, by iteration, it follows that
\hat{\nu}^{u}_{x}
is supported in the intersection of a nested sequence of atoms of
\hat{T}^{-k}\mathfrak{B}_{0}
and hence is atomic.
∎

4.3.9 Proposition
.

Suppose that for
\hat{\nu}
-a.e.
\hat{q}=(q,\omega,s)\in\hat{Q}

the measure
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{q}}
is supported on an embedded submanifold
N_{\hat{q}}\subset\mathcal{W}^{q}[\hat{q}]
. Then

h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T})\leq H(\mu)+\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}(-\lambda_{i})\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{q})\cap T_{q}N_{\hat{q}}.

Moreover, equality holds if and only if

\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{q}}
equivalent to the Lebesgue measure on
N_{\hat{q}}
for a.e.
\hat{q}\in\hat{Q}
.

Proof.

Let
\xi
be a measurable partition of
\hat{Q}
with

(i)

\xi\prec\hat{T}(\xi)

(ii)

\xi(\hat{q})\subset N_{\hat{q}}

(iii)

\xi(\hat{q})
contains an open neighborhood of
\hat{q}
in
N_{\hat{q}}
.

We may take
\xi(\hat{q})=N_{\hat{q}}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}
.
Then
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T})=H(\mu)+h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},\xi)
and so we conclude that

h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},\xi)\leq\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{q})\cap T_{q}N_{\hat{q}}
. It follows exactly as in the proof of
[
Led84
, Thm. 3.4]
that equality holds if and only if
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{q}}
is equivalent to the Lebesgue measure on
N_{\hat{q}}
for a.e.
\hat{q}
.
∎

4.3.10.
Entropy and the endgame

The following is the random dynamics version of

Proposition 4.2.15
:

4.3.11 Proposition
.

Suppose
\nu
is a
\mu
-stationary measure on
X
which is a lift of
a
\mu
-stationary measure on
Q
(also denoted by
\nu
). Suppose
\hat{x}\to U^{+}(\hat{x})
is a family of subgroups compatible with the measure

\hat{\nu}
on
\hat{X}
. Let
\mathcal{Z}
be as in (
4.3.2
).
Suppose also that

(4.3.12)

the sum of the Lyapunov exponents along
\mathcal{Z}
is non-negative.

Furthermore, suppose that for
\hat{\nu}
-a.e.
x\in X
,
(
4.3.6
) holds.
Then,
\nu
is
\mu
-invariant (see
Definition 1.1.1
).
Also,

(i)

U^{+}(x,\omega,s)
depends only on
(\sigma(x),\omega,s)
, where
\sigma:X\to Q
is the covering map of Section
2.5
.

(ii)

For almost all
\hat{q}\in\hat{Q}
, the
conditional of
\hat{\nu}
along
\mathcal{W}^{u}[\hat{q}]
is the Haar
measure along the orbit
U^{+}[\hat{q}]
.

(iii)

For almost all
\hat{q}\in\hat{Q}
there exists a subgroup
U^{-}(\hat{q})
of

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{s}[\hat{q}])
such that the conditional of
\hat{\nu}
along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[\hat{q}]
is the Haar measure along the orbit
U^{-}[\hat{q}]\equiv U^{-}(\hat{q})q
. In particular,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{q}]=U^{-}[\hat{q}]
.

(iv)

The sum of the Lyapunov exponents of the bundle

\mathcal{Z}
is
0
, where
\mathcal{Z}
is as in (
4.3.2
).

Proof.

From (
4.3.6
), for a.e.
\hat{x}=(x,\omega,s)
,
we have

\mathcal{Z}(x)\cap E^{u}(\hat{x})\subset T_{x}U^{+}[\hat{x}].

By
Proposition 4.3.8
(i)
,

\displaystyle h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T})

\displaystyle\geq H(\mu)+\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap T_{x}U^{+}[\hat{x}]

\displaystyle\geq H(\mu)+\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap\mathcal{Z}(x).

In the above, the term
H(\mu)=h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T},W_{1}^{+})
is the contribution of the “combinatorial unstable”
W_{1}^{+}
.

Similarly, by the definition of
\mathcal{Z}(x)

we have
T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]\subset\mathcal{Z}(x)
whence by
Proposition 4.3.8
(iii)
,

\displaystyle h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})+\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(x)\cap T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]

\displaystyle\leq h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})+\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap\mathcal{Z}(x).

In the above,
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})
denotes the contribution of the “combinatorial stable”
W_{1}^{-}
to the entropy. Note by
Proposition 4.3.8
(iv)

that
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})\leq H(\mu)
.

In view of (
4.3.12
),

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq H(\mu)-h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})+\sum_{\lambda_{i}}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap\mathcal{Z}(x)

\displaystyle=H(\mu)-h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})+\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap\mathcal{Z}(x)+\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap\mathcal{Z}(x)

\displaystyle\leq-h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1})+h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T})

Since
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T})=h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1})
, we conclude that
h_{\hat{\nu}}(\hat{T}^{-1},W_{1}^{-})=H(\mu)
, hence
Proposition 4.3.8
(iv)
implies that
\nu
is
\mu
-invariant.

We also get that

(4.3.13)

H(\mu)+\sum_{\lambda_{i}<0}-\lambda_{i}\dim\left(E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]\right)=h_{\hat{\nu}}\left(\hat{T}^{-1}\right)=h_{\hat{\nu}}\left(\hat{T}\right)=\\
=H(\mu)+\sum_{\lambda_{i}>0}\lambda_{i}\dim\left(E^{\lambda_{i}}(\hat{x})\cap T_{x}U^{+}[\hat{x}]\right).

By
Proposition 4.3.9
, for a.e.
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}
,
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x}}
is absolutely continuous on
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]
.

Analogously to the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]
, let

\mathcal{L}^{u}[\hat{x}]\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[\hat{x}]
denote the smallest algebraic subset of full
\hat{\nu}^{u}_{\hat{x}}
measure. Note that since the measure on
\hat{X}
is a lift of the measure on
\hat{Q}
,
\mathcal{L}^{u}[\hat{x}]
depends only on
\sigma(x)
where
\sigma:\hat{X}\to\hat{Q}
is the projection map.

Let
\check{\mu}
be the measure on
\Diff_{\infty}(Q)
given by

\check{\mu}(g)=\mu(g^{-1})
. Since
\nu
is
\mu
-invariant, it is
also
\check{\mu}
-invariant, and in particular

\check{\mu}
-stationary. We have

\mathcal{W}^{u}[x,\omega,s]=\mathcal{W}^{s}[x,\check{\omega},s]\qquad\mathcal{W}^{s}[x,\omega,s]=\mathcal{W}^{u}[x,\check{\omega},s],

where

\check{\omega}=\dots,\omega_{2}^{-1},\omega_{1}^{-1},\omega_{0}^{-1},\omega_{-1}^{-1},\omega_{-2}^{-1}\dots,

Note that for any
E
,
\mu^{\mathbb{Z}}(\{\omega\in E\})=\check{\mu}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\{\check{\omega}\in E\})
.
Then,

\hat{\nu}^{u}_{(x,\omega,s)}=\hat{\nu}^{s}_{(x,\check{\omega},s)}\qquad\hat{\nu}^{s}_{(x,\omega,s)}=\hat{\nu}^{u}_{(x,\check{\omega},s)}.

In particular,
\mathcal{L}^{u}[x,\omega,s]=\mathcal{L}^{s}[x,\check{\omega},s]
for a.e.
\hat{x}\in\hat{X}

and since
\hat{\nu}^{s}_{\hat{x}}
is absolutely continuous on
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]
,
it follows that
\hat{\nu}^{u}_{\hat{x}}
is absolutely continuous on
\mathcal{L}^{u}[\hat{x}]
.
Note that
U^{+}[\hat{x}]\subset\mathcal{L}^{u}[\hat{x}]
, and we must have

\dim U^{+}[\hat{x}]=\dim\mathcal{L}^{u}[\hat{x}]
(or else we contradict (
4.3.13
)).
Thus,
U^{+}[\hat{x}]=\mathcal{L}^{u}[\hat{x}]
since
\mathcal{L}^{u}[\hat{x}]
is irreducible (see
§4.2.2
), and in particular,
U^{+}[\hat{x}]=U^{+}[\sigma(\hat{x})]
.

Let
U^{-}(x,\omega,s)=U^{+}(x,\check{\omega},s)
. Then,
U^{-}(\hat{x})
is a subgroup of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{s}[\hat{x}])
and
\mathcal{L}^{s}[\hat{x}]=U^{-}(\hat{x})x\equiv U^{-}[x]
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 1.1.11
and
Theorem 4.1.9
.

In view of
Corollary 4.1.5
and

Lemma 4.3.3
, (
4.3.6
)
holds.
Note that the bundle
\mathcal{Z}
is non-random.
Therefore, in view of the assumption
in
Theorem 1.1.11
or

Theorem 4.1.9
,
(
4.3.12
) holds.
The rest of the assertions follow from

Proposition 4.3.11
.
∎

4.3.14.
Uniform Expansion

4.3.15 Proposition
.

Let
\mu
be as in

Theorem 1.1.11
, and suppose
suppose for
1\leq d<\dim Q
,
\mu
satisfies
the uniform expansion condition
Definition 1.1.3
.
Let
\nu
be a
\mu
-stationary measure. Then, for every
non-random
\nu
-measurable proper subbundle of
TQ
the sum
of the Lyapunov exponents is positive (except for the zero bundle).

If
Q
is a symplectic manifold and
\mu
preserves the symplectic form on
Q
and has uniform expansion on isotropic subspaces, then there exists no non-random
\nu
-measurable proper subbundle of
TQ
on which the sum of the Lyapunov exponents is negative.

Proof.

This follows immediately from iterating

Definition 1.1.3
. For the last sentence, note
that for any symplectic subbundle, the sum of the Lyapunov exponents
is
0
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 1.1.5
.

This follows immediately from
Theorem 1.1.11
and
Proposition 4.3.15
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 1.1.9
.

We apply
Theorem 4.1.9
.
Let
\mathcal{T}(x)=T_{x}U^{+}\oplus T_{x}U^{-}
. Then, by

Theorem 4.1.9
(iii), the sum of the
Lyapunov exponents along
\mathcal{T}
is
0
. But, in view of

Proposition 4.3.15
and the
assumption in the statement of

Theorem 1.1.9
, for any subbundle

\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
of
TQ
with
1\leq\dim\mathcal{T}^{\prime}<\dim Q
the sum of the Lyapunov expoents along
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is positive. Therefore, either
\dim\mathcal{T}=0
or
\dim\mathcal{T}=\dim Q
.

If
\dim\mathcal{T}=0
then by
[
LX06
]

(and using the assumption that there are no
0
Lyapunov exponents), the dimension of
\nu
is
0
.
1
1

1

It may be possible to avoid the assumption that there are no
0
exponents in this part of the argument.
Then, by
Proposition 2.4.9
,
\nu
has an atomic part.
Then by the ergodicity of
\hat{T}
,
\nu
is atomic, and all atoms
have the same measure. Therefore
\nu
is finitely supported.

Now suppose
\dim\mathcal{T}=\dim Q
, which implies that the conditional measures on the (Pesin) stables and unstables are absolutely continuous. Furthermore, we are assuming that there are no zero Lyapunov exponents.
Then,
\nu
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure by
[
LY85b
, Corollary H]
.

The proof of ergodicity of the volume measure is identical to that in

[
DK07
, §10]
.
Indeed, the uniform expansion gaps property guarantees that the conclusion of
[
DK07
, Cor. 4(c)]
holds, with
d/2
replaced by the dimension where Lyapunov exponents switch from positive to negative (see also
[
Zha19
, §3.2]
).
Then, the arguments in
[
DK07
, §10.3-6]
hold and yield the result.
∎

4.4.
Teichmüller Dynamics

We now proceed to the proofs of the results stated in
§1.3
.
The notation follows loc. cit. and we do not repeat it here.

4.4.1.
Some lemmas on Lie groups

Let
G=\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
,
G^{m}=G\times G\times\dots G
(
m
times). Let
G_{\Delta}\subset G^{m}
be the diagonally embedded copy of
G
in
G^{m}
.

Let
\beta
be a partition of
\{1,\dots,m\}
. Let
\sim_{\beta}
denote the associated equivalence relation, i.e.
k\sim_{\beta}l
iff
k
and
l
are in the same atom of
\beta
. Let

G_{\beta}=\{(g_{1},\dots,g_{m})\in G^{m}\;\>:\;\>g_{j}=g_{k}\text{ if }j\sim_{\beta}k\}

Note that
G_{\beta}\cap G_{\gamma}=G_{\delta}
where
\delta
is the smallest partition such that both
\beta
and
\gamma
are refinements of
\delta
. Also, if
\beta
is the partition of
\{1,\dots,m\}
which has only one atom, then
G_{\beta}=G_{\Delta}
.

We will also need the following generalization. Suppose
S
is a
(possibly empty) subset of
\{1,\dots,m\}
and suppose
\beta
is a
partition of
S
. We set

\tilde{G}_{\beta}=\{(g_{1},\dots,g_{m})\in G^{m}\;\>:\;\>g_{j}=e\text{ if }j\not\in S,\\
\qquad g_{j}=g_{k}\text{ if $j\in S$, $k\in S$, $j\sim_{\beta}k$}\}

Let
Z^{m}
denote the center of
G^{m}
.

We recall the following standard lemma.

4.4.2 Lemma
.

Suppose
F
is a subgroup of
G^{m}
which is normalized by
G_{\Delta}
. Then there exists a subset
S
of
\{1,\dots,m\}
, a partition
\beta
of
S
and a subgroup
Z^{\prime}
of
Z^{m}
such that

F=\tilde{G}_{\beta}Z^{\prime}
.

As an immediate corollary we have the following:

4.4.3 Corollary
.

Suppose
F
is a connected subgroup of
G^{m}
which contains
G_{\Delta}
. Then,
F=G_{\beta}
for some partition
\beta
of
\{1,\dots,m\}
.

Proof of
Lemma 4.4.2
.

Let
H:=\operatorname{PSL}(2,\mathbb{R}):=G/Z
be the quotient by the center.
It suffices to prove the analogous statement for
H^{m},H_{\Delta}
and with
H_{\beta},\widetilde{H}_{\beta}
as before.
We recall the Goursat lemma: for any groups
H_{1},H_{2}
, subgroups
S\subset H_{1}\times H_{2}
that project surjectively to each factor are classified by normal subgroups
N_{i}\subset H_{i}
an an isomorphism
f\colon H_{1}/N_{1}\tilde{\longrightarrow}H_{2}/N_{2}
.
The group
S
is the preimage in
H_{1}\times H_{2}
of the graph of
f
in
H_{1}/N_{1}\times H_{2}/N_{2}
.

We proceed by induction on
m
, the statement for
m=1
being immediate.
Let
F_{m-1}\subset H^{m-1}
be the projection of
F
to the first
m-1
factors.
It satisfies the inductive assumptions so
F_{m-1}=\widetilde{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}
with
\beta^{\prime}
a partition of a subset of
\{1,\dots,m-1\}
.
We apply the Goursat lemma to
F\subset F_{m-1}\times H
, let
N_{1}\subset F_{m-1},N_{2}\subset H
be the corresponding groups.
Since
H
is simple, either
N_{2}={\bm{1}}
or
N_{2}=H
.

If
N_{2}=H
then
F\cong F_{\beta^{\prime}}\times H
, clearly of the needed form.
If
N_{2}={\bm{1}}
then
F_{m-1}/N_{1}\cong H
, and the normal subgroups of
F_{m-1}
with this property are of the form
H_{\beta^{\prime\prime}}
, with
\beta^{\prime\prime}
obtained from
\beta^{\prime}
by removing a full equivalence class
C
from
\beta^{\prime}
, with
C\subseteq\{1,\dots,m-1\}
.
Note that the only automorphisms of
\operatorname{PSL}(2,\mathbb{R})
are inner, or given by
g\mapsto\left(g^{-1}\right)^{t}
.
Now the constraint that
F
is normalized by
H_{\Delta}
implies that
F\cong F_{\beta}
, where
\beta
is obtained from
\beta^{\prime}
by adding the equivalence class
C\cup\{m\}
.
∎

4.4.4.
Stables, unstables and projections.

Suppose
q=(q_{1},\dots,q_{m})
. Let
p_{j}
denote the natural map from
H^{1}(M_{j},\Sigma_{j},\mathbb{R})
to
H^{1}(M_{j},\mathbb{R})
, and let
p:H^{1}(M,\Sigma,\mathbb{R})\to H^{1}(M,\mathbb{R})
denote
\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}
.
Let

H^{\perp}(q_{j})=\{v\in H^{1}(M_{j},\Sigma_{j},\mathbb{R})\;\>:\;\>\text{$p(v_{j})$ is symplectically orthogonal to}\\
\text{the plane spanned by $p_{j}(\operatorname{Re}q_{j})$ and $p_{j}(\operatorname{Im}q_{j})$.}\}

Let

H^{\perp}(q)=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}H^{\perp}(q_{j})

Let
H^{+}(q_{j})=\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Im}q_{j}
,
H^{-}(q_{j})=\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Re}q_{j}
. Let

H^{+}(q)=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}H^{+}(q_{j})\qquad H^{-}(q)=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}H^{-}(q_{j})

Then
if we identify
\mathcal{H}(\vec{\alpha})
with
H^{1}(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{C})

using the period map, then

\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]=\{q+v\;\>:\;\>v\in H^{1}(M,\Sigma,\mathbb{R}),\qquad p_{j}(v)\wedge p_{j}(\operatorname{Im}q)=0,\forall j\}

The condition on the wedge product, or equivalently the symplectic pairing, is needed so that
q+v\in\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
(and not
\mathcal{H}(\vec{\alpha})
). Thus,

(4.4.5)

\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]=q+H^{+}(q)+H^{\perp}(q)\equiv q+\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)

(4.4.6)

\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]=q+iH^{-}(q)+iH^{\perp}(q)\equiv q+\mathcal{W}^{s}(q)

Then
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
is naturally identified with
T_{q}\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
, and similarly,

\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
is naturally identified with
T_{q}\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
.

Let
\iota_{\tau}\colon W^{s}(q)\to W^{u}(q)
denote the linear isomorphism given by for
c_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
,
v_{j}\in H^{\perp}_{j}

(4.4.7)

\iota_{\tau}\big(i(c_{1}\operatorname{Re}q_{1}+v_{1}),\dots,i(c_{m}\operatorname{Re}q_{m}+v_{m})\big)=\\
=\left(c_{1}\tau^{2}\operatorname{Im}q_{1}+\tau v_{1},\dots,c_{m}\tau^{2}\operatorname{Im}q_{m}+\tau v_{m}\right)

Let
\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{q}[q^{\prime}]
denote the unique intersection point of
\mathcal{W}^{cs}[q^{\prime}]
with
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
.

Let
n_{\tau}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\tau\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\subset N\subset SL(2,\mathbb{R})
.
Let
f_{q,\tau}:\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
be defined as

f_{q,\tau}(q^{\prime})=n_{\tau}^{-1}\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{q}(n_{\tau}q^{\prime}).

Let
f_{q,\tau,t}:\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
denote
g_{-t}\circ f_{g_{t}q,\tau}\circ g_{t}
.

4.4.8 Lemma
.

Suppose
q\in\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
,
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
.
Write
q=(q_{1},\dots,q_{n})
,
q^{\prime}=(q_{1}^{\prime},\dots,q_{n}^{\prime})
. Then, in view of (
4.4.6
),

q^{\prime}_{j}=q_{j}+i(c_{j}\operatorname{Re}q_{j}+v_{j})

for some
c_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
and
v_{j}\in H^{\perp}_{j}
. Then, we have

(a)

f_{q,\tau}(q^{\prime})=\big(\phi_{q_{1},\tau}(c_{1},v_{1}),\dots,\phi_{q_{m},\tau}(c_{m},v_{m})\big)
, where

\phi_{q,\tau}(c,v)=q-\frac{c\tau^{2}}{1+c\tau}\operatorname{Im}q+\frac{\tau}{1+c\tau}v.

(b)

f_{q,\tau,t}(q^{\prime})=\left(\phi_{q_{1},\tau,t}(c_{1},v_{1}),\dots,\phi_{q_{m},\tau,t}(c_{m},v_{m})\right)
, where

\phi_{q,\tau,t}(c,v)=q-\frac{e^{-2t}c\tau^{2}}{1+e^{-2t}c\tau}\operatorname{Im}q+\frac{e^{-2t}\tau}{1+e^{-2t}c\tau}v.

(c)

The derivative of
f_{q,\tau}(q^{\prime})
with respect to
q^{\prime}

at
q^{\prime}=q
is
\iota_{\tau}
, where
\iota_{\tau}
is as in (
4.4.7
).

Proof.

We begin with the proof of (a). It is enough to work one factor at a time.
It is also convenient to express the coordinates as a
2\times\dim H^{1}(M,\Sigma)
matrix:

q_{j}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix}\quad q_{j}^{\prime}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
c&1&v\end{bmatrix}\text{ w.r.t. }\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Re}q_{j}&\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Im}q_{j}&H^{\perp}(q_{j})\\
i\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Re}q_{j}&i\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Im}q_{j}&iH^{\perp}(q_{j})\end{bmatrix}

Then
\SL_{2}(\mathbb{R})
acts by ordinary matrix multiplication, whereas center-stable projection acts as:

\displaystyle\pi_{q}^{cs}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b&v\\
c&d&w\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&\tfrac{b}{a}&\tfrac{v}{a}\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\pi_{g_{t}q}^{cs}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b&v\\
c&d&w\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}e^{t}&e^{t}\tfrac{b}{a}&e^{t}\tfrac{v}{a}\\
0&e^{-t}&0\end{bmatrix}\text{ since }g_{t}q=\begin{bmatrix}e^{t}&0&0\\
0&e^{-t}&0\end{bmatrix}.

We now compute directly:

\displaystyle n_{\tau}q_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1+\tau c&\tau&\tau v\\
c&1&v\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\pi_{q}^{cs}n_{\tau}q_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&\tfrac{\tau}{1+\tau c}&\tfrac{\tau}{1+\tau c}v\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle n_{\tau}^{-1}\pi_{q}^{cs}n_{\tau}q_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&\tfrac{-\tau^{2}c}{1+\tau c}&\tfrac{\tau}{1+\tau c}v\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix}

For the second part, the calculation is analogous:

\displaystyle g_{t}q_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}e^{t}&0&0\\
e^{-t}c&e^{-t}&e^{-t}v\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle n_{\tau}g_{t}q_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}e^{t}+\tau e^{-t}c&\tau e^{-t}&\tau e^{-t}v\\
e^{-t}c&e^{-t}&e^{-t}v\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\pi_{g_{t}q}^{cs}n_{\tau}g_{t}q_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}e^{t}&\frac{\tau}{e^{t}+\tau e^{-t}c}&\frac{\tau v}{e^{t}+\tau e^{-t}c}\\
0&e^{-t}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle n_{-\tau}\pi_{q}^{cs}n_{\tau}g_{t}q_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}e^{t}&\frac{-\tau^{2}e^{-2t}c}{e^{t}+\tau e^{-t}c}&\frac{\tau v}{e^{t}+\tau e^{-t}c}\\
0&e^{-t}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle g_{-t}n_{-\tau}\pi_{q}^{cs}n_{\tau}g_{t}q_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{-\tau^{2}e^{-t}c}{e^{t}+\tau e^{-t}c}&\frac{\tau e^{-t}v}{e^{t}+\tau e^{-t}c}\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{-\tau^{2}e^{-2t}c}{1+\tau e^{-2t}c}&\frac{\tau e^{-2t}v}{1+\tau e^{-2t}c}\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix}

which precisely matches the claimed formulas.

Part (c) follows immediately from part (a) by taking the derivative at
q_{j}^{\prime}=q_{j}
(which corresponds to
c_{j}=0
,
v_{j}=0
).
∎

4.4.9 Lemma

(No negative subbundles)
.

Suppose
V\subset H^{1}(M,\Sigma,\mathbb{R})
is a subbundle which is
equivariant under the action of the Kontsevich-Zorich cocycle. Then,
the sum of the Lyapunov exponents of
V
is non-negative. Also if
V

is an equivariant subbundle of
H^{1}(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{C})
, then the
sum of the Lyapunov exponents of
g_{t}
on
V
is non-negative.

Proof.

For a subbundle of a single factor
H^{1}(M_{j},\Sigma_{j})
the result follows from
[
EM18
, Thm. A.3]
.
Given an arbitrary
V
, proceed by induction.
Indeed, its projection to the last factor satisfies the claim, and the kernel is contained in the preceding
n-1
factors.
∎

4.4.10 Lemma
.

For a.e.
q\in Q
,
\nu_{q}^{s}
gives measure
0
to any proper algebraic subset of
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
.

A closely related result (concerning only the foliation by fast stable manifolds) is established in
[
LX11
]
; we follow the same method.

Proof.

Suppose, by contradiction, that there exists a minimal
d<\dim\mathcal{L}^{s}
such that for a.e.
q
there exists at least one algebraic irreducible
\mathcal{D}^{s}\subset\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
with
\nu^{s}_{q}(\mathcal{D}^{s})>0
and
\dim\mathcal{D}^{s}=d
.
Note that for any two such
\mathcal{D}^{s}
, their intersection has
\nu^{s}_{q}
-measure zero by minimality of
d
.
There are at most countably many such
\{\mathcal{D}^{s}_{i}[q]\}_{i\in I(q)}
, and their union has full
\nu^{s}_{q}
-measure.
Our goal is to show that
\nu^{s}_{q}
is supported on a single one, implying that
\mathcal{D}^{s}[q]=\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
.

First, note that for a.e.
q
there is a single
\mathcal{D}^{s}_{i}
denoted
\mathcal{D}^{s}[q]
such that
q\in\mathcal{D}^{s}[q]
.
Indeed, since
\nu_{s}^{q}
is supported on the union of the
\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s}[q]
(by ergodicity), if there was a positive measure set inside the intersection of two (or more)
\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s}[q]
then we could consider algebraic sets of lower dimension.

Consider now the partition
\mathfrak{D}
with atoms
\mathfrak{D}[q]:=\mathcal{D}^{s}[q]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
.
It is a measurable partition, since for each pair
(k,m)
we can construct a standard measure space (fibering over
Q
) whose fiber over
q
consists of
m
polynomials of degree at most
k
on
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
, whose vanishing locus intersects
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
.
On this space, the set of irreducible subvarieties of dimension
d
with positive
\nu_{q}^{s}
-mass is measurable, and we can take the union over all
(k,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
.

Note also that
\mathfrak{D}
is an
invariant
partition, i.e.
g_{t}\left(\mathfrak{D}[q]\right)\subseteq\mathfrak{D}\left[g_{t}q\right]
for
t\geq 0
.
Furthermore, we have for
t\geq 0
that
g_{t}(\mathfrak{D})\vee\mathfrak{D}=g_{t}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-})\vee\mathfrak{D}
, which will be useful when computing entropy.

Next, we recall the
mutual information function
of two measurable partitions
\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B}
:

I_{\nu}(q;\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{B}):=-\log\Big(\nu_{\mathfrak{B}}[q]\big([q]_{\mathfrak{A}}\big)\Big)

and relative entropy:

H_{\nu}(\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{B}):=\int_{Q}I_{\nu}(q;\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{B})d\nu(q)

Recall from
[
Rok67
, §7.1]
that for
g_{t}
-invariant measurable partition
\mathfrak{A}
we have

h_{\nu}\left(g_{t};\mathfrak{A}\right):=H_{\nu}\left(\mathfrak{A}|g_{t}^{-1}\mathfrak{A}\right)\text{ and }h_{\nu}(g_{t}):=\sup_{\mathfrak{A}}h_{\nu}(g_{t};\mathfrak{A})

where the
\sup
is over all
g_{t}
-invariant
\mathfrak{A}
(see
[
Rok67
, §9]
).
Note that since
g_{t}
is invertible we have
H_{\nu}(\mathfrak{A}|g_{t}^{-1}\mathfrak{A})=H_{\nu}(g_{t}\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{A})
.

We claim first that, because
\mathfrak{D}
is finer and countably many of its atoms form an atom of
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}
, we have (for fixed
t_{0}>0
):

(4.4.11)

\displaystyle h_{\nu}\left(g_{t_{0}};\mathfrak{D}\right)\geq h_{\nu}\left(g_{t_{0}};\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}\right)

Indeed, for any partition
\mathfrak{A}
with
g_{t_{0}}(\mathfrak{A})
finer than
\mathfrak{A}
, we have

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{n}I_{\nu}\left(q;g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{A}\right)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\left[I_{\nu}\left(q;g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{A}|g_{t_{0}}^{n-1}\mathfrak{A}\right)+\cdots+I_{\nu}\left(q;g_{t_{0}}\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{A}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{n}\left[I_{\nu}\left(g_{t_{0}}^{-(n-1)}q;g_{t_{0}}\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{A}\right)+\cdots+I_{\nu}\left(q;g_{t_{0}}\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{A}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{n\to+\infty}\int_{Q}I_{\nu}\left(q;g_{t_{0}}\mathfrak{A}|\mathfrak{A}\right)d\nu(q)=h_{\nu}(g_{t_{0}};\mathfrak{A})

where in the last line we used the Birkhoff ergodic theorem.
We next note that

I_{\nu}\left(q;\mathfrak{D}|\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}\right)=-\log\left(\nu_{q}^{s}\left([q]_{\mathfrak{D}}\right)\right)\in[0,+\infty)

is a.e. finite.
We next compute, using that
g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{D}=g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}\vee\mathfrak{D}
:

\displaystyle I_{\nu}\left(q;g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{D}|\mathfrak{D}\right)

\displaystyle=I_{\nu}\left(q;g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}\vee\mathfrak{D}|\mathfrak{D}\right)=-\log\left(\frac{\nu_{q}^{s}([q]_{g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}}\cap[q]_{\mathfrak{D}})}{\nu_{q}^{s}\left([q]_{\mathfrak{D}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\log\left({\nu_{q}^{s}([q]_{g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}}\cap[q]_{\mathfrak{D}})}\right)+\log\left({\nu_{q}^{s}\left([q]_{\mathfrak{D}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\log\left({\nu_{q}^{s}([q]_{g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}}}\right)+\log\left({\nu_{q}^{s}\left([q]_{\mathfrak{D}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=I_{\nu}\left(q;g_{t_{0}}^{n}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}|\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}\right)+\log\left({\nu_{q}^{s}\left([q]_{\mathfrak{D}}\right)}\right)

Dividing by
n
and sending it to
+\infty
yields
Eqn. (4.4.11)
.

We now compute the difference.
With
t_{0}>0
fixed as above, let
B:=\mathfrak{B}_{0}[q]
be an on atom of
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}
, and let
\{B_{j}\}_{j\in J(q,t_{0})}
be the atoms of
g_{t_{0}}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-})
contained in
B
, so we have
B=\coprod B_{j}
.
Recall that
\nu_{q}^{s}
is the conditional (probability) measure of
\nu
on
B
.
We then set

m_{j}:=\nu_{q}^{s}(B_{j})\quad d_{i,j}:=\nu_{q}^{s}\left(B_{j}\cap\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s}[q]\right)\quad\text{so}\quad\sum_{j}m_{j}=1\quad\sum_{i}d_{i,j}=m_{j}.

We also set
d_{i}:=\sum_{j}d_{i,j}=\nu_{q}^{s}(\mathcal{D}_{i}^{s}[q]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q])
.
The contribution of
B
to
h_{\nu}(g_{t_{0}};\mathfrak{B})
is (with the standard convention
0\log 0:=0
):

\sum_{j}-\log(m_{j})\cdot m_{j}

and to
h_{\nu}(g_{t_{0}};\mathfrak{D})
it is

\sum_{i}d_{i}\cdot\left(\sum_{j}-\log\left(\tfrac{d_{i,j}}{d_{i}}\right)\cdot\tfrac{d_{i,j}}{d_{i}}\right)=\\
=\sum_{j}\sum_{i}-\log\left(\tfrac{d_{i,j}}{d_{i}}\right)\cdot\tfrac{d_{i,j}}{d_{i}}\cdot d_{i}\leq\sum_{j}-\log\left(m_{j}\right)m_{j}

where in the last line we applied Jensen to the concave function
-x\log x
, with values
d_{i,j}/d_{i}
and weights
d_{i}
.
Integrating over
Q
yields
h_{\nu}\left(g_{t};\mathfrak{D}\right)\leq h_{\nu}(g_{t};\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-})
so combined with
Eqn. (4.4.11)
, it follows that the entropies agree, and furthermore we have a.e. equality in Jensen.
From the equality case of Jensen we conclude that
d_{i,j}/d_{i}=m_{j}
, for all
i,j
, for a.e.
q
.

Therefore, the conditional measures
\nu_{\mathfrak{D},q}
and
\nu^{s}_{q}
, with respect to the partitions
\mathfrak{D},\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}
, give the same mass to each atom
B_{j}
of
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}\vee g_{t_{0}}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-})
.
We now repeat the argument with
t_{0}\to+\infty
and note that
\bigvee_{t\geq 0}g_{t}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-})
is the partition of
Q
into points.
Therefore, for a.e.
q
the conditional measures for
\mathfrak{D},\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}
agree and hence there is a single
\mathcal{D}[q]=\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
.
∎

4.4.12 Lemma
.

Fix
\eta>0
arbitrary.
Fix also a linear identification of
W^{s}(q)
with
\mathbb{R}^{m}
(measurably in
q
) such that
T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is given by the vanishing of the last
m-\dim\mathcal{L}^{s}
coordinates.
There exists a function
c_{\eta,m,d}(q)
with
c_{\eta,m,d}(q)>0
for a.e.
q
, such
that the following holds:
Suppose
h
is a polynomial of degree
d
on
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
with

|D^{I}h(q)|=1,

where
D^{I}
is a partial derivative of degree
m
, and
I
is a multi-index involving only derivatives in the first
\dim\mathcal{L}^{s}
coordinates.

Then,

\nu^{s}_{q}\left(\{q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]\;\>:\;\>\left|h\left(q^{\prime}\right)\right|<c_{\eta,m,d}(q)\}\right)\leq\eta\nu^{s}_{q}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{q}
denote the space of polynomials
h
of
degree
d
on
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
, consisting of all polynomials on
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
modulo those that vanish on
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
.
Define next

\mathcal{P}_{q}^{\ast}:=\{h\in\mathcal{P}_{q}\;\>:\;\>\sup_{q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]\cap\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]}|h(q^{\prime})|=1\}\subset\mathcal{P}_{q}

which is a compact set.
Therefore, for a.e.
q

there exists a constant
C=C(q,d,m)>0
such that for
h\in\mathcal{P}_{q}^{\ast}
and all the (finitely many) multi-indices
I
as in the statement, we have:

(4.4.13)

|D^{I}h(q)|<C.

Suppose the assertion of the lemma is not true.
Then, there exists a sequence
h_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{q}
and
\delta_{n}\to 0

such that
|D^{I}h_{n}(q)|=1
and

\nu^{s}_{q}(\{q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]\;\>:\;\>|h_{n}(q^{\prime})|<\delta_{n}\})\geq\eta\nu^{s}_{q}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]).

Let
s_{n}=\sup_{q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}[q]\cap\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]}|h_{n}(q^{\prime})|
, and
let
\bar{h}_{n}=h_{n}/s_{n}
. Then,
\bar{h}_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{q}^{\ast}
.
We have, by (
4.4.13
),

\frac{1}{s_{n}}=\left|D^{I}\bar{h}_{n}(q)\right|\leq C,

Then, if
|h_{n}(q^{\prime})|<\delta_{n}
then,

|\bar{h}_{n}(q^{\prime})|<C\delta_{n}
. Thus,

\nu^{s}_{q}\left(\left\{q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]\;\>:\;\>|\bar{h}_{n}(q^{\prime})|<C\delta_{n}\right\}\right)\geq\eta\nu^{s}_{q}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]).

Since
\mathcal{P}_{q}^{\ast}
is compact, the polynomials
\bar{h}_{n}
converge to a
polynomial
\bar{h}_{\infty}
. Then,

\nu^{s}_{q}(\{q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]\;\>:\;\>|\bar{h}_{\infty}(q^{\prime})|=0\})\geq\eta\nu^{s}_{q}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]).

Since
\bar{h}_{\infty}
is not the zero polynomial,
this contradicts
Lemma 4.4.10
.
∎

4.4.14.
The QNI condition on
\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
.

Let
X
be the measurable cover of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
constructed
in §
2.5
.

4.4.15 Proposition
.

Suppose the QNI condition does not hold. Then,

(4.4.16)

\bar{\pi}_{x}^{cs}(nx^{\prime})\subset U^{+}[q],\\
\text{for a.e.\ $x\in X$ for all
$x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]$, and all $n\in N$}

Proof.

Define

\psi_{x}(x^{\prime},\tau)=d\left(n_{\tau}x^{\prime},\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{x^{\prime}}\left(U^{+}[x]\right)\right)^{2},\qquad\text{ where }n_{\tau}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\tau\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

We break up the proof into two parts.

Claim 1: There exists
\alpha_{0}>0
such that for any
\delta>0
, there exists
c(\delta)>0
such that for a
(1-\delta)
-fraction of
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\ell}^{-}[x]
, there exists some
\tau\in\left[-e^{-\ell/2},e^{-\ell/2}\right]
such that
\psi_{x}(x^{\prime},\tau)>c(\delta)e^{-\alpha_{0}\ell}
.

Claim 2:
Suppose that for
x^{\prime}_{1/2}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\ell/2}^{-}[x_{1/2}]
there exists
u^{\prime}x^{\prime}_{1/2}\in\mathcal{B}_{\ell/2}[x^{\prime}_{1/2}]
with

d^{u}\left(u^{\prime}x^{\prime}_{1/2},\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{x^{\prime}}\left(U^{+}[x]\right)\right)\geq\varepsilon.

Then for any
\delta>0
there exists
a(\delta)>0
such that, for a
(1-\delta)
-fraction of
ux_{1/2}\in\mathcal{B}_{\ell/x}[x_{1/2}]
we have

d^{u}\left(U^{+}[x^{\prime}_{1/2}],\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{x^{\prime}}\left(ux_{1/2}\right)\right)\geq a(\delta)\varepsilon.

Note that
N\subset U^{+}(q)
so Claim 1 implies the assumption of Claim 2 holds for a
(1-\delta)
fraction of
x^{\prime}_{1/2}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\ell/2}^{-}[x_{1/2}]
.
In turn, Claim 2 implies that the QNI condition as in
Definition 3.2.2
holds and completes the proof.
Claim 2 however is a direct consequence of
[
EM18
, Lemma 6.18]
, so it remains to show Claim 1.

To proceed, note that for each
x
the function
\psi_{x}
is real-analytic in
x^{\prime}
and
\tau
.
It also suffices to prove that there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
, with the same quantifies on
x^{\prime},c(\delta)
we have that

\left|\frac{d^{k}}{d\tau^{k}}\psi_{x}\left(x^{\prime},\tau\right)\Big|_{\tau=0}\right|>c(\delta)e^{-\alpha_{1}\ell}

and from this derive the claim, using the same argument as in
Lemma 4.4.12
.

Suppose (
4.4.16
) does not hold. Then,

\psi_{x}
is not identically
0
for
x
in a set of positive
measure. Then, by equivariance
\psi_{x}
is not identically
0
for
almost all
x
.
Then, there exists
m
and
k
such that for almost
all
x
, there exists a multi-index
I
of length
m
such that

\frac{\partial^{I}}{\partial^{I}x^{\prime}}\left(\frac{d}{d\tau}\right)^{k}\psi_{x}(x^{\prime},\tau)\Big|_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x^{\prime}=x}}{{\tau=0}}}\neq 0.

Then for any
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
and a constant
c(\delta)>0
such that for
x_{1/2}\in K
,

\left|\frac{\partial^{I}}{\partial^{I}x^{\prime}_{1/2}}\left(\frac{d}{d\tau_{1/2}}\right)^{k}\psi_{x_{1/2}}(x^{\prime}_{1/2},\tau_{1/2})\Big|_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x^{\prime}_{1/2}=x_{1/2}}}{{\tau_{1/2}=0}}}\right|\geq c(\delta).

Write
x_{1/2}=g_{\ell/2}x
,

x^{\prime}_{1/2}=g_{\ell/2}x^{\prime}
where
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}^{-}_{0}[x]
. Let
\tau_{1/2}=e^{-\ell}\tau
, so that
n_{\tau_{1/2}}=g^{-\ell/2}n_{\tau}g^{\ell/2}
.
Then,

\left|\frac{\partial^{I}}{\partial^{I}x^{\prime}}\left(\frac{d}{d\tau}\right)^{k}\psi_{x_{1/2}}\left(g_{\ell/2}x^{\prime},e^{-\ell}\tau\right)\Big|_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x^{\prime}=x}}{{\tau=0}}}\right|\geq c(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}.

where
\alpha>0
depends only on the Lyapunov spectrum.
Choose
M>2\alpha
.
Then, there exists an integer
d
depending
only on the Lyapunov spectrum,
m
, and
k
, and for every
\delta>0
a compact set

K_{1}
with
\nu(K_{1})>1-\delta

such that for
x_{1/2}\in K_{1}
there exists a polynomial

P_{x_{1/2}}(\cdot)
of degree
d
given by a Taylor expansion, such that for
x^{\prime}_{1/2}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\ell/2}^{-}[x_{1/2}]
we have:

\left|\frac{\partial^{I^{\prime}}}{\partial^{I^{\prime}}x^{\prime}_{1/2}}\left[\left(\frac{d}{d\tau_{1/2}}\right)^{k}\psi_{1/2}(x^{\prime}_{1/2},\tau_{1/2})\big|_{\tau_{1/2}=0}-P_{x_{1/2}}(x^{\prime}_{1/2})\right]\right|\leq C(\delta)e^{-M\ell},

for all multi-indices
{I^{\prime}}
with
|{I^{\prime}}|\leq m
.
Therefore,

(4.4.17)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial^{I}}{\partial^{I}x^{\prime}}P_{x_{1/2}}(g_{\ell/2}x^{\prime})\Big|_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x^{\prime}=x}}{{\tau=0}}}\right|\geq c(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}.

Then, by
Lemma 4.4.12
applied to (a
normalized version of) the polynomial
P_{x_{1/2}}(g_{\ell/2}x^{\prime})
for
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[x]
,
for at least
(1-\delta)
-fraction of
x^{\prime}_{1/2}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\ell/2}^{-}[x_{1/2}]
,

\left|P_{x_{1/2}}\left(x^{\prime}_{1/2}\right)\right|\geq c(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}.

Since
M>2\alpha
, by
Eqn. (4.4.17)

we get least
(1-\delta)
-fraction of
x^{\prime}_{1/2}\in\mathfrak{B}_{\ell/2}^{-}[x_{1/2}]
,

(4.4.18)

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{d}{d\tau}\right)^{k}\psi_{x_{1/2}}\left(x^{\prime}_{1/2},e^{-\ell}\tau\right)|_{\tau=0}\right|\geq c(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}

and this completes the proof.
∎

4.4.19.
Proving linearity of the stable and unstable supports

4.4.20 Proposition
.

Suppose (
4.4.16
) holds. Then
\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})

invariant.
In addition, there exists an
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-equivariant map

L
from
\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
to
\mathbb{C}
-linear subspaces of

H^{1}(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{C})
, such that
L
is the complexification of an
\mathbb{R}
-linear subspace
L_{\mathbb{R}}\subset H^{1}\left(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{R}\right)
, and
such that for a.e.
q\in\mathcal{H}^{1}(\vec{\alpha})
, we have that
q\in L(q)
, and the conditional measure
\nu^{u}_{q}
of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
is supported on
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]\cap L(q)
and of Lebesgue class on it, and the conditional measure
\nu^{s}_{q}
of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
is supported on
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]\cap L(q)
and of Lebesgue class on it.

Proof.

Since
nx\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
, we have, by the definition of
\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{x}
,
\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{nx}=\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{x}
. Suppose (
4.4.16
) holds. Then

\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{nx}(nx^{\prime})\subset U^{+}[nx],\\
\text{for a.e.\ $x\in X$ for all
$x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]$, for all $n\in N$}

Then, taking the derivative with respect to
x^{\prime}
at
x^{\prime}=x
, we get that for
x
in a set of full measure and all
n\in N
,

\pi^{cs}_{nx}(n\cdot T_{x}\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]))\subset T_{nx}U^{+}[nx].

Then, by
Lemma 4.2.7
, for a.e.
x\in X
,
(
4.2.8
) holds.
Let
\mathcal{Z}(x)
be as in (
4.2.4
), and let
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(x)
be as
(
4.2.5
). Then, by
Lemma 4.4.9
,
(
4.2.16
) holds. Thus, we may apply

Proposition 4.2.15
. In particular, we get that

U^{+}[x]
depends only on
\sigma(x)
,

\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-invariant, the stable and unstable supports are homogeneous,

\mathcal{Z}(q)=\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(q)=T_{q}U^{+}[q]\oplus T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q],

and the sum of the Lyapunov exponents along
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is
0
.

Note that since
U^{+}
is
N
-invariant, (
4.4.16
) implies that
for a.e.
q\in\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
for all
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
, for all
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
,

(4.4.21)

n_{\tau}^{-1}\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{q}(n_{\tau}q^{\prime})\in U^{+}[q],\\

Thus, for all
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
, and for all
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
,

f_{q,\tau}(q^{\prime})\in U^{+}[q].

Differentiating this at
q^{\prime}=q
and using
Lemma 4.4.8
(c), we get

\iota_{\tau}(T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q])\subset T_{q}U^{+}[q].

Alternatively,

(4.4.22)

T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\subset\iota_{\tau}^{-1}(T_{q}U^{+}[q]).

Also, since both
U^{+}
and
\mathcal{L}^{s}
are
g_{t}
-equivariant, we have
for all
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
, and for all
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and all
t\in\mathbb{R}

(4.4.23)

f_{q,\tau,t}(q^{\prime})\in U^{+}[q].

(Initially (
4.4.23
) holds for
t
small, so we can think of the Kontsevich-Zorich cocycle as constant. But then, by analyticity, it holds for all
t
).

4.4.24 Claim
.

We have
\forall\tau\in\mathbb{R}
:

(4.4.25)

\iota_{\tau}(T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q])=T_{q}U^{+}[q].

In particular,

\dim\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]=\dim U^{+}[q].

Proof of claim.

We have the Lyapunov decomposition of the Kontsevich-Zorich cocycle:

H^{1}(M,\Sigma,\mathbb{R})=\bigoplus_{k}E^{\lambda_{k}}.

Then,

T_{q}U^{+}[q]=\bigoplus_{k}E^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}U^{+}[q],

and

T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]=\bigoplus_{k}iE^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q].

Note that

(4.4.26)

\iota_{\tau}(iE^{\lambda_{k}}(q))=E^{\lambda_{k}}(q)

where on the
right-hand side we have identified
H^{1}(M,\Sigma,\mathbb{R})
with
\{x\in H^{1}(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{C})\;\>:\;\>\operatorname{Im}x=0\}
. Under this identification,

T_{q}U^{+}[q]
is a
P
-invariant subbundle of
H^{1}(M,\Sigma,\mathbb{R})
, and
thus, by
Lemma 4.4.9
,

(4.4.27)

\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}\dim E^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}U^{+}[q]\geq 0.

The Lyapunov exponent of
g_{1}
on
E^{\lambda_{k}}
is
1+\lambda_{k}
,
and the Lyapunov exponent of
g_{1}
on
iE^{\lambda_{k}}
is

-1+\lambda_{k}
, and
0\leq\lambda_{k}\leq 1
. Then, using
(
4.4.26
), (
4.4.22
) and (
4.4.27
),

(4.4.28)

\sum_{k}(1-\lambda_{k})\dim(iE^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q])\leq\\
\leq\sum_{k}(1-\lambda_{k})\dim(\iota_{\tau}^{-1}(E^{\lambda_{k}}(q))\cap\iota_{\tau}^{-1}(T_{q}U^{+}[q]))\\
=\sum_{k}(1-\lambda_{k})\dim(E^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}U^{+}[q])=\\
\sum_{k}(1+\lambda_{k})\dim(E^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}U^{+}[q])-2\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}\dim E^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}U^{+}[q]\leq\\
\leq\sum_{k}(1+\lambda_{k})\dim(E^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}U^{+}[q]).

But, since the sum of the Lyapunov exponents along
\mathcal{Z}
is
0
, we have

\sum_{k}(1-\lambda_{k})\dim(iE^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q])=\sum_{k}(1+\lambda_{k})\dim(E^{\lambda_{k}}(q)\cap T_{q}U^{+}[q]).

Thus, all the inequalities in (
4.4.28
) are equalities, and thus

T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]=\iota_{\tau}^{-1}(T_{q}U^{+}[q]).

This concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

Let
H^{\mathcal{L}}(q)=H^{-}(q)\cap T_{q}\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
. Then,
H^{\mathcal{L}}(q)
is the tangent space to the orbit through
q
of a subgroup
\bar{N}^{\mathcal{L}}(q)\subset\bar{N}^{m}
.
By ergodicity of the measure,
\bar{N}^{\mathcal{L}}(q)
is independent of
q
.
Since the conditional measure of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
is of Lebesgue class along
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
, and at
\nu^{s}
-a.e. point
\bar{N}^{\mathcal{L}}
is tangent to
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
, it follows that
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is
\bar{N}^{\mathcal{L}}
-invariant.
It is now a standard argument that the conditional measures
\nu^{s}
, and hence
\nu
, are
\bar{N}^{\mathcal{L}}
-invariant.

Let

(4.4.29)

H^{U}(q)=H^{+}(q)\cap T_{q}U^{+}[q]=\iota_{\tau}(H^{\mathcal{L}}(q)).

Then,
H^{U}(q)
is the tangent space to the orbit through
q
of a subgroup
N^{U}(q)\subset N^{m}
.

The same argument shows that
\nu
is
N^{U}
-invariant. Also we have
N^{U}=\iota(N^{\mathcal{L}})
, where
\iota:\bar{N}^{m}\to N^{m}

is defined by

\iota\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
s_{1}&1\end{pmatrix},\dots,\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
s_{m}&1\end{pmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&s_{1}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\dots,\begin{pmatrix}1&s_{m}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right)

Then, by
Corollary 4.4.3
,

\bar{N}^{\mathcal{L}}
and
N^{U}
generate
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})_{\beta}
where
\beta
is the smallest partition such that
\bar{N}^{\mathcal{L}}\subset\SL(2,\mathbb{R})_{\beta}
and
N^{U}\subset\SL(2,\mathbb{R})_{\beta}
.
In particular,
\mathcal{L}^{s}
is
\bar{N}_{\beta}
-invariant, where

\bar{N}_{\beta}=N^{m}\cap\SL(2,\mathbb{R})_{\beta}
.

4.4.30 Claim
.

For a.e.
q
,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]=\bar{N}_{\beta}(\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\cap H^{\perp}[q])
.

Proof of claim.

We have, for all
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
,

f_{q,\tau,t}(q^{\prime})\in U^{+}[q]
.
From
Lemma 4.4.8
(b), we have, as
t\to\infty
,

f_{q,\tau,t}(q^{\prime})=q+e^{-2t}\iota_{\tau}(q^{\prime}-q)+O(e^{-4t})

Thus, for all
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
,
\iota_{\tau}(q^{\prime}-q)\in T_{q}U^{+}[q]
.
In particular, if we write

q^{\prime}=q+iw+iv

with
w\in H^{-}(q)
,
v\in H^{\perp}(q)
, we have
\iota_{\tau}(w)\in H^{U}(q)
, which implies, in view of (
4.4.29
), that
w\in H^{\mathcal{L}}(q)
. Thus, there exists
\bar{n}\in\bar{N}^{L}
such that
\bar{n}q=q+iw
. Then,
\bar{n}^{-1}q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\cap H^{\perp}[q]
.
Since
\bar{N}^{L}\subset\bar{N}_{\beta}
, the claim follows.
∎

4.4.31 Claim
.

For a.e.
q
,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is star-shaped with respect to
q
. In other words, for any
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

(4.4.32)

q+t(q^{\prime}-q)\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q].

Proof of claim.

In view of the
\bar{N}_{\beta}
-invariance of
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
and
Claim 4.4.30
, it is enough to prove (
4.4.32
) for
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\cap iH^{\perp}[q]
. But then,
Lemma 4.4.8
(a) implies that

(4.4.33)

f_{q,\tau}(q^{\prime})=q+\iota_{\tau}(q^{\prime}-q)\in U^{+}[q]\cap H^{\perp}[q].

Since for
\tau\neq 0
,
f_{q,\tau}
is injective, and we have
\dim\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]=\dim U^{+}[q]
, we get, for all
\tau\neq 0
,

f_{q,\tau}(\mathcal{L}^{s}[q])=U^{+}[q]
.
Then,
(f_{q,\tau^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ f_{q,\tau})(\mathcal{L}^{s}[q])=\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
.
Also,
(f_{q,\tau^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ f_{q,\tau})(\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\cap iH^{\perp}[q])=\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\cap iH^{\perp}[q]
. But, it follows from (
4.4.33
) that for
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]\cap iH^{\perp}[q])
,

(f_{q,\tau^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ f_{q,\tau})(q^{\prime})=q+(\tau-\tau^{\prime})(q^{\prime}-q).

Since
\tau^{\prime}
is arbitrary, the claim follows.
∎

We now continue the proof of
Proposition 4.4.20
.
We have for
x\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[x]=\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
. Applying
Claim 4.4.31
to
x
in place of
q
, we get that

\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is star-shaped with respect to any
x\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
.

Suppose
x
,
y\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
are arbitrary.
Then, since
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is star-shaped with respect to
x
, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
x+t(y-x)\in\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
. Then,
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
contains the line
through
x
and
y
. This implies that
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
is an affine
subspace of
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
. The same argument shows that
U^{+}[q]
is an
affine subspace of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
, and then (
4.4.25
)
shows that
\iota(\mathcal{L}^{s}[q])=U^{+}[q]
. Let

L(q)=\GL(2,\mathbb{R})_{\beta}\left((U^{+}[q]-q)+i(\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]-q)\right).

Then
L(q)
is a complex-linear subspace of
H^{1}(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{C})
, which is moreover the complexification of an
\mathbb{R}
-linear subspace due to
Eqn. (4.4.25)
.
The proposition now follows since

\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})_{\beta}
-invariant, and the conditional measures of
\nu

along
U^{+}[q]
and
\mathcal{L}^{s}[q]
are Lebesgue.
∎

4.4.34.
Completion of the proof

4.4.35 Theorem
.

(cf.
[
EM18
, Theorem 2.1]
)

Suppose
\nu
is an ergodic
P
-invariant measure on

\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
. Then,
\nu
is
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})

invariant. In addition, there exists an
\SL(2,\mathbb{R})
-equivariant map

L
from
\mathcal{H}_{1}(\vec{\alpha})
to
\mathbb{C}
-linear subspaces of

H^{1}(M,\Sigma;\mathbb{C})

such that for a.e.
q\in\mathcal{H}^{1}(\vec{\alpha})
,
q\in L(q)
, the
conditional measure
\nu^{u}_{q}
of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
is the
restriction of Lebegue measure to

\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]\cap L(q)
and the conditional measure
\nu^{s}_{q}
of
\nu

along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
is the restriction of Lebesgue measure to

\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]\cap L(q)
.

Proof.

The result follows from
Proposition 4.4.20
, since we can apply
Theorem 4.1.2
until the QNI condition no longer holds, in which case we apply
Proposition 4.4.15
and conclude that
Eqn. (4.4.16)
holds.
∎

The derivation of
Theorem 1.3.3
and

Theorem 1.3.4
are exactly the same
as the corresponding arguments in
[
EM18
, Sections 16-19]

and
[
EMM15
]
.

4.5.
Outline of the proof of

Theorem 4.1.2
.

We begin by describing a set of general preliminary tools, and proceed to an outline of the proof in
§4.5.5
.

4.5.1.
Normal forms, Linearization, and the Space of Cosets

The unstable manifolds
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
admit normal form coordinates and a subresonant structure, see
Appendix C
and
Appendix D
.
In particular, there exist finite-dimensional Lie groups
\mathbf{G}^{sr}(q)\supset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
acting on
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
by subresonant, resp. strictly subresonant maps.
Furthermore, we can “linearize” the unstable dynamics via a linear cocycle
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)
and equivariant embeddings
L_{q}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]\hookrightarrow L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)
.

Next, given a compatible family of subgroups
U^{+}(q)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
as in
Definition 3.1.5
, we consider the space
\mathcal{C}(q)
of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
-translates of the
U^{+}(q)
-orbit
U^{+}[q]:=U^{+}(q)\cdot q
.
To study its dynamics, we linearize it again via a linear cocycle
L\mathcal{C}
and equivariant embedding
\mathcal{C}(q)\hookrightarrow L\mathcal{C}(q)
(see
Section 5
).

Because the compatible family of subgroups
U^{+}
is only defined on the finite cover
X
and not on
Q
(see
§2.5.3
) many of our constructions are carried out on
X
; see
§2.5
for the relevant constructions.

We recall also the measurable partition
\mathfrak{B}_{0}[q]
subordinated to the unstables, and we will also work with
\mathcal{B}_{0}[q]:=U^{+}[q]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[q]
and the corresponding
\mathcal{B}_{0}(q)\subset U^{+}(q)
.

4.5.2.
Factorization

Continuing with a compatible family of subgroups
U^{+}(q)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
, we will construct in
Section 7
and
Section 8
:

•

A cocycle
V^{s}
which is smooth along stable manifolds, and has stable holonomy, together with a measurable
g_{t}
-equivariant section
\iota_{s}\colon Q\to V^{s}
.
The section yields a measurable
g_{t}
-equivariant embedding

F^{s}_{q}\colon\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]\to V^{s}(q)

by evaluating the section at
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
and moving the value to
V^{s}(q)
by the holonomy.

•

A
4
-variable map

(4.5.3)

\displaystyle V^{s}(q)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)}L\mathcal{C}(g_{t}uq_{1})

where
L\mathcal{C}
is the linearization cocycle from
§4.5.1
(see
§5.1.5
for its construction and
Proposition 5.1.14
for its key property).
The map is obtained as a composition

V^{s}(q)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell)}L\mathcal{C}(q_{1})\xrightarrow{u^{*}}L\mathcal{C}(uq_{1})\xrightarrow{g_{t}}L\mathcal{C}(g_{t}uq_{1})

•

Let
U^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{t}uq_{1}]
be the curve represented by
\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)(F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime}))
.
The Hausdorff distance between
U^{+}[g_{t}q_{1}^{\prime}]
and
U^{\prime}
(measured at
g_{t}uq_{1}
) is exponentially small in
\ell
.

•

It follows from the properties of
L\mathcal{C}
that the Hausdorff distance between
U^{+}[g_{t}uq_{1}]
and
U^{\prime}
measured at
g_{t}uq_{1}
is, up to a (slowly varying in the basepoint) multiplicative constant, equal to
\left\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)(F^{s}(q^{\prime}))\right\|
.

•

The norm also satisfies bi-Lipschitz estimates, see
§8.4
.

•

An interpolation map, which is subresonant:

\widetilde{\phi}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]\text{ and satisfies }\widetilde{\phi}(y_{1/2})=z_{1/2}.

We note that what is needed for subsequent arguments are a number of properties of the above constructions (particularly
\mathcal{A}_{2}
).
To construct the above objects however, we will make use of the “half-way points”
y_{1/2}=g_{-\ell/2}uq_{1}
and
z_{1/2}=\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y_{1/2}]\cap\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{-\ell/2}q_{1}^{\prime}]
(for two bottom-linked
Y
-configurations
Y,Y^{\prime}
, see
§4.5.7
).

4.5.4.
Inert subspaces

To study the divergence of two
U^{+}
-translates, we note that the cocycle
L\mathcal{C}
contains a tautological
1
-dimensional subspace
L^{\tau}\mathcal{C}
spanned by the point corresponding to
U^{+}(q)
.
We work with the cocycle
\mathbf{H}:=L\mathcal{C}/L^{\tau}\mathcal{C}
, and descend
\mathcal{A}_{2}
from
Eqn. (4.5.3)
to take values
\mathbf{H}
.
We will also work with the natural map
\mathbf{j}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathbf{H}
, obtain as the composition
\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow L\mathcal{C}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{H}
.

In
§10.1
we introduce the notion of
inert
subbundles
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}\subset\mathbf{H}
and
\mathbf{E}:=\oplus\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}
.
Then (using Zimmer amenable reduction) we can refine the Lyapunov decomposition of
\mathbf{E}
as
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}=\oplus\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{j}
and introduce an indexing set
ij\in\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
.
Furthermore, we obtain scalar multiplicative cocycles
\lambda_{ij}(q;t)
measuring growth in
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{j}
.

The index set
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
carries a “synchronization” equivalence relation (see
Proposition 10.5.1
) with equivalence class denoted
[ij]\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
and associated subcocycles
\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}
.
The properties of these decompositions are contained in
§10.5
.

Associated to the cocycle
\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}
we obtain subsets
\mathcal{E}_{ij}(q)\subset\mathcal{C}(q)
defined as
\mathbf{j}^{-1}\left(\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}\right)
(see
§11.1.3
).
This leads to measurable equivalence relations

\sim_{ij}
(see

§11.1.5
) with atoms

\Psi_{ij}[q]\subset\mathfrak{B}_{0}[q]
(see

§11.1.28
) and associated
leafwise measures
\widetilde{f}_{ij}[q]
(coming from conditional measures
f_{ij}(q)
).

It is these leafwise measure that will be ultimately shown to have extra invariance.

4.5.5.
Choosing the eight points

Fix a constant
\alpha\gg 1
and only take
t\leq\alpha\ell
.

A simplified scheme for choosing the eight points, analogously to

[
EL18
]
, is as follows: (see Figure
4.5.9
)

(i)

Choose
{q}_{1}
in some good set, such that in particular for
most
t
we have
g_{t}{q}_{1},g_{-t}q_{1}\in K_{*}
,
and for most
u
and most
t
, we have
g_{t}u{q}_{1}\in K_{*}
.

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{A}_{2}({q}_{1},u,\ell,t)
be as in
Eqn. (4.5.3)
(see
§8.3
for more details), and define
{q}=g_{-\ell}{q}_{1}
and
{q}_{2}=g_{t}u{q}_{1}
, where
t=\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)
is the solution to the equation
\|\mathcal{A}_{2}({q}_{1},u,\ell,t)\|=\epsilon

(see
§8.4.13
for
\tau_{(\epsilon)}
).

By
Lemma 8.4.14
, for fixed
{q}_{1}
,
u
,
\epsilon
, the function
\tau_{(\epsilon)}({q}_{1},u,\ell)
is bilipshitz in
\ell
, and by
Proposition 8.4.15
for most choices of
\ell
, we have
{q},q_{2}\in K_{*}
.

(iii)

For all
ij\in\tilde{\Lambda}
(see
§4.5.4
) let
t_{ij}=t_{ij}({q}_{1},u,\ell)
be defined by the equation
\lambda_{ij}(u{q}_{1},t)=\lambda_{ij}({q}_{1},t_{ij})
.
Since
\lambda_{ij}(x,t)
is bilipshitz in
t
, the same argument shows that for most choices of
\ell
, we have
{q}_{3,ij}:=g_{t_{ij}}{q}_{1}\in K_{*}
.

(iv)

Let
M_{u}\subset V^{s}_{*}
(see
§8.1.2
for
V^{s}_{*}
) be the subspace of
Lemma 9.2.1
for the linear map
\mathcal{A}_{2}({q}_{1},u,\ell,t)
restricted to
V^{s}_{*}({q})
.
By
Proposition 9.2.2
, we can choose
{q}^{\prime}\in K_{*}
with
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}_{loc}[q]
, such that
\|F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime})\|\approx 1
and
such that
F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime})
avoids most of the subspaces
M_{u}
as
u
varies over
\mathcal{B}_{0}(q)
(recall that
\mathcal{B}_{0}(q)\subset U^{+}(q)
comes from intersecting with the Markov partition
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
).
Then, for most
u
we have:

d^{\mathcal{H}}_{loc}(U^{+}[{q}_{2}],\phi^{-1}_{\tau}(U^{+}[{q}_{2}^{\prime})])\approx\|\mathcal{A}_{2}({q}_{1},u,\ell,t)F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime})\|\approx\\
\approx\|\mathcal{A}_{2}({q}_{1},u,\ell,t)\|\|F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime})\|\approx\epsilon,

as required, where
\phi_{\tau}
is the evolved interpolation map, which satisfies estimates spelled out in
§6.3
and in particular does not move points too much.

(v)

By an argument based on
Proposition 10.5.2
,
for most choices of
u
the point
{q}_{2}^{\prime}
is close to
\Psi_{ij}[q_{2}]
for some
ij\in\tilde{\Lambda}
(see
§4.5.4
for notation).

(vi)

We can now proceed and let
{q}_{1}^{\prime}=g_{\ell}{q}^{\prime}
,
{q}_{2}^{\prime}=g_{t}u{q}_{1}^{\prime}
where
t=\tau_{(\epsilon)}({q}_{1},u,\ell)
, and let
{q}_{3,ij}^{\prime}=g_{t_{ij}}{q}_{1}^{\prime}
.
Recall that
{\nu}
is
g_{t}
-invariant and
U^{+}
-invariant.
Since
\lambda_{ij}({q}_{1},t_{ij})=\lambda_{ij}(u{q}_{1},t)
, and one can show that
\lambda_{ij}(uq_{1}^{\prime},t)\approx\lambda_{ij}(q_{1}^{\prime},t_{ij})
, we have

(4.5.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{f}_{ij}[{q}_{2}]&\propto A_{*}\tilde{f}_{ij}[{q}_{3,ij}]\\
\tilde{f}_{ij}[{q}_{2}^{\prime}]&\propto A^{\prime}_{*}\tilde{f}_{ij}[{q}_{3,ij}^{\prime}]\end{split}

where
A,A^{\prime}
are almost the same (bounded) strictly subresonant map.
Here and below,
\propto
means that the measures are proportional, whereas
\approx
will mean that the measures are close in the appropriately metrized space of leafwise measures up to scale (see
Definition 11.1.37
).

Since
{q}_{3,ij}
and
{q}_{3,ij}^{\prime}
are very close, we can ensure that

\tilde{f}_{ij}[{q}_{3,ij}^{\prime}]\approx\tilde{f}_{ij}[{q}_{3,ij}]
.
One might expect to find
\tilde{f}_{ij}[q_{2}]\approx\tilde{f}_{ij}[q_{2}^{\prime}]
but a more careful analysis (see
Proposition 11.2.3
) shows that the maps
A
and
A^{\prime}
of
Eqn. (4.5.6)
are not exactly the same.
We get

\tilde{f}_{ij}[{q}_{2}^{\prime}]\approx\psi(q_{2},q_{2}^{\prime})_{*}\tilde{f}_{ij}[{q}_{2}].

where
\psi(q_{2},q_{2}^{\prime})
is defined in
Eqn. (11.2.11)
.
Applying the argument with a sequence of
\ell
’s going to infinity,
and passing to a limit along a subsequence,
we obtain points
{\tilde{q}_{2}}
and
{\tilde{q}_{2}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[\tilde{q}_{2}]

where
\tilde{q}_{2}^{\prime}
is not in
U^{+}[\tilde{q}_{2}]
.
We also have that (in some appropriate sense)

\psi(q_{2},q_{2}^{\prime})
converges to
\wp^{+}(\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{2}^{\prime})

satisfying

\tilde{f}_{ij}[\tilde{q}_{2}^{\prime}]\propto\wp^{+}(\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{2}^{\prime})_{*}\tilde{f}_{ij}[\tilde{q}_{2}]

Here
\wp^{+}(\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{2}^{\prime})
is a strictly subresonant map of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[\tilde{q}_{2}]
arising from the measurable connection
P^{+}
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
(see
§B.2.6
and
§D.2
).

A standard argument
§12.2
then shows there exist a closed subgroup
U^{+}_{new}(\tilde{q}_{2})
compatible with the measure and with
U^{+}(\tilde{q}_{2})\subsetneq U^{+}_{new}(\tilde{q}_{2})
such that
\tilde{f}_{ij}[\tilde{q}_{2}]
is
U^{+}_{new}(\tilde{q}_{2})
-invariant.

4.5.7.
Y-configurations

The formal proof uses the same ideas, but we need to take a bit more care, mostly because we also need to make sure that
{q}_{2}^{\prime}
and
{q}_{3,ij}^{\prime}
belong to
K_{*}
.
We now give a slightly more precise outline of the strategy.

We will make use of the notion of
Y
-configurations
Y(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau,t_{0})
with
q_{1}\in X,u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
and
\ell,\tau,t_{0}\geq 0
(see
§6.1
).
The data in a
Y
-configuration determines the points
q,q_{2},q_{3}
(see
Definition 6.1.2
and
Figure 4.5.9
), and for a compact set
K
we say
Y\in K
if
q,q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}\in K
.
Two
Y
-configurations
Y,Y^{\prime}
can be
bottom-linked
(see
Definition 6.1.8
) which means that
\ell=\ell^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
, and
right-linked
(see
Definition 6.1.9
) if in addition
t_{0}=t_{0}^{\prime}
.

4.5.8.
Left-balanced,
ij
-top-balanced, and
ij
-balanced

We will say (see
§9.1
) that a
Y
-configuration is
(C,\varepsilon)-
left-balanced
if
\left\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\right\|\in\left[\tfrac{1}{C}\varepsilon,C\varepsilon\right]
.
Note that this condition does not depend on the upper-right leg of the
Y
-diagram (i.e. the parameter
t
).
Furthermore, given
\varepsilon
there exists a unique
\tau_{(\varepsilon)}:=\tau_{(\varepsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)
such that
Y(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau_{(\varepsilon)},t)
is
(1,\varepsilon)
-left-balanced (by monotonicity of
\left\|\mathcal{A}_{2}\right\|
, see
Eqn. (8.4.2)
).

Figure 4.5.9.
Left: The general arrangement of points in the argument.
Right: More detailed notation.
Points in a transparent gray blob are exponentially close in the parameter
\ell
, for some exponent that depends only on the Lyapunov spectrum, and constants that depend on the Lusin set.

Given
ij\in\tilde{\Lambda}
, we will say (see
Definition 11.2.2
) that a
Y
-diagram is
(C,ij)
-top-balanced
if
q_{3}=g_{t_{3}}^{ij}(q_{1}),q_{2}=g_{t_{2}}^{ij}(uq_{1})
and
|t_{2}-t_{3}|\leq C
.
Again, note that given
q_{1},u,\ell,\tau
there exists a unique
t_{0}
such that
Y(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau,t_{0})
is
(0,ij)
-top-balanced.

Finally, we will (see
Definition 12.1.5
) denote by
Y_{ij,balanced}(q_{1},u,\ell)
the unique
Y
-configuration which is both
(1,\varepsilon)
-left-balanced and
(0,ij)
-top-balanced.

The arguments of (i),(ii), (iii) of
§4.5.5
above and Fubini’s theorem show that for an appropriate compact
K
and an almost full density set of
\ell
, there are many
Y
-configurations with
Y_{ij,balanced}(q_{1},u,\ell)\in K
, see
Eqn. (12.1.10)
below for the exact statement.

4.5.10.
Coupled
Y
-configurations

We say that two
Y
-configurations
Y=Y_{ij}({q}_{1},u,\ell)
and
Y^{\prime}=Y_{ij}({q}_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})
with the same
ij
are
coupled
if

\ell=\ell^{\prime}
,
u=u^{\prime}
,
{q}(Y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{W}^{s}_{loc}[{q}(Y)]
, and also the conditions in (iv) of
§4.5.5
above are satisfied.

Then the argument described (iv), specifically
Proposition 9.2.2
, shows that we can (for most values
of
\ell
) choose points
{q}_{1}
,
{q}_{1}^{\prime}
such that for most

u
and all
ij
, the
Y
-configurations
Y_{ij}({q}_{1},u,\ell)
and

Y_{ij}({q}_{1}^{\prime},u,\ell)
are both good and also are coupled
(see
§12.1.11
“Choice of parameters #2” below for the precise statement).

We then choose
u
as in (v).
(See
Claim 12.1.40
,
Claim 12.1.48
, and
§12.1.53
“Choice of parameters #5”).

We are now almost
done, except for the fact that the lengths of the legs of

Y_{ij}=Y_{ij}({q}_{1},u,\ell)
and
Y_{ij}^{\prime}=Y_{ij}({q}_{1}^{\prime},u,\ell)
are not same. (The bottom
leg of
Y_{ij}
has length
\ell
, and so does the bottom leg of
Y^{\prime}_{ij}
, but
the two top legs of
Y_{ij}
can potentially have different lengths than
the corresponding legs of
Y^{\prime}_{ij}
).
We show that the lengths of the corresponding legs are close (see
Eqn. (12.1.54)
and
Eqn. (12.1.58)
).
Then we proceed to part (vi).

5.
The space
\mathcal{C}
and the cocycles
L\mathcal{C}
and
\mathbf{H}

5.1.
Some results on nilpotent Lie groups

In this subsection, we will take the group
N
to be the strictly subresonant maps
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}
and
U^{+}
to be given by groups as in
Definition 3.1.5
.
In particular, the nilpotent Lie group
N
will have nontrivial automorphisms given by the conjugation action of the subresonant maps
\mathbf{G}^{sr}
(cf.
Proposition C.2.7
).

5.1.1.
Setup

All our nilpotent Lie groups are connected and simply connected, so that most arguments can be translated to Lie algebras via the exponential map.
The Lie groups are denoted in Roman font, such as
N,U^{+}
and the Lie algebras in corresponding Gothic script
\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{u}^{+}
etc. without further mention.
Assume that we have a weight filtration
N_{\leq\lambda_{i}}
such that
[N_{\leq\lambda_{i}},N_{\leq\lambda_{j}}]\subseteq N_{\leq\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}}
and all
\lambda_{i}
are strictly negative.
It also induces a filtration on
U^{+}
by
U^{+}_{\leq\lambda_{i}}:=U^{+}\cap N_{\leq\lambda_{i}}
and analogously on Lie algebras.

5.1.2 Remark

(On filtrations)
.

We could either take
N=N_{\leq-1}
and then the induced filtration is the lower central series.
Or, in cases of interest for dynamics we could take on
\mathfrak{n}
the Lyapunov filtration.

We are grateful to Brian Chung for providing the proof of the next result.

5.1.3 Lemma

(Transverse structure of quotients)
.

Let
N
be a nilpotent Lie group and
U^{+}
a Lie subgroup.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{v}\subset\mathfrak{n}
is a subspace satisfying
\mathfrak{v}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{+}=\mathfrak{n}
.
Consider the maps

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{+}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phantom{XX}\exp_{2}\phantom{XX}}N\xrightarrow{\phantom{XX}\log\phantom{XX}}\mathfrak{n}

\displaystyle v\oplus u

\displaystyle\mapsto\exp(v)\cdot\exp(u)=:\exp_{2}(u,v)

and call their composite
ch_{\mathfrak{v}}
, which is a polynomial map.
Assume that we additionally have:

\mathfrak{n}_{\leq\lambda_{i}}=(\mathfrak{u}^{+}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{\leq\lambda_{i}})\oplus(\mathfrak{n}_{\leq\lambda_{i}}\cap\mathfrak{v}).

where
\mathfrak{u}_{\leq\lambda_{i}}^{+}:=\mathfrak{u}^{+}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{\leq\lambda_{i}}
is the induced filtration on
\mathfrak{u}^{+}
.
Then:

(i)

The two weight filtrations on
\mathfrak{v}
agree, one coming from the identification with
\mathfrak{n}/\mathfrak{u}^{+}
, the other from viewing it as a subspace of
\mathfrak{n}
.
Equip
\mathfrak{v}
with the corresponding subresonant structure.
The linear projection map
\pi_{\mathfrak{v}}\colon\mathfrak{n}\to\mathfrak{v}
is subresonant, as is the inclusion
\mathfrak{v}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{n}
.

(ii)

The map
ch_{\mathfrak{v}}
is a strictly subresonant polynomial map and has a polynomial inverse, which is also strictly subresonant.

(iii)

Furthermore, given two such
\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}
as above, the composition
\pi_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}}\circ ch_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}}^{-1}\circ ch_{\mathfrak{v}_{2}}
viewed as a map
\mathfrak{v}_{2}\to\mathfrak{v}_{1}
(where
\pi_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}}
is the linear projection in
\mathfrak{n}
to
\mathfrak{v}_{1}
along
\mathfrak{u}^{+}
) is a subresonant map.

Proof.

Observe that the assumption is equivalent to the existence of a direct sum decomposition for each of
\mathfrak{u}^{+},\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{n}
so as to have

\mathfrak{n}_{\lambda_{i}}=\mathfrak{v}_{\lambda_{i}}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{+}_{\lambda_{i}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathfrak{g}_{\leq\lambda_{i}}=\bigoplus_{\mu\leq\lambda_{i}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mu}\quad\forall\mathfrak{g}\in\{\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{u}^{+},\mathfrak{v}\}.

The uniqueness of weight filtration on
\mathfrak{v}
follows, and the corresponding inclusions and projections are subresonant (linear) maps.

Recall next the general Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula

e^{v}e^{u}=e^{u+v+\psi_{1}(u,v)+\psi_{2}(u,v)+\cdots}

where
\psi_{i}(u,v)
involves
i
-fold commutators of
u
and
v
.
The map
ch_{\mathfrak{v}}
is precisely the exponent on the RHS.

To check that
ch_{\mathfrak{v}}
is subresonant, observe that the first term is the identity map so it suffices to check that each subsequent term is a subresonant polynomial map from
\mathfrak{n}
to itself of strictly negative degree (see also
§C.1.6
).
Each of these maps however is a composition of maps of the form
\Lambda^{2}\mathfrak{n}\xrightarrow{[,]}\mathfrak{n}
and
\mathfrak{n}\otimes\mathfrak{n}\to\Lambda^{2}\mathfrak{n}
, which are clearly subresonant (and linear), as well as the polynomial map
\mathfrak{n}\to\mathfrak{n}\otimes\mathfrak{n}
given by
(v,u)\mapsto v\otimes u
for the decomposition
\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{v}\oplus\mathfrak{u}^{+}
.
This is also a subresonant map, again since it is a composition of the subresonant linear map
\mathfrak{n}\to\mathfrak{n}\oplus\mathfrak{n}
given by
\pi_{\mathfrak{v}}\oplus 1-\pi_{\mathfrak{v}}
and the subresonant polynomial map
\mathfrak{n}\oplus\mathfrak{n}\to\mathfrak{n}\otimes\mathfrak{n}
given by multiplying coordinates.

Inverses (when they exist) of subresonant maps are subresonant, and since
ch_{\mathfrak{v}_{i}}
has invertible derivative the last claim also follows.
∎

5.1.4 Corollary

(Subresonant structure on the quotient space)
.

The manifold
C:=N/U^{+}
carries a natural subresonant structure, given by selecting any
\mathfrak{v}\subset\mathfrak{n}
satisfying the conditions of
Lemma 5.1.3
and using
ch_{\mathfrak{v}}
to obtain charts on it.

Furthermore, let
\Aut(N,U^{+})
be the Lie group of automorphisms of
N
that preserve the subgroup
U^{+}
(as a set), and additionally assume that the action of
\Aut(N,U^{+})
preserves the weight filtration on
N
.
Then
\Aut(N,U^{+})
acts by subresonant maps on
C
.

Additionally, there exists a linear representation
LC
of
\Aut(N,U^{+})
and polynomial embedding
C\hookrightarrow LC
such that subresonant automorphisms of
C
are induced by linear maps on
LC
.
Furthermore,
LC
admits a filtration by subrepresentations, such that the associated quotients are tensor powers of the action on
\mathfrak{n}/\mathfrak{u}^{+}
.

Proof.

The compatibility of subresonant charts on
C
is the content of
Lemma 5.1.3
(iii).

The linear action of
\Aut(N,U^{+})
on
\mathfrak{n}
by Lie algebra automorphisms is conjugated by the exponential map to the action at the Lie group level (by the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula).
Furthermore, given one
\mathfrak{v}\subset\mathfrak{n}
satisfying the assumptions of
Lemma 5.1.3
, its image under elements of
\Aut(N,U^{+})
still satisfies the assumptions, since
\Aut(N,U^{+})
is required to preserve the weight filtration of
\mathfrak{n}
.
Following through the identifications and induced maps, it follows that
\Aut(N,U^{+})
acts on
C
by subresonant polynomial maps.

To construct the linearizing representation
LC
, we apply the construction from
§C.1.11
and use
Proposition C.1.15
.
∎

5.1.5.
Construction of the associated bundles

Working on the finite cover
X
, we apply now the above formalism to the setting of unstable manifolds
\mathcal{W}^{u}
