, assuming a compatible family of subgroups
U^{+}(x)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)=:N
as in
Definition 3.1.5
.
Define
\mathcal{C}(x):=\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)/U^{+}(x)
, which is a measurable fiber bundle over a set of full measure.
The fibers are equipped with a subresonant structure, as per
Corollary 5.1.4
.
Because the
g_{t}
-dynamics preserves
U^{+}(x)
as a subgroup of
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
, it also acts by subresonant maps on
\mathcal{C}(x)
.
Let
L\mathcal{C}\to X
be the linearization cocycle provided by
Corollary 5.1.4
, so that we have an equivariant embedding
j\colon\mathcal{C}(x)\hookrightarrow L\mathcal{C}(x)
.

Recall next that we have the “Gauss–Manin” connection
P^{ssr}_{GM}(q,q^{\prime})
between
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
and
\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q^{\prime})
, defined in
§3.1.2
.

Definition 3.1.5
requires that for
x^{\prime}\in U^{+}[x]
and in a set of full measure, we have that
P^{sr}_{GM}(x,x^{\prime})(U^{+}(x))=U^{+}(x^{\prime})
.
Therefore, we have induced identifications

P^{L\mathcal{C}}_{GM}\colon L\mathcal{C}(x)\to L\mathcal{C}(x^{\prime})\quad\text{for }x^{\prime}\in U^{+}[x]\cap X_{0}

where
X_{0}
is a fixed set of full measure.

5.1.6 Proposition
.

The cocycle
g_{t}\colon L\mathcal{C}\to L\mathcal{C}
over
g_{t}\colon X\to X
can be
put in Jordan normal form.

Proof.

By the definition of
X
, the cocycle
g_{t}\colon\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})\to\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})
can be put in to Jordan normal form when lifted to the finite cover
X\to Q
.
Consider the cocycle
E(x):=\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x))/\Lie(U^{+}(x))
, which agrees with the derivative
D_{U^{+}(x)}g_{t}\colon\mathcal{C}(x)\to\mathcal{C}(g_{t}x)
at
U^{+}(x)
.
As a quotient of the cocycle
g_{t}\colon\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})\to\Lie(\mathbf{G}^{ssr})
,
E
can be put in to Jordan normal form when lifted to
X
by
Proposition B.3.4
(i).
The linearization cocycle
g_{t}\colon L\mathcal{C}\to L\mathcal{C}
has a block triangular structure, whose diagonal blocks are subcocycles of tensor powers of
E
, as in
Corollary 5.1.4
.
By
Proposition B.3.4
(iii) and
Proposition B.3.4
(ii),
L\mathcal{C}
can be put in to Jordan normal form.
∎

5.1.7.
The set
\mathcal{C}[x]

Let
\mathcal{C}[x]=\{hU^{+}[x]\;\>:\;\>h\in\mathcal{C}(x)\}
. Then the natural map
\mathcal{C}(x)\to\mathcal{C}[x]
is a bijection. We will view the points of
\mathcal{C}[x]
as
submanifolds inside
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
and speak about Hausdorff distances
(denoted by
\dist_{Hausd}
).

5.1.8.
The tautological subspace

Define
L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}(x)\subset L\mathcal{C}(x)
to be the tautological line
spanned by
j(U^{+}(x))
viewed as a point in
j(\mathcal{C}(x))\subset L\mathcal{C}(x)
.
Quotienting by it has the effect of moving this basepoint to the origin of the vector space on which we have a linear cocycle.

5.1.9.
The cocycle
\mathbf{H}

Define the quotient cocycle:

(5.1.10)

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}:=L\mathcal{C}/L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}

Vectors in
\mathbf{H}
, and some of its subbundles
\mathbf{E}
defined later, will be denoted
{\mathbf{v}}
.
When a distinction is necessary, we will write

\mathbf{j}\colon\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{H},\quad\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})=\bigl(L\circ j(\mathcal{U})\bigr)/L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}

for the natural inclusion.
By
Theorem B.3.5
(v)
, since
L\mathcal{C}
can be put in Jordan normal form on
X
, so can
\mathbf{H}
.

5.1.11.
Measurable connections on
\mathbf{H}

Recall that the finite cover
X
is constructed by bringing
\Lie\mathbf{G}^{ssr}
to Jordan normal form.
The measurable connections
P^{\pm}
lift to
X
and preserve all
g_{t}
-invariant subcocycles of
\Lie\mathbf{G}^{ssr}
on
X
by the construction of lifts of stable and unstable manifolds on
X
and the Ledrappier invariance principle (see
§2.5.5
and
Corollary B.2.7
).
The cocycle
\mathbf{H}
is obtained from elementary linear-algebraic constructions with
\Lie\mathbf{G}^{ssr}
and
\Lie U^{+}
, hence acquires induced measurable connections
P^{\pm}
.

5.1.12.
Metric structures, distances

Using the norm on
\mathbf{H}
provided by
Proposition B.4.1
, together with the embedding
\mathbf{j}\colon\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{H}
, yields a distance function on
\mathcal{C}(x)
denoted
d_{\mathcal{C}}
.

Recall that the metric
d^{u}
is defined
in
§2.5.8
.
Recall that the partition
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
is chosen so that any atom of

\mathfrak{B}_{0}
has diameter at most
1
in the
d^{u}(\cdot,\cdot)
metric (see
Remark A.5.10
).
We make a measurable choice of a distinguished point
x^{\ast}
in each
atom
\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]
.
Let
B^{\ast}[x]
be the ball in the
d^{u}(\cdot,\cdot)
metric of radius
1
centered at
x^{\ast}
. Then, for all
x
,

\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]\subset B^{\ast}[x]\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[x].

Let also
d^{x}_{\mathcal{H},loc}
denote the Hausdorff distance on
\mathcal{C}[x]

when its elements are viewed as subsets of
B^{\ast}[x]
.

(5.1.13)

\displaystyle d^{x}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(c_{1},c_{2}):=\dist_{Hausd}\left(c_{1}\cap B^{\ast}[x],c_{2}\cap B^{\ast}[x]\right).

We also have the reference basepoint
c_{0}(x):=U^{+}[x]\in\mathcal{C}[x]
, which by assumption is
g_{t}
-equivariant.

5.1.14 Proposition

(Relation to Hausdorff distance)
.

There exist
g_{t}
-tempered functions
A_{1}(x),A_{2}(x),A_{3}(x)
with the following properties.

(i)

Let
\mathfrak{v}(x)\subset\mathfrak{g}^{ssr}(x)
be the family of transversals obtained as orthogonal complements to
\mathfrak{u}^{+}(x)
for admissible metrics (see
§B.2.8
).
Then these transversals satisfy the assumptions in
Lemma 5.1.3
.
Endow
\mathcal{C}(x)
with euclidean metrics
d_{ch}
coming from
\mathfrak{v}(x)
via the charts
ch_{\mathfrak{v}(x)}
.
Then we have

\frac{1}{A_{1}(x)}\cdot d_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2})\leq d_{ch}(c_{1},c_{2})\leq A_{1}(x)\cdot d_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2})

for
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathcal{C}(x)
in the ball of radius
1

for
d_{\mathcal{C}}
around the basepoint
c_{0}
.

(ii)

Additionally, for
c_{1},c_{2}
intersecting

B^{\ast}[x]
we have:

\frac{1}{A_{2}(x)}\cdot d_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2})\leq d^{x}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(c_{1},c_{2})\leq A_{2}(x)\cdot d_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2})

where
d^{x}_{\mathcal{H},loc}
is the Hausdorff distance from

Eqn. (5.1.13)
.

(iii)

Also, for
c_{1},c_{2}
as above we have

\frac{1}{A_{3}(x)}d^{x}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(c_{1},c_{2})\leq\min(r(x),\dist_{Hausd}\left(c_{1}\cap B^{u}(x;r(x)),c_{2}\cap B^{u}(x;r(x))\right))\\
\leq A_{3}(x)d^{x}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(c_{1},c_{2})

where
r(x)
is as in
Remark 2.2.8
.

Proof.

The family of transversals
\mathfrak{v}(x)\subset\mathfrak{g}^{ssr}
satisfies the assumptions of
Lemma 5.1.3
by the definitions of admissible metrics, since the Lyapunov subspaces will yield the required direct sum decompositions of
\mathfrak{g}^{ssr}
.

The derivative of the chart map
ch_{\mathfrak{v}}
at the origin is the identification between
\mathfrak{v}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{ssr}/\mathfrak{u}^{+}
and is in fact an isometry for admissible metrics on both sides.
Furthermore the (injective) chart maps
ch_{\mathfrak{v}}\colon\mathfrak{v}\to\mathcal{C}(x)\subset L\mathcal{C}(x)
are given by polynomial expressions.
We can identify all the objects in question with a model situation, independently of
x
, and compare metrics at the cost of introducing multiplicative factors that vary in a tempered way along the
g_{t}
-orbit of
x
.
The first claim then follows.

For part (ii) we continue to work in a fixed model situation (with fixed metric, groups
\mathbf{G}^{ssr},U^{+}
, etc.) independent of
x
.
Let
c_{0}\in\mathcal{C}
be the basepoint corresponding to
U^{+}
viewed as an element of
\mathcal{C}
.
For the rest of the proof, we will write
U^{+}[x]\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
with the understanding that the argument applies to
a full measure set of
x
, up to tempered multiplicative
factors.

Now for any
X\in\mathfrak{v}
with
\left\|X\right\|\leq 1
, the claimed upper bound for
d_{\mathcal{H}}(c_{0},\exp(X)c_{0})
is clear.
By applying further transformations of the form
\exp(Y)
with
Y\in\mathfrak{v},\left\|Y\right\|\leq 1
the upper bound on
d_{\mathcal{H}}(c_{1},c_{2})
for arbitrary
c_{1},c_{2}
in the unit ball of
\mathcal{C}
follows.
Both claims follow since
\exp(Y)
has bounded derivatives on the ball of radius
1
when
\left\|Y\right\|\leq 1
.

For the lower bound, using an
\exp(Y)
as before, it suffices to show it for
d_{\mathcal{H}}(c_{0},\exp(X)c_{0})
.
Now, restrict first to
X
in the unit sphere in
\mathfrak{v}
, it yields a compact (and finite-dimensional) family of vector fields on
\mathcal{W}^{u}
.
Each vector field is not identically zero on
U^{+}[x]\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
(otherwise it would give a trivial transformation of
U^{+}[x]
).
Since the vector fields are also real-analytic, there exist constants
\varepsilon,c>0
such that for any
X\in\mathfrak{v}
with
\left\|X\right\|=1
, there exists
p\in U^{+}[x]\cap\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[x]
such that
\left\|X(p)\right\|\geq c
and the angle between
X(p)
and
T_{p}U^{+}[x]
is at least
\varepsilon
, where
X(p)
denotes the vector field induced by
X
at
p
.

It follows that for any
X\in\mathfrak{v}\setminus 0
with
\left\|X\right\|_{\mathfrak{v}}\leq 1
there exists
p\in U^{+}[x]\cap\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[x]
such that the angle between
X(p)
and
T_{p}U^{+}[x]
is at least
\varepsilon
, and
\left\|X(p)\right\|\geq c\left\|X\right\|_{\mathfrak{v}}
.
Furthermore there exists a constant
A>0
such that
X
has
C^{1}
-norm bounded by
A^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[x]
.
The desired lower bound now follows for
\left\|X\right\|\leq\frac{1}{A^{\prime\prime}}\leq 1
for some
A^{\prime\prime}=A^{\prime\prime}(A,c,\varepsilon)
, by exponentiating the vector field
X
and following along
p
.
Note finally that scalings are among the subresonant maps of

\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
, so by rescaling (or equivalently redefining the
metric) we can assume that the result holds for
\left\|X\right\|\leq 1
and modified constants.

Part (iii) follows from Part (ii) and a compactness argument.
∎

5.1.15.
Local Hausdorff distance on unstable

5.1.16 Proposition

(Hausdorff distance and norm of vector)
.

Consider
g\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])
and
\mathcal{U}=gU^{+}[x]\in\mathcal{C}[x]
. Let

\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{j}(gU^{+}(x))\in\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{C}(x))\subset\mathbf{H}(x).

Then there exists a measurable function
c_{1}(x)>0
with the
following property: Suppose
\|{\mathbf{v}}\|\leq 1
.
Then we have

\frac{1}{c_{1}(x)}\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|\leq d^{x}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[x],\mathcal{U})\leq c_{1}(x)\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|.

(We use the convention that
d^{x}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[x],\mathcal{U})=1
if
\mathcal{U}

does not intersect
B^{\ast}[x]
). Also, if
r(x)
is as in

Remark 2.2.8
,

\frac{1}{c_{1}(x)}\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|\leq\dist_{Hausd}\left(U^{+}[x]\cap B^{u}[x,r(x)],\mathcal{U}\cap B^{u}[x,r(x)]\right)\leq c_{1}(x)\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|.

(In the above equation we use the convention that
\dist_{Hausd}(A,B)=r(x)
if
A
or
B
is empty).

Proof.

This follows from
Proposition 5.1.14
(ii)
and (iii), and the fact that for a.e.
x\in X
, the image of the
linearization map
j:\mathcal{C}(x)\to L\mathcal{C}(x)
is transverse to the
tautological subspace
\mathcal{C}^{\tau}(x)
.
∎

Recall that
L_{y}:\mathcal{W}^{u}[y]\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y)
denotes the linearization map.
Given
h\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[y])
, we denote by
(L_{y}h)
the linear endomorphism of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y)
such that
(L_{y}h)\circ L_{y}=L_{y}\circ h
.

5.1.17 Lemma
.

There exist measurable functions
C_{1}:X\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
c_{2}:X\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that the following holds:

(a)

Suppose
h\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[y])
is such that

\|L_{y}h-I\|\leq c_{2}(y)
. Then

d_{\mathcal{H},loc}^{y}(hU^{+}[y],U^{+}[y])\leq C_{1}(y)\|L_{y}h-I\|.

(b)

For any
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}[y]
with
d_{\mathcal{H},loc}^{y}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},U^{+}[y])<c_{2}(y)
there exists
h\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[y])
such that

\|L_{y}h-I\|\leq C(y)d_{\mathcal{H},loc}^{y}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},U^{+}[y]).

Proof.

We begin the proof of (a).
Let
\tilde{h}=L_{y}h
. Then,
L_{y}\circ h=\tilde{h}\circ L_{y}
.
Note that by
Lemma E.5.2
,

L_{y}
is bilipshitz with constant

\kappa(y)>1
.
Then, for any
z\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[y]
with
d^{u}(y,z)\leq 1
, we have

\|L_{y}z\|\leq\|L_{y}y\|+\kappa(y)
. Then,

d^{u}(hz,z)\leq\kappa(y)\|L_{y}(hz)-L_{y}(z)\|=\|(\tilde{h}-I)L_{y}z\|\leq\\
\leq\|\tilde{h}-I\|\|L_{y}z\|\leq\kappa(y)(\|L_{y}y\|+\kappa(y))\|\tilde{h}-I\|.

This implies (a).

We now begin the proof of (b). We can choose
h
so that
h=\exp(H)

where
H\in\mathfrak{v}(y)
where
\mathfrak{v}(y)
is as in

Proposition 5.1.14
.
By
Proposition 5.1.14
, there exists

C(y)
such that

C(y)^{-1}\|H\|_{y}\leq d^{y}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[y],hU^{+}[y])\leq C(y)\|H\|_{y},

where
\|\cdot\|_{y}
is the norm on
\mathfrak{v}(y)
considered in

Proposition 5.1.14
.

Let
\rho_{y}
denote the homomorphism from
\mathbf{G}_{ssr}(W^{u}[y])
to

GL(LW^{u}[y])
, and let
\phi_{y}
denote the corresponding map of
the Lie algebras. Then, there exists
C_{2}(y)>1
such that

C_{2}(y)^{-1}\|H\|_{y}\leq\|\phi_{y}(H)\|_{y}\leq C_{2}(y)\|H\|_{y},

where in the middle term
\|\cdot\|_{y}
is a norm on the Lie algebra
of
GL(LW^{u}[y])
.

Note that given
C_{3}(y)
there exists
C_{4}(y)
such that for any
M

in the Lie algebra of

\GL(LW^{u}[y])
with
\|M\|_{y}\leq C_{3}(y)
, we have

\|\exp(M)-I\|_{y}\leq C_{4}(y)\|M\|_{y}

Then,

\|L_{y}h-I\|_{y}=\|\rho_{y}(\exp H)-I\|_{y}=\|\exp(\phi_{y}(H))-I\|_{y}\leq C\|H\|_{y}.

∎

5.2.
The change of basepoint map

5.2.1.
Setup

Suppose
U^{+}(x)
is a compatible family of subgroups in the sense of

Definition 3.1.5
, with associated full
measure set
X_{0}
as in
§5.1.5
.
Let
\mathcal{C}\to X_{0}
and
L\mathcal{C}\to X_{0}
be the associated fiber bundle and linearization bundle from
§5.1.5
.
Recall that the constructions in
§5.1.5
yield
linear maps

P^{L\mathcal{C}}_{GM}(x,ux)\colon L\mathcal{C}(x)\to L\mathcal{C}(ux)

assuming
x,ux\in X_{0}
and
u\in U^{+}(x)
.

The properties of the next construction are the subject of Lemma 6.5 of
[
EM18
]
.

5.2.2 Definition

(u-star)
.

With notation as above, for
x,ux\in X_{0}
and
u\in U^{+}(x)
, define:

u_{*}(x,ux)\colon L\mathcal{C}(x)\to L\mathcal{C}(ux)

as
P^{L\mathcal{C}}_{GM}(x,ux)
.

5.2.3 Remark

(Equivariance and endpoint dependence of
u_{*}
)
.

From the definition of
u_{*}
via the Gauss–Manin connection, it follows that whenever
x_{2}=ux_{1}
the map
u_{*}(x_{1},x_{2})
depends only on
x_{1},x_{2}
and not the particular choice of
u\in U^{+}(x_{1})
such that
x_{1}=ux_{2}
.

Furthermore, it is also immediate that
u_{*}(x_{1},x_{2})
intertwines the action of
g_{t}
, namely
u_{*}(g_{t}x_{1},g_{t}x_{2})\circ g_{t}=g_{t}\circ u_{*}(x_{1},x_{2})
.

Note that for measure-generic points
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
, we only have a map
P^{+}(x,x^{\prime})\colon L\mathcal{C}(x)\to L\mathcal{C}(x^{\prime})
, but in general no “Gauss–Manin” connection such as the one on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
.
Nonetheless, on cocycles and points for which
P^{+}
and
u_{*}
are both defined, they agree by
Proposition 5.2.4
on
inert subspaces
, which are defined in
§10.1
.

5.2.4 Proposition

(
u_{*}
preserves flags)
.

Let
L\mathcal{C}(x)^{\geq\bullet}
be the backwards Lyapunov flag of the cocycle
L\mathcal{C}
at
x
in the set of full measure
X_{0}
.

Then, for
u\in U^{+}(x)
with
ux\in X_{0}
, we have that
u_{*}(x,ux)
takes the backwards Lyapunov flag at
x
to that at
ux
, and its action on the associated graded agrees with the holonomy map from
§B.2.6
.

Proof.

The collection of maps
P^{L\mathcal{C}}_{GM}(x,ux)
give holonomies in the sense of
§B.2.2
.
They are also smooth when
u
varies in
U^{+}(x)
.

The statement of the proposition is then a consequence of standard properties of holonomies, see
§B.2.9
.
∎

5.2.5.
On
\mathbf{H}

Recall that in
Eqn. (5.1.10)
we are considering a quotient of
L\mathcal{C}
by the one-dimensional tautological subbundle corresponding to the choice of
U^{+}(x)
, viewed as the identity coset in
\mathcal{C}(x)=\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)/U^{+}(x)
.
The statement of
Proposition 5.2.4
holds in this
case as well, because both the measurable connection and

P^{L\mathcal{C}}_{GM}
preserve this tautological section.

5.2.6 Lemma
.

For every
\delta>0
there is a compact set
K\subset X
of measure
1-\delta
, and
C(\delta)<+\infty
such that given
x\in K
and
y\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]\cap K
, we have
\|u_{*}(x,y)\|\leq C(\delta)
.

Remember
u_{*}(x,y)
is defined in
Definition 5.2.2
and the norm is measured with respect to the good norms given by
B.4.1
. Remember also that
u_{*}(y,x)=(u_{*}(x,y))^{-1}
.

Proof.

Let
r(x,y)=\max\{\|u_{*}(x,y)\|,\|u_{*}(y,x)\|\}
.
Let us define on
X\times X
the measure
\hat{\nu}
by
\int\phi(x,z)d\hat{\nu}=\int\int\phi(x,z)d\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{x}(z)d\nu(x)
, where
\nu_{x}^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}
are the conditional measures from
§10.3.10
.
Notice that
\hat{\nu}
is supported on the set of points
(x,z)
s.t.
z\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]
.
Since
r(x,z)<+\infty
for
\hat{\nu}
-a.e.
(x,z)
and
u_{*}
depends measurably on
(x,z)
, we have as
C\to+\infty
that
\hat{\nu}\left(\{(x,z):r(x,z)>C\}\right)\to 0
.

Let
C>0
be such that

\hat{\nu}(\{(x,z):r(x,z)>C\})<\frac{1}{4}\delta

and let
C(\delta)=C^{2}
. We get by Markov’s inequality and since

\hat{\nu}\left(\{(x,z):r(x,z)>C\}\right)=\int\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{x}(\{z:r(x,z)>C\}))d\nu(x)<\frac{1}{4}\delta.

that

\displaystyle\nu\left(\{x\in X:\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{x}\left(\{z:r(x,z)>C\}\right)\geq 1/4\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\hat{\nu}\left(\{(x,z):r(x,z)>C\}\right)<\delta

and hence

\nu\left(\{x\in X:\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{x}\left(\{z:r(x,z)\leq C\}\right)\geq 3/4\}\right)>1-\delta.

Let
K\subset\{x\in X:\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{x}\left(\{z:r(x,z)\leq C\}\right)\geq 3/4\}
be compact with
\nu(K)\geq 1-\delta
and remember that
C(\delta)=C^{2}
and that
u_{*}(y,x)=(u_{*}(x,y))^{-1}
and
u_{*}(x,z)u_{*}(z,y)=u_{*}(x,y)
.

Finally, if
x,y\in K
and
y\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]
, then

\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{x}\left(\{z:r(x,z)\leq C\}\right)\geq 3/4
and
\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{y}\left(\{z:r(y,z)\leq C\}\right)\geq 3/4
. Since
y\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]
,

\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{x}=\nu^{\mathcal{B}_{0}}_{y}
and so there is
z\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]
such that simultaneously
r(x,z)\leq C
and
r(y,z)\leq C
which gives

\|u_{*}(x,y)\|\leq\|u_{*}(x,z)\|\|u_{*}(z,y)\|\leq r(x,z)r(y,z)\leq C^{2}=C(\delta).

∎

6.
Y
-configurations

6.1.
Definitions

6.1.1.
Setup

For the rest of this subsection, all the points considered are on the finite cover
X\to Q
.

6.1.2 Definition

(
Y
-configuration)
.

Suppose
\alpha_{3}>1
.
(The constant
\alpha_{3}
will be fixed in
§8.1.22
).
Fix
q_{1}
,
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
.
Also fix parameters
\ell,\tau,t_{0}>0
and
0<\tau\leq\alpha_{3}\ell
.
A
Y
-configuration

Y(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau,t_{0})

is the collection of points

•

q_{1}

•

q:=g_{-\ell}q_{1}

•

q_{2}:=g_{\tau}uq_{1}

•

q_{3}:=g_{t_{0}}q_{1}
.

6.1.3 Definition

(pre-
Y
-configuration)
.

A
pre-
Y
-configuration
Y_{pre}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)
is the
collection of points

•

q_{1}

•

q:=g_{-\ell}q_{1}

•

q_{2}:=g_{\tau}uq_{1}
.

6.1.4.
Notation.

If
Y
is a
Y
-configuration or a pre-
Y
-configuration, we denote
its points by
q(Y)
,
q_{1}(Y)
,
q_{2}(Y)
and if applicable

q_{3}(Y)
. Similarly, we use the notation
\ell(Y)
,
u(Y)
,
\tau(Y)
,
t_{0}(Y)

to retrieve the parameters defining
Y
. We also use the notations

q_{1/2}(Y)
to denote
g_{-\ell(Y)/2}q_{1}(Y)=g_{\ell(Y)/2}q(Y)
,
and
y_{1/2}(Y)
to denote
g_{-\ell(Y)/2}u(Y)q_{1}(Y)
.

6.1.5.
Notation.

If
Y
is a
Y
-configuration, and there is no potential for
confusion, we will occasionally write
q,q_{1/2},y_{1/2},q_{1},q_{2},q_{3},\ell,u,\tau,t_{0}
for

q(Y),q_{1/2}(Y),y_{1/2}(Y),q_{1}(Y),q_{2}(Y),q_{3}(Y),\ell(Y),u(Y),\tau(Y),t_{0}(Y)

respectively. Also if
Y^{\prime}
is a
Y
-configuration we will write

q^{\prime},q^{\prime}_{1/2},y^{\prime}_{1/2}
,
q_{1}^{\prime},q_{2}^{\prime},q_{3}^{\prime},\ell^{\prime},u^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},t_{0}^{\prime}
for

q(Y^{\prime}),q_{1/2}(Y^{\prime}),y_{1/2}(Y^{\prime})
,
q_{1}(Y^{\prime})
,
q_{2}(Y^{\prime})
,
q_{3}(Y^{\prime})
,

\ell(Y^{\prime})
,
u(Y^{\prime})
,
\tau(Y^{\prime})
,
t_{0}(Y^{\prime})
respectively.

We use the same notational convention for pre-
Y
-configurations.

6.1.6.
Warning

We emphasize that in order for a configuration of points to be called
a
Y
or pre-
Y
configuration, the condition
\tau<\alpha_{3}\ell

must be satisfied.

6.1.7 Definition
.

(
Y\in K
)

Let
K\subset X
be a subset, and let
Y
be a
Y
-configuration or a
pre-
Y
-configuration. Using the notation
§
6.1.5
,
we say that
Y\in K
if
q\in K
,
q_{1}\in K
,
q_{1/2}\in K
,
uq_{1}\in K
,
g_{-\ell/2}uq_{1}\in K
,
q_{2}\in K
, and (provided
Y
is a

Y
-configuration),
q_{3}\in K
.

6.1.8 Definition

(bottom-linked
Y
-configurations)
.

If
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are two
Y
-configurations or pre-
Y
configurations,
we say (using the notation §
6.1.5
) that
Y

and
Y^{\prime}
are
bottom-linked
if
\ell=\ell^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]\subset\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
.

Note that
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
is a subset of the finite cover
X
, so not only are the projections of
q,q^{\prime}
to
Q
in the same stable manifold, but their
U^{+}
-groups are identified by
P^{-}(q,q^{\prime})
.

6.1.9 Definition

(right-linked
Y
-configurations)
.

If
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are two bottom-linked
Y
-configurations, we say that
Y
and
Y^{\prime}

are
right-linked
if
t_{0}=t_{0}^{\prime}
. In that case
q_{3}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}[q_{3}]
.

6.1.10 Definition

(
z
-points from
Y
-configurations)
.

Suppose
Y,Y^{\prime}
are two bottom-linked
Y
-configurations.
Define the associated
z
-points:

z_{1/2}:=\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y_{1/2}]\cap\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]

Note that the intersection of the two manifolds gives a point on
Q
, and it is lifted to the finite cover using the flag
\mathfrak{F}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
(see
§2.5.4
).

In
§6.2.3
we will define further points
z^{\prime\prime}
.

6.1.11 Definition

(left-shadowing
Y
-configurations up to time
t
)
.

Suppose
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are two bottom linked
Y
or pre-
Y

configurations, and suppose
0\leq t\leq\tau(Y)
.
Using the notation

§6.1.5
,
we say that
Y^{\prime}
is
(k,\varepsilon)
-left-shadows
Y
up to time
t
if for all

0\leq s\leq t
, we have that:

g_{\ell/2+s}z_{1/2}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{s}u^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}]

where
\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}
denotes the local unstable (see
§A.2.10
) lifted to
X
(see
§2.5.4
).

In
§6.3.9
, we will choose once and for all values of
k,\varepsilon
and only refer to
left-shadowing
without reference to
k,\varepsilon
.

Note that, in view of
§6.1.6
, assuming that for
\delta>0
and an appropriate compact
K
of measure at least
1-\delta
, if
y_{1/2}\in K
then:

(6.1.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{1/2}}\left(g_{\ell/2+s}\left(\sigma(z_{1/2})\right),\sigma(g_{s}uq_{1})\right)\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha^{\prime}\ell}\\
d^{Q}\left(g_{\ell/2+s}\left(\sigma(z_{1/2})\right),\sigma(g_{s}uq_{1})\right)\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha^{\prime}\ell}\\
\text{ and therefore }g_{\ell/2+s}\left(\sigma(z_{1/2})\right)\in\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[\sigma(g_{s}uq_{1})].\end{split}

for some
\alpha^{\prime}>0
(independent of
\delta
), where
\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}
denotes the Lyapunov charts (see
§2.2.7
).

6.1.13 Definition

(left-shadowing
Y
-configurations)
.

Suppose
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are two bottom linked
Y
or pre-
Y

configurations, and suppose
0\leq t\leq\tau(Y)
.
Using the notation
§
6.1.5
, we say that
Y^{\prime}

left-shadows
Y
if
Y^{\prime}
left-shadows
Y
up to
\tau(Y)

and

\tau(Y^{\prime})=\tau(Y)
.

6.2.
Interpolation map

In this section, most constructions occur on
Q
, except for
Proposition 6.2.13
which involves the finite cover
X
.

6.2.1.
Setup

Let
E
be a smooth and natural cocycle (see
Definition E.1.2
).
Let
\mathcal{F}^{s}E
denote the bundle of flags in
E
, with the same dimensions as the (forward, aka stable) Lyapunov flags.
At forward-regular points, we have a measurable equivariant choice of such flags
E^{\leq\bullet}
.

We will free use notation and terminology on center-stable manifolds from
§A.4
.
In particular, recall that we fixed once and for all a
C^{\infty}
realization of the center-stable manifolds.

6.2.2 Lemma

(Subexponential separation)
.

For any
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
of measure at least
1-\delta
such that for every
y\in K
the center-stable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y]
is defined, and furthermore there exists a decreasing function
r(N^{\prime})>0
with
r(N^{\prime})\leq e^{-N^{\prime}/2}
, defined for
N^{\prime}\geq 1
with the following properties.

For a constant
\Lambda>2
that only depends on the Lyapunov spectrum, and
C(N^{\prime})>0
a constant, and for all
s,t\in\left[0,\frac{N^{\prime}}{2\Lambda}\log\frac{1}{d^{Q}(y,z)}\right]
, we have the following.
Suppose that
y\in K
and
z\in\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y]
with
d^{Q}(y,z)\leq r(N^{\prime})
, then for any
\overline{z}
such that
d^{Q}(z,\overline{z})\leq d(y,z)^{N^{\prime}}
and furthermore
\overline{z}\in g_{-s}\mathcal{W}^{cs}[g_{s}y]

we have that, setting
\overline{z}_{t}:=g_{t}\overline{z}
:

\begin{split}d^{Q}(\overline{z}_{t},g_{t}z)&\leq C(N^{\prime})e^{\Lambda t}d^{Q}(y,z)^{N^{\prime}}\\
d^{Q}(g_{t}z,g_{t}y)&\leq C(N^{\prime})e^{\tfrac{1}{N^{\prime}}t}d^{Q}(y,z)\end{split}

Furthermore, let
\chi_{E,i}
be the chart
for
g_{t}y
(see
Eqn. (E.1.4)
and the discussion following it).
Then we have analogous estimates for flags:

\begin{split}\dist\bigl(\chi_{E,i}(E^{\leq\bullet}_{g_{t}y}(\overline{z}_{t})),\chi_{E,i}(g_{t}E^{\leq\bullet}_{y}(z)\bigr)&\leq C(N^{\prime})e^{\Lambda t}d^{Q}(y,z)^{N^{\prime}}\\
\dist\bigl(\chi_{E,i}(g_{t}E^{\leq\bullet}_{y}(z)),\chi_{E,i}\left(E^{\leq\bullet}(g_{t}y)\right)\bigr)&\leq C(N^{\prime})e^{\tfrac{1}{N^{\prime}}t}d^{Q}(y,z)\end{split}

where
\dist
is defined in
§A.0.1
.

Note that at the maximal allowed value of
t
, the right-hand side of the all the above inequalities is bounded by
C(N^{\prime})d^{Q}(y,z)^{\tfrac{1}{2}}
.

Proof.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large; as
k\to+\infty
we will achieve arbitrarily large
N^{\prime}
in the statement.
Let
\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{fake,k}[y]
and
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{fake,k}[y]\mapsto E^{\leq\bullet}_{fake,k,y}(z^{\prime})
be the fake objects constructed in
§A.4.5
.
Recall also that we fixed a
C^{\infty}
realization of the center-stable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y]
and the forwards flag of
z\in\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y]\mapsto E^{\leq\bullet}_{y}(z)
along
\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y]
.
There is a measurable function
y\mapsto R_{r}(y)
such that if
y\in V_{i}
(the
i
th trivialization chart for
E
) then for any
z\in\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y]
there is
\bar{z}^{k}\in\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{fake,k}[y]
with the following properties:

(i)

We have

d^{Q}(z,\bar{z}^{k})\leq R_{r}(y)d^{Q}(y,z)^{k}

and analogously for the flags:

\dist\bigl(\chi_{E,i}(E^{\leq\bullet}_{fake,k,y}(\bar{z}^{k})),\chi_{E,i}(E^{\leq\bullet}_{y}(z))\bigr)\leq R_{r}(y)d^{Q}(y,z)^{k}

(ii)

For constants
\varepsilon(k)\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
we have:

d^{Q}(g_{t}y,g_{t}\bar{z}^{k})\leq C_{1}(y)e^{\varepsilon(k)t}d^{Q}(y,\bar{z}^{k})

and analogously for the flags:

\dist\bigl(\chi_{E,i}(E^{\leq\bullet}_{fake,k,y}(g_{t}\bar{z}^{k})),\chi_{E,i}(E^{\leq\bullet}_{y}(g_{t}z))\bigr)\leq C_{1}(y)e^{\varepsilon(k)t}d^{Q}(y,\bar{z}^{k})

We may assume
K
was chosen so that
R_{r}(y),C_{1}(y)
above are uniformly bounded from above by some
C_{2}(k)
and the distance
d^{Q}
is uniformly comparable with the Lyapunov distance at
y
.

Now we estimate:

\displaystyle d^{Q}(g_{t}y,g_{t}z)

\displaystyle\leq d^{Q}(g_{t}y,g_{t}\bar{z}^{k})+d^{Q}(g_{t}\bar{z}^{k},g_{t}z)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(k)\left[e^{\varepsilon(k)t}d^{Q}(y,\bar{z}^{k})+e^{\Lambda t}d^{Q}(\bar{z}^{k},z)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(k)\left[e^{\varepsilon(k)t}d^{Q}(y,\bar{z}^{k})+e^{\Lambda t}d^{Q}(y,z)^{k}\right]

and this is the first inequality that we claimed.
Note that the exponent
\Lambda
can be taken to be the maximal Lyapunov exponent of the flow (or the fixed scalar
2
, whichever is larger).

The estimate for flags is analogous.
∎

6.2.3.
Double-prime points

Recall that there exist
C_{1},\alpha_{1}>0
such that
d^{Q}(y_{1/2},z_{1/2})\leq C_{1}e^{-\alpha_{1}\ell}
.
Choose
N^{\prime}
in
Lemma 6.2.2
such that
r(N^{\prime})=C_{1}e^{-\alpha_{1}\ell}
(note that
r(N^{\prime})\to 0
and
N^{\prime}\to+\infty
as
\ell\to+\infty
).
Let
K_{0}
be a compact set of measure at least
1-\tfrac{\delta}{10}
on which the Oseledets theorem on
E
, its flags, etc. are uniform and transverse with constant
C_{\delta}
.
Using the Birkhoff ergodic theorem, let
K\subset K_{0}
be a compact set of measure at least
1-\delta
such that for any
y\in K
, whenever
\ell>\ell(\delta)
, we have that for some
t^{\prime\prime}\in\left[\tfrac{N^{\prime}}{2}\ell,N^{\prime}\ell\right]
that
g_{t^{\prime\prime}}y\in K_{0}
.
Fix such a measurable choice of
t^{\prime\prime}(y,\ell)
and set:

y^{\prime\prime}:=g_{t^{\prime\prime}}y_{1/2}\text{ and }z^{\prime\prime}:=g_{t^{\prime\prime}}z_{1/2}.

With this notation, we have:

6.2.4 Lemma

(
Y
-configurations in uniform Pesin sets)
.

Let
E\to Q
be a smooth and natural cocycle. Fix

\varepsilon>0
and
N
. Let

\Lambda_{E}

denote the Lyapunov spectrum of
E
, and let

Q^{\pm,\Lambda_{E}}_{N,\varepsilon}
denote the uniform Pesin sets
defined in

§E.1.5
.

If
y_{1/2}\in Q^{\pm,\Lambda_{E}}_{N,\varepsilon}
,
q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in Q^{\pm,\Lambda_{E}}_{N,\varepsilon}
, and
y^{\prime\prime}\in Q^{-,\Lambda_{E}}_{N,\varepsilon}
, and if
\ell>0
is sufficiently large, then
z_{1/2}
and
z^{\prime\prime}
are in the uniform backwards Pesin set
Q^{-,\Lambda_{E}}_{3N,3\varepsilon}
.

Proof.

The backwards regularity of
z_{1/2}
follows from that of
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
, which is assumed, combined with the fact that
z_{1/2}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[q_{1/2}^{\prime}]
and furthermore
d^{Q}(z_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})\leq C(\delta)e^{-\sigma\ell}
where
\sigma>0
depends only on the Lyapunov spectrum while
C(\delta)
is a constant that only depends on the choice of Lusin set
K
of measure at least
1-\delta
.

To establish the claim for
z^{\prime\prime}
, it suffices to do so assuming that
M=\ell/2+t^{\prime\prime}
is integral.
We consider the sequence of transformations defined by

A_{n}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}g_{-1}|_{E(g_{-n}y^{\prime\prime})}&0\leq n\leq M\\
P^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\circ P^{+}(y_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\circ g_{-1}|_{E(g_{-M}y^{\prime\prime})}&n=M\\
g_{-1}|_{E(g_{-n+M}q^{\prime}_{1/2})}&M+1\leq n\end{cases}

Note that
g_{-M}y^{\prime\prime}=y_{1/2}
.
Define also

B_{n}^{\prime}=g_{-1}|_{E(g_{-n}z^{\prime\prime})}\quad\text{for }0\leq n

Note that
A_{n}^{\prime},B_{n}^{\prime}
are linear transformations between fibers of cocycles at different points.
Let now
A_{n},B_{n}
be the sequence of matrices obtained from
A_{n}^{\prime},B_{n}^{\prime}
by applying the local trivializations
\chi_{E,i}
associated to the smooth and natural cocycle
E
, see
§E.1.3
.
Concretely

A_{n}=\chi_{E,i_{n+1}}(x_{n+1})\circ A_{n}^{\prime}\circ\chi_{E,i_{n}}^{-1}(x_{n})

where
x_{n}
is the sequence of points determined from the definition of
A_{n}^{\prime}
, and analogously
B_{n}
is defined using a sequence
y_{n}:=g_{-n}z^{\prime\prime}
.
We will show that the sequence of transformations
A_{n}
is
(\epsilon,N)
-forwards regular, and furthermore that
\left\|A_{n}-B_{n}\right\|
satisfies the assumptions of
Theorem A.1.9
, which then gives the desired claim.

The
(3\varepsilon,3N)
-regularity of
A_{n}
follows from the corresponding statement for
A_{n}^{\prime}
, defining the backwards flag for
A_{n}^{\prime}
by pushing the backwards flags of
y^{\prime\prime}
along
g_{-t}
and
P^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\circ P^{+}(y_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})
.
Now use that the local trivializations satisfy tempered bounds, see
§E.1.3
, and note that
P^{+}(y_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})
and
P^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})
preserve the Oseledets decompositions, and are furthermore bounded (in fact exponentially close to the identity if using the local trivializations and the fact that
q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2},y_{1/2}
belong to the Lusin set
K
).
∎

Consider now two bottom-linked
Y
-configurations.
We will omit the dependence of the points on the
Y
-configurations and follow the notation of
§6.1.5
and simply write
q_{\bullet},q^{\prime}_{\bullet}
etc. for their points and also
z_{1/2},y_{1/2}
, etc. as in
Definition 6.1.10
.

For the next statement, recall from
Lemma 6.2.4
that
z_{1/2}
is backwards-regular so it has a backwards flag
E^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
, and that
z_{1/2}
and
y_{1/2}
are center-stably related.

We also set
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{{\ell,C_{0}}}\subset\mathcal{F}^{s}E(z^{\prime\prime})
to consist of flags that are
C_{0}
-transverse to the unstable flags at
z^{\prime\prime}
.

6.2.5 Proposition

(Fake forward flags on fake center-stables)
.

Let
K,K_{0}
be the compact sets as in
§6.2.3
.
Then

(i)

for any two flags
F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell,C_{0}}^{s}E(z_{1/2})
, we
have

\dist(g_{t}F_{1},g_{t}F_{2})\leq C_{0}e^{-{N^{\prime}}\alpha_{E}\ell}\quad\forall t\in[0,\alpha_{3}\ell].

where
\alpha_{E}>0
and
\ell
is larger than some
\ell(E)>0
depending on the Lyapunov spectrum of
E
.

(ii)

The choice of flag of
E^{\leq\bullet}_{y_{1/2}}(z_{1/2})
as defined in
§A.4.6
is contained in
\mathcal{F}_{\ell,C_{0}}^{s}E(z_{1/2})
.

(iii)

For
F\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell,C_{0}}^{s}E(z_{1/2})
denote by
E^{\lambda_{i}}_{F}(z_{1/2})
the components of the splitting of
E(z_{1/2})
induced by the Oseledets filtration
E^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
and the flag
E_{y_{1/2}}^{\leq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
.
Then for any
t\in[0,\alpha_{3}\ell]
and any
i
:

\dist\left(\chi_{E,j}\left(E^{\lambda_{i}}(g_{t}y_{1/2})\right),g_{t}E^{\lambda_{i}}_{F}(z_{1/2})\right)\leq e^{-\alpha_{E}\ell},

where
\chi_{E,j}
is an ambient chart that contains
g_{t}y_{1/2}
, and
\alpha_{E}>0
depends only on
E
and the Lyapunov spectrum.

(iv)

Suppose that for
\hat{y}_{1/2}:=g_{s/2}y_{1/2}
with
s\in[0,\ell]
we have
\hat{y}_{1/2}\in K
.
Set
\hat{z}_{1/2,s}:=\mathcal{W}^{cs}[\hat{y}_{1/2}]\cap\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{s/2}q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
and
\hat{z}_{1/2}:=g_{-s}\hat{z}_{1/2,s}
.
Let also
\mathcal{F}_{\ell-s,C_{0}}^{s}E(\hat{z}_{1/2,s})
be the set set of flags at
\hat{z}_{1/2,s}
obtained by the construction above, and set

\mathcal{F}_{\ell-s,C_{0}}^{s}E(\hat{z}_{1/2}):=g_{-s}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\ell-s,C_{0}}^{s}E(\hat{z}_{1/2,s})\right).

Then we have the estimate

\dist\Big(\chi_{E,j}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\ell,C_{0}}^{s}E({z}_{1/2})\right),\chi_{E,j}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\ell-s,C_{0}}^{s}E(\hat{z}_{1/2})\right)\Big)\leq e^{-N_{2}^{\prime}\ell},

where
N_{2}^{\prime}\to+\infty
as
N_{1}^{\prime}\to+\infty
.

Proof.

To establish (i), we apply

Lemma 6.2.4
to
conclude that
z^{\prime\prime}\in Q^{-,\Lambda_{E}}_{3N,3\varepsilon}
(i.e. it is
backwards regular).
Therefore,
g_{-t}
is exponentially contracting on the space of flags so applying
g_{-t^{\prime\prime}}
we find that for any two
F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell,C_{0}}^{s}E(z_{1/2})
we have

\dist(F_{1},F_{2})\leq C_{0}e^{-{N^{\prime}}\alpha_{E}^{\prime}\ell}

for some
\alpha_{E}^{\prime}>0
that only depends on the Lyapunov spectrum of
E
.
Now we can apply
g_{t}
for
t\in[0,\alpha_{3}\ell]
to this estimate and conclude part (i) for an appropriate choice of
\alpha_{E}
and
\ell>\ell(E)
to guarantee that
N^{\prime}
is sufficiently large.

For conclusion (ii), since
y^{\prime\prime}\in K_{0}
and
z^{\prime\prime}
is exponentially close to
y^{\prime\prime}
, by
Lemma 6.2.2
applied to
y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}
we have that

\chi_{E,j}\left(g_{\ell/2+t^{\prime\prime}}E^{\leq\bullet}_{y_{1/2}}(z_{1/2})\right)

is exponentially close to the flag
\chi_{E,j}\left(E^{\leq\bullet}_{y^{\prime\prime}}[z^{\prime\prime}]\right)
, which is itself exponentially close to
\chi_{E,j}(E^{\leq\bullet}[y^{\prime\prime}])
since
y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}
are exponentially close.

Since
y^{\prime\prime}
is biregular (with specified bounds) the flags
E^{\leq\bullet}[y^{\prime\prime}]
and
E^{\geq\bullet}[y^{\prime\prime}]
are transverse.
Also
\chi_{E,j}(E^{\geq\bullet}[z^{\prime\prime}])
and
\chi_{E,j}(E^{\geq\bullet}[y^{\prime\prime}])
are exponentially close by shadowing in
Lemma 6.2.4
combined with the Hölder estimates of
Proposition E.2.6
(i)
.
We conclude that the evolved stable flag
\chi_{E,j}(g_{\ell/2+t^{\prime\prime}}E^{\leq\bullet}_{y_{1/2}}(z_{1/2}))
is sufficiently transverse to the unstable flag
\chi_{E,j}(E^{\geq\bullet}(z^{\prime\prime}))
.

For part (iii), observe that we proved in the previous part that if
F=E_{y_{1/2}}^{\leq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
then the filtrations determining each of the flags are exponentially close, therefore so are the flags.
The same estimate holds for any other
F\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell,C_{0}}^{s}E(z_{1/2})
so the claim follows.

The estimates in (iv) are analogous to
Lemma 6.2.2
with
z:=z_{1/2}
and
\overline{z}_{t}:=\hat{z}_{1/2}
, noting that
d^{Q}(y_{1/2},z_{1/2})^{N^{\prime}_{1}}\leq e^{-N_{2}^{\prime}\ell}
with
N_{2}^{\prime}\to+\infty
as
N_{1}^{\prime}\to+\infty
.
Then, for any sufficiently large
k\in\mathbb{N}
we consider the part-time equivariant fake flags
E^{\leq\bullet}_{y_{1/2},k,fake}(z_{1/2,k})
, where
z_{1/2,k}:=\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{k,fake}[y_{1/2}]\cap\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
. and verify that for each of
z_{1/2},\hat{z}_{1/2}
the distances to the
z_{1/2,k}
-flags satisfy the required bounds.
∎

For the next statement, recall that forward dynamically defined (fdd) cocycles are introduced in
Definition E.1.9
; we will use the analogous backwards dynamically defined (bdd) cocycles and make use of the fact that
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
is bdd, see
Proposition E.3.6
.

6.2.6 Proposition

(Fake future flag and subresonant map)
.

Consider the cocycle
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
used for the linearization of subresonant normal forms for the unstable manifolds (see
Theorem D.1.2
), which is backwards dynamically defined (bdd) and in particular smooth along unstables.
Let
E
be an associated smooth and natural cocycle, with bdd subcocycles
S_{1}\subset S_{2}\subseteq E
such that
L\mathcal{W}^{u}\cong S_{2}/S_{1}
(see
Proposition E.1.13
for their existence).
We can then choose
C_{0}
sufficiently large and apply
Proposition 6.2.5
to have the following properties.

There exists a measurable choice of flag
\tilde{F}\in\mathcal{F}_{C_{0},\ell}^{s}E(z_{1/2})
(where
\mathcal{F}_{C_{0},\ell}^{s}E(z_{1/2})
is provided by

Proposition 6.2.5
)
that furthermore induces a flag
\tilde{F}^{sr}
on
S_{2}/S_{1}(z_{1/2})\cong L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})
, with the following properties:

(i)

There exists a linear map

\tilde{\Phi}_{1}\colon L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y_{1/2})\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})

such that
\tilde{F}^{sr}=\tilde{\Phi}_{1}(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y_{1/2})^{\leq\bullet})

i.e. the flag
\tilde{F}^{sr}
is the image of the stable flag
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y_{1/2})^{\leq\bullet}
at
y_{1/2}
.

(ii)

There exists a subresonant map

\tilde{\phi}_{1}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]

such that its linearization is
\tilde{\Phi}_{1}
.

Proof of
Proposition 6.2.6
.

It suffices to perform the constructions at the points
y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}
, and then apply
g_{-t^{\prime\prime}}
to obtain them at
y_{1/2},z_{1/2}
.
As a preliminary, note that for some
constant
C_{1}
depending on the compact set
K
, and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}>0

depending on the Lyapunov spectrum, we have the bound

d^{Q}(y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\leq C_{1}e^{-\alpha^{\prime\prime}\ell}
.
Furthermore,
z^{\prime\prime}
is in a backwards-uniform Pesin set by
Lemma 6.2.4
, i.e. there exist
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\varepsilon_{0}>0
, which depend on the compact set
K_{0}
in
Proposition 6.2.5
such that
z^{\prime\prime}\in Q^{-,\Lambda_{E}}_{N_{0},\varepsilon_{0}}
in the sense of
§E.1.5
.

We will now construct a subresonant map

\phi^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z^{\prime\prime}]
.
Let then
I_{y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}\colon L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y^{\prime\prime})\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z^{\prime\prime})
be the
identification provided by
Eqn. (E.2.10)
.
By
Lemma E.5.3
, it follows that
there exists
h\in\GL\left(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y^{\prime\prime})\right)
such that

\displaystyle h\cdot\left(I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}\right)^{-1}\left(L_{z^{\prime\prime}}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[z^{\prime\prime}])\right)

\displaystyle=L_{y^{\prime\prime}}\left(\mathcal{W}^{u}[y^{\prime\prime}]\right)

on linearized unstables

\displaystyle h\cdot\left(I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}\right)^{-1}\left(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z^{\prime\prime})^{\geq\bullet}\right)

\displaystyle=L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y^{\prime\prime})^{\geq\bullet}

on flags

and

\left\|h-{\bm{1}}\right\|\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}\ell}

where
L_{x}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(x)
is the linearization map,
and
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(x)^{\geq\bullet}
is the unstable flag of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(x)
, and
\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}>0
is some constant that only depends on the Lyapunov spectrum.
Let
\Phi^{\prime\prime}=I_{y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}\circ h^{-1}
.
Then,
\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L_{y^{\prime\prime}}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[y^{\prime\prime}])=L_{z^{\prime\prime}}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[z^{\prime\prime}])
,

\Phi^{\prime\prime}(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y^{\prime\prime})^{\geq\bullet})=L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z^{\prime\prime})^{\geq\bullet}
,
and therefore, by

Corollary C.1.32
,

\Phi^{\prime\prime}
is a linearization of a subresonant map
\phi^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z^{\prime\prime}]
.

Using the notation of
Proposition 6.2.5
, it now suffices to construct
\phi^{\prime\prime}_{E}\colon E(y^{\prime\prime})\to E(z^{\prime\prime})
with the following properties.
It preserves the filtration

S_{1}\subset S_{2}\subset E
on both spaces, and it takes the stable
flag
E(y^{\prime\prime})^{\leq\bullet}
into the the set of flags in

\mathcal{F}^{s}E(z^{\prime\prime})
which are
C_{0}
-transverse to the unstable flag

E(z^{\prime\prime})^{\geq\bullet}
, where
C_{0}
is the same absolute constant
as in
Proposition 6.2.5
.
Furthermore
\phi^{\prime\prime}_{E}
is required to induce the subresonant
polynomial map
\phi^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z^{\prime\prime}]
just constructed.
Indeed,
given such a
\phi^{\prime\prime}_{E}
, we can apply
g_{-t^{\prime\prime}}
(where
t^{\prime\prime}
is
such that
y^{\prime\prime}=g_{t^{\prime\prime}}y_{1/2}
) to the flag

\phi^{\prime\prime}_{E}\left(E(y^{\prime\prime})^{\leq\bullet}\right)
to obtain
\tilde{F}

at
z_{1/2}
.
By

Proposition 6.2.5
the flag

\tilde{F}
is in
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{C_{0},\ell}E(z_{1/2})
.
Because the

g_{t}
-dynamics is by subresonant maps on unstables, the resulting
flag
\tilde{F}^{sr}
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})
is also induced by a
subresonant map

\tilde{\phi}=g_{-t^{\prime\prime}}\circ\phi^{\prime\prime}\circ g_{t^{\prime\prime}}
.
So all the
desired conclusions are satisfied.

We now construct
\phi^{\prime\prime}_{E}
.
First, recall that by
Proposition E.2.6
, the bundles
S_{i}
are Hölder-continuous on the uniform backwards Pesin set
Q^{-,\Lambda_{E}}_{N_{0},\varepsilon_{0}}
to which
y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}
belong.
So there exists
C_{2},\beta_{2}
such that in the smooth local trivialization of
E
at
y^{\prime\prime}
we have

d_{Gr}(S_{i}(z^{\prime\prime}),S_{i}(y^{\prime\prime}))\leq C_{2}d(z^{\prime\prime},y^{\prime\prime})^{\beta_{2}}\leq C_{1}C_{2}^{\beta_{2}}e^{-\beta_{2}\alpha_{1}^{\prime\prime}\ell}

where
d_{Gr}
is the distance in the Grassmannian.
Let us also note that the identifications
I_{y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}
used in the construction of
\phi^{\prime\prime}
are built in
Eqn. (E.2.10)
using the same trivialization of
E
, the maps
S_{i}
into the Grassmannian, and orthogonal projection.
Let also
I_{y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}^{E}
be the associated identification between the fibers of
E
at
y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}
.
Then our desired
\phi^{\prime\prime}_{E}
is
\widetilde{h}\circ I_{y^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}^{E}
, where
\widetilde{h}
is the lift of
h\in\GL(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y^{\prime\prime}))
to
E(y^{\prime\prime})
under the identification
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y^{\prime\prime})\cong S_{2}(y^{\prime\prime})\cap S_{1}^{\perp}(y^{\prime\prime})
, and extension as the identity on the orthogonal complement.
Then the desired
C_{0}
-transversality of the flags
\phi^{\prime\prime}_{E}\left(E^{\leq\bullet}(y^{\prime\prime})\right)
and
E^{\geq\bullet}(z^{\prime\prime})
follows from the boundedness of the trivialization of
E
in the chart at
y^{\prime\prime}
, and the Hölder regularity of the unstable flags of bdd cocycles restricted to uniform Pesin sets, see
Proposition E.2.6
.
∎

6.2.7 Theorem

(Fake holonomies and strictly subresonant maps)
.

(i)

There exists a decomposition
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})=\oplus L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}}
such that taking associated flags
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\geq\lambda_{i}}
gives the backward/unstable Lyapunov filtration of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
, and taking the flags
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\leq\lambda_{i}}
gives the filtration
\tilde{F}^{sr}
from
Proposition 6.2.6
.

(ii)

There exists a map:

P^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})\colon L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{1/2}^{\prime})\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})

taking the Lyapunov decomposition on the left side to the decomposition on the right provided by
(i)
, and on the associated graded coincides with the standard unstable holonomy.
Furthermore
P^{+}_{fake}
is induced by a strictly subresonant map

\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\xrightarrow{\wp_{fake}^{+}}\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]

(note that these are the same submanifold) taking
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
to
z_{1/2}
.

(iii)

Suppose that for some
N^{\prime}_{1}>0
, we have a
\hat{z}_{1/2}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
with
d^{Q}(z_{1/2},\hat{z}_{1/2})\leq e^{-N_{1}^{\prime}\ell}
.
Suppose also that we have an analogous decomposition
\oplus L\mathcal{W}^{u}(\hat{z}_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}}
with

d^{Gr}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\left(P_{GM}(z_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}},P_{GM}(\hat{z}_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})L\mathcal{W}^{u}(\hat{z}_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}}\right)\leq e^{-N_{1}^{\prime}\ell}\quad\forall i

where
P_{GM}
denotes the Gauss–Manin connection on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
and
d^{Gr}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}
denotes the distance on Grassmannian at
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
.
Then the analogous map
P^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},\hat{z}_{1/2})
, after post-composing with the Gauss–Manin connection, satisfies the bound:

\left\|P^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},{z}_{1/2})\circ P^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},\hat{z}_{1/2})^{-1}\right\|_{L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})}\leq e^{-N_{2}^{\prime}\ell}

where the map inside the norm is conjugated by the Gauss–Manin connection to be viewed as a map of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
, and with
N_{2}^{\prime}\to+\infty
as
N_{1}^{\prime}\to+\infty
.

Recall that we defined strictly subresonant maps of an unstable manifold in
§3.1.1
.
Recall also from
§3.1.2
that there exists a “Gauss–Manin” connection
P_{GM}
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
for points on the same unstable by
Theorem D.1.2
(which lists its properties).
To show that
\wp_{fake}^{+}
in the above proposition is strictly subresonant we must check that the linear map
P_{GM}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})\circ P^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
is induced by a strictly subresonant map in the sense of
Definition C.2.6
.

Proof.

Note that the point
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
is biregular.
The map
P^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
is unique and well-defined given its requirements to map decompositions in
(ii)
and to agree with the standard holonomy on graded pieces.
It suffices to show that it is the linearization of
some
subresonant map, since then the map
P_{GM}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})\circ P^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
will be strictly subresonant by
Definition C.2.6
and its action on the graded Lyapunov pieces.

To exhibit the required subresonant map
consider the composed subresonant maps

\wp_{tmp}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{\wp^{-}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},q_{1/2})}\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}]\xrightarrow{\wp^{+}(q_{1/2},y_{1/2})}\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]\xrightarrow{\tilde{\phi}_{1}}\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]

where
\tilde{\phi}_{1}
is provided by
Proposition 6.2.6
, and
\wp^{\pm}
by
Proposition D.2.2
.
By construction each subresonant maps admits a linearization which furthermore preserves the decompositions of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
at the source and target.
So the resulting linearization preserves the decompositions, but it might not act as the standard holonomy on each graded piece.

Next we use the semidirect product structure of subresonant maps provided by
Proposition C.2.7
(iii)
, using the Lyapunov splitting at
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
to define resonant maps.
We thus have
\wp_{tmp}=\wp_{tmp}^{ssr}\cdot\wp_{tmp}^{r}
where
\wp_{tmp}^{ssr}
is strictly subresonant and
\wp_{tmp}^{r}
is resonant.
The linearization of
\wp_{tmp}^{r}
preserves the Lyapunov splitting at
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
, while
\wp_{tmp}^{ssr}
acts as the identity (i.e. the Gauss–Manin connection) on the associated graded pieces since it is strictly subresonant.
Since on associated graded pieces the Gauss–Manin connection and the standard holonomies agree, it follows that the map
\wp_{tmp}^{ssr}
has all the required properties of
\wp^{+}_{fake}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2})
.

To prove the last part, we note that the corresponding bounds hold for the distances between the map
\widetilde{\phi}_{1}
associated to
z_{1/2}
and its analogue
\hat{\phi}_{1}
associated to
\hat{z}_{1/2}
, from the assumption on the decompositions of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
.
Indeed, the bounds on the decomposition follow from
Proposition 6.2.5
(iv)
, and the construction of
\widetilde{\phi}_{1}
from
Proposition 6.2.6
.
Following through the above construction of
\wp^{+}_{fake}
yields the bound on the distance between the
P^{+}_{fake}
-maps associated to
z_{1/2}
and
\hat{z}_{1/2}
.
∎

Figure 6.2.8.
Interpolating the curves.

6.2.9.
Defining the interpolation map

We are now ready to define two key maps that appear in later arguments.
First define the map
\tilde{\phi}
by the composition:

(6.2.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}^{\prime}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wp^{+}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wp^{+}_{fake}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2})}

Here
\wp^{+}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})
and
\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})
are defined in
Proposition D.2.2
.

The linearization of each arrow then induces a linear map
\tilde{\Phi}=L\tilde{\phi}
by the composition:

(6.2.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y_{1/2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{1/2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{1/2}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{+}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{+}_{fake}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2})}

Note that
\tilde{\Phi}
and
\tilde{\phi}
are distinct from the maps
\tilde{\Phi}_{1},\tilde{\phi}_{1}
from
Proposition 6.2.6
.

6.2.12.
Interpolation map and generalized subspaces

The construction of the interpolation maps
\tilde{\Phi},\tilde{\phi}
can be performed without passing to the finite cover
X\to Q
, in other words the map only depends on the images of the points under the map
\sigma
.
Note that the map
\wp^{+}_{fake}
is also defined only in terms of data on
Q
.

Suppose now that
U^{+}(q)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
is a family of generalized subspaces, defined for
q\in X
, as in
Definition 3.1.5
.
Now the measurable connection maps
P^{\pm}(\bullet,\bullet)
are
defined on
X
, and furthermore these maps have the property

P^{\pm}(x,y)U^{+}(x)P^{\pm}(y,x)=U^{+}(y)
for
x,y\in X
in a full measure set, see

§2.5.5
.

6.2.13 Proposition

(Interpolation and generalized subspaces)
.

Suppose that
U^{+}(q)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(q)
is a family of groups compatible with the measures, and let
U^{+}[q]\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
be the corresponding family of orbits, for all
q
in a full measure set.
Assume that
y_{1/2}\in U^{+}[q_{1/2}]
.

Then
U^{+}[y_{1/2}]=U^{+}[q_{1/2}]
and the subresonant map
\tilde{\phi}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]
taking
y_{1/2}
to
z_{1/2}
defined by (
6.2.10
) is such that the image
\tilde{\phi}\left(U^{+}[y_{1/2}]\right)
is the image of
U^{+}[q_{1/2}^{\prime}]
under the strictly subresonant map
\wp_{fake}^{+}
.

Proof.

The map
\tilde{\phi}
is obtained at the composition of

\wp^{+}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2}),\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})
and

\wp^{+}_{fake}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2})
.
The maps on cocycles
P^{+}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})
and
P^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})
induce maps on Lie algebras and preserve the family of
g_{t}
-equivariant subalgebras
\mathfrak{u}^{+}
, by the Ledrappier invariance principle.
It follows that

P^{+}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})U^{+}(y_{1/2})P^{+}(q_{1/2},y_{1/2})=U^{+}(q_{1/2})
and since by assumption
y_{1/2}\in U^{+}[q_{1/2}]
, it follows that in fact
U^{+}[y_{1/2}]=U^{+}[q_{1/2}]
.

Similarly
\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})(U^{+}[q_{1/2}])=U^{+}[q_{1/2}^{\prime}]
, and the final conclusion follows since
\wp^{+}_{fake}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2})
is strictly subresonant by
Theorem 6.2.7
.
∎

6.2.14.
Properties needed for subsequent arguments

We collect the properties that we derived in this section and that will be used in the next ones:

(i)

There exists a decomposition
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})=\oplus L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}}
, such that the associated unstable flag is compatible with the one from
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
, while the “fake” stable flag is provided by
Proposition 6.2.6
.

(ii)

The interpolation map
\widetilde{\phi}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]
is such that its linearization takes the Lyapunov decomposition at
y_{1/2}
to the decomposition at
z_{1/2}
.

(iii)

Furthermore,
\widetilde{\phi}
satisfies
Proposition 6.2.13
.

Note that the interpolation map and its construction does not depend on the compatible family of subgroups
U^{+}
.

6.3.
Estimates on evolved interpolation map

Let
Y
,
Y^{\prime}
be two bottom-linked
Y
configurations. Using the
notation §
6.1.5
, let
y_{1/2}=g_{-\ell/2}uq_{1}
, and let
z_{1/2}=\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y_{1/2}]\cap\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
.

Recall the map
\tilde{\phi}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]
given in (
6.2.10
) and its linearization

\tilde{\Phi}\colon L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y_{1/2})\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})

given by (
6.2.11
) in

6.2.9
.
Let
z=g_{\ell/2}z_{1/2}
, so that
z\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1}^{\prime}]
and it is also exponentially (in
\ell
) close to
\mathcal{W}^{cs}[uq_{1}]
.

Given
0<\tau<\alpha_{3}\ell
, define
{\phi_{\tau}}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{\tau}uq_{1}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{\tau}z]
by

(6.3.1)

{\phi_{\tau}}:=g_{\tau+\ell/2}\circ\tilde{\phi}\circ g_{-(\tau+\ell/2)}.

We remark that
{\phi_{\tau}}
is defined using
Y
and
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
. In the future, we may denote
\phi_{\tau}
by

(6.3.2)

{\phi_{\tau}}(Y,q^{\prime})\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{\tau}uq_{1}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{\tau}z].

6.3.3 Theorem

(Interpolation does not move points much)
.

There exists
0<\alpha<1
such that for all sufficiently large
k
and sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
the following holds.
For every
\delta>0
there exists a
compact set
K
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
such that the following
holds:
Suppose
Y=Y_{pre}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)
and
Y^{\prime}=Y_{pre}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)
are two bottom-linked
pre-
Y
-configurations, and suppose that
Y^{\prime}

(k,\varepsilon)
-left-shadows
Y
.
Suppose that, using the notation of

§6.1.5
,
q
,
q_{1/2}
,
q_{1}
,
uq_{1}
,
g_{-\ell/2}uq_{1}
,

q^{\prime}
,
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
,
q_{1}^{\prime}
all belong to
K
.
Note that
q_{2}
is not necessarily assumed in
K
.

Then, for all
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{\tau}uq_{1}]
,

\displaystyle d^{Q}(x,\phi_{\tau}(x))

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}.

Recall that
\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[q]\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
is the unit ball around
q
in the
d^{u}
-distance.

The proof of
Theorem 6.3.3

relies on
Lemma E.5.2
and
Lemma E.5.3
and will be given in
§6.3.8
, after we establish the following preliminary results.

6.3.4 Proposition
.

There exists
\beta_{2},\beta_{1/2}>0
such that for all sufficiently large
k
and sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
the following holds.
For every
\delta>0
there exists a
compact set
K
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
such that the following
holds:
Suppose
Y=Y_{pre}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)
and
Y^{\prime}=Y_{pre}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)
are two bottom-linked
pre-
Y
-configurations, and suppose that
Y^{\prime}

(k,\varepsilon)
-left-shadows
Y
.
Suppose, using the notation of

§6.1.5
and
Definition 6.1.7
that
Y\in K

and also
q^{\prime}
,
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
,
q_{1}^{\prime}
all belong to
K
.

Let
z_{1/2}
be the unique intersection point of

\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{loc}[y_{1/2}]
and
\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
, and let
z_{2}=g_{\tau+\ell/2}z_{1/2}
.
Then we have the estimates:

\displaystyle\left\|L\widetilde{\phi}-I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{q_{1/2},z_{1/2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq H_{1/2}(\delta)e^{-\beta_{1/2}\ell}

\displaystyle\left\|\gr_{\bullet}L\widetilde{\phi}-\gr_{\bullet}I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{q_{1/2},z_{1/2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq H_{1/2}(\delta)e^{-\beta_{1/2}\ell}

(6.3.5)

\displaystyle\left\|\gr_{\bullet}L\phi_{\tau}(q_{2},z_{2})-\gr_{\bullet}I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{q_{2},z_{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq H_{2}(\delta)e^{-\beta_{2}\ell}

Note that by
Lemma 6.2.4
the point
z_{1/2}
is in a uniform Pesin set, as is
q_{1/2}
by assumption.

Proof.

We recall some notation first:
d^{\mathcal{L}}_{q}
denotes the distance in the

(k,\varepsilon)
-Lyapunov chart centered at
q
, and
\alpha
is the minimal rate of expansion along unstables.
We reduce first the second estimate to the first one.

We have
d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{1/2}}(z_{1/2},y_{1/2})\leq e^{-\alpha\ell/2}
whence for all

0\leq t\leq\alpha_{3}\ell
,

d^{\mathcal{L}}_{g_{t+\ell/2}y_{1/2}}(g_{t+\ell/2}z_{1/2},g_{t+\ell/2}y_{1/2})\leq e^{2\varepsilon(t+\ell/2)-\alpha\ell/2}.

Similarly, for all
0\leq s\leq t+\ell/2
we have

d^{\mathcal{L}}_{g_{-s}q_{2}}(g_{-s}z_{2},g_{-s}q_{2})\leq e^{2\varepsilon(t+\ell/2-s)-\alpha\ell/2}\leq e^{2\varepsilon(\alpha_{3}\ell+\ell/2)-\alpha\ell/2}\leq e^{-\alpha\ell/4}

by taking
\varepsilon
sufficiently small.
With
\gamma=\frac{\alpha}{8(\alpha_{3}+\frac{1}{2})}
we have

e^{-\alpha\ell/4}\leq e^{-\alpha\ell/8}e^{-\gamma s}

for all
0\leq s\leq t+\ell/2\leq(\alpha_{3}+1/2)\ell.

We then apply
Proposition E.2.15
with the cocycle
g_{s}^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}
starting at
q_{1/2},z_{1/2}
for time
s\in[0,t+\ell/2]
, we find that, for
\varepsilon_{1}>0
arbitrarily small, if
k\geq k_{0}(E),\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}(E,\varepsilon_{1}))
we have that

\left\|\gr_{\bullet}L\phi_{\tau}(q_{2},z_{2})-\gr_{\bullet}I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{q_{2},z_{2}}\right\|\leq e^{\varepsilon_{1}(t+\ell/2)}\left\|\gr_{\bullet}L\widetilde{\phi}-\gr_{\bullet}I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{q_{1/2},z_{1/2}}\right\|

It now suffices to prove the second estimate and take
\varepsilon_{1}>0

sufficiently small.
Note that the second estimate follows from the first, so we have reduced to the first inequality.

Recall that
E
is the smooth and natural cocycle from
Proposition 6.2.6
associated to
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
.
Let
\widetilde{F}\in\mathcal{F}^{s}_{C_{0},\ell}\left(z_{1/2}\right)
be the flag constructed in that proposition, which is in particular also adapted to
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
.
Then
Proposition 6.2.5
(iii)
with
t=0
implies that (everything is for the ambient trivialization
\chi_{E}
of
E
):

(6.3.6)

\displaystyle\dist\left(\chi_{E,j}\left(E^{\lambda_{i}}\left(y_{1/2}\right),E^{\lambda_{i}}_{\widetilde{F}}\left(z_{1/2}\right)\right)\right)\leq e^{-\alpha_{E}\ell}.

We can ensure that for the compact set
K
, there exists
r(\delta,E)>0
and
C(\delta,E,k)\geq 1
such that for
q\in K
the trivializations
\chi_{E,j}
and
\exp_{q}^{E}
are defined on
Q[q;r(\delta,E)]
(see
§E.2.1
) and the
C^{k}
-norm of
\chi_{E,j}^{-1}\circ\exp_{q}^{E}
is bounded by
C_{0}:=C(\delta,E,k)
on
B(q;r(\delta,E))
.

Recall now that
y_{1/2},q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in K
.
We can assume that
\ell
is large enough such that the following hold:

•

d^{Q}
between any of the points involved is less than
r(\delta,E)
.

•

The trivializations
\exp_{q}^{E}
and
I^{E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}}
(see
Eqn. (E.2.8)
) are uniformly Hölder-comparable with a constant
C_{1}\geq 1
and exponent
\beta(E,L\mathcal{W}^{u})
.

We can therefore pass freely (at a cost of a uniform constant) between trivializations at
y_{1/2},q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2}
and
\chi_{E,j}
, so all estimates stated below are for
\chi_{E,j}
.
We will add the subscript
(E,j)
to any map expressed in this trivialization.

It follows from
Corollary E.2.26
that exists
\gamma_{0}>0
such that the measurable connection maps satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|P^{+}_{E,j}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})-{\bm{1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}e^{-\gamma_{0}\ell}

\displaystyle\left\|P^{-}_{E,j}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})-{\bm{1}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}e^{-\gamma_{0}\ell}

and additionally, using
Eqn. (6.3.6)
, we also have that

\left\|P^{+}_{fake,E,j}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})-{\bm{1}}\right\|\leq C_{0}e^{-\gamma_{0}\ell}.

Combining these estimates we find that

\left\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{E,j}-{\bm{1}}\right\|\leq C_{2}e^{-\gamma_{0}\ell}

where
\widetilde{\Phi}_{E,j}
is defined on
E
analogously to the definition in
Eqn. (6.2.11)
of
\widetilde{\Phi}
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
.

Since the trivializations
\exp_{q}^{E}
and
I^{E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{q,-}
(see
Eqn. (E.2.8)
) are Hölder-comparable on
K
by assumption we conclude that

\left\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{E}-I^{E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y_{1/2},z_{1/2}}\right\|\leq C_{3}e^{-\gamma_{0}\ell}

where
\widetilde{\Phi}_{E}\colon E(y_{1/2})\to E(z_{1/2})
is the map induced by
\widetilde{\Phi}_{E,j}
.
We finally note that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{E}
induces
\widetilde{\Phi}=L\widetilde{\phi}
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
, and the induced map by
I^{E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}}
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
satisfies

\left\|I^{E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y_{1/2},z_{1/2}}-I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y_{1/2},z_{1/2}}\right\|\leq C_{4}e^{-\gamma_{1}\ell}

as maps
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y_{1/2})\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}\left(z_{1/2}\right)
and hence the desired claim follows.
∎

6.3.7 Lemma

(
L\phi_{\tau}
and
I
are close)
.

There exists
0<\alpha<1
and for every
\delta>0
there exists a
compact set
K
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
such that the following
holds:
Suppose
Y=Y_{pre}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)
and
Y^{\prime}=Y_{pre}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)
are two bottom-linked
pre-
Y
-configurations, and suppose that
Y^{\prime}

(k,\varepsilon)
-left-shadows
Y
.
Suppose, using the notation of

§6.1.5
and
Definition 6.1.7
that
Y\in K

and also
q^{\prime}
,
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
,
q_{1}^{\prime}
all belong to
K
.

Then

\left\|\left(I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{g_{\tau}uq_{1},g_{\tau}z}\right)^{-1}\circ L\phi_{\tau}-\mathbf{1}\right\|\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}.

Proof.

The inequality on
\gr_{\bullet}L\mathcal{W}^{u}
follows from
Eqn. (6.3.5)
, so to conclude the same on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
we need to estimate the distance between the corresponding decompositions.

Set
y:=uq_{1}
and consider the Lyapunov splitting
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(g_{\tau}y)=\oplus L\mathcal{W}^{u}(g_{\tau}y)^{\lambda_{i}}
, and the decomposition

L\mathcal{W}^{u}(g_{\tau}z)=\oplus L\mathcal{W}^{u}(g_{\tau}z)^{\lambda_{i}}
, where
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(g_{\tau}z)^{\lambda_{i}}=g_{\tau+\ell/2}L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}}
and

L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}}
is as in

Theorem 6.2.7
.
The map
L\phi_{\tau}
intertwines these decompositions.
The map
I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{g_{\tau}uq_{1},g_{\tau}z}
intertwines the
associated backwards flags
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(g_{\tau}y)^{\geq\lambda_{i}}
and

L\mathcal{W}^{u}(g_{\tau}z)^{\geq\lambda_{i}}
(since these give the backwards
Lyapunov filtration).

Recall that the map
I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{x,y}
is defined in
Eqn. (E.2.10)
using a smooth and natural cocycle
E
with bdd subcocycles
S_{1}\subseteq S_{2}\subseteq E
such that
L\mathcal{W}^{u}\cong S_{2}/S_{1}
.
Recall also that the splitting
\oplus L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}}
is constructed in
Proposition 6.2.6
from a flag
\widetilde{F}
at
z_{1/2}
.
Now
Proposition 6.2.5
(iii)
shows that, in some ambient chart
\chi_{E,j}
containing
y
, the splittings
\chi_{E,j}\left(E^{\lambda_{i}}(g_{\tau}y)\right)
and
g_{\tau}E^{\lambda_{i}}_{\widetilde{F}}(z)
are exponentially close, with exponent depending only on
E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}
and constant depending on
\delta
.

We also know from
Proposition E.2.6
that
S_{1}\left(g_{\tau}y\right)
and
S_{2}\left(g_{\tau}z\right)
are exponentially close when expressed in the chart
\chi_{E,j}
, with exponent depending only on
E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}
and constant depending on
\delta
.
We recall next that the trivializations
\chi_{E,j}
and
I^{E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}}
of
E
are Hölder-equivalent (see
Eqn. (E.2.8)
) and the trivialization
I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}
and the one induced by
I^{E,L\mathcal{W}^{u}}
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
are Hölder-equivalent as well (see the compatibility claim in
Proposition E.2.9
).

We now conclude that
I_{g_{\tau}y,g_{\tau}z}^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}\left(L\mathcal{W}^{u}(g_{\tau}y)^{{\lambda_{i}}}\right)
and
g_{\tau}L\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\widetilde{F}}(z)^{{\lambda_{i}}}
are exponentially close and
Eqn. (6.3.5)
completes the proof.
∎

6.3.8.
Proof of
Theorem 6.3.3

Let us introduce the notation
x^{\prime\prime}=\phi_{\tau}(x)
, and for this proof only
y_{\tau}:=g_{\tau}uq_{1}
, and
z_{\tau}:=g_{\tau}z
.

Given any compact set
K_{1}
of measure
1-\delta_{1}
with
\delta_{1}>0
sufficiently small (depending on
\delta
and
k
), we first reduce to the case when
y_{\tau}\in K_{1}
.
Given an arbitrary
y_{\tau}
, and since
y_{0}\in K
and we can assume by Birkhoff that a point in
K
visits
K_{1}
for a
(1-\tfrac{1}{2}\delta_{1})
-fraction of its time, we conclude that for some
\tau_{1}\in[\tau(1-\tfrac{3}{5}\delta_{1}),(1-\tfrac{1}{10}\delta_{1})\tau]
we have
y_{\tau_{1}}\in K_{1}
.
The estimates in
Remark 2.2.5
then imply that the distances gets distorted by a factor of
A_{k}e^{\delta_{1}\kappa_{k}\tau}
for some
A_{k}(\delta)
.
Now it suffices to pick
\delta_{1}
sufficiently small depending on
k
.
We require, in addition, that for
y\in K_{1}
the Lyapunov distance
d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y}
is bi-Lipschitz with
d^{Q}
up to a factor of
C_{1}(\delta_{1})
, that for
z\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[y]
the quantity
d^{u}(y,z)
is bi-Lipschitz with
d^{Q}(y,z)
up to a factor of
C_{1}(\delta_{1})
, and that
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
such that
e^{-\tfrac{1}{10}\delta_{1}(\lambda_{+}-\varepsilon)\ell_{0}}C_{1}(\delta_{1})^{2}\leq 1
(where
\lambda_{+}
is the smallest strictly positive Lyapunov exponents).
With this assumption we have that
g_{-\delta_{1}\ell/10}\left(\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{-\delta_{1}\ell/10}y]\right)\subset\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[y]
.
Note that we can always increase the constant
C(\delta)
to account for the case when
\ell,\tau
are smaller than the required lower bounds.

To proceed to the proof, recall that
L_{q}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]\hookrightarrow L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)
denotes the linearization map based at a point
q
, and note that

L_{z_{\tau}}x^{\prime\prime}=(L\phi_{\tau})(L_{y_{\tau}}x).

Using
Lemma E.5.3
to obtain
h
below, let
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[y_{\tau}]
be the unique point such that

L_{y_{\tau}}x^{\prime}=h\cdot\left(I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y_{\tau},z_{\tau}}\right)^{-1}(L_{z_{\tau}}x^{\prime\prime})\text{ inside }L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y_{\tau})

By the triangle inequality

d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{\tau}}(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{\tau}}(x,x^{\prime})+d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{\tau}}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})

it suffices to estimate each summand on the right.

For the first summand, note that by
Lemma E.5.3
there exists
h\in\GL(L\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{\tau}])
and that we have

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{0}}d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{\tau}}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\left\|L_{y_{\tau}}x-L_{y_{\tau}}x^{\prime}\right\|=\left\|L_{y_{\tau}}x-h\cdot\left(I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y_{\tau},z_{\tau}}\right)^{-1}(L_{z_{\tau}}x^{\prime\prime})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|L_{y_{\tau}}x-h\cdot\left(I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y_{\tau},z_{\tau}}\right)^{-1}(L\phi_{\tau})(L_{y_{\tau}}x)\right\|

For the first inequality we used
Lemma E.5.2
.
But now
L_{y_{\tau}}x
is a vector of uniformly bounded norm in
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(y_{\tau})
since
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[y_{\tau}]
, the norm of
{\bm{1}}-h
is bounded using
Lemma E.5.3
by
\kappa_{1}e^{-\alpha_{1}\ell}
, the norm of
\left(I^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{y_{\tau},z_{\tau}}\right)^{-1}(L\phi_{\tau})-{\bm{1}}
is bounded using
Lemma 6.3.7
by
e^{-\alpha_{2}\ell}
.
It follows that the last expression above is bounded by
\kappa_{3}e^{-\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}
for appropriate
\kappa_{3},\alpha_{2}^{\prime}
where
\alpha_{2}^{\prime}
depends only on the Lyapunov spectrum and
\kappa_{3}
on
\delta
.

To estimate the second term, i.e.
d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{\tau}}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})
, we will use
Proposition E.5.5
with
y=y_{\tau}
and
z=g_{\tau}z
:

d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{\tau}}(x^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime})\leq\kappa_{4}\left(\left\|I_{y_{\tau},g_{\tau}z}^{-1}\circ L_{g_{\tau}z}(x^{\prime\prime})-L_{y_{\tau}}(x^{\prime})\right\|+d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{\tau}}(g_{\tau}z,y_{\tau})\right)^{\beta_{4}}

Now the second term in the parentheses is bounded by
\kappa_{5}e^{-\alpha_{5}l}
for appropriate
\kappa_{5},\alpha_{5}
.
Using the definition of
x^{\prime}
at the start of the proof, we can rewrite the first term in the parentheses as

\left\|I_{y_{\tau},g_{\tau}z}^{-1}\circ L_{g_{\tau}z}(x^{\prime\prime})-h\cdot I^{-1}_{y_{\tau},z_{\tau}}\circ L_{g_{\tau}z}(x^{\prime\prime})\right\|

Now the norm of
L_{z_{\tau}}(x^{\prime\prime})
is bounded by some
\kappa_{6}

since
x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[z_{\tau}]
, the norm of

I^{-1}_{y_{\tau},z_{\tau}}
is bounded by
\kappa_{7}
, and finally the
norm of
h-{\bm{1}}
is bounded by
\kappa_{1}e^{-\alpha_{1}\ell}
as before.
The conclusion follows.
∎

6.3.9.
Choice of
(k,\varepsilon)

We now fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large with the following properties.
First, we require that
Theorem 6.3.3
and
Proposition 6.3.4
holds.
We additionally require that for the constant
\alpha_{3}
from
§8.1.22
, we have that
\varepsilon\alpha_{3}
is less than any of the positive constants
\alpha
arising in the preceding sections, as well as
Section 8
through
Section 12
.

From now on, left-shadowing of
Y
-diagrams means
(k,\varepsilon)
-left shadowing in the sense of
Definition 6.1.11
.

7.
Factorization

The main goal of this section is
Theorem 7.4.3
.
Recall the
Y
-configuration notation from
Section 6
.
Let
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
be two bottom linked pre-
Y
configurations. We use
the notation
§6.1.5
. A function
Z(Y,Y^{\prime})

is called admissible if it depends only on
q
,
u
,
\ell
,
q^{\prime}
, and
not on
u^{\prime}
,
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
. Thus, if
Z
is an admissible
function, we may write
Z(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)
for
Z(Y,Y^{\prime})
.

7.1.
Basic constructions

7.1.1 Definition

(half-time factorizable)
.

Let
T\to Q
be a measurable a.e. defined vector bundle.
Suppose
q\in Q
,
\ell>0
,
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(g_{\ell}q)
, and
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}_{loc}[q]
.
A measurable admissible function
Z(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)
with values in
T(q_{1/2})
is
factorizable of order
N>0
if there exist:

•

Cocycles
V^{s},V^{u}
, smooth along stables, resp. unstables, and admitting stable resp. unstable holonomies, equipped with
g_{t}
-equivariant measurable sections
\iota_{s},\iota_{u}
.

Define
g_{t}
-equivariant embeddings
F_{q}^{s}\colon\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]\hookrightarrow V^{s}(q)
(depending measurably on
q
) by

F_{q}^{s}(p)=H^{s}(p,q)\iota_{s}(p)\text{ where }H^{s}\text{ is the stable holonomy of }V^{s}.

Define the analogous construction for points on
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
and
F_{q}^{u}
.

•

A measurable family of bilinear maps

{\mathscr{A}}(q_{1/2})\colon V^{s}(q_{1/2})\times V^{u}(q_{1/2})\to T(q_{1/2}).

such that for any
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
of measure at least
1-\delta
, as well as constants
\ell_{0}=\ell_{0}(K,N)
and
C=C(K,N)
, such that provided
q,q^{\prime},q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime},y_{1/2},q_{1},uq_{1}
all belong to
K
, we have:

\left\|Z(q,q^{\prime},y,\ell)-{\mathscr{A}}(q_{1/2})\left(g_{\ell/2}F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime}),g_{-\ell/2}F_{q_{1}}^{u}(uq_{1})\right)\right\|\leq Ce^{-N\ell}

provided that
\ell>\ell_{0}
.

When
Z(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)
is factorizable to any order
N>0
, we will say that
Z
is factorizable.

With our assumptions and notation as above we have

\displaystyle g_{\ell/2}F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime})

\displaystyle=F_{q_{1/2}}^{s}(q_{1/2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle g_{-\ell/2}F_{q_{1}}^{u}(uq_{1})

\displaystyle=F_{q_{1/2}}^{u}(y_{1/2})

so we can rewrite the main inequality above as

\left\|Z(q,q^{\prime},y,\ell)-{\mathscr{A}}(q_{1/2})\left(F_{q_{1/2}}^{s}(q_{1/2}^{\prime}),F_{q_{1/2}}^{u}(y_{1/2}\right)\right\|\leq Ce^{-N\ell}

Figure 7.1.2.
The initial, and halfway points.

7.1.3 Remark

(On factorizability)
.

(i)

The target bundle
T\to Q
is not necessarily a cocycle over
g_{t}
, although in our intended application (
Theorem 7.4.3
) it will be.
However, at intermediate stages we will also work with some
T
that don’t have a cocycle structure.

(ii)

We will call
N
the order of approximation of the factorizable map
Z
and
V^{s/u},T
the associated bundles.
When the dependence on
N
is relevant, we write
V^{s/u,(N)}
for the bundle; note that
T
is independent of
N
.
Similarly, the maps
F
and
\mathscr{A}
can depend on
N
, and when this dependence is relevant we write it as
F^{(N)}
and
\mathscr{A}^{(N)}
.

(iii)

When we can choose
V^{s/u},\iota_{s},\iota_{u}
and

\mathscr{A}
independently of
N
, to make
Z=\mathscr{A}\circ F
equal on the nose, we will say that
Z
is

exactly factorizable
.

(iv)

In most statements and proofs below,
N
will be fixed and the discussion will hold for every choice of
N
.
In other situations,
we will apply the assumptions of factorizability of
Z
with a larger
N
to conclude the factorizability of another function
Z^{\prime}
for a smaller
N^{\prime}
, but with
N^{\prime}
going to infinity as
N
goes to infinity.

(v)

We will also make use of the following notation:

(7.1.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2}):=\lim_{\ell\to+\infty}Z(g_{-\ell/2}q_{1/2},g_{-\ell/2}q_{1/2},0,\ell)\in T(q_{1/2})

Let us see that this in fact makes sense.
Because of the equivariance of the sections
\iota_{s}
and
\iota_{u}
we have

\mathscr{A}(q_{1/2})\big(g_{\ell/2}\iota_{s}(q),g_{-\ell/2}\iota_{u}(q_{1})\big)=\mathscr{A}(q_{1/2})\big(\iota_{s}(q_{1/2}),\iota_{u}(q_{1/2})\big)

so the expression that approximates
Z(q,q,0,\ell)
is
in fact independent of
\ell
.
We can send
\ell
to
+\infty
and define alternatively

\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2}):=\mathscr{A}(q_{1/2})\big(\iota_{s}(q_{1/2}),\iota_{u}(q_{1/2})\big)

Note that the expression on the right-hand side, although a priori dependent on the bundles
V^{s/u}
and degree of approximation
N
, must in fact be independent of these choices since it is the limit of
Z(q,q,0,\ell)
as
l\to+\infty
.

7.1.5 Proposition

(Contraction in
V^{s}
)
.

Let
Z
be factorizable and fix a degree of approximation
N
.
Let
V^{s}
be a bundle associated to a factorizable function as in
Definition 7.1.1
and let
F_{q}^{s}\colon\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]\hookrightarrow V^{s}(q)
be the
g_{t}
-equivariant embedding given by the equivariant section
\iota_{s}
of
V^{s}
(analogous statements will hold for the unstable objects).
Then for any
\delta>0
we can choose the compact set
K
of measure at least
1-\delta
such that there exist constants with the following additional properties.

(i)

For
q,q^{\prime}\in K
and
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
, the difference
\iota_{s}(q)-H^{s}(q^{\prime},q)\iota_{s}(q^{\prime})\in V^{s}(q)
is in the negative Lyapunov subspaces of
V^{s}
, while the section
\iota_{s}(q)
is in the zero Lyapunov subspace of
V^{s}
.

The analogous property holds for
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
,
\iota_{u}
and
V^{u}
except that the difference is in the positive Lyapunov subspaces.

(ii)

There exists
\beta>0
depending on
Z
and
N
, and a constant
C
depending on
Z,N
and
K
, such that we have the estimates

\left\|F^{s}_{q_{1/2}}(q_{1/2})-F^{s}_{q_{1/2}}\left(q_{1/2}^{\prime}\right)\right\|\leq Ce^{-\beta\ell}

where the norm is in the fiber
V^{s}\left(q_{1/2}\right)
.

The analogous estimate for
F^{u}_{q_{1/2}}(q_{1/2})-F^{u}_{q_{1/2}}(y_{1/2})
holds.

(iii)

For
\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2})
defined in
Eqn. (7.1.4)
we have the estimate

\left\|\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2})-Z(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)\right\|\leq Ce^{-\delta_{0}\ell}

for some
\delta_{0}>0
that only depends on
Z
.

Note that the degree of approximation
N
does not appear in part (iii) of the proposition above.

Proof.

Part (ii) follows from (i) and the equivariance of the sections.
The proofs for the stable and unstable are analogous, so we
only cover the stable case. In this proof only, we write
V

for
V^{s}
.

For (i) we can take
-\delta
to be half the largest strictly negative Lyapunov exponent (so, closest to
0
) of
V
and further restrict to a compact set where the Oseledets theorem holds uniformly.
It is clear that
\iota_{s}(q)
is in the zero Lyapunov subspace since it is equivariant, and since the stable holonomy
H^{s}_{V}
preserves the Lyapunov filtration, it suffices to show that the projection of
\iota_{s}(q)-H^{s}_{V}(q^{\prime},q)\iota_{s}(q^{\prime})
to
\gr_{0}^{s}V:=V^{\leq 0}/V^{<0}
vanishes.
But if we consider the projection
\iota_{0}(q)
of
\iota_{s}(q)
to
\gr^{s}_{0}V
, the induced holonomy map
H^{s}_{V}
, which agrees with the holonomy on graded bundles, will preserve
\iota_{0}
by the Ledrappier Invariance Principle
Corollary B.2.7
.
The conclusion then follows.

For (iii), pick
some
degree of approximation
N>0
and apply (ii) on a compact set of almost full measure where
\left\|\mathscr{A}\right\|
is bounded by some constant.

∎

7.1.6 Proposition

(Polynomials and factorizability)
.

(i)

Suppose that
Z(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)
satisfies the definition of factorizability in
Definition 7.1.1
with bundles
V^{s/u,(N)},T
, but
\mathscr{A}^{(N)}
is a polynomial map (and such data is available for every
N
).
Then
Z
is factorizable, i.e. we can replace the bundles
V^{s/u,(N)}
by different ones to satisfy the definition of factorizability with
\mathscr{A}^{(N^{\prime})}
bilinear.

(ii)

If a function
Z(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)
is factorizable and
f\colon T\to T^{\prime}
is a measurable map of bundles, which is fiberwise polynomial, then
f(Z)
is also factorizable.

Proof.

A polynomial map between finite-dimensional vector spaces
W_{1}\xrightarrow{p}W_{2}
can be factored as

W_{1}\xrightarrow{\Sym^{\leq d}}\Sym^{\leq d}W_{1}\xrightarrow{Lp}W_{2}

where the degree of
p
is at most
d
, and
Lp
is a linear map, while the (Veronese) embedding
\Sym^{\leq d}
of
W_{1}
is not linear.

For (i), we replace
F_{q}
by
\Sym^{\leq d}\circ F_{q}
and thus replace
V^{(N)}
by the bundle
\Sym^{\leq d}V^{(N)}
.
The compact sets
K
are taken so that the degree of
\mathscr{A}^{(N)}
is bounded by
d
.

Part (ii) follows from (i) since
\mathscr{A}_{f}^{(N)}:=f\circ\mathscr{A}^{(N)}
is now a polynomial map and can be used to factorize
f(Z)
.
∎

Let us derive a useful consequence.

7.1.7 Corollary

(Assembly of factorizable maps)
.

Suppose that
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k}
are factorizable, with target bundles
T_{1},\ldots,T_{k}
and set
Z=Z_{1}\oplus\cdots Z_{k}
.

(i)

Suppose
f\colon T_{1}\times\cdots\times T_{k}\to T^{\prime}
is a measurable, fiberwise polynomial map.
