Then
f(Z)
is also factorizable.

(ii)

Suppose
f\colon T_{1}\times\cdots\times T_{k}\setminus S\to T^{\prime}
is a measurable, fiberwise smooth map, where
S
is an equivariant measurable subset that restricts to a closed set in a.e. fiber, and such that
\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2})
is almost surely not in
S
.
Then the
f(Z)
is also factorizable.

(iii)

Suppose
F\subset T_{1}\times\cdots\times T_{k}
is a measurable subset that restricts to a smooth submanifold in a.e. fiber, and that furthermore almost surely
Z(q,q^{\prime},u,l)\in F\left(q_{1/2}\right)
.
Suppose also that
f\colon F\to T
is a measurable, fiberwise smooth function.
Then
f(Z)
is factorizable.

An instance of (i) in the above corollary is when
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
are valued in
T_{1}=\Hom(E,T^{\prime})
and
T_{2}=\Hom(T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime})
respectively, and
f(Z_{1},Z_{2})\in\Hom(E,E^{\prime\prime})
is their composition.

Proof.

Part (i) is an immediate consequence of
Proposition 7.1.6
.
For part (ii) we will use the estimates in
Proposition 7.1.5
.
Fix a desired degree of approximation
N
as in
Definition 7.1.1
for
f(Z)
.
Let also
V^{s},V^{u}
etc. be provided by the definition of factorizability of
Z
to degree
M
.

We restrict to a compact set
K
of almost full measure such that there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that
\dist\left(\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2}),S(q_{1/2})\right)\geq\varepsilon_{0}
.
For any given
D\in\mathbb{N}
, we can further choose
K
(still of almost full measure) to assume that the
C^{D}
-norm of
f
on the fibers over
q_{1/2}\in K
are bounded by some
c(D,K)
at
\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2})
on a ball of radius
\varepsilon_{0}
.
We can now Taylor-expand
f(Z)
along the fibers as

f(Z)=\sum_{|I|<D}a_{I}\left(\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2})\right)\left[\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2})-Z\right]^{I}+O_{D,K}\left(\left\|\widetilde{Z}(q_{1/2})-Z\right\|^{D}\right)

By
Proposition 7.1.5
we can arrange the last term to be
O\left(e^{-D\cdot\delta_{0}\ell}\right)
for some fixed
\delta_{0}
.
We can take
D
sufficiently large so that
D\cdot\delta_{0}>N
.
For the remaining polynomial expression in
Z
we can use part (i) to deduce that it is factorizable, and hence we can choose appropriate bundles to approximate it to degree
N
.

To prove the last part, we restrict in each fiber to a neighborhood of
F
, such that the fiberwise nearest-point projection
\pi_{F}
to
F
is smooth and well-defined.
Then claim then follows by applying the preceding part to
\widetilde{f}:=f\circ\pi_{F}
.
∎

7.2.
Some Factorizable Functions

7.2.1.
On normal forms and holonomies

Recall that the existence of holonomies is equivalent to the existence of trivializations of the bundle (smooth along stables, say) such that the transition maps, and the dynamics, are constant maps.
The property of a cocycle
E
to be smooth along stables is defined in
Definition E.2.5
(as well as
§D.3.2
), its holonomies
H^{-}_{\gr^{\bullet\leq}E}
(on the associated graded) is constructed in
Corollary E.2.20
, and the measurable connection
P^{-}_{E}
(for any admissible cocycle) is introduced in
§B.2.6
.
When the cocycle
E
is clear from context, we will write
H^{-},P^{-}
for the corresponding holonomy and measurable connection.

Factorizing holonomies is accomplished by the next result:

7.2.2 Proposition

(Sections of bundles with holonomy are factorizable)
.

Suppose that
E
is a cocycle smooth along stables, and equipped with a stable holonomy
H^{-}
which is itself smooth along stables.

(i)

If
s
is a measurable
g_{t}
-equivariant section of
E
then
H^{-}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},q_{1/2})\circ s(q_{1/2}^{\prime})\in E(q_{1/2})
is precisely factorizable.

(ii)

The map

H^{-}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},q_{1/2})\circ P^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})\in\End\left(E(q_{1/2})\right)

is also precisely factorizable.

The analogous statements along unstables hold.

Proof.

The equivalent definition of smooth holonomies is that there exist smooth local trivializations of the bundle along the stable manifolds such that the transition maps, and the dynamics, act by constant maps.
In other words, in the right choice of coordinates the maps
H^{-}
are the identity.

For the section
s
, observe that by the definition of holonomy, since
s(q_{1/2}^{\prime})=g_{\ell/2}s(q^{\prime})
we have that

H^{-}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},q_{1/2})\circ s(q_{1/2}^{\prime})=A(q,\ell/2)\circ H^{-}(q^{\prime},q)\circ s(q^{\prime})

where
A(q,\ell/2)
denotes the cocycle action on the bundle
E
from
q
to
q_{1/2}=g_{\ell/2}q
.
We can then take
F_{q}(q^{\prime}):=H^{-}(q^{\prime},q)\circ s(q^{\prime})
and
V^{s}=E
,
V^{u}
again trivial, and
\mathscr{A}(q_{1/2})
to be the identity map.

For the measurable connection
P^{-}
, recall that its definition is via the measurable isomorphism
\iota_{E}\colon\gr_{\bullet}E\to E
at the Oseledets-biregular points.
The bundle
\gr_{\bullet}E
carries a natural holonomy
H^{-}
(since the irreducible factors have a single Lyapunov exponent) and so does
E
, thus
\iota_{E}(q)\in\Hom\left(\gr_{\bullet}E,E\right)(q)
is an equivariant section and by part (i) it is factorizable.

The map that we explicitly want to factorize is (rewriting it
using
\iota_{E}
):

(7.2.3)

\displaystyle H^{-}_{E}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},q_{1/2})\circ P^{-}_{E}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})=\underbrace{H^{-}_{E}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},q_{1/2})\circ\iota_{E}(q_{1/2}^{\prime})\circ H^{-}_{\gr_{\bullet E}}\left(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime}\right)}_{H^{-}_{\Hom}\left(q_{1/2}^{\prime},q_{1/2}\right)\circ\iota_{E}(\widetilde{q^{\prime}})}\circ\iota_{E}^{-1}(q_{1/2})

where the underbraced expression is the transport of
\iota_{E}(\widetilde{q^{\prime}})
via the holonomy of the bundle
\Hom(\gr_{\bullet}E,E)
.
By part (i) this expression is factorizable, and since
\iota_{E}^{-1}(q_{1/2})
is also clearly factorizable, the desired conclusion follows from
Corollary 7.1.7
.
∎

Recall that
L_{q}^{\pm}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u/s}[q]\hookrightarrow L\mathcal{W}^{u/s}(q)
denotes the linearization maps from

Theorem D.1.2
.
We begin with a basic construction:

7.2.4 Proposition

(
y_{1/2}
and
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
are factorizable)
.

(i)

The point
L_{q_{1/2}}^{-}\left(q^{\prime}_{1/2}\right)\in L\mathcal{W}^{s}(q_{1/2})
is precisely factorizable.

(ii)

The point
L_{q_{1/2}}^{+}\left(y_{1/2}\right)\in L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{1/2})
is precisely factorizable.

Proof.

The discussion is the same for both
y_{1/2}
and
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
so we only treat the latter.
The cocycle
V^{s}
is taken to be
L\mathcal{W}^{s}
which has holonomies
P^{GM}_{L\mathcal{W}^{s}}
by construction and the measurable section
\iota_{s}(q)\in V^{s}(q)
such that
L_{q_{1/2}}^{-}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})=P^{GM}_{L\mathcal{W}^{s}}\left(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2}\right)\iota_{s}(q_{1/2}^{\prime})
.
The claim then follows directly from
Proposition 7.2.2
(i)
.
∎

We now combine the previous propositions with
Corollary 7.1.7
.
For the manifold
Q
, recall that we have a locally finite collection of charts
\chi_{i}
.
For a smooth and natural bundle
E
on
Q
, fix locally finite trivializing charts
\chi_{E,i}
which are compatible with the charts
\chi_{i}
, see
§E.1.3
.
On compact sets
K
, we can assume that only finitely many charts are used and there is a Lebesgue number of the charts, so all points that are sufficiently close for our considerations are in the same chart.

7.2.5.
Connection from smooth charts

Suppose that
E
is a smooth and natural cocycle, which is therefore equipped with a family of global charts
\chi_{E,i}
on open sets
U_{i}
.
Then, for every
x,y\in U_{i}
we obtain an identification

P^{\chi,i}_{E}(x,y)\colon E(x)\tilde{\longrightarrow}E(y)

which varies smoothly as
x,y
vary in
U_{i}
.

7.2.6 Proposition

(Factorizability in smooth global charts)
.

Suppose
E
is a smooth and natural cocycle.

(i)

Consider the trivial bundle
Q\times\mathbb{R}^{\dim Q}
and let
\chi_{i}
denote smooth charts on
Q
containing
q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2},y_{1/2}
.
Then the functions
\chi_{i}^{-1}(y_{1/2}),\chi_{i}^{-1}(y_{1/2})
and
\chi_{i}^{-1}(q_{1/2}^{\prime})
, viewed as valued in the trivial bundle
\mathbb{R}^{\dim Q}
, are factorizable.

(ii)

Suppose
s
is a
g_{t}
-equivariant measurable section of
E
.
Then with notation as in
§7.2.5
we have that

P_{E}^{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})s(q_{1/2})\in E\left(q_{1/2}\right)

is factorizable.

(iii)

Recall that
P^{-}_{E}
denotes the measurable connection of
E
, see
§B.2.6
.
Then the map

P_{E}^{\chi,i}\left(q_{1/2}^{\prime},q_{1/2}\right)\circ P^{-}_{E}\left(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime}\right)\in\End E\left(q_{1/2}\right)

is factorizable.

Note that in applying this proposition we loose precise factorizability and only have factorizability.

Proof.

For (i), note that
L_{q_{1/2}}^{-}\left(q^{\prime}_{1/2}\right)\in L\mathcal{W}^{s}(q_{1/2})
is factorizable by
Proposition 7.2.4
.
Then
Corollary 7.1.7
(iii)
applied to post-composition with the smooth maps
\chi_{i}^{-1}\circ\left(L_{q_{1/2}}^{-}\right)^{-1}\colon L\mathcal{W}^{s}(q_{1/2})\to\mathbb{R}^{\dim Q}
then gives the claim.
Here we denote by
\left(L_{q_{1/2}}^{-}\right)^{-1}\colon L_{q_{1/2}}^{-}\left(\mathcal{W}^{s}[q_{1/2}]\right)\to\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]
the inverse of the linearization map, restricted to its image; it is a smooth bijection.

The factorizability of
\chi_{i}^{-1}(y_{1/2})
and
\chi_{i}^{-1}\left(q_{1/2}\right)
is analogous.

Recall from
Corollary D.3.6
that associated to
E
there is a cocycle
L^{-}E
and equivariant linear embedding
L^{-}_{q}\colon E(q)\to L^{-}E(q)
, such that furthermore
L^{-}E
admits a holonomy denoted
P^{GM}_{L^{-}E}
; note that
E
is not invariant under this holonomy.

Let now
\Psi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\colon E(q_{1/2})\to L^{-}E(q_{1/2})
be given
by

\Psi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}=P^{GM}_{L^{-}E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ L^{-}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\circ P_{E}^{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2}).

Then
\Psi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}
is a smooth function of
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
. Therefore,
the factorizability of

\Psi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}
follows from combining
(i)
with

Corollary 7.1.7
(iii)
. Let

\Phi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}:L^{-}E(q_{1/2})\to E(q_{1/2})

denote
\Psi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}^{-1}
precomposed with the
nearest point projection from
L^{-}E(q_{1/2})
to the image of

\Psi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}
. Then,
\Phi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}
is also factorizable by

Corollary 7.1.7
.
Now,
(ii)

follows since

P_{E}^{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ s(q^{\prime}_{1/2})=\Phi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\circ P^{GM}_{L^{-}E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ(L^{-}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\circ s)(q^{\prime}_{1/2})

and
P^{GM}_{L^{-}E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ(L^{-}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\circ s)(q^{\prime}_{1/2})

is factorizable by

Proposition 7.2.2
.
Also,
(iii)

follows since

P_{E}^{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ P^{-}_{E}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})=\\
=P_{E}^{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ(L^{-}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}})^{-1}\circ P^{-}_{L^{-}E}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\circ L^{-}_{q_{1/2}}=\\
=\Phi_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\circ(P^{GM}_{L^{-}E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ P^{-}_{L^{-}E}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2}))\circ L^{-}_{q_{1/2}},

the expression in parenthesis is factorizable by

Proposition 7.2.2
, and

L^{-}_{q_{1/2}}
is available as part of the assembly map.

∎

7.2.7 Remark

(Factorizability of equivariant subspaces)
.

Suppose that
E
is smooth and natural, and
V\subset E
is a measurable
g_{t}
-equivariant subbundle.

Note that
V(q^{\prime}_{1/2})=P_{E}^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})V(q_{1/2})
, and

V(q_{1/2})
is available in the assembly map, while

P_{E}^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})
is factorizable in the sense of

Proposition 7.2.6
(iii)
.
We will thus say that
P^{\chi,i}_{E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})V\left(q^{\prime}_{1/2}\right)
is factorizable, although we have not formally defined factorizability of a subspace.

The construction and factorizability of
V(y_{1/2})
is analogous.

7.2.8.
Jets of manifolds and ideals of functions

Suppose
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
are two germs of smooth maps, with
\phi_{i}(\mathbf{0})=\mathbf{0}
, and
\rk D\phi_{i}(\mathbf{0})=d\leq n
.
We will say that they have
image tangent to order
k
if there exists a germ of diffeomorphism
\chi
of
(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbf{0})
such that
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}\circ\chi
have the same Taylor expansion up to order
k
.
We let
\mathcal{M}_{k}
denote such an equivalence class.
Let also
\mathcal{T}_{k}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbf{0})
denote the finite-dimensional ring of Taylor coefficients of functions at
\mathbf{0}
up to order
k
.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{k}
determines an ideal
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{M}_{k}\subset\mathcal{T}_{k}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbf{0})
given by those functions that vanish up to order
k
when pulled back by some representative
\phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
from
\mathcal{M}_{k}
.

Let us explain how to recover
\mathcal{M}_{k}
from
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{M}_{k}
(assuming
k\geq 1
):

7.2.9 Proposition

(Taylor coefficients from ideals of functions)
.

Suppose that we have the decomposition
\mathbb{R}^{n}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-d}
and we have a germ of manifold
\mathcal{M}_{k}
such that the tangent space of
\mathcal{M}_{k}
projects isomorphically to the
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-factor.

(i)

Then there exists a unique degree
k
polynomial map
\phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-d}
in the equivalence class determining
\mathcal{M}_{k}
, such that
\phi(\mathbf{x})=(\mathbf{x},\psi(\mathbf{x}))
with
\psi\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{n-d}
.

(ii)

Furthermore, if we fix a basis of equations
\xi_{1},\dots\xi_{n-d}
cutting out the tangent space of
\mathcal{M}_{k}
, as well as lifts
\widetilde{\xi}_{i}\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{M}_{k}
, then these lifts generate the ideal
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{M}_{k}
and the Taylor coefficients of
\psi
are rational functions of the coefficients of
\widetilde{\xi}_{i}
.

Proof.

The first claim is an immediate consequence of the constant rank theorem, which in this case requires only a finite-dimensional calculation.
Let therefore
\psi=(\psi_{1},\dots\psi_{n-d})
be the corresponding function such that
\mathcal{M}_{k}
is its graph.

For the second claim, let
\mathbf{y}=(y_{1},\dots y_{n-d})
be coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{n-d}
.
Then one set of generators for the ideal is
y_{i}-\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
and the second claim for them is clear.
For any other set of generators
\widetilde{\xi}_{i}
, there exist uniquely determined polynomials
p_{i}^{j}
such that
y_{i}-\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x})\equiv\sum p_{i}^{j}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\widetilde{\xi}_{j}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
up to order
k
.
Note that the constant terms of
p_{i}^{j}
are a linear matrix, necessarily inverse to the first order term matrix in
\mathbf{y}
of the
\widetilde{\xi}_{j}
.
Subsequent coefficients of
p_{i}^{j}
amount to solving further linear equations in the coefficients of
\widetilde{\xi}_{j}
, guaranteed to have solutions since
\widetilde{\xi}_{j}
are generators.
∎

In our intended applications, given a basis
\xi_{i}
of linear functions cutting out the tangent space, we will take the unique lifts
\widetilde{\xi}_{i}
that minimize the natural background norm on the space of
k
-jets of functions.

7.2.10.
Factorizing Taylor Coefficients

Let
\mathcal{J}^{k}Q
denote the smooth and natural cocycle of
k
-jets of
real-valued smooth functions, and furthermore the fibers have a ring
structure. We fix locally trivializing charts
\chi_{\mathcal{J}^{k}Q,i}
for

\mathcal{J}^{k}Q
which are compatible with the charts
\chi_{i}
, see
§E.1.3
.
Let
\mathcal{I}^{u}\mathcal{J}^{k}Q\subset\mathcal{J}^{k}Q
be the subcocycle of functions that
vanish to order
k
along the unstable manifolds. This is an
ideal for the ring structure and uniquely determines the
k
-jet of
the unstable manifold.

Similarly, let
\mathcal{I}^{cs}\mathcal{J}^{k}Q\subset\mathcal{J}^{k}Q
denote the ideal of
functions that vanish to order
k
along the center-stable
manifold. (The center stable manifold may not be well-defined, but
it’s
k
-jet, hence the cocycle
\mathcal{I}^{cs}\mathcal{J}^{k}Q
is well-defined, see

Proposition A.4.4

(ii)
.)

Now suppose
E
is a smooth and natural cocycle. We fix locally
trivializing charts
\chi_{E,i}
for
E
which are
compatible with the charts
\chi_{i}
.
Let
E^{\geq\bullet}
denote the unstable Lyapunov
flag of
E
.
Then, for almost all
q
,
E^{\geq\bullet}(q)
is a
well-defined point in the flag variety
\mathcal{F}^{\geq\bullet}E(q)
.
Similarly,
if
E^{\leq\bullet}
denotes the stable Lyapunov flag of
E
, then for
almost all
q
,
E^{\leq\bullet}(q)
is a
well-defined point in the flag variety
\mathcal{F}^{\leq\bullet}E(q)
.
Also,
for
z\in\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y_{1/2}]
(not necessarily forwards regular), we
denote by
E_{y_{1/2}}^{\leq\bullet}(z)
the forwards flag defined in

§A.4.6
.

7.2.11.
Trivializing maps to bundles, along submanifolds

Suppose
E\to Q
is a smooth and natural cocycle.
Let
\mathcal{F}_{E}\to Q
be an associated bundle with fiber the set of flags in
E
of fixed dimensions (omitted from the notation), e.g. the Grassmannian of
k
-planes for some
k
.
Using the charts
\chi_{i}
we will trivialize the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{E}
with the maps
P^{\chi,i}_{E}(x,q_{1/2})
to identify each fiber with the fiber at
q_{1/2}
, for
x
in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
q_{1/2}
.
Next, we assume given at
q_{1/2}
a flag
Z(q_{1/2})\in\mathcal{F}_{E}(q_{1/2})
.
Then, using an orthogonal decomposition to turn the flag
Z
into a decomposition
E(q_{1/2})=\oplus Z_{i}(q_{1/2})
, we introduce the (standard) charts on
\mathcal{F}_{E}(q_{1/2})
near
Z(q_{1/2})
into the vector space
\oplus_{j>i}\Hom\left(Z_{i},Z_{j}\right)
.

Suppose now that we have a smooth section
\psi\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{F}_{E}
over an unstable manifold.
Using the trivialization
\mathcal{F}_{E}|_{U_{i}}\cong U_{i}\times\mathcal{F}_{E}(q_{1/2})
and the previous charts, we obtain a function
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\xrightarrow{\psi_{Z}}\oplus_{j>i}\Hom(Z_{i},Z_{j})
.
Again in the charts
\chi_{i}
starting from the decomposition
T_{q_{1/2}}Q=W^{u}(q_{1/2})\oplus W^{cs}(q_{1/2})
, let
W^{\bullet}_{\chi,i}(q_{1/2})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the affine linear subspaces that pass through
\chi_{i}(q_{1/2})
and are tangent to the respective unstable/center-stable submanifolds.
Then we know that
\chi_{i}\left(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\right)
is the graph of a smooth function

(7.2.12)

\displaystyle\eta^{u}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\colon W^{u}_{\chi,i}(q_{1/2})\to W^{cs}_{\chi,i}(q_{1/2})

Extend now
\psi
to a scalar-valued function
\psi_{\chi,i}\colon\chi_{i}(U_{i})\to\oplus_{j>i}\Hom(Z_{i},Z_{j})
defined on all of
\chi_{i}(U_{i})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
where

(7.2.13)

\displaystyle\psi_{\chi,i}=\psi_{Z}\circ\chi_{i}^{-1}\circ({\bm{1}},\eta^{u}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}})\circ\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{u}

where
\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{u}
is the projection in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
to
\mathcal{W}^{u}_{\chi,i}(q_{1/2})
along
\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{\chi,i}(q_{1/2})
.

We will make use of analogous constructions along the center-stable manifold with
y_{1/2}
instead of
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
.
Here is a first application:

7.2.14 Proposition

(Factorizing the unstable along the stable)
.

The subspaces

P^{\chi,i}_{\mathcal{J}^{k}Q}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\mathcal{I}^{u}\mathcal{J}^{k}Q(q^{\prime}_{1/2})\text{ and }P^{\chi,i}_{\mathcal{J}^{k}Q}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})\mathcal{I}^{cs}\mathcal{J}^{k}Q(y_{1/2})

are factorizable in the sense of
Remark 7.2.7
.

Furthermore, for any order
k
the Taylor coefficients of
\eta^{u}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}
as defined in
Eqn. (7.2.12)
are factorizable, and the same holds for its analogue
\eta^{cs}_{y_{1/2}}
.

Proof.

The fist assertion is an immediate consequence of

Proposition 7.2.6

(iii)
.
The second one follows from the first, combined with
Proposition 7.2.9
.
∎

7.2.15.
Realized
C^{\infty}
jets of center-stable manifolds and flags of cocycles

For the next statement, we recall and will use the notation from the appendix
§A.4.6
.

7.2.16 Proposition

(Factorizing the jet of the unstable flag)
.

Let
E\to Q
be a smooth and natural cocycle.

(i)

Let
S\subset E
be a backwards dynamically defined subbundle.
Let
\Gr E
denote the Grassmannian of
E
, and let
\psi^{S}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\to\Gr E
denote the map taking
z\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
to
S(z)
.
Let
\psi^{S}_{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
be the associated (vector-space valued) map constructed in
§7.2.11
, using the ambient charts.
Then, for any order
k
the Taylor coefficients at
\chi_{i}(q_{1/2}^{\prime})
of
\psi^{S}_{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
are factorizable.

(ii)

Let
\psi^{u}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{F}^{\geq\bullet}E
denote the map taking
z\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
to the Oseledets flag
E^{\geq\bullet}(z)
.
Let
\psi^{u}_{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
be the associated map constructed in
§7.2.11
.
Then, for any order
k
the Taylor coefficients at
\chi_{i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
of
\psi^{u}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}
are factorizable.

(iii)

Let
\psi^{cs}(y_{1/2})\colon\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{F}^{\leq\bullet}E
denote the map taking
z\in\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y_{1/2}]
to the flag

E_{y_{1/2}}^{\leq\bullet}(z)
.
Let
\psi^{cs}_{\chi,i}(y_{1/2})
be the associated map constructed in
§7.2.11
.
Then, for any order
k
, the Taylor coefficients at
\chi_{i}(y_{1/2})
of
\psi^{cs}_{\chi,i}(y_{1/2})
is factorizable.

Proof.

Note that since the components
E^{\geq\lambda_{i}}
of the flag
E^{\geq\bullet}

are backwards dynamically defined, (ii) follows immediately from (i).

Let now
\mathcal{J}^{k}E
denote the smooth and natural cocycle of
k
-jets of
real-valued smooth functions on the total space of the bundle
E
.
(This is naturally a cocycle over the total space of
E
, but we
restrict it to a cocycle over
Q
which is the zero section of the
total space of
E
).

For the proof of (i), let

I^{S}(q)=\{(x,v)\;\>:\;\>x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q],v\in S(x)\}.

Then
I^{S}(q)
is a smooth (immersed) submanifold of the total space of
E
.
Let
I^{S}\mathcal{J}^{k}E(q)\subset\mathcal{J}^{k}E(q)
denote the ideal of jets of functions which vanish to order
k
on
I^{S}(q)
.
Then,
I^{S}\mathcal{J}^{k}E
is a measurable equivariant subbundle of
\mathcal{J}^{k}E
and thus by
Proposition 7.2.6

(iii)
,
P^{\chi,i}_{\mathcal{J}^{k}E}(I^{S}\mathcal{J}^{k}E(q^{\prime}_{1/2}))
is factorizable.
Next, by
Proposition 7.2.9
the ideal
I^{S}\mathcal{J}^{k^{\prime}}E(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
determines the Taylor coefficients of
\psi^{S}
at
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
.
Using that all the other functions need to pass from
\psi^{S}
to
\psi^{S}_{\chi,i}
appearing in
Eqn. (7.2.13)
are defined at
q_{1/2}
, combined with
Corollary 7.1.7
, yields the claim.

The proof of (iii) is virtually identical using the equivariance of
k
-jets in
Proposition A.4.4
.
∎

Recall from
§B.2.6
that associated to the unstable filtration of a smooth cocycle we have the associated graded bundle
\gr_{\bullet}^{u}E
which is smooth along unstables and admits a smooth unstable holonomy
H^{+}
.
Using a chart
\chi_{i}
for
E
that identifies each fiber with some
\mathbb{R}^{m_{E}}
, we have an identification
\gr^{u}_{\bullet}E\to E\to\mathbb{R}^{m_{E}}
by using the orthogonal complement with respect to the standard Euclidean structure on
\mathbb{R}^{m_{E}}
to split the filtration defining
\gr^{u}_{\bullet}E
, followed by the trivializing chart of
E
.
Let
H^{+}_{\chi,i}(x,y)\colon\mathbb{R}^{m_{E}}\to\mathbb{R}^{m_{E}}
be the conjugation of the holonomy map by these trivializing charts, with
y\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
.

7.2.17 Proposition

(Factorizing the jet of holonomy of the associated graded)
.

Recall from
§7.2.11
that
\bar{\pi}_{u}^{cs}
is the projection in the chart
\chi_{i}
along the (linear) center-stable manifold to the unstable, and
\left({\bm{1}},\eta^{u}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\right)
is the map which gives the unstable through
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
in the same chart.

Then for any
k
the Taylor coefficients of order
k
of

H^{+}_{\chi,i}\left(q^{\prime}_{1/2},\chi_{i}^{-1}\circ({\bm{1}},\eta^{u}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}})\circ\bar{\pi}^{cs}_{u}(-)\right)\colon\mathbb{R}^{\dim Q}\to\End(\mathbb{R}^{m_{E}})

are factorizable.

Proof.

We proceed analogously to the proof of
Proposition 7.2.16
.
Namely, let

\displaystyle IH^{+}(q):=

\displaystyle\{(x,H)\colon x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q],H\in\Hom\left(E(q),E(x)\right)\text{ such that }

\displaystyle H\left(E^{\geq\bullet}(q)\right)=E^{\geq\bullet}(x)\text{ and }gr^{u}_{\bullet}H=H^{+}(x,q)\}

where
\gr^{u}_{\bullet}H
denotes the map induced by
H
on the associated graded.
Then
IH^{+}(q)
is a smooth submanifold of the linear bundle
\Hom(E(q),E)
where we view
E(q)
as a fixed vector space, and so the fiber above
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q]
is
\Hom(E(q),E(x))
.
The intersection of
IH^{+}(q)
with a fiber is naturally a smooth algebraic subset (acted upon freely and transitively by the group of linear transformations that act as the identity on the associated graded).

To obtain the relevant cocycle on our space
Q
, consider first
Q\times Q
, with the cocycle
\Hom(E_{1},E_{2})
where
E_{i}
corresponds to the pullback of
E
under projection to the
i
-th factor.
Then we have the cocycle of jets
\mathcal{J}^{k}\Hom(E_{1},E_{2})
, which we can restrict to the diagonal
Q\hookrightarrow Q\times Q
and denote by
\mathcal{J}^{k}_{\Delta}\Hom(E_{1},E_{2})
.
Then
IH^{+}(q)
determines an ideal of functions
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{H}^{+}(q)\subset\mathcal{J}^{k}_{\Delta}\Hom(E_{1},E_{2})
vanishing on it.

It now follows from
Proposition 7.2.9
that the Taylor coefficients of the desired function are factorizable.
∎

7.2.18 Remark

(Factorizing holonomy)
.

Suppose that
E
is a bdd cocycle, smooth along unstables, and equipped with a holonomy map
H^{+}_{E}
.
Then the analogous
H^{+}_{\chi,i}
from
Proposition 7.2.17
is factorizable, with the same proof.
We will apply this remark to
E=L\mathcal{W}^{u}
.

7.3.
More factorizable functions

7.3.1.
Setup

In this section, we will continue to work extensively with the charts

\chi_{i}\colon Q\to\mathbb{R}^{\dim Q}
and assume that all points with subscript

{1/2}
are in a single such chart.

Recall that center-stable manifolds are defined in

§A.4.6
.

7.3.2 Proposition

(
z_{1/2}
is factorizable)
.

The point

\chi_{i}\left(\mathcal{W}^{cs}_{loc}\left[y_{1/2}\right]\bigcap{\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}\left[q_{1/2}^{\prime}\right]}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{\dim Q}\text{ see \autoref{fig:halfway_diagram}}

is factorizable.

Proof.

For
q_{1/2}
, we can apply a smooth coordinate transformation
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1/2})
after
\chi_{i}
so as to arrange that, with coordinates
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
:

•

q_{1/2}
lands at
(0,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

•

The center-stable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{cs}[q_{1/2}]
becomes
y=0
.

•

The unstable manifold
\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}]
becomes
x=0
.

We will write
\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\tilde{y}_{1/2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
for the images of the corresponding points on the coordinate planes.
The coordinates of these points are factorizable, by
Proposition 7.2.6
.

In these coordinates, the unstable manifold at
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
and center-stable manifold at
y_{1/2}
can be written using smooth functions
\xi^{u}_{\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime}}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\xi^{cs}_{\tilde{y}_{1/2}}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
as

\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}^{\prime}]=\text{image of}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime}+\xi^{u}_{\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime}}(y)\\
y\end{bmatrix}\quad\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y_{1/2}]=\text{image of}\begin{bmatrix}x\\
\tilde{y}_{1/2}+\xi^{cs}_{\tilde{y}_{1/2}}(x)\end{bmatrix}

In view of

Proposition 7.2.14
,
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the Taylor coefficients of

\xi^{\bullet}_{\bullet}
up to degree
k
are
factorizable.
Also, there exist
c_{k}
and
\delta_{k}>0

such that the
C^{k}
-norm of these functions are bounded by

c_{k}e^{-\delta_{k}\ell}
, both in their dependence on
x/y
and

q_{1/2}^{\prime}/y_{1/2}
.

To find the point of intersection via the implicit function theorem, we can write

F\left(x,y,\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{1/2}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime}-x+\xi^{u}_{\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime}}(y)\\
y-\tilde{y}_{1/2}-\xi^{cs}_{\tilde{y}_{1/2}}(x)\end{bmatrix}

The differential of this function is

DF=\begin{bmatrix}-1&O(e^{-\delta_{k}\ell})&1+O(e^{-\delta_{k}\ell})&0\\
O(e^{-\delta_{k}\ell})&1&0&1+O(e^{-\delta_{k}\ell})\end{bmatrix}

Note that
F(0,0,0,0)=(0,0)
and the first block is invertible, so we can write
F^{-1}(0,0)
as

(x,y)=G_{0}(\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{1/2})=\left(\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{1/2}\right)+G_{1}(\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{1/2})

where furthermore we have an estimate
\left\|D^{k}G\right\|\leq O\left(e^{-\delta_{k}l}\right)
in the range where
q_{1/2}^{\prime}
and
y_{1/2}
are themselves
O\left(e^{-\delta_{0}\ell}\right)
.

By successive applications of
Corollary 7.1.7
, it follows that the point
\tilde{z}_{1/2}=(\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{1/2})+G_{1}(\tilde{q}_{1/2}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{1/2})
is itself factorizable.
∎

7.3.3.
Trivializing bdd bundles

Suppose
L
is a bdd cocycle as defined in
Definition E.1.9
, with
E
an associated smooth and natural cocycle, and two bdd subcocycles
S_{1}\subset S_{2}\subset E
such that
L\cong S_{2}/S_{1}
.
Suppose
\chi_{E,i}
are charts trivializing
E
.
Denote by
L_{\chi,i}\subset\mathbb{R}^{\dim E}
the subspace obtained by taking the orthogonal complement of
\chi_{E,i}(S_{1})
inside
\chi_{E,i}(S_{2})
, with respect to the standard Euclidean inner product.

More generally, given a filtration
L^{\geq\bullet}
of
L
by bdd cocycles, we again let
\gr^{k}_{\chi,i}L
denote the orthogonal complement of
\chi_{E,i}\left(S_{1}+L^{\geq k-1}\right)
inside
\chi_{E,i}\left(S_{1}+L^{\geq k}\right)
.
In the case when the filtration is the Oseledets filtration of
L
, using the splitting provided by this construction, we denote by
H^{+}_{\chi,i}
the holonomy map with respect to the decomposition
L_{\chi,i}\cong\oplus_{k}\gr^{k}_{\chi,i}L
.

Recall now that
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
is a subquotient of the smooth and natural cocycle

E
and is itself bdd, by
Proposition E.3.6
.
In what follows, we are fixing a trivialization of the bundle
E
,
and assuming that all the points with subscript
1/2
belong to a
single chart.

7.3.4 Lemma
.

The backwards Lyapunov filtration
(L\mathcal{W}^{u})^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
is a bdd cocycle, and in the notation of
§7.3.3
let the subspace
\left(L\mathcal{W}^{u}\right)^{\geq\bullet}_{\chi,i}(z_{1/2})
is factorizable.

Furthermore, the holonomy on the associated graded

H^{+}_{\chi,i}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2}):\gr^{u}_{\chi,i}L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})\to\gr^{u}_{\chi,i}L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})
is factorizable.

Proof.

The cocycle
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
is backward dynamically defined (bdd) by
Proposition E.3.6
and thus there exists a smooth and natural cocycle
E
and equivariant subbundles
S_{2}
and
S_{1}
of
E
, smooth along
unstables, such that
L\mathcal{W}^{u}=S_{2}/S_{1}
.
We will work exclusively in the trivialization
\chi_{E,i}
of
E
and omit it from the notation below, as well as from the subscripts for the objects, but these are always implicitly present.

The unstable flag
E^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
is smooth as a
function of
z_{1/2}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
. We can then approximate

E^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
using a Taylor series based at
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
.
In view of
Proposition 7.2.16
,
the Taylor coefficients of this Taylor series are factorizable.
Thus, the flag
E^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
is factorizable.

Since
S_{2}
and
S_{1}
are bdd and hence also smooth as a function of
z_{1/2}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
, we can also approximate them using a Taylor series
based at
q^{\prime}_{1/2}
. In view of

Proposition 7.2.16
, the Taylor
coefficients of these Taylor series are also factorizable. Therefore,
we can factorize
S_{2}(z_{1/2})
and
S_{1}(z_{1/2})
. Since the desired flag

(L\mathcal{W}^{u})^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
can be recovered from
E^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
,

S_{2}(z_{1/2})
and
S_{1}(z_{1/2})
, we have shown the factorizability
of
(L\mathcal{W}^{u})^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
.

Let
H^{+}_{E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2}):\gr^{u}E(q^{\prime}_{1/2})\to\gr^{u}E(z_{1/2})
denote the
holonomy of
E
on the associated graded
\gr^{u}E
. Then,
H^{+}_{E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
is smooth as a function of
z_{1/2}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
. We approximate it using a Taylor series based at

q^{\prime}_{1/2}
. By

Proposition 7.2.17
, the
coefficients of this Taylor series are factorizable. Therefore

H^{+}_{E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
is factorizable.

Since at every bireguar point
q
,

S_{2}(q)=\oplus_{i}S_{2}(q)\cap E^{\lambda_{i}}(q)
, and
S_{2}
is
backwards dynamically defined, we have

S_{2}^{\geq\lambda_{i}}=S_{2}\cap E^{\geq\lambda_{i}}
. Hence,

\gr^{u}S_{2}\equiv\bigoplus_{i}S_{2}^{\geq\lambda_{i}}/S_{2}^{\geq\lambda_{i+1}}=\bigoplus_{i}(S_{2}\cap E^{\geq\lambda_{i}})/(S_{2}\cap E^{\geq\lambda_{i+1}}).

Let
h_{2}:\gr^{u}S_{2}\to\gr^{u}E
denote
\bigoplus_{i}h_{2,i}
, where

h_{2,i}:(S_{2}\cap E^{\geq\lambda_{i}})/(S_{2}\cap E^{\geq\lambda_{i+1}})\to E^{\geq\lambda_{i}}/E^{\geq\lambda_{i+1}}

is defined by
h_{2,i}(v)=v+E^{\geq\lambda_{i+1}}
.
Then,
h_{2}
is an injective
equivariant map. Let
h_{1}:\gr^{u}S_{1}\to\gr^{u}S_{2}

be the analoguous map for
S_{1}
. Note that
\gr^{u}L\mathcal{W}^{u}=\gr^{u}(S_{2}/S_{1})
is
naturally isomorphic to
\gr^{u}S_{2}/h_{1}(S_{1})
, and also that
h_{2}

induces an isomorphism between
\gr^{u}S_{2}/h_{1}(S_{1})
and
h_{2}(S_{2})/(h_{2}\circ h_{1})(S_{1})
. Let
S_{2}^{\prime}=h_{2}(S_{2})
,
S_{1}^{\prime}=(h_{2}\circ h_{1})(S_{1})
. Then,
S_{1}^{\prime}\subset S_{2}^{\prime}\subset\gr^{u}E
, and we have an
equivariant map
j_{q}:\gr^{u}L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)\to\gr^{u}E(q)
.

By the Ledrappier
invariance principle,
S_{2}^{\prime}
and
S_{1}^{\prime}
are invariant by

H^{+}_{E}
.
Therefore,
j_{x}^{-1}\circ H^{+}_{E}(q,x)\circ j_{q}:L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(x)
is a smooth holonomy map.
Since such maps are unique (see
[
KS13
, Prop. 4.2(c)]
for the case of a single Lyapunov block, and we are working on a direct sum of such blocks), this map has to coincide with the
standard holonomy

H^{+}(q,x)
. Thus,
H^{+}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
, which is the holonomy on the associated graded of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}=S_{2}/S_{1}
, can be recovered from
H^{+}_{E}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
,

S_{2}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
,
S_{1}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
,
S_{2}(z_{1/2})
and
S_{1}(z_{1/2})

and the flags
E^{\geq\bullet}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})
,
E^{\geq\bullet}(z_{1/2})
. Since all of these
quantities were shown to be factorizable,
H^{+}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
is also factorizable.
∎

7.3.5 Proposition

(Factorizability of realized flags)
.

The forward flag
\chi_{E,i}\left(E^{\leq\bullet}_{y_{1/2}}(z_{1/2})\right)
as defined in

§A.4.6
is factorizable.

Proof.

The forward flag
E^{\leq\bullet}_{y_{1/2}}(z_{1/2})
is smooth as a
function of
z_{1/2}\in W^{cs}[y_{1/2}]
.
We can then approximate

\chi_{E,i}\left(E^{\leq\bullet}_{y_{1/2}}(z_{1/2})\right)
using a Taylor series based at
\chi_{i}(y_{1/2})
.
In view of
Proposition 7.2.16
,
the Taylor coefficients of this Taylor series are factorizable.
Thus, the flag
\chi_{E,i}(E^{\leq\bullet}_{y_{1/2}}(z_{1/2}))
is factorizable.
∎

7.3.6 Corollary
.

The flags
\chi_{E,i}\left(\tilde{F}\right)
and
\chi_{E,i}\left(\tilde{F}^{sr}\right)
of
Proposition 6.2.6
are
factorizable.

Proof.

The factorizability of
\tilde{F}
follows immediately from

Proposition 7.3.5
, the fact that

\tilde{F}\in\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\ell}E(z_{1/2})
and

Proposition 6.2.5
.
The factorizability of
\tilde{F}^{sr}
follows from the
factorizability of
\tilde{F}
and of the subspaces
S_{1}(z_{1/2})
,

S_{2}(z_{1/2})
(established in the course of the proof of

Lemma 7.3.4
).
∎

7.3.7 Lemma
.

The map
P^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2}):L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q^{\prime}_{1/2})\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})
of

Theorem 6.2.7
, after conjugation by
\chi_{E,i}
is factorizable.

Proof.

In view of
Corollary 7.3.6
and

Lemma 7.3.4
the decomposition

L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})=\oplus L\mathcal{W}^{u}(z_{1/2})^{\lambda_{i}}

of
Theorem 6.2.7

is factorizable, after applying
\chi_{E,i}
, is factorizable.
Also, in view of
Lemma 7.3.4
,
the holonomy maps from

q^{\prime}_{1/2}
to
z_{1/2}
on the associated graded are factorizable.
Now the assertion of the lemma follows from

Corollary 7.1.7
.
∎

7.4.
Factorizability of the interpolation map.

7.4.1.

Recall that we defined in
§6.2.9
the interpolation map

\widetilde{\phi}\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]

For the next construction, we use the (canonical) identifications
\mathcal{W}^{u}[y_{1/2}]=\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}]
and
\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]=\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
.

Define then
Z_{1/2}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)
to be the element in
L\mathcal{C}(q_{1/2})
defined by

(7.4.2)

\displaystyle Z_{1/2}\left(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell\right)=L\left(\tilde{\phi}^{-1}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\right)\right).

In this formula
L
refers to the linearization map

\mathcal{C}[q_{1/2}]\to L\mathcal{C}[q_{1/2}]
.
For the next statement, we equip
L\mathcal{C}
with the norm
\|\cdot\|
constructed in
Proposition B.4.1
.

7.4.3 Theorem

(Half-time factorizability of 3-variable map)
.

The function
Z_{1/2}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
is half-time factorizable in the sense of
Definition 7.1.1
.
Therefore, for every
N\in\mathbb{N},\delta>0
, there exists a compact set
K
of measure at least
1-\delta
, a cocycle
V^{s}
, a
g_{t}
-equivariant embedding
F_{q}^{s}\colon\mathcal{W}^{s}[q]\hookrightarrow V^{s}(q)
and a linear map

\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)\colon V^{s}(q)\to L\mathcal{C}(q_{1/2})
, such that provided
q,q^{\prime},q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in K
we have for any
q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}^{-}_{0}[q]\cap K
that:

(7.4.4)

\left\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})-L\left(\tilde{\phi}^{-1}(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}])\right)\right\|\leq e^{-N\ell}.

Furthermore, we can choose
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
so that

(7.4.5)

\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)F^{s}_{q}(q)\in L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}(q_{1/2}),

where
L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}
is the tautological subspace defined in
§
5.1.8
.

In the above statement, we absorbed the cocycle
V^{u}\left(g_{-\ell/2}uq\right)
and its equivariant section into the map
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)
.

Proof.

Recall from
§6.2.9
that
\tilde{\phi}=\wp^{+}_{fake}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})\circ\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\circ\wp^{+}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})
.
Let

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=\wp^{+}(q_{1/2},y_{1/2})\circ\wp^{-}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ\wp^{+}_{fake}(z_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\circ{\bm{1}}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})\circ\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}\circ{\bm{1}}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})\circ\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{1/2}]

where
{\bm{1}}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})\colon\mathcal{W}^{u}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[z_{1/2}]
is the tautological identification.
Then we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{-1}(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}])=h\left(U^{+}[q_{1/2}]\right)

since
\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})(U^{+}[q_{1/2}])=U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]
.

Let
H:L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{1/2})\to L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{1/2})
denote
L_{q_{1/2}}\circ h\circ L_{q_{1/2}}^{-1}
. Recall that for
a,b\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[c]
,

L_{b}\circ\wp^{+}(a,b)\circ L_{a}^{-1}=P^{+}(a,b),

and

L_{b}\circ L_{a}^{-1}=P^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}_{GM}(a,b).

Then we can rewrite the linearization of
h
as:

H=P_{GM}^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})\circ P^{+}(q_{1/2},y_{1/2})\circ P^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\circ P^{*}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})\circ P^{-}(q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2}),

where
P^{*}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})=P^{+}_{fake}(z_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})P_{GM}^{L\mathcal{W}^{u}}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{1/2})
.
We have that
P^{*}(q^{\prime}_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2})
is
factorizable by
Remark 7.2.18
and
Lemma 7.3.7
. Therefore
H
is factorizable by

Proposition 7.2.6
(iii)
and

Lemma 7.3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{G}(x)
denote the image of

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])
under the linearization map
L_{x}
. Then
\mathcal{G}(x)

is a subgroup of the general linear group of
L\mathcal{W}^{u}(x)
. Let
\Psi_{x}:\mathcal{G}(x)\to L\mathcal{C}(x)
be the unique map such that
\Psi_{x}(L_{x}f)=L(fU^{+}[x])
, for any
f\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])
.
Then,

Z_{1/2}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)=\Psi_{q_{1/2}}(H).

The map
\Psi_{q_{1/2}}
is clearly factorizable (as it only depends
on data at
q_{1/2}
). Therefore, since
H
is factorizable, and in
view of
Corollary 7.1.7
,
Z
is
factorizable.

Therefore there exist
F^{s}
,
F^{u}
,
\mathscr{A}
such that
for any
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
of measure at least

1-\delta
, constants
\ell_{0}=\ell_{0}(K,N)
and
C=C(K,N)
, such that
provided
q,q^{\prime},q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime},y_{1/2},q_{1},uq_{1}
all belong to
K
,
and
\ell>\ell_{0}
, we have:

(7.4.6)

\left\|Z_{1/2}(q,q^{\prime},y,\ell)-{\mathscr{A}}(q_{1/2})(g_{\ell/2}F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime}),g_{-\ell/2}F_{q_{1}}^{u}(uq_{1}))\right\|\leq Ce^{-N\ell}

Note that
Z_{1/2}(q,q,u,\ell)\in L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}(q_{1/2})
. Therefore, there
exists
v_{0}\in L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}(q_{1/2})
such that

\|\mathscr{A}(q_{1/2})(g_{\ell/2}F_{q}^{s}(q),g_{-\ell/2}F_{q_{1}}^{u}(uq_{1}))-v_{0}\|\leq e^{-N\ell}.

We may find a linear map
L_{0}:L\mathcal{C}(q_{1/2})\to L\mathcal{C}(q_{1/2})
with
\|L_{0}-I\|=O(e^{-N\ell})
such that

L_{0}\circ\mathscr{A}(q_{1/2})(g_{\ell/2}F_{q}^{s}(q),g_{-\ell/2}F_{q_{1}}^{u}(uq_{1}))=v_{0}.

Then, (
7.4.6
) still holds with
\mathscr{A}

replaced by
L_{0}\circ\mathscr{A}
. We now define

\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)\colon V^{s}(q)\to L\mathcal{C}(q_{1/2})

as

\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)v=L_{0}\circ\mathscr{A}(q_{1/2})\left(g_{\ell/2}(v),g_{-\ell/2}F^{u}_{q}(uq_{1})\right)

for
v\in V^{s}(q)
where
u_{*}
is as in
Definition 5.2.2
.
Then
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
is a linear
map. The estimate (
7.4.4
) (for a
different
N
) follows
from (
7.4.6
) and
Lemma 5.2.6
.
Also (
7.4.5
) holds by construction.
∎

8.
The
\mathcal{A}
operators

8.1.
A lower bound for
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}

For now on, we work with the cocycles
V^{s}
and map
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(-,-,-)
from
Theorem 7.4.3
, for some
N
sufficiently large to be fixed in
Eqn. (8.4.12)
.

8.1.1.
The subspace
V^{s}_{*}(q)

For a.e.
x\in X
, let
\nu_{x}^{s}
denote the leaf-wise measure of
\nu
along
\mathcal{W}^{s}[x]
,
normalized so that
\nu^{s}_{x}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[x])=1
.

For a.e
q\in X_{0}
, let
V^{s}_{*}(q)\subset V^{s}(q)
denote the smallest subspace
containing the support of the measure
(F^{s}_{q})_{*}\hat{\nu}^{s}_{q}
, where

\hat{\nu}^{s}_{q}
is the restriction of
\nu^{s}_{q}
to
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
.
By the Markov property of the partition
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}
, we see that
for
t>0
we have that

g_{-t}V^{s}_{*}(q)\subset V^{s}_{*}(g_{-t}q)
.
Therefore by ergodicity of

g_{-t}
, the dimension of
V^{s}_{*}(q)
is almost everywhere constant,
and
g_{t}V^{s}_{*}(q)=V^{s}_{*}(g_{t}q)
almost everywhere.

8.1.2.
The norm
\|\cdot\|_{V^{s}_{*}}

Let
V^{s}_{-}(q)\subset V^{s}_{*}(q)
denote the direct sum of the
Lyapunov subspaces of
V^{s}_{*}(q)
with negative Lyapunov
exponent. Then, by
Proposition 7.1.5
,

V^{s}_{*}(q)=\mathbb{R}F^{s}_{q}(q)\oplus V^{s}_{-}(q).

By a standard Lyapunov norm construction, we can
measurably pick an inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}
on

V^{s}_{*}(q)
, such that the following hold:

(a)

\mathbb{R}F^{s}_{q}(q)
and
V^{s}_{-}(q)
are orthogonal.

(b)

\|F^{s}_{q}(q)\|_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}=1
, where
\|v\|_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}=\langle v,v\rangle_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}^{1/2}
.

(c)

There exists
\kappa_{1}(N)>1
depending only on
N

and the Lyapunov spectrum such that for
F\in V^{s}_{-}(q)
and
t>0
,

(8.1.3)

\displaystyle e^{-\kappa_{1}(N)t}\|F\|_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}\leq\|g_{t}F\|_{V^{s}_{*}(g_{t}q)}\leq e^{-\kappa_{1}(N)^{-1}t}\|F\|_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}.

Note that
F^{s}_{q}(q)\neq 0
on a set of full measure because
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)F^{s}_{q}(q)\neq 0
.

8.1.4 Lemma
.

Let
\tilde{\nu}_{q}
denote the measure on
V^{s}_{-}(q)
which is the
pushforward of restriction of the conditional measure
\nu^{s}_{q}
to

\mathcal{W}^{s}_{loc}[q]
by the map
q^{\prime}\to F_{q}(q^{\prime})-F_{q}(q)
. Then for every

\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
with
\nu(K)\geq 1-\delta
and constants
C(\delta)
and
\rho(\delta)>0
such that
for any
q\in K
and any subspace
V^{\prime}\subset V^{s}_{-}(q)
,

\tilde{\nu}_{q}(B(q,C(\delta)\setminus Nbhd_{\rho(\delta)}(V^{\prime}))\geq 1-\delta.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the definitions (cf.
[
EM18
, Lemma 5.4,
Lemma 5.5]
).
∎

8.1.5 Corollary
.

For every
\delta>0
there exists
a compact subset
K\subset X

with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
such that for any
q\in K
and any subset

E
with
\tilde{\nu}_{x}(E)>(1-\delta)\tilde{\nu}_{q}(V_{-}^{s})
there
exists a basis
\{v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\}
of
V^{s}_{-}
such that for all
1\leq j\leq n
we have
v_{j}\in E
,
\|v_{j}\|\leq C(\delta)
and the distance
between
v_{j}
and the span of
\{v_{1},\dots,v_{j-1}
is at least

\rho(\delta)
.

Proof.

This follows by repeated application of

Lemma 8.1.4
.
∎

8.1.6.
Bounds on cocycles

Let
\kappa>1
be such that we have the following bounds for
t\geq 0
:

(8.1.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}e^{\kappa^{-1}t}d^{u}(x,y)&\leq d^{u}(g_{t}x,g_{t}y)\\
e^{\kappa^{-1}t}d^{\mathcal{L}}_{z}(x,y)&\leq d^{\mathcal{L}}_{g_{t}z}(g_{t}x,g_{t}y)\leq e^{\kappa t}d^{\mathcal{L}}_{z}(x,y)\\
\end{split}

where
x,y\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[z]
in the first line, and
x,y\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[z]\cap\mathcal{L}_{k,\varepsilon}[z]
in the second.
Additionally, we require that for any
c\in L\mathcal{C}(x)
, or any
c\in\mathbf{H}(x)
(where
\mathbf{H}(x):=L\mathcal{C}(x)/L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}(x)
is defined in
Eqn. (5.1.10)
) we have for
t\geq 0
:

(8.1.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}e^{\kappa^{-1}t}\left\|v\right\|\leq&\left\|g_{t}v\right\|\leq e^{\kappa t}\left\|v\right\|\\
e^{\kappa^{-1}t}\left\|c\right\|\leq&\left\|g_{t}c\right\|\leq e^{\kappa t}\left\|c\right\|.\end{split}

For the bounds on linear cocycles in
Eqn. (8.1.8)
, the corresponding norms are defined in
Proposition B.4.1
.
We choose
\kappa
large enough so that the same bounds hold in the stable directions.
Note also that all of the cocycles listed above depend only on the Lyapunov spectrum of the measure and not the degree of approximation
N
.

8.1.9.
Defining
\mathcal{A}_{1}

Let
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
be as in
Theorem 7.4.3
, recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)

\displaystyle\colon V^{s}(q)\to L\mathcal{C}(q_{1/2})

and set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)=g_{\ell/2}\circ\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(g_{-\ell}q_{1},u,\ell)

\displaystyle\colon V^{s}(q)\to L\mathcal{C}(q_{1})

Note that with our conventions (also later on for
\mathcal{A}_{2}
) we have that
\mathcal{A}_{i}
takes values in
L\mathcal{C}(q_{i})
.
We also define

(8.1.10)

Z_{1}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)=g_{\ell/2}\circ Z_{1/2}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell).

8.1.11 Lemma

(Almost-equivariance of
\mathcal{A}_{1/2},\mathcal{A}_{1}
)
.

For every
N>0
there exists
N^{\prime}>0
with
N^{\prime}\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
such that the following holds:
for every
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
such that if

q\in K
,
g_{\ell/2}q\in K
,
g_{-\ell/2}ug_{\ell}q\in K
,

0.9\ell<s<\ell
,
\hat{q}:=g_{\ell-s}q\in K
,
q_{1}:=g_{\ell}q\in K
, and
g_{s/2}\hat{q}\in K
,
g_{-s/2}ug_{s}\hat{q}\in K

we have, for
\ell>\ell_{0}(\delta)
,

(8.1.12)

\displaystyle\|Z_{1}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)-Z_{1}(\hat{q},\hat{q}^{\prime},u,s)\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}

(8.1.13)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\circ\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(\hat{q},u,s)\circ g_{\ell-s}\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}

(8.1.14)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)-\mathcal{A}_{1}(q_{1},u,s)\circ g_{\ell-s}\|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}.

In the last two bounds the norm
\|\cdot\|

refers to the operator norm with respect to the norms
\|\cdot\|_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}
in the domain and
\|\cdot\|_{L\mathcal{C}(g_{-\ell/2}ug_{\ell}q)}
in the codomain.

Proof.

We treat each of the above bounds separately.

Proof of
Eqn. (8.1.12)

We first argue that

\|Z_{1/2}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}Z_{1/2}(\hat{q},\hat{q}^{\prime},u,s)\|\leq e^{N^{\prime}_{1}\ell}

with
N^{\prime}_{1}\to+\infty
as
N\to+\infty
.
Assuming this bound, note that since
Z_{1}
differs from
Z_{1/2}
by an application of
g_{\ell/2}
(by
Eqn. (8.1.10)
), and the cocycle in which
Z
takes values, namely
L\mathcal{C}
is independent of
N
and hence has fixed Lyapunov spectrum, the desired bound follows with
N^{\prime}=N^{\prime\prime}-\Lambda_{L\mathcal{C}}
where
\Lambda_{L\mathcal{C}}
is the largest Lyapunov exponent of
L\mathcal{C}
.

Next, recall from
Eqn. (7.4.2)
that

Z_{1/2}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)=L\left(\tilde{\phi}^{-1}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\right)\right)

where
L
is a linearization map and
\widetilde{\phi}
is the interpolation map from
§6.2.9
.
Note that both the linearization
L
and
U^{+}[q^{\prime}]
are
g_{t}
-equivariant, therefore in order to prove

(8.1.15)

\displaystyle\left\|Z_{1/2}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\circ Z_{1/2}(\hat{q},\hat{q}^{\prime},u,s)\right\|\leq e^{-N_{2}^{\prime}\ell}

it suffices to establish the bound (where we now explicitly include the dependence of
\widetilde{\phi}
on
q,q^{\prime},u,\ell
):

\left\|L\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}L\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}(\hat{q},\hat{q}^{\prime},u,s)g_{(\ell-s)/2}\right\|\leq e^{-N_{2}^{\prime\prime}\ell}

with
N_{2}^{\prime\prime}\to+\infty
as
N\to+\infty
, and then use again that the cocycle in which the functions take values is independent of
N
.
For notational simplicity, we will prove the same bound but for
\widetilde{\phi}
instead of
\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}
.
Recall again from
§6.2.9
that

\widetilde{\phi}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)=\wp^{+}_{fake}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2})\circ\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})\circ\wp^{+}(y_{1/2},q_{1/2})

where
\wp^{+}_{fake}(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2})
is the map from
Theorem 6.2.7
.
Again
\wp^{\pm}
are
g_{t}
-equivariant, and the norms
\left\|L\wp^{-}(q_{1/2},q_{1/2}^{\prime})\right\|
and
\left\|L\wp^{+}\left(y_{1/2},q_{1/2}\right)\right\|
are uniformly bounded (since
q_{1/2},q^{\prime}_{1/2},y_{1/2}
are biregular), so it suffices to prove that

\left\|L\wp^{+}_{fake}\left(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2}\right)-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\circ L\wp^{+}_{fake}\left(\hat{q}_{1/2}^{\prime},\hat{z}_{1/2}\right)g_{(\ell-s)/2}\right\|\leq e^{-N^{\prime}_{3}\ell}.

Since
g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\hat{q}_{1/2}^{\prime}=q_{1/2}^{\prime}
the second term above can be rewritten as
L\wp^{+}_{fake,\ell-s}\left(q_{1/2}^{\prime},g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\hat{z}_{1/2}\right)

where
\wp^{+}_{fake,\ell-s}
is defined by taking the fake forward flag at
\hat{z}_{1/2}
from
Theorem 6.2.7
and applying
g_{-(\ell-s)/2}
to it.
We can thus rewrite the difference above as:

L\wp^{+}_{fake}\left(q_{1/2}^{\prime},z_{1/2}\right)\left({\bm{1}}-L\wp^{+}_{fake,fake,\ell-s}(z_{1/2},g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\hat{z}_{1/2})\right)

where
\wp^{+}_{fake,fake,\ell-s}
is defined using the fake forward stable flags at
z_{1/2}
and
g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\hat{z}_{1/2}
, as well as the backwards flags (defined and equivariant at both points) and the holonomy on the associated graded associated to the backwards flag.

However,
Theorem 6.2.7
(iii)

implies that
\left\|{\bm{1}}-L\wp^{+}_{fake,fake,\ell-s}\right\|\leq e^{-N_{5}^{\prime}\ell}
and concludes the proof.

Proof of
Eqn. (8.1.14)
and
Eqn. (8.1.13)

Note that
Eqn. (8.1.14)
is implied by
Eqn. (8.1.13)
, with exponent
N^{\prime}-\kappa
going to infinity as
N
goes to infinity.
Indeed the terms inside the norm differ only by an application of
g_{\ell/2}
, which has norm bounded by
e^{\kappa\ell}
and
\kappa
is independent of
N
(see
Eqn. (8.1.8)
).

To prove
Eqn. (8.1.13)
, let
K^{\prime}
be
the compact set from
Theorem 7.4.3
but
of measure at least
1-\delta^{2}
. We may also assume that

Corollary 8.1.5
holds for
K^{\prime}
and
\delta

replaced by
\delta^{2}
.

Let now
K\subseteq K^{\prime}
be its compact subset of measure at least
1-2\delta
of points of density for the stable conditionals
\nu^{s}_{q}|_{\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}}
provided by
Lemma A.6.3
, and such that
C_{\delta}
is uniformly bounded on
K
by some constant
C(\delta)
.
In particular if
q\in K
then
\nu^{s}_{q}(K^{\prime}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{t}^{-}[q])\geq(1-\delta)\nu^{s}_{q}(\mathfrak{B}_{t}^{-}[q])
for
t\geq 0
.

By assumption
q,\hat{q}=g_{\ell-s}q\in K
so let
q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]\cap K^{\prime}
and
\hat{q}^{\prime}=g_{\ell-s}q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{s}[\hat{q}]
with
\hat{q}^{\prime}\in K
.
This can be arranged for
q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
of density at least
1-2\delta
.

Recall that
q_{1}=g_{\ell}q=g_{s}\hat{q}
, and
let
Z_{1/2}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
be as in

§7.4.1
.
Then, we proved earlier in
Eqn. (8.1.15)
that:

\left\|Z_{1/2}(q,q^{\prime},u,\ell)-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\circ Z_{1/2}(\hat{q},\hat{q}^{\prime},u,s)\right\|\leq e^{-N_{2}^{\prime\prime}\ell}

Therefore, by
Theorem 7.4.3
and
§
8.1.2
, since
q^{\prime}\in K^{\prime}
and
g_{\ell-s}q^{\prime}\in K^{\prime}
we have:

\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\circ\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(\hat{q},u,s)F^{s}_{\hat{q}}(g_{\ell-s}q^{\prime})\|\leq e^{-N^{\prime}\ell},

where
N^{\prime}\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
.
By the equivariance of
F^{s}
we have
F^{s}_{\hat{q}}(g_{\ell-s}q^{\prime})=g_{\ell-s}F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})
. Therefore,

(8.1.16)

\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\circ\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(\hat{q},u,s)\circ g_{\ell-s}F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})\|\\
\leq e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}.

We now choose a basis
for
V^{s}_{-}(q)
as in
Corollary 8.1.5
consisting
of vectors for which (
8.1.16
) holds. Then,

\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)-g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\circ\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(\hat{q},u,s)\circ g_{\ell-s}\|\leq C(\delta)e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}.

After replacing
N^{\prime}
by
N^{\prime}+1
and choosing
\ell
large enough, we
see that
Eqn. (8.1.13)
holds.
∎

8.1.17.
A lower bound on
\mathcal{A}_{1/2},\mathcal{A}_{1}

Let now
N
be as in
Theorem 7.4.3
.
Let
\mathcal{B}^{X}
denote the set of pairs
(q_{1},u)
where
q_{1}\in X
and
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
.

8.1.18 Lemma

(Choice of
\alpha_{2}
)
.

Suppose the family
q\to U^{+}(q)
satisfies the QNI condition (see

Definition 3.2.2
).
Then, there exists
\alpha_{2}>0
and
N_{0}
(depending only on the family
q\to U^{+}(q)
) such that for any
N>N_{0}
there exists a measurable
function
\ell_{0}:\mathcal{B}^{X}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that for almost every
q_{1}\in X

and
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
and any
\ell>\ell_{0}(q_{1},u,N)
there exists a nonzero
F_{-}\in V^{s}_{-}(g_{-\ell}q_{1})
such that

(8.1.19)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(g_{-\ell}q_{1},u,\ell)F_{-}\|_{L\mathcal{C}(q_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\geq e^{-\alpha_{2}\ell}\|F_{-}\|_{V^{s}_{-}(g_{-\ell}q_{1})}

(8.1.20)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)F_{-}\|_{L\mathcal{C}(q_{1})}

\displaystyle\geq e^{-\alpha_{2}\ell}\|F_{-}\|_{V^{s}_{-}(g_{-\ell}q_{1})}

In this lemma,
N
is the parameter of

Definition 7.1.1
.

Proof.

As in the proof of
Lemma 8.1.11
, the bound in
Eqn. (8.1.20)
follows from that in
Eqn. (8.1.19)
, with the same
\alpha_{2}
, by applying
g_{\ell/2}
and noting again that on the cocycle
L\mathcal{C}
the forward flow is expanding.

Let
K
,
\ell_{0}
,
\alpha_{0}
be as in
Definition 3.2.2
.
Suppose first that
\ell>\ell_{0}
is such that
q_{1/2}\equiv g_{-\ell/2}q_{1}\in K
, and also
q\equiv g_{-\ell}q_{1}\in K
.
Suppose also that
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
is such that
y_{1/2}\equiv g_{-\ell/2}uq_{1}\in S
,
where
S=S(q_{1/2},\ell)
is as in
Definition 3.2.2
.

Let
\alpha
be as in

Theorem 6.3.3
.
Note that if
Theorem 6.3.3
holds with some
\alpha>0
then it also holds for any smaller
\alpha>0
.
Similarly, if the QNI condition
Definition 3.2.2
holds for some
\alpha_{0}
, then it holds for any larger
\alpha_{0}
as well.

Suppose then that
\alpha_{0}\gg\alpha
, by a uniform factor that only depends on the Lyapunov spectrum.
Then since
z_{1/2}\in\mathcal{W}^{cs}[y_{1/2}]
, by the QNI condition we have that

d^{Q}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}],z_{1/2}\right)\geq e^{-\alpha_{0}\ell}.

By selecting the compact set
K
accordingly, we have (since
q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in K
)

d^{\mathcal{L}}_{q^{\prime}_{1/2}}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}],z_{1/2}\right)\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{1}(\delta)}d^{Q}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}],z_{1/2}\right)\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{1}(\delta)}e^{-\alpha_{0}\ell}.

Note that the two sets are in the same unstable.
We would like to obtain an estimate of this form, but after applying
\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}
.
To do so, consider
q^{\prime}_{t}:=g_{t}q^{\prime}_{1/2},z_{t}:=g_{t}z_{1/2},y_{t}:=g_{t}y_{1/2}
and in the time interval
(\alpha_{0}-\tfrac{\alpha}{10})[\tfrac{\ell}{\kappa},\kappa\ell]
choose
t
such that
q^{\prime}_{t},y_{t}\in K_{1}
for a compact set
K_{1}
(with further standard dynamical assumptions) and such that we also have:

e^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{100}\alpha\ell}\geq d^{\mathcal{L}}_{q^{\prime}_{t}}\left(U^{+}\left[q^{\prime}_{t}\right],z_{t}\right)\geq e^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{10}\ell}

using
Eqn. (8.1.7)
.
Using
Theorem 6.3.3
, that
\phi_{t}\left(y_{t}\right)=z_{t}
, and the triangle inequality we have that

d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{t}}\left(y_{t},\phi_{t}^{-1}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{t}]\right)\right)\geq C_{3}(\delta)e^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{10}\ell}-2C_{2}(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}\geq e^{-\tfrac{\alpha}{5}\ell}

Explicitly, setting
m_{t}\in\phi_{t}^{-1}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{t}]\right)
to be a minimizer of the distance to
y_{t}
, we have that
d^{Q}(m_{t},\phi_{t}(m_{t}))\leq C_{2}(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}
and
d^{Q}(y_{t},\phi_{t}(y_{t}))\leq C_{2}(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}
(and
\phi_{t}(y_{t})=z_{t}
) by
Theorem 6.3.3
.
We finally pass between
d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{t}}
and
d^{Q}
at the cost of another
C_{3}(\delta)
to obtain the above inequality.
Applying now
g_{-t}
we find that

d^{\mathcal{L}}_{y_{1/2}}\left(y_{1/2},\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\right)\right)\geq e^{-\kappa t}e^{\tfrac{\alpha}{5}\ell}\geq e^{-\left[\kappa^{2}(\alpha_{0}-\tfrac{\alpha}{10})+\tfrac{\alpha}{5}\right]\ell}.

Finally, again using that
y_{1/2}\in K
we obtain that

d^{Q}\left(y_{1/2},\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\right)\right)\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{1}(\delta)}e^{-\left[\kappa^{2}(\alpha_{0}-\tfrac{\alpha}{10})+\tfrac{\alpha}{5}\right]\ell}\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{1}(\delta)}{e^{-\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}}

where
\alpha_{2}^{\prime}:=\kappa^{2}(\alpha_{0}-\tfrac{\alpha}{10})+\tfrac{\alpha}{5}
only depends on the Lyapunov spectrum and the QNI condition.
Since
y_{1/2}\in U^{+}[q_{1/2}]
, it follows that

d^{q_{1/2}}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[q_{1/2}],\widetilde{\phi}^{-1}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\right))\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{1}^{\prime}(\delta)}{e^{-\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}}

where
d^{q}_{\mathcal{H},loc}
is the local Hausdorff distance defined in
Eqn. (5.1.13)
.
Let
\gamma^{+}(q_{1/2})\in L\mathcal{C}(q_{1/2})
be the vector corresponding to
U^{+}[q_{1/2}]
and similarly for
\gamma^{+}(z_{1/2})
corresponding to
\tilde{\phi}^{-1}\left(U^{+}[q^{\prime}_{1/2}]\right)
.
Using now
Proposition 5.1.14
, we have that

\left\|\gamma^{+}(z_{1/2})-\gamma^{+}(q_{1/2})\right\|\geq\frac{1}{C_{2}(\delta)}e^{-\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}.

Let us now write
F_{q}(q^{\prime}):=F_{q}(q)+F_{q,-}(q^{\prime})
with
F_{q,-}(q^{\prime})\in V^{s}_{-}(q)
and the coefficient of
F_{q}(q)
is
1
by
Proposition 7.1.5
(i)
.
By
Theorem 7.4.3
we have

\left\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,l)F_{q}(q^{\prime})-\gamma^{+}(z_{1/2})\right\|\leq C_{3}(\delta)e^{-N\ell}\text{ and }\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,l)F_{q}(q)=\gamma^{+}(q_{1/2}).

We conclude that for all
q^{\prime}\in S^{\prime}\cap K
we have:

\left\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)F_{q,-}(q^{\prime})\right\|=\left\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)\big(F_{q}(q)-F_{q}(q^{\prime})\big)\right\|\geq\\
\geq\left\|\gamma^{+}(q_{1/2})-\gamma^{+}(z_{1/2})\right\|-C_{1}(\delta)e^{-N\ell}\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{2}(\delta)}e^{-\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}-C_{1}(\delta)e^{-N\ell}

If we choose
N>10\alpha_{2}^{\prime}
then for
\ell
large enough we get

\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)F_{q,-}(q^{\prime})\|\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{2}(\delta)}e^{-2\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}.

Next, note that
\left\|F_{q,-}(q^{\prime})\right\|\leq C_{3}(\delta)e^{-\beta(N)\ell}
by
Proposition 7.1.5
(ii), for some
\beta
that depends on
N
.
Note that in fact and upper bound of
O_{\delta}(1)
suffices.
Since
S^{\prime}\cap K
contains most of the measure of
\mathfrak{B}^{-}_{0}[q]
, it follows
that for
u
such that
g_{-\ell/2}uq_{1}\in S(q_{1/2},\ell)

(8.1.21)

\left\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)|_{V^{s}_{-}(q)}\right\|\geq\tfrac{1}{C(\delta)}e^{-2\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}.

By the Birkhoff ergodic theorem, we may assume that for
q_{1}\in K

and
L>L_{0}(\delta)
,

|\{\ell\in[0,L]\;\>:\;\>g_{-\ell}q_{1}\in K\}|\geq(1-c_{1}(\delta))L,

where
c_{1}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
. Then,

|\{(u,\ell)\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})\times[0,L]\;\>:\;\>g_{-\ell q_{1}}\in K\}\text{
and }g_{-\ell/2}uq_{1}\in S(q_{1},\ell)\}|(1-c_{1}(\delta))L,

where
c_{2}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
. (The measure
|\cdot|
on the left hand side of the above
equation is the product of the measures
|\cdot|
on
\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})

and on
[0,L]
).
Then, by Fubini’s theorem (applied to
\mathcal{B}_{0}[q_{1}]\times[0,L]
) there
exists
Q_{1}^{\prime}(q_{1})\subset\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
with
|Q_{1}^{\prime}(q_{1})|\geq(1-c_{2}(\delta))|\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})|
such that for
u\in Q_{1}^{\prime}(q_{1})
and
L

sufficiently large depending on
\delta
,

|\{\ell\in[0,L]\;\>:\;\>g_{-\ell}q_{1}\in K\text{
and }g_{-\ell/2}uq_{1}\in S(q_{1},\ell)\}|\geq(1-c_{3}(\delta))L,

where
c_{2}(\delta)
and
c_{3}(\delta)
tend to
0
as
\delta\to 0
.

Now suppose
u\in Q_{1}^{\prime}(q_{1})
, and suppose
\ell
is sufficiently
large depending on
\delta
. We can choose
0<s<\ell
such that

\ell-s<c_{4}(\delta)\ell
where
c_{4}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
such that
g_{-s}q_{1}\in K
and
g_{-s/2}uq_{1}\in S(q_{1},s)
.
Then, by (
8.1.21
),

\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(g_{-s}q_{1},u,s)\|\geq\tfrac{1}{C(\delta)}e^{-2\alpha_{2}^{\prime}s}.

We choose
\delta>0
sufficiently small so that

c_{4}(\delta)(\kappa+\kappa_{1}(N))<\kappa\alpha_{2}^{\prime}
.
By §
8.1.2
(c) and

Proposition B.4.1
(iv) applied to
L\mathcal{C}

\|g_{-(\ell-s)/2}\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(g_{-s}q_{1},u,s)\circ g_{\ell-s}\|\geq\\
\geq\tfrac{1}{C(\delta)}e^{-(2\kappa\alpha_{2}^{\prime}+c_{4}(\delta)(\kappa+\kappa_{1}(N)))\ell}\geq\tfrac{1}{C(\delta)}e^{-3\kappa\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}.

Then, by
Lemma 8.1.11
,

\|\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(g_{-\ell}q_{1},u,\ell)\|\geq\tfrac{1}{C(\delta)}e^{-3\kappa\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\ell}-e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}.

Since
\alpha_{1}
,
\kappa
and
\kappa_{1}
are independent of
\delta
,

c_{4}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0

and
N^{\prime}\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
, we can choose
\delta
small
enough (dependent on
N
) and
N_{0}
large enough so that for
\ell>\ell_{0}^{\prime}(\delta,N)
,
(
8.1.19
) holds with
\alpha_{2}=4\kappa\alpha_{2}^{\prime}
.

As
\delta
decreases to
0
, the sets
\{(q_{1},u)\;\>:\;\>q_{1}\in K=K(\delta),u\in Q_{1}^{\prime}(q_{1})\}
exhaust
\mathcal{B}^{X}
. Therefore, we can
define
\ell_{0}(q_{1},u,N)
to be
\ell_{0}^{\prime}(\delta,N)
where
\delta
is the
largest number such that
q_{1}\in K(\delta)
,
u\in Q_{1}^{\prime}(\delta)
.
∎

8.1.22.
Choice of
\alpha_{3}
.

We choose
\alpha_{3}>0
such that

\alpha_{3}>4\kappa\alpha_{2},

where
\alpha_{2}
is as in
Lemma 8.1.18
and
\kappa
is as in
Eqn. (8.1.8)
.
Then, for any
\ell>0
,

(8.1.23)

e^{\kappa^{-1}(\ell/2+\alpha_{3}\ell)}e^{-\alpha_{2}\ell}>1,

8.2.
Modifying
\mathcal{A}_{1}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)

In the rest of this subsection, we assume that the family
q\to U^{+}(q)
satisfies the QNI condition (see
Definition 3.2.2
).

For the next statement, the constant
\kappa_{1}(N)
is defined in
Eqn. (8.1.3)
to control the contraction on
V^{s}_{-}
.

8.2.1 Proposition

(Modifying
\mathcal{A}_{1}
to be Lipschitz in time)
.

We can modify the map
\mathcal{A}_{1}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
, denoted
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{new}
so that

Theorem 7.4.3
still holds (with
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}^{new}:=g_{-\ell/2}\circ\mathcal{A}_{1}^{new}
), and for any
\delta>0
there exists a compact
K
of measure at least
1-\delta
such that provided
q,q_{1/2},q_{1},q^{\prime},q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in K
we have

\left\|\mathcal{A}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)-\mathcal{A}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u\ell)\right\|\leq e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}

where
N^{\prime}
is as in
Lemma 8.1.11

Furthermore, there exists a measurable function
\ell_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,\cdot):Q\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}

and there exists
\rho<\kappa_{1}(N)^{-1}/2
so that for any
\ell>\ell_{0}^{\prime\prime}(q_{1},N)+\ell_{0}^{\prime\prime}(uq_{1},N)
,
any
F\in V^{s}_{-}(g_{-\ell}q_{1})
with

(8.2.2)

\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)F\right\|\geq e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\|F\|

and for any
0<s<\ell/(2\kappa_{1}(N))
, we have:

e^{-\rho s}\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)F\right\|\leq\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)g_{-s}F\left\|\leq e^{\rho s}\right\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)F\right\|.

After the proof of this result, we will write
\mathcal{A}_{i}
instead of
\mathcal{A}_{i}^{new}
and simply assume that
\mathcal{A}_{i}
satisfies the conclusions.

Proof.

Choose
\delta>0
so that
100\delta\kappa_{1}(N)<N^{\prime}/4
, where
\kappa_{1}(N)
is defined in
Eqn. (8.1.3)
.
Let
K
be as in
Lemma 8.1.11
.
Let

(8.2.3)

E_{good}(q_{1})=\{\ell\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\;\>:\;\>g_{-\ell}q_{1}\in K\text{ and }g_{-\ell/2}q_{1}\in K\}.

Let
\ell_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,\cdot):Q\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be such that for
\ell>\ell_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x,N)
,

(8.2.4)

|\{s\in[0,\ell]\;\>:\;\>g_{-s}x\in K\}|>(1-10\delta)\ell.

Fix
D\gg 0
.
For a fixed
q_{1}
, we inductively pick
\ell_{j}\in E_{good}(q_{1})
to
be as small as possible provided that
\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}\geq D
.
Now suppose
\ell>\ell_{0}^{\prime\prime}(q_{1},N)+\ell_{0}^{\prime\prime}(uq_{1},N)
, and suppose
j

is such that
\ell_{j-1}\leq\ell\leq\ell_{j}
.
Then, by (
8.2.3
) and (
8.2.4
),

\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}<100\delta\ell_{j}
. Write

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j}={\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell_{j})
,

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j-1}={\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell_{j-1})
.

For
\ell_{j-1}\leq\ell\leq\ell_{j}
, we define

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)=\frac{\ell-\ell_{j-1}}{\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j}\circ g_{\ell_{j}-\ell}+\frac{\ell_{j}-\ell}{\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j-1}\circ g_{\ell-\ell_{j-1}}

Note that by
Lemma 8.1.11
,
Eqn. (8.1.14)
, we have:

\left\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j-1}\circ g_{-(\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1})}\right\|\leq e^{-N^{\prime}\ell_{j}},

where
N^{\prime}\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
and so in particular

(8.2.5)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{A}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)-\mathcal{A}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)\right\|\leq e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}

Suppose first that
\ell_{j-1}\leq\ell\leq\ell+s\leq\ell_{j}
.
Then,

\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)\circ g_{-s}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)=\frac{s}{\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}}\left(\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j-1}\circ g_{\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}}\right)\circ g_{\ell-\ell_{j}}.

Thus, for any
F\in V^{s}_{-}(g_{-\ell}q_{1})
,

\left\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)\circ g_{-s}F-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)F\right\|\leq\\
\frac{s}{|\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}|}\left\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j}-\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{j-1}\circ g_{\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}}\right\|\left\|g_{\ell-\ell_{j}}F\right\|\leq\\
\leq\frac{s}{D}e^{\kappa_{1}(N)(\ell-\ell_{j-1})-N^{\prime}\ell_{j}}\|F\|\leq\frac{s}{D}e^{-(3N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\|F\|.

Now suppose

(8.2.6)

\left\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)F\right\|\geq e^{-(N^{\prime}/2)\ell}\|F\|.

Then,

\left(1-\frac{s}{D}e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\right)\leq\frac{\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)\circ g_{-s}F\|}{\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)F\|}\leq\left(1+\frac{s}{D}e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\right).

Recall that we are assuming that

\ell_{j-1}\leq\ell<\ell+s\leq\ell_{j}
, and
\ell_{j}-\ell_{j-1}<100\delta\ell_{j}
. Therefore
0<s<100\delta\ell
.
Then, for any
\rho>0
,
provided that
\ell
is large enough,

(8.2.7)

e^{-\rho s}\leq\frac{\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)\circ g_{-s}F\|}{\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell)F\|}\leq e^{\rho s}.

Indeed, we have

e^{-s\rho}\leq\max\left(1-\frac{s\rho}{100},1-\frac{1}{100}\right)\text{assuming }s\geq 0,\rho\leq\tfrac{1}{10}

If
s\rho\leq 1
then we have

1-\frac{s\rho}{100}\leq 1-\frac{s}{D}e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}

as soon as
\ell
is sufficiently large (depending on
\rho
), and if
s\rho\geq 1
then we have

1-\frac{1}{100}\leq 1-\frac{s}{D}e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}

again as soon as
\ell
is sufficiently large (recall that
s\leq 100\delta\ell
).
Analogously
1+\frac{s}{D}e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\leq e^{\rho s}
for
\ell
sufficiently large depending on
\rho
and thus
Eqn. (8.2.7)
follows.

It remains to relax the assumption that
\ell+s<\ell_{j}
. Suppose
(
8.2.2
) holds. Then, in view of

Lemma 8.1.11
, for

0<s<\ell/(2\kappa_{1}(N))
,

\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)g_{-s}F\right\|\geq\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)F\right\|-e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}\left\|F\right\|\\
\geq\left(e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}-e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}\right)\|F\|\geq\\
\geq\tfrac{1}{2}e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}e^{-\kappa_{1}(N)s}\|g_{-s}F\|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}e^{-(1/2+N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\left\|g_{-s}F\right\|.

Using
Eqn. (8.2.5)
we conclude that

\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)g_{-s}F\right\|\geq\tfrac{1}{4}e^{-(1/2+N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\left\|g_{-s}F\right\|.

Thus, provided
N^{\prime}
is large enough and
\ell
is large enough and
0<s<\ell/(2\kappa_{1}(N))
,

\|\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{new}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)g_{-s}F\|\geq e^{-(N^{\prime}/2)\ell}\|g_{-s}F\|.

Then, the estimate (
8.2.7
) can be iterated,
and the condition (
8.2.6
) persists under iteration.
Thus (
8.2.7
) holds for any
0<s<\ell/(2\kappa_{1}(N))
.
∎

From now on, we assume that
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
and

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
are such that
Proposition 8.2.1
holds.

8.3.
The 4-variable
\mathcal{A}_{2}

Recall that the cocycle
\mathbf{H}
is defined in

§5.1.9
.
We define

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\colon V^{s}_{*}(q)\to\mathbf{H}(q_{2})

as

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)=g_{t+\ell/2}\circ u_{*}(q_{1/2},y_{1/2})\circ\mathcal{A}_{1/2}(q,u,\ell)\mod L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}(q_{2})

where
u_{*}
is defined in
Definition 5.2.2
.
Note that because
u_{*}
is
g_{t}
-equivariant, we have an equivalent definition of
\mathcal{A}_{2}
as:

(8.3.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)=g_{t}\circ u_{*}(q_{1},uq_{1})\circ\mathcal{A}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)\mod L\mathcal{C}^{\tau}(q_{2})

using the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{1}
from
§8.1.9
.
Note that we have

(8.3.2)

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau+t)=g_{t}\circ\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau).

Also, in view of
Eqn. (7.4.5)
,

(8.3.3)

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q)=0.

8.4.
Bilipshitz estimates

We denote by

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|

the operator norm of
\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)
with respect to the
dynamical norms
\|\cdot\|_{V^{s}_{*}}
on
V^{s}_{*}(g_{-\ell}q_{1})

and
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathbf{H}(g_{t}uq_{1})
.

8.4.1 Lemma

(Future bilipschitz)
.

We have, for
t>s
,

(8.4.2)

e^{\kappa^{-1}(t-s)}\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)\|\leq\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\|\leq\\
\leq e^{\kappa(t-s)}\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)\|,

where
\kappa
is as in
Eqn. (8.1.8)
.

Proof.

Let
q=g_{-\ell}q_{1}
. Let
F\in V^{s}_{*}(q)
be such that
\|F\|_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}=1
and

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\|=\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)F\|.

Then, using
Proposition B.4.1
(iv)
,

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\|=\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)F\|=\|g_{t-s}\circ\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)F\|\\
\leq e^{\kappa(t-s)}\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)F\|\leq e^{\kappa(t-s)}\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)\|.

Thus proves the upper bound in (
8.4.2
).
To prove the lower bound,
let
F\in V^{s}_{*}(q)
be such that
\|F\|_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}=1
and

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)\|=\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)F\|.

Then, using
Proposition B.4.1
(iv)
,

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\|\geq\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)F\|=\|g_{t-s}\circ\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)F\|\\
\geq e^{\kappa^{-1}(t-s)}\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)F\|=e^{\kappa^{-1}(t-s)}\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,s)\|.

∎

8.4.3 Lemma

(Past bilipschitz)
.

There exists
N_{0}>1
such that if the parameter

N
in
Theorem 7.4.3
is greater
than
N>N_{0}
, then there exist
\kappa_{3}=\kappa_{3}(N)>1
and

\beta=\beta(N)>0
and
a function
\ell_{0}:X\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}

such that for
\ell>\ell_{0}(q_{1},N)+\ell_{0}(uq_{1},N)
,
\tau<\alpha_{3}\ell

and
0<s<\ell/(2\kappa_{1}(N))
,

(8.4.4)

e^{-\kappa_{3}s}\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\leq\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|\leq\\
\leq e^{-\kappa_{3}^{-1}s}\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|,

provided
\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|>e^{-\beta\ell}
.

Proof.

Let
q=g_{-\ell}q_{1}
.
Let
N^{\prime}
be as in
Lemma 8.1.11
and

Proposition 8.2.1
, so that
N^{\prime}\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
. Let
\beta=N^{\prime}/8
.

Preliminary bound.
We first prove that

(8.4.5)

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|\geq e^{-(N^{\prime}/8)\ell}-e^{-(N^{\prime}-\kappa\alpha_{3})\ell}.

Let
F^{\prime}\in V^{s}_{*}(q)
be such that

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{\prime}\|=\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\|F^{\prime}\|
. In
view of (
8.3.3
) and §
8.1.2
(a), we have

F^{\prime}\in V^{s}_{-}(q)
. We have

(8.4.6)

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{\prime}\|=\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\|F^{\prime}\|\geq e^{-(N^{\prime}/8)\ell}\|F^{\prime}\|.

By
Lemma 8.1.11
and
Eqn. (8.3.1)
:

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)g_{s}F^{\prime}-{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{\prime}\|=\\
\left\|g_{\tau}\circ u_{*}(q_{1},uq_{1})\circ\left[{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)g_{s}-{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)\right]F^{\prime}\right\|\leq\\
\leq C(q_{1},uq_{1})e^{\kappa\tau-N^{\prime}\ell}\|F^{\prime}\|\leq C(q_{1},uq_{1})e^{-(N^{\prime}-\kappa\alpha_{3})\ell}\|F^{\prime}\|.

for a constant
C(q_{1},uq_{1})
depending measurably on the points.
Therefore using
Eqn. (8.4.6)
,

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)g_{s}F^{\prime}\|\geq\left(e^{-(N^{\prime}/8)\ell}-C(q_{1},uq_{1})e^{-(N^{\prime}-\kappa\alpha_{3})\ell}\right)\|F^{\prime}\|\geq\\
\geq\left(e^{-(N^{\prime}/8)\ell}-C(q_{1},uq_{1})e^{-(N^{\prime}-\kappa\alpha_{3})\ell}\right)\|g_{s}F^{\prime}\|

since
s>0
and
\left\|F^{\prime}\right\|\geq\left\|g_{s}F^{\prime}\right\|
.
This completes the proof of
Eqn. (8.4.5)
.

In view of
Proposition 8.2.1
, for
0<s<\ell/(2\kappa_{1}(N))
,
restricted to
V^{s}_{-}
we have

(8.4.7)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)\circ g_{-s}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)\circ H_{\ell,s}

where
H_{\ell,s}:V^{s}_{-}(q)\to V^{s}_{-}(q)
is a linear map
satisfying

(8.4.8)

\max(\|H_{\ell,s}F^{\prime}\|,\|H_{\ell,s}^{-1}F^{\prime}\|)\leq e^{\rho s}\|F^{\prime}\|,

for any
F^{\prime}\in V^{s}_{-}(q)
satisfying

(8.4.9)

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)F^{\prime}\|_{V^{s}_{*}(q)}\geq e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\|F^{\prime}\|.

Therefore, by applying
g_{\tau}\circ u_{*}(q_{1},uq_{1})
to
Eqn. (8.4.7)
we find:

{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\circ g_{-s}={\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\circ H_{\ell,s},

and so

{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)={\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\circ H_{\ell,s}\circ g_{s}.

when restricting the domain to
V^{s}_{-}
.

Lower bound.
We now prove the lower bound in
Eqn. (8.4.4)
.
Let
F^{\prime}
be as in
Eqn. (8.4.6)
.
We have

(8.4.10)

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)F^{\prime}\|\geq e^{-\kappa\tau}\left\|u_{*}(uq_{1},q_{1})\right\|\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{\prime}\|\geq\\
\geq C(q_{1},uq_{1})e^{-((N^{\prime}/8)+\kappa\alpha_{3})\ell}\|F^{\prime}\|\geq e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\|F^{\prime}\|

once
\ell_{0}(q_{1}),\ell_{0}(uq_{1})
are sufficiently large.
Therefore
Eqn. (8.4.9)
holds as long as
N
and
therefore
N^{\prime}
are large enough.
Let
F=g_{-s}\circ H_{\ell,s}^{-1}F^{\prime}
so that
F^{\prime}=H_{\ell,s}\circ g_{s}F
.
Then,
F\in V^{s}_{-}(g_{-s}q)
. We may normalize
F
so that
\|F\|_{V^{s}_{-}(g_{-s}q)}=1
.
Using §
8.1.2
(c) and (
8.4.8
),

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|\geq\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)F\|=\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\circ H_{\ell,s}\circ g_{s}F\|=\\
=\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\|g_{s}\circ H_{\ell,s}F\|\geq e^{-(\kappa_{1}(N)+\rho)s}\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|.

Thus the lower bound in
Eqn. (8.4.4)
holds.

Upper bound.
We now prove the upper bound in (
8.4.4
).
Let
F\in V^{s}_{*}(g_{-s}q)
be such that
\|F\|_{V^{s}_{*}(g_{-s}q)}=1
and

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|=\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)F\|.

In view of
Eqn. (8.3.3)
and §
8.1.2
(a), we have
F\in V^{s}_{-}(g_{-s}q)
.
Let
F^{\prime}=g_{s}F
, then we claim
Eqn. (8.4.9)
holds for it.
Indeed, if this is not the case, then by

Lemma 8.1.11
,

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)F\|\leq\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)g_{s}F\|+e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}\|F\|=\\
=\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell)F^{\prime}\right\|+e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}\leq e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}\left\|F^{\prime}\right\|+e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}\leq\\
\leq e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}e^{-\kappa_{1}(N)^{-1}s}+e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}

where we used that
\left\|F^{\prime}\right\|=\left\|g_{s}F\right\|\leq e^{-\kappa_{1}(N)^{-1}s}
(and
\left\|F\right\|=1
).
Then,

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|=\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)F\|=\\
=\|g_{\tau}\circ u_{*}(q_{1},uq_{1})\circ{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)F\|\leq C(q_{1},uq_{1})\cdot e^{\kappa\tau}\left(e^{-(N^{\prime}/4)\ell}e^{-\kappa_{1}(N)^{-1}s}+e^{-N^{\prime}\ell}\right).

However, since
\tau<\alpha_{3}\ell
and
s>0
, this contradicts

Eqn. (8.4.5)
as long as
N
(and thus
N^{\prime}
)
are large enough.
Therefore, (
8.4.9
) holds.
Then, using §
8.1.2
(c) and (
8.4.8
),

\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|=\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)F\|=\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\circ H_{\ell,s}\circ g_{s}F\|\\
\leq\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\|H_{\ell,s}\circ g_{s}F\|\leq e^{\left(-\kappa_{1}(N)^{-1}+\rho\right)s}\|{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|.

By
Proposition 8.2.1
the exponent
\rho
can be made arbitrarily small and positive and this proves the upper bound in (
8.4.4
).
∎

8.4.11.
Choice of
N
.

We now choose the parameter
N
in
Theorem 7.4.3
so that

(8.4.12)

e^{\kappa(4\alpha_{3}+1/2)\ell}e^{-N\ell}<e^{-\ell},

and also so that
Lemma 8.1.18
and

Lemma 8.4.3
hold.

8.4.13.
Defining
\tau_{\epsilon}

For
\epsilon>0
, almost all
q_{1}\in X_{0}
, almost all
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
and
\ell>0
, let

\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)=\sup\{\tau\;\>:\;\>\tau>0\text{ and }\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\leq\epsilon\}.

8.4.14 Lemma
.

There exists
\kappa_{4}>1
(depending only on the
Lyapunov spectrum) with the following property: for almost all
q_{1}\in X_{0}
,
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
, for all
\ell>\ell_{0}(q_{1})+\ell_{0}(uq_{1})
and
s>0
,

\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)+\kappa_{4}^{-1}s<\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)<\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)+\kappa_{4}s.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the lemma assuming
0<s<\ell/(2\kappa_{1}(N))
.
Let
\tau=\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)
,
\tau_{s}=\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell+s)
. We have

\epsilon=\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|=\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau_{s})\|.

Therefore,

\frac{\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau_{s})\|}{\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|}=\frac{\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|}{\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|}

In view of
Lemma 8.4.3
, this implies

e^{\kappa_{3}^{-1}s}\leq\frac{\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau_{s})\|}{\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell+s,\tau)\|}\leq e^{\kappa_{3}s}.

Now,
Lemma 8.4.1
implies
