\kappa^{-1}\kappa_{3}^{-1}s\leq\tau_{s}-\tau<\kappa\kappa_{3}s.

so
\kappa_{4}:=\kappa\kappa_{3}
suffices.
∎

8.4.15 Proposition
.

There exists
\kappa>1
depending only on the Lyapunov spectrum, and
such that for almost all
q_{1}\in X_{0}
, almost all
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(q_{1})
, any
\ell>\ell_{0}(q_{1})+\ell_{0}(uq_{1})
and any measurable
subset
E_{bad}\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

|\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,E_{bad})\cap[\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,0),\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)]|\leq\kappa|E_{bad}\cap[0,\ell]|

and

|\{t\in[0,\ell]|\;\>:\;\>\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,t)\in E_{bad}\}|\leq\kappa|E_{bad}\cap[\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,0),\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)]|.

Proof.

This follows immediately from
Lemma 8.4.14
.
∎

For future use we record the following restatement of
Theorem 7.4.3
, in terms of
\mathcal{A}_{2}
rather than
\mathcal{A}_{1/2}
:

8.4.16 Proposition
.

There exists a constant
\alpha>0
and
for every
\delta>0
there is a compact set

K
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
such that the following holds: Suppose

Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are two bottom-linked pre-
Y
configurations, such that

Y^{\prime}
left-shadows
Y
. Suppose
Y\in K
and also (using the notation
§
6.1.5
)
q^{\prime}\in K
,
q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in K
,
q_{1}^{\prime}\in K
. Then,
the linear map
\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\colon V^{s}_{*}(q)\to\mathbf{H}(q_{2})
satisfies

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})=\mathbf{j}\big(\phi_{\tau}^{-1}\bigl(U^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime}]\bigr)\big)+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}).

This is a simplified formulation of
Theorem 7.4.3
since the constant
N
has now been fixed, and hence also the cocycle
V^{s}_{*}
.

9.
Some technical lemmas

9.1.
Left-balanced
Y
-configurations

9.1.1 Definition

(Left-balanced
Y
-configurations)
.

Fix
C\geq 1
. We say a pre-
Y
-configuration or a
Y
-configuration
Y
is

(C,\epsilon)
-left-balanced
if using the notation of

§6.1.5
and
Eqn. (8.3.1)
we have

\frac{1}{C}\epsilon\leq\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\leq C\epsilon.

Equivalently,
Y
is
(C,\epsilon)
-left-balanced if using the
notation of
§6.1.5
and
§8.4.13
we have

|\tau-\tau_{(\epsilon)}(q_{1},u,\ell)|<C^{\prime}

for some
C^{\prime}
depending on
C
.

We denote by
Y^{(\epsilon)}_{pre,lb}(q_{1},u,\ell)
the unique

(1,\epsilon)
-left-balanced pre-
Y
configuration
Y
with
q_{1}(Y)=q_{1}
,
\ell(Y)=\ell
,
u(Y)=u
.

9.2.
A selection lemma

9.2.1 Lemma
.

For any
\rho>0
there is a constant
c(\rho)
with the following
property:
Let
A:\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{W}
be a linear map between Euclidean spaces.
Then there exists a proper subspace

\mathcal{M}\subset\mathcal{V}
such that for any
v
with

\|v\|=1
and
d(v,\mathcal{M})>\rho
, we have

\|A\|\geq\|Av\|\geq c(\rho)\|A\|.

Furthermore, if
A,\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W}
vary measurably on some parameter, then
\mathcal{M}
can be chosen to vary measurably as well.

Proof.

The matrix
A^{t}A
is symmetric, so it has a complete
orthogonal set of eigenspaces
W_{1},\dots,W_{m}
corresponding to
eigenvalues
\mu_{1}>\mu_{2}>\dots\mu_{m}
. Let
\mathcal{M}=W_{1}^{\perp}
.
∎

In this subsection, let
(\mathcal{B},|\cdot|)
be a standard Lebesgue space equipped with a finite measure space.

The following is a slight modification of

[
EM18
, Proposition 5.3]

since we are using partition atoms and not balls. However, the proof
is virtually unaffected.

9.2.2 Proposition
.

For every
\delta>0
there exist constants

c_{1}(\delta)>0
,
\epsilon_{1}(\delta)>0

with
c_{1}(\delta)\to 0
and
\epsilon_{1}(\delta)\to 0

as
\delta\to 0
, and also constants

\rho(\delta)>0
,
\rho^{\prime}(\delta)>0
, and

C(\delta)<\infty
such that the following holds:

For any subset
K^{\prime}\subset X_{0}
with
\nu(K^{\prime})>1-\delta
,
there exists a subset
K\subset K^{\prime}

with
\nu(K)>1-c_{1}(\delta)

such that the following holds: suppose for each
x\in X_{0}
we have a
measurable map from
\mathcal{B}
to proper subspaces of

V^{s}_{*}(x)
, written as

u\to\mathcal{M}_{u}(x)
, where
\mathcal{M}_{u}(x)
is
a proper subspace of
V^{s}_{*}(x)
. Then, for
any
q\in K
there exists
q^{\prime}\in K^{\prime}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
with

(9.2.3)

C(\delta)^{-1}\leq\|F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime})\|_{V^{s}(q)}\leq C(\delta)

and such that

(9.2.4)

d_{V^{s}(q)}(F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime}),\mathcal{M}_{u}(q))>\rho(\delta)\\
\text{\rm for at least
$(1-\epsilon_{1}(\delta))$-fraction of $u\in\mathcal{B}$. }

9.3.
A switching lemma

Recall that left-shadowing
Y
-configurations are introduced in
Definition 6.1.13
.

9.3.1 Lemma
.

There exists
\alpha>0
and
for every
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K
and for every

\ell>0
a subset
K_{\ell}\subset K

with
\nu(K_{\ell})>1-c(\delta)
with
c(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
and

c_{1}(\delta)>0
such that the
following holds. Suppose
Y
,
Y^{\prime}
are bottom linked
pre-
Y
-configurations, with
Y\in K
and
Y^{\prime}\in K
. Suppose also
that
Y^{\prime}
left-shadows
Y
and
Y
left-shadows
Y^{\prime}
. Also
suppose (using the notation §
6.1.5
) that
q\in K_{\ell}
,
q^{\prime}\in K_{\ell}
. Then, assuming
\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\leq c_{1}(\delta)
and
\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)\|\leq c_{1}(\delta)
, we have

(9.3.2)

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)\|\leq C(\delta)\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}),

and

(9.3.3)

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|\leq C(\delta)\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)\|+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}).

9.3.4.
Proof .

Let
K=K(\delta)
be the set where

Proposition 8.4.16
and

Theorem 6.3.3

apply. We also assume that the functions

C_{1}(\cdot)
,
c_{1}(x)
,
C(x)

of
Lemma 5.1.17
and

Proposition 5.1.16

are bounded by
C(\delta)
for
x\in K
, and that the function
r(x)

of
Lemma 2.5.9
is bounded from below by

C(\delta)^{-1}
for
x\in K
.

Recall that

F_{q}^{s}(q^{\prime})=H^{s}_{V^{s}}(q^{\prime},q)\iota_{s}(q^{\prime}),

where
\iota_{s}(q^{\prime})\in V^{s}(q^{\prime})
is an equivariant section, and

H^{s}_{V^{s}}(q^{\prime},q)
is a holonomy map. For any
\delta>0
we may
choose a compact set
K^{\prime\prime}
with
\nu(K^{\prime\prime})>1-\delta
such that
for all
q\in K^{\prime\prime}
,

(9.3.5)

\|\iota_{s}(q)\|_{V^{S}(q)}\leq C(\delta)

and also that

(9.3.6)

\|H^{s}_{V^{s}}(q^{\prime},q)\|\leq C(\delta)

for all
q\in K^{\prime\prime}
,
q^{\prime}\in K^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]
.
Let
K^{\prime}=K^{\prime\prime}\cap K\cap g_{-\ell/2}K\cap g_{-\ell}K
.
Then, let
K_{\ell}
be the subset denoted by
K
in

Proposition 9.2.2
.

By symmetry, it is enough to prove
(
9.3.2
). Since
q,q^{\prime}\in K^{\prime\prime}
, we have

(9.3.7)

\|F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})\|_{V^{s}(q)}\leq C(\delta).

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
denote the subspace
as in
Lemma 9.2.1
for
\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)
.
Note that by (
7.4.5
),

F^{s}_{q^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
By
Proposition 9.2.2
(with the function
u\to\mathcal{M}_{u}
the constant function
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
) we can choose
q^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{-}[q]\cap K^{\prime}

with
d_{V^{s}(q^{\prime})}(F^{s}_{q^{\prime}}(q^{\prime\prime}),\mathcal{M}^{\prime})>\rho(\delta)
.
Then, by (
9.3.5
) and (
9.3.6
)

(9.3.8)

\|F^{s}_{q^{\prime}}(q^{\prime\prime})\|_{V^{s}(q^{\prime})}\leq C(\delta).

Then, again by (
9.3.6
),

(9.3.9)

\|F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime\prime})\|_{V^{s}(q)}\leq C(\delta).

By
Lemma 9.2.1
, we have

(9.3.10)

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)\|\leq C(\delta)\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q^{\prime}}(q^{\prime\prime})\|.

Recall that using our notational conventions
§
6.1.5
,
q_{2}=g_{\tau}uq_{1}
,
q_{2}^{\prime}=g_{\tau}u^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}
.
Using the notation as in (
6.3.2
), let
\phi_{\tau}=\phi_{\tau}(Y,q^{\prime}):\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{2}^{\prime}]
and
\phi_{\tau}^{\prime\prime}=\phi_{\tau}(Y,q^{\prime\prime}):\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{2}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime\prime}]
be the interpolation maps relative to
uq_{1}
, and let

\phi^{\prime}_{\tau}=\phi_{\tau}(Y^{\prime},q^{\prime\prime}):\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{2}^{\prime}]\to\mathcal{W}^{u}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime\prime}]
be the interpolation
map relative to
u^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}
. Then,

\mathcal{U}\equiv\phi_{\tau}^{-1}(U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}])\in\mathcal{C}[q_{2}]

\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\phi^{\prime\prime}_{\tau})^{-1}(U^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime\prime}])\in\mathcal{C}[q_{2}],

and

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\equiv(\phi^{\prime}_{\tau})^{-1}(U^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime\prime}])\in\mathcal{C}[q_{2}^{\prime}].

By
Proposition 8.4.16
,

(9.3.11)

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})+O(e^{-\alpha\ell})

(9.3.12)

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime\prime})=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})+O(e^{-\alpha\ell})

(9.3.13)

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q^{\prime}}(q^{\prime\prime})=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}).

Note that by our assumptions,
\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|
,
\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\|
and

\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|
are all bounded by
2c_{1}(\delta)C(\delta)
. We can choose

c_{1}(\delta)
so that for
x\in K
,
c_{1}(\delta)C(\delta)\ll r(x)
, where

r(x)
is as in §
2.2.8
.
By
Proposition 5.1.16
,
and since
q_{2}\in K
,
q_{2}^{\prime}\in K
,

C(\delta)^{-1}\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|\leq d_{\mathcal{H},loc}^{q_{2}}(\mathcal{U},U^{+}[q_{2}])\leq C(\delta)\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|,

C(\delta)^{-1}\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|\leq d_{\mathcal{H},loc}^{q_{2}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime},U^{+}[q_{2}])\leq C(\delta)\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|,

(9.3.14)

C(\delta)^{-1}\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\|\leq d_{\mathcal{H},loc}^{q_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}])\leq C(\delta)\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\|.

By
Lemma 5.1.17
there exist
h,h^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{2}])

such that

\mathcal{U}=hU^{+}[q_{2}],\qquad\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime\prime}U^{+}[q_{2}],

and

\|L(h)-I\|_{L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{2})}\leq C(\delta)\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|,\qquad\|L(h^{\prime\prime})-I\|_{L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{2})}\leq C(\delta)\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|.

Then,

\|L(h^{\prime\prime}\circ h^{-1})-I\|_{L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{2})}\leq C(\delta)(\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|+\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|).

It then follows from
Lemma E.5.2

that for
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[q_{2}]
,

(9.3.15)

d^{u}(x,h^{\prime\prime}\circ h^{-1}x)\leq C(\delta)(\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|+\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|).

Let

h^{\prime}=(\phi^{\prime}_{\tau})^{-1}\circ\phi_{\tau}^{\prime\prime}\circ h^{\prime\prime}\circ h^{-1}\circ\phi_{\tau}^{-1}.

Then,

h^{\prime}U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}]=(\phi^{\prime}_{\tau})^{-1}\circ\phi_{\tau}^{\prime\prime}\circ h^{\prime\prime}\circ h^{-1}(hU^{+}[q_{2}])=(\phi^{\prime}_{\tau})^{-1}\circ\phi_{\tau}^{\prime\prime}\circ(\phi_{\tau}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}(U^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime\prime}])=\mathcal{U}^{\prime}.

By (
9.3.15
) and

Theorem 6.3.3
,
for
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[q_{2}^{\prime}]
,

d^{Q}(\sigma(x),\sigma(h^{\prime}x))\leq C(\delta)(\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|+\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|).

Then, using
Lemma 2.5.9
,

d^{u}(x,h^{\prime}x)\leq C(\delta)(\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|+\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|).

Therefore, since
h^{\prime}U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}]=\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
,

d_{\mathcal{H},loc}^{q_{2}^{\prime}}(U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}],\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\leq C(\delta)(\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|+\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|).

Then, by (
9.3.14
),

\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})\|\leq C(\delta)(\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U})\|+\|\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime})\|).

Therefore, using (
9.3.11
), (
9.3.12
) and
(
9.3.13
), we get

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q^{\prime}}(q^{\prime\prime})\|\leq C(\delta)(\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})\|+\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime\prime})\|)+O(e^{-\alpha\ell})\\
\leq C_{1}(\delta)\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}),

where for the last estimate we used (
9.3.7
) and
(
9.3.9
). Now, (
9.3.2
) follows from
(
9.3.10
).
∎

For the next statement, see
Definition 9.1.1
for left-balanced
Y
-configurations.

9.3.16 Corollary
.

There exists
\alpha>0
and
for every
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K^{\prime}
with
\nu(K^{\prime})>1-c(\delta)
with
c(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
and for every

\ell>0
there exists a compact set
K_{\ell}
with
\nu(K_{\ell})>1-c^{\prime}(\delta)
with
c^{\prime}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0

such that the
following holds. Suppose
Y=Y_{pre}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)
and
Y_{1}=Y_{pre}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau_{1})
are
(1,\epsilon)
-left-balanced pre-
Y

configurations. (Note that
Y
and
Y_{1}
share the entire bottom
leg). Suppose also that
Y\in K^{\prime}
,
Y_{1}\in K^{\prime}
,
q\in K_{\ell}
,
and also that
0\leq s\leq\max(\tau,\tau_{1})

is such that
u_{1}q_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{s}[uq_{1}]
. Then, for
\ell

sufficiently large depending on
\delta
and
\epsilon
,

(9.3.17)

(1/4)\kappa^{-2}(\tau-s)-C(\delta)<\tau_{1}-s<4\kappa^{2}(\tau-s)+C(\delta),

where
\kappa
is as in

Proposition B.4.1
(iv)
.

In particular, note that if
s\in[\tau,\tau_{1}]
then this yields a bound on
|\tau-\tau_{1}|
depending on
\delta
.

Proof.

The conclusion is symmetric in the exchange of
\tau
and

\tau_{1}
(up to modifying
C(\delta)
), so we may assume that
\tau_{1}\leq\tau
.
Let
K
be as in
Lemma 9.3.1
. By the
ergodic theorem, we can
choose
K^{\prime}\subset K
and
T_{0}=T_{0}(\delta)
such that for all
x^{\prime}\in K^{\prime}
and any
L>T_{0}(\delta)
,

\{t^{\prime}\in[L,2L]\;\>:\;\>g_{-t^{\prime}}x^{\prime}\in K\}\geq(1-\kappa^{-2}/4)L.

Let
s^{\prime}=\min(\tau_{1},s)
.
Then, we can find
0<t\leq s^{\prime}
such that setting
t^{\prime}:=\tau-t
we have
g_{-t^{\prime}}(g_{\tau}uq_{1})=g_{t}uq_{1}\in K
, and similarly
g_{t}u_{1}q_{1}\in K
and furthermore with
L:=T_{0}(\delta)+(\tau-s^{\prime})
we have:

\tau-t=t^{\prime}\leq(1+\kappa^{-2}/2)L

and thus:

(9.3.18)

s^{\prime}-t<(1+\kappa^{-2}/2)(\tau-s^{\prime})+T_{1}(\delta).

Since
\epsilon<1
, we may
also ensure that
\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\|<c_{1}(\delta)
, and

\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,t)\|<c_{1}(\delta)
where
c_{1}(\delta)
is as
in
Lemma 9.3.1
.
By
Remark A.5.10
and

Lemma 9.3.1
with
q_{1}^{\prime}=q_{1}
,
\tau=t
and

u^{\prime}=u_{1}
, we get

C(\delta)^{-1}\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\|-O(e^{-\alpha\ell})\leq\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,t)\|\\
\leq C(\delta)\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\|+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}),

By
Lemma 8.4.1
, and since
\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)\|=\epsilon
, we have

e^{-\kappa(\tau-t)}\epsilon\leq\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,t)\|\leq e^{-\kappa^{-1}(\tau-t)}\epsilon.

Similarly, since
\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau_{1})\|=\epsilon
, by
Lemma 8.4.1
,

e^{-\kappa(\tau_{1}-t)}\epsilon\leq\|\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,t)\|\leq e^{-\kappa^{-1}(\tau_{1}-t)}\epsilon.

Combining the last three displayed equations, we get, for
\ell

sufficiently large depending on
\delta
and
\epsilon
,

\kappa^{-2}(\tau-t)-C(\delta)<\tau_{1}-t\leq\kappa^{2}(\tau-t)+C(\delta).

In view of (
9.3.18
), this implies

(9.3.19)

(1/2)\kappa^{-2}(\tau-s^{\prime})-C(\delta)\leq\tau_{1}-s^{\prime}\leq 2\kappa^{2}(\tau-s^{\prime})+C(\delta).

Recall that
s^{\prime}=\min(\tau_{1},s)
.
If
s^{\prime}=s
, then it follows immediately from
(
9.3.19
) that (
9.3.17
)
holds. If
s^{\prime}=\tau_{1}
, then it follows from
(
9.3.19
) that

0<\tau-s<\tau-s^{\prime}<C(\delta),

and thus (
9.3.17
) still holds.
∎

9.4.
Matching and volume distortion

9.4.1.
Partitions and
U^{+}

Recall that the measurable partition
\mathfrak{B}_{0}[\cdot]
is defined in
§A.5
.
For
\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}>0
, let

\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{(\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})}[x]=\{y\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]\;\>:\;\>d^{u}(y,\partial\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x])\geq\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}\},

and let

\mathcal{B}_{0}^{(\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})}[x]=\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{(\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})}[x]\cap U^{+}[x].

Note that
\mathcal{B}_{0}^{(\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})}
admits a “Haar measure”
|\cdot|
that is defined in
§A.5.5
.

9.4.2 Lemma
.

For every
\delta>0
and every
\epsilon_{0}^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small
depending on
\delta
there exists
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{0}(\delta,\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})>0
and a compact set

K\subset X
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
so that the following holds:

Suppose
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
.
Suppose
Y=Y_{pre}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)
and

Y^{\prime}=Y_{pre}(q_{1}^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)
are bottom-linked
pre-
Y
-configurations such that
Y^{\prime}
left-shadows
Y
.
Suppose
Y\in K
, and using the notation
§
6.1.5
,
q^{\prime}\in K
,
q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in K
,
q_{1}^{\prime}\in K
. Suppose also that

d^{q_{2}}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[q_{2}],\phi_{\tau}^{-1}(U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}]))\leq\epsilon,

and

(9.4.3)

d^{Q}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{B}_{0}[q_{1}],\mathcal{B}_{0}[q_{1}^{\prime}])<\epsilon,

where
d^{Q}_{\mathcal{H}}
denotes the Hausdorff distance of the projection to

Q
, with respect to the ambient distance
d^{Q}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

Let

A_{\tau}=U^{+}[q_{2}]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{(2\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})}[q_{2}],

A_{\tau}^{\prime}=U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}]\cap\phi_{\tau}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{(\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})}[q_{2}]).

Then,

\frac{|g_{-\tau}A_{\tau}|}{|\mathcal{B}_{0}[q_{1}]|}\leq\kappa(\delta)\frac{|g_{-\tau}A_{\tau}^{\prime}|}{|\mathcal{B}_{0}[q_{1}^{\prime}]|},

for an appropriate constant
\kappa(\delta)
.

9.4.4.
Proof of
Lemma 9.4.2
.

In the proof of
this lemma we normalize the measure
|\cdot|
on
U^{+}[q_{1}]
so
that
|\mathcal{B}_{0}[q_{1}]|=1
and similarly we normalize the measure

|\cdot|
on
U^{+}[q_{1}^{\prime}]
so that
|\mathcal{B}_{0}[q_{1}^{\prime}]|=1
.

Let
A_{0}=g_{-\tau}A_{\tau}
,
A_{0}^{\prime}=g_{-\tau}A_{\tau}^{\prime}
.
Let
\phi_{\tau}
be the interpolation map.
Let
\tilde{A}_{\tau}=\phi_{\tau}^{-1}(A^{\prime}_{\tau})
. Then,

\tilde{A}_{0}\equiv g_{-\tau}\tilde{A}_{\tau}=\phi_{0}^{-1}(A^{\prime}_{0}).

Since the Hausdorff distance between
U^{+}[q_{2}]
and
\phi_{\tau}^{-1}(U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}])
is bounded by
\epsilon
, by
Lemma 5.1.17

there exists
h_{\tau}\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{2}])

with
\|L_{q_{2}}h_{\tau}-{\bm{1}}\|_{L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q_{2})}\leq C(\delta)\epsilon

and such that

\phi_{\tau}^{-1}(U^{+}[q_{2}^{\prime}])=h_{\tau}U^{+}[q_{2}]
.
There exits
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{0}(\delta,\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})>0

so that if
q\in K
then for any
h\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q])
with

\|L_{q}h-{\bm{1}}\|_{L\mathcal{W}^{u}(q)}<C(\delta)\epsilon_{0}
, we have that

h\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{(2\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})}[q]\subset\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{(\epsilon_{0}^{\prime})}[q].

Then, since
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
, we have that

h_{\tau}A_{\tau}\subset\tilde{A_{\tau}}.

Let
h_{0}=g_{-\tau}\circ h_{\tau}\circ g_{\tau}\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[uq_{1}])
.
Then,
h_{0}
is exponentially close to the identity, and
h_{0}A_{0}\subset\tilde{A}_{0}=\phi_{0}^{-1}(A_{0}^{\prime})
. Thus,

(\phi_{0}\circ h_{0})A_{0}\subset A_{0}^{\prime}.

Recall that
\phi_{0}=\wp^{+}(q_{1}^{\prime},uq_{1}^{\prime})\circ\wp^{-}(q_{1},q_{1}^{\prime})\circ\wp^{+}(uq_{1},q_{1})
where
\wp^{\pm}
denotes the subresonant maps induced by the measurable connections
P^{\pm}
on
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
.
In particular, since all the points are in the compact set
K
we can assume that
\phi_{0}
has uniformly bounded Jacobian.
Therefore

|A_{0}|\leq\kappa(\delta)|A_{0}^{\prime}|
for an appropriate constant
\kappa(\delta)
.
∎

The following is a trivial modification of

[
EM18
, Lemma 12.8]
.

9.4.5 Lemma
.

Suppose
P
and
P^{\prime}
are finite measure subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with

|P|=|P^{\prime}|
, admitting partitions

P=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{N}P_{j},\quad P^{\prime}=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{N}P^{\prime}_{j},

Let

Q\subset P
and
Q^{\prime}\subset P^{\prime}
be subsets with
|Q|>(1-\delta)|P|
,

|Q^{\prime}|>(1-\delta)|P^{\prime}|
.

Suppose there exists
\kappa>1
such that for all
1\leq j\leq N
,

|P_{j}{\cap Q}|\leq\kappa|P_{j}^{\prime}|.

Then there exists
\hat{Q}\subset Q
with
|\hat{Q}|\geq(1-2\kappa\delta)|P|
such that for all
1\leq j\leq N
, if

\hat{Q}\cap P_{j}\neq\emptyset
, then
Q^{\prime}\cap P_{j}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
.

9.4.6.
Proof.

Let
J=\{j\;\>:\;\>P_{j}\cap Q\neq\emptyset\}
, and let

J^{\prime}=\{j\;\>:\;\>Q^{\prime}\cap P_{j}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset\}
, and let

\hat{Q}=\{x\in Q\;\>:\;\>\text{ for all $j$ with $x\in P_{j}$, we have
$j\in J^{\prime}$.}\}

Thus, if
x\in Q\setminus\hat{Q}
, then there exists
j\in J
with

x\in Q\cap P_{j}
but
j\not\in J^{\prime}
.
Then,

|Q\setminus\hat{Q}|\leq\sum_{j\in J\setminus J^{\prime}}|Q\cap P_{j}|\leq\kappa\sum_{j\not\in J^{\prime}}|P_{j}^{\prime}|\leq\kappa|(Q^{\prime})^{c}|,

since if
j\not\in J^{\prime}
then
P_{j}^{\prime}\subset(Q^{\prime})^{c}
. Thus,
|Q\setminus\hat{Q}|\leq\kappa\delta|P|
, and so
|\hat{Q}|\geq(1-2\kappa\delta)|P|
.
∎

9.5.
Transport Lemmas

9.5.1 Lemma

(
\mathcal{A}_{2}
and
u_{*}
are compatible)
.

There exists
\alpha>0
and
for every
\delta>0
there exists a subset
K\subset Q
with

\nu(K)>1-\delta
such that the following holds:
Suppose
Y=Y_{pre}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)
,
Y_{1}=Y_{pre}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau)

and
Y^{\prime}=Y_{pre}(q^{\prime},u^{\prime},\ell,\tau)
are pre-
Y
-configurations. (Note
that
Y
and
Y_{1}
share the entire bottom leg). Suppose
Y^{\prime}
and
Y

are bottom-linked, and also that
Y^{\prime}
left-shadows

Y
. Suppose also that
u_{1}q_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{\tau}[uq_{1}]
, so in particular,

Y_{1}
also left-shadows
Y
.

Furthermore, suppose
Y\in K
,
Y_{1}\in K
and also (using the
notation §
6.1.5
)
q^{\prime}\in K
,
q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in K
.
Then,

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})=(g_{\tau}\circ u_{*}(uq_{1},u_{1}q_{1})\circ g_{-\tau})\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}).

Proof.

By
Proposition 8.4.16
,

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})=\mathbf{j}\big(\phi_{\tau}^{-1}\bigl(U^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime}]\bigr)\big)+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}).

Let
h\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{\tau}uq_{1}])
be such that

\phi_{\tau}^{-1}\bigl(U^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime}]\bigr)=hU^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}]

Then, also by
Proposition 8.4.16
,

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})=\mathbf{j}\big((\phi^{\prime}_{\tau})^{-1}\bigl(U^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime}]\bigr)\big)+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}),

where
\phi^{\prime}_{\tau}
is the interpolation map with
u
replaced by
u_{1}
.
Note that
g_{\tau}u_{1}q_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(g_{\tau}uq_{1})
.
By

Theorem 6.3.3
, for
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{\tau}uq_{1}]
we have

d^{Q}(x,\phi_{\tau}(x))\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}

and

d^{Q}(x,\phi_{\tau}^{\prime}(x))\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}

and so

d^{Q}(x,(\phi_{\tau}^{\prime})^{-1}\circ\phi_{\tau}(x))\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha\ell}

for
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}_{loc}[g_{\tau}uq_{1}]
.
Then
by
Lemma 5.1.17

there exists
h_{1}\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[q_{\tau}uq_{1}])
with
\|L_{x}h_{1}-I\|=O(e^{-\alpha\ell})
such that

(\phi_{\tau}^{\prime})^{-1}\bigl(U^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}^{\prime}]\bigr)=(\phi_{\tau}^{\prime})^{-1}\circ\phi_{\tau}\bigl(hU^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}]\bigr)=h_{1}hU^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}].

Therefore,

\mathbf{j}(h_{1}hU^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}])=\mathbf{j}(hU^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}])+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}).

By the equivariance of
u_{*}
,

g_{\tau}\circ u_{*}(uq_{1},u_{1}q_{1})\circ g_{-\tau}=u_{*}(g_{\tau}uq_{1},g_{\tau}u_{1}q_{1}).

By the definition of
u_{*}
, the map
u_{*}(g_{\tau}uq_{1},g_{\tau}u_{1}q_{1}):\mathbf{H}(g_{\tau}uq_{1})\to\mathbf{H}(g_{\tau}u_{1}q_{1})
satisfies

u_{*}(g_{\tau}uq_{1},g_{\tau}u_{1}q_{1})\,\mathbf{j}(hU^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}])=\mathbf{j}(hU^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}])\in\mathbf{H}(g_{\tau}u_{1}q_{1}),

where the vector
\mathbf{j}(hU^{+}[g_{\tau}q_{1}])
on the left hand side is
considered to be an element of
\mathbf{H}(g_{\tau}uq_{1})
. Now the statement
follows.
∎

For the next statement, see
Definition 9.1.1
for left-balanced
Y
-configurations.
It is an enhancement of the previous result by allowing
u_{1}q_{1}
to be in the larger partition atom
\mathcal{B}_{\tau-L}[uq_{1}]
(at the cost of requiring the
Y
-configurations to be left-balanced):

9.5.2 Lemma
.

For every
\delta>0
there exists a subset
K^{\prime}\subset Q
with

\nu(K^{\prime})>1-c_{1}(\delta)
with
c_{1}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0

and for every
\ell>0
there exists a subset
K_{\ell}\subset Q
with

\nu(K_{\ell})>1-c_{2}(\delta)
with
c_{2}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0

such that if
\epsilon
is sufficiently small depending on
\delta

and
\ell
is sufficiently large depending on
\delta
and
\epsilon
,
the following holds:

Suppose
Y=Y^{(\epsilon)}_{pre,lb}(q_{1},u,\ell)
and
Y_{1}=Y^{(\epsilon)}_{pre,lb}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell)

are
(1,\epsilon)
-left-balanced pre-
Y
-configurations. (Note
that
Y
and
Y_{1}
share the entire bottom leg). Suppose
Y^{\prime}
is a
pre-
Y
configuration which is bottom-linked to
Y
and left-shadows

Y
. Furthermore, suppose
Y\in K^{\prime}
,
Y_{1}\in K^{\prime}
and also (using the
notation §
6.1.5
)
q\in K_{\ell}
,
q^{\prime}\in K_{\ell}
,
q^{\prime}_{1/2}\in K^{\prime}
.

Let
\tau=\tau(Y)
,
\tau_{1}=\tau(Y_{1})
.
Suppose
0\leq L\ll\tau
is such that
u_{1}q_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{\tau-L}[uq_{1}]
. (In particular, this implies that
Y_{1}
shadows
Y
up to time

\tau-L
). Finally, suppose that

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})={\mathbf{v}}+{\mathbf{w}},

with
{\mathbf{w}}=O(e^{-\alpha\ell})
. Then,

(9.5.3)

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau_{1})F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})=(g_{\tau_{1}}\circ u_{*}(uq_{1},u_{1}q_{1})\circ g_{-\tau}){\mathbf{v}}+\\
O(e^{-\alpha\ell+2\kappa^{3}L}).

Proof.

Let
K_{\ell}
be as in
Lemma 9.3.1
and

Corollary 9.3.16
, and let

K
be the intersection of the sets
K
of

Lemma 9.5.1
and
Lemma 5.2.6

and the set
K^{\prime}
of
Corollary 9.3.16
.

By the ergodic theorem
we can choose
K^{\prime}\subset K
with
\nu(K^{\prime})>1-c_{1}(\delta)
with

c_{1}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
and
L_{0}=L_{0}(\delta)>0

such that for all
x\in K^{\prime}
and any
L>L_{0}(\delta)
,

\{t\in[L,2L]\;\>:\;\>g_{-t}x\in K\}\geq(1-\kappa^{-4}/4)L,

where
\kappa
is as in

Proposition B.4.1
(iv)
.
By
Corollary 9.3.16
applied with
s=\tau-L
(whose conditions
u_{1}q_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{s}[uq_{1}]
and
s\in[0,\max(\tau,\tau_{1})]
are satisfied by construction), we have

(9.5.4)

\tfrac{1}{6}\kappa^{-2}L<\tau_{1}-(\tau-L)<6\kappa^{2}L

Then, we can find
t\in[L,2L]
such that (taking
x=g_{\tau}uq_{1},x_{1}=g_{\tau u_{1}q_{1}}\in K^{\prime}
) we have

g_{\tau-t}uq_{1}\in K
,
g_{\tau-t}u_{1}q_{1}\in K
.
By
Lemma 5.2.6
,
there exists
C=C(\delta)
such that

\|u_{*}(g_{\tau-t}uq_{1},g_{\tau-t}u_{1}q_{1})\|\leq C(\delta).

Since
g_{-t}:\mathbf{H}(g_{\tau}uq_{1})\to\mathbf{H}(g_{\tau-t}uq_{1})
is a
contraction, we have

(9.5.5)

\|u_{*}(g_{\tau-t}uq_{1},g_{\tau-t}u_{1}q_{1})\circ g_{-t}\|\leq C(\delta).

Using
Lemma 9.5.1
for the first line

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau-t)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})

\displaystyle=u_{*}(g_{\tau-t}uq_{1},g_{\tau-t}u_{1}q_{1})\circ\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau-t)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})+O(e^{-\alpha\ell})

\displaystyle=u_{*}(g_{\tau-t}uq_{1},g_{\tau-t}u_{1}q_{1})\circ g_{-t}\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u,\ell,\tau)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})+O(e^{-\alpha\ell})

\displaystyle=u_{*}(g_{\tau-t}uq_{1},g_{\tau-t}u_{1}q_{1})\circ g_{-t}{\mathbf{v}}+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}),

(9.5.6)

\displaystyle=(g_{\tau-t}\circ u_{*}(u_{1}q_{1},uq_{1})\circ g_{-\tau}){\mathbf{v}}+O(e^{-\alpha\ell}),

where to pass from the second to the third line we have used
(
9.5.5
), and to pass to the last line we used the
equivariance of
u_{*}
.
We have

(9.5.7)

\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau_{1})F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})=g_{\tau_{1}-(\tau-t)}\circ\mathcal{A}_{2}(q_{1},u_{1},\ell,\tau-t)F^{s}_{q}(q^{\prime})

In view of (
9.5.4
),
0<\tau_{1}-(\tau-t)<6\kappa^{2}L
. Thus, (
9.5.3
)
follows from (
9.5.6
),
(
9.5.7
) and
Proposition B.4.1
(iv)
.
∎

10.
Divergence Estimates, Time Changes, and Foliations

Outline of section

The goal of this section is to adapt the constructions from §8 and §9 of
[
EM18
]
.

10.1.
Inert subspaces

10.1.1.
Setup

Here we follow §8 of
[
EM18
]
.
Recall that
\mathbf{H}
was defined in
Eqn. (5.1.10)
.
For ease of notation, we denote the Lyapunov spectrum of
\mathbf{H}
by
\{\lambda_{i}\}
and use the indexing convention
\lambda_{i}>\lambda_{i+1}
.
Recall that we have the Oseledets decomposition

\mathbf{H}=\oplus_{\lambda_{i}}\mathbf{H}^{\lambda_{i}}

and associated Oseledets filtrations
\mathbf{H}^{\geq\lambda_{i}},\mathbf{H}^{\leq\lambda_{i}}
.

10.1.2.
Definition of
U^{+}
-inert subspaces

Recall that
\mathcal{B}_{0}(x)
is defined in
Eqn. (A.5.6)
.
Define now the following coarsening of the Oseledets filtration:

\mathbf{H}^{\leq\lambda_{i}}_{in}(x):=\{{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{H}(x)\colon\text{ for Haar-a.e. }u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(x),\text{ we have }\\
u_{*}(x,ux){\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{H}^{\leq\lambda_{i}}(ux)\}

where
u_{*}(x,y)
was defined in
§5.2
.
In other words, these are vectors which keep growing at rate at most
\lambda_{i}
into the future, when pushed to any nearby point along
U^{+}[x]
.
From the definition it follows that
\mathbf{H}^{\leq\lambda_{i}}\subseteq\mathbf{H}^{\leq\lambda_{i-1}}
(with
\lambda_{i}<\lambda_{i-1}
) but this filtration might well be trivial.

Define now the
inert subbundles
:

\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}:=\mathbf{H}^{\leq\lambda_{i}}_{in}\cap\mathbf{H}^{\geq\lambda_{i}}

Here are some basic properties, immediate from the definitions:

10.1.3 Proposition

(Properties of inert subbundles)
.

On a subset of
X
of full measure:

(i)

The bundles
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}
are
g_{t}
-equivariant and so yield
g_{t}
-cocycles.

(ii)

We have that
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}\subset\mathbf{H}^{\lambda_{i}}
.

(iii)

For Haar-a.e.
u\in U^{+}(x)
we have that

\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}(ux)=u_{*}(x,ux)\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}(x).

See
[
EM18
, §8.1]
for the proofs.
Define now the sum of inert subbundles:

\mathbf{E}=\bigoplus_{\lambda_{i}}\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}\subset\mathbf{H}.

Note that the subspace corresponding to the top Lyapunov exponent is in
\mathbf{E}
.
The following is a direct translation of Prop. 8.5(a) of
[
EM18
]
.

10.1.4 Proposition

(Convergence to
\mathbf{E}
)
.

For every
\delta>0
there exists a compact set
K\subset Q
of measure at least
1-\delta
, and a constant
L_{2}(\delta)
with the following properties.
Take
q\in K
and
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{H}(q)
.
For any
L^{\prime}>L_{2}(\delta)
there exists
t\in(L^{\prime},2L^{\prime})
such that for a set of
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(g_{-t}q)
of Haar measure at least
(1-\delta)|\mathcal{B}_{0}(g_{-t}q)|
we have

d\left(\frac{g_{s}\circ u_{*}(g_{-t}q,ug_{-t}q)\circ g_{-t}\mathbf{v}}{g_{s}\circ u_{*}(g_{-t}q,ug_{-t}q)\circ g_{-t}\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{E}(g_{s}ug_{-t}q)\right)\leq C(\delta)e^{-\alpha\cdot t}

where
s>0
satisfies

\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|=\left\|g_{s}\circ u_{*}(g_{-t}q,ug_{-t}q)\circ g_{-t}\mathbf{v}\right\|

and the exponent
\alpha
depends only on the Lyapunov spectrum.

The statement says that by applying
g_{-t}
to a vector
\mathbf{v}
, followed by a typical
u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(g_{-t}q)
, and then by
g_{s}
, the resulting vector will be close to the inert subspace.
We refer for the detailed proof to
[
EM18
, Prop. 8.5(a)]
, which is based on the lemmas preceding it.

10.1.5 Proposition

(Agreement of
u_{*}
and
P^{+}
)
.

For a set of full measure of
q,q^{\prime}
, if
q^{\prime}=uq\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[q]
, the restriction to inert subspaces of the map
u_{*}(q,q^{\prime})\colon\mathbf{E}(q)\to\mathbf{E}(q^{\prime})
agrees with the standard measurable connection
P^{+}(q,q^{\prime})
as defined in
§B.2.6
, when
P^{+}
is restricted to inert subspaces as well.

The above result is Lemma 9.1 of
[
EM18
]
.

Proof.

By
Proposition 5.2.4
the map
u_{*}
preserves the backwards Lyapunov filtration and agrees with the standard measurable connection on the associated graded.
On the other hand, for the inert subbundles the map
u_{*}
has the property that
u_{*}(q,q^{\prime})\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}(q)=\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}(q^{\prime})
, by
Proposition 10.1.3
.
Therefore it has no off-diagonal components relative to the Oseledets decomposition.
It follows that it must agree with
P^{+}
on the inert subspaces
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}
.
∎

10.2.
Jordan canonical form of a cocycle

10.2.1.
Setup

Recall, from
Theorem B.3.5

that for any measurable cocycle
\mathbf{E}
, with Lyapunov decomposition
\mathbf{E}=\oplus\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}
, we have a further filtration in each Lyapunov block
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j}\supsetneq\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j-1}
.
Furthermore, the cocycle is block-diagonal under the action of
g_{t}
, and for a metric which will be denoted
\left\|\right\|
we have that

(10.2.2)

\displaystyle\left\|g_{t}{\mathbf{v}}\right\|=e^{\lambda_{ij}(q;t)}\left\|{\mathbf{v}}\right\|\quad\forall{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j}/\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j-1}(q).

Note that by
Proposition B.4.1
(v),
\lambda_{ij}(-;t)
is constant on the atoms of the partition
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
.
Furthermore, they are monotonic in
t
by

Proposition B.4.1
(iii) and the cocycle property of

\lambda_{ij}
. Furthermore,
Proposition B.4.1
(iii) and the
cocycle property of
\lambda_{ij}
imply that

(10.2.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}|t-t^{\prime}|\leq|\lambda_{ij}(q;t)-\lambda_{ij}(q;t^{\prime})|\leq{\kappa}|t-t^{\prime}|

where
\kappa
depends only on the Lyapunov spectrum.

Define also

\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{j}\text{ as the orthogonal complement of }\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j-1}\text{ inside }\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j}

with respect to the metric
\left\|-\right\|
introduced in
Proposition B.4.1
.
In
[
EM18
, §9.1]
this is denoted by
\mathbf{E}_{ij}^{\prime}
.
The notation
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{j}
will only be needed for this section.

We will denote by
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
the “fine” Lyapunov spectrum consisting of pairs
(i,j)\in\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
such that
\lambda_{i}
is in the Lyapunov spectrum of the cocycle and
j=1\ldots n_{i}
denotes the index of the sub-block corresponding to
\lambda_{i}
in the Jordan normal form of the cocycle.

10.2.4.
Time changes

The functions
\lambda_{ij}(q;t)
are monotonic in
t
by
Proposition B.4.1
(iii)
combined with the cocycle property of
\lambda_{ij}(q;t)
.
Therefore we can define the time-changed flow
g^{ij}_{t}
with the property that the expansion factor in
\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j}/\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j-1}
is constantly
\lambda_{i}
, i.e.

\left\|g_{t}^{ij}v\right\|=e^{\lambda_{i}t}\left\|v\right\|.

We record the following elementary fact:

10.2.5 Proposition

(Backwards nesting of partitions)
.

Suppose that
q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}[q]
.
Then for any
\lambda_{i}
and
j\in[1,n_{i}]
we have that

g_{-t}^{ij}q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}[g_{-t}^{ij}q].

This is immediate from the constructions, see also
[
EM18
, Lemma 9.2]
.

10.3.
The foliations
\mathcal{F}_{ij}
and parallel transport
\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime})

In this section, we define and establish basic properties of certain partitions
\mathcal{F}_{ij}
of the center-unstable manifolds that are adapted to later arguments.

10.3.1.
The map
\mathbf{R}

From now on,
X_{0}\subset X
is a set of full measure on which the objects used below are defined.
For
q\in X_{0}
, let

G[q]=\{g_{s}ug_{-t}q\;\>:\;\>t\geq 0,s\geq 0,u\in\mathcal{B}_{0}(g_{-t}q)\}\subset X.

For
q^{\prime}=g_{s}ug_{-t}q\in G[q]
, let

(10.3.2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime})=g_{s}\circ u_{*}(g_{-t}q,g_{-s}q^{\prime})\circ g_{-t}\colon\mathbf{H}(q)\to\mathbf{H}(q^{\prime}).

Here
g_{s}
is the cocycle dynamics on
\mathbf{H}
and
u_{*}(g_{-t}q,g_{-s}q^{\prime}):\mathbf{H}(g_{-t}q)\to\mathbf{H}(g_{-s}q^{\prime})
is as in

Definition 5.2.2
.
The map
\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime})
does not depend on the choice of
s,t,u
by the properties listed in
Remark 5.2.3
.
We will typically consider
\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime})
as a map from
\mathbf{E}(q)\to\mathbf{E}(q^{\prime})
.

10.3.3.
Growth of vectors

By
Eqn. (10.2.2)

for any
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{j}(q)
, and any

q^{\prime}=g_{s}ug_{-t}q\in G[q]
, there exist
{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{j}(q^{\prime})
with
\|{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}\|=\|{\mathbf{v}}\|
and also
{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbf{E}^{\lambda_{i}}_{\leq j-1}(q^{\prime})
such that:

(10.3.4)

\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}=e^{\lambda_{ij}(q,q^{\prime})}{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime\prime}

where:

(10.3.5)

\lambda_{ij}(q,q^{\prime})=\lambda_{ij}(q;-t)+\lambda_{ij}(ug_{-t}q;s).

10.3.6.
The foliations
\mathcal{F}_{ij}

For
q\in X_{0}
and
\lambda_{i},j
as in
§10.2.4
, let

\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
denote the set of
q^{\prime}\in G[q]
such that there exists

\ell\geq 0
with the property that

(10.3.7)

g^{ij}_{-\ell}q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[g^{ij}_{-\ell}q].

By
Proposition 10.2.5
, if
Eqn. (10.3.7)
holds
for some
\ell
then it also holds for any larger
\ell
.
Alternatively,

\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]=\left\{g_{\ell}^{ij}ug_{-\ell}^{ij}q\colon\ell\geq 0,\quad u\in\mathcal{B}(g_{-\ell}^{ij}q)\right\}\subset X.

By
Eqn. (10.3.5)
it follows that

(10.3.8)

\lambda_{ij}(q,q^{\prime})=0\qquad\text{ if $q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]$.}

We will refer to the sets
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
as
leaves
.
Locally at
q
, the leaf
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
through
q
is a piece of
U^{+}[q]
.
More precisely, for
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
:

(10.3.9)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[q^{\prime}]\right)=U^{+}[q^{\prime}]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[q^{\prime}].

Note that for any compact subset
A\subset\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
there exists a large enough
\ell
such that
g^{ij}_{-\ell}(A)
is contained in a set of
the form
\mathcal{B}_{0}[z]\subset U^{+}[z]
for some
z\in Q_{0}
.
Then the same holds for
g^{ij}_{-t}(A)
and any
t>\ell
.

10.3.10.
Measures on leaves

By
§A.6.5

the sets
\mathcal{B}_{0}[q]
support a “Lebesgue measure”
|\cdot|
,
i.e. the pushforward of the Haar measure on
U^{+}(q)/U^{+}_{q}(q)
to
\mathcal{B}_{0}[q]
under the action map
u\to uq

(recall that
U^{+}_{q}(q)
is the stabilizer of
q
in
U^{+}(q)
).

It follows that the leaves
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
also support a Lebesgue
measure (defined up to normalization), which will also be denoted by

|\cdot|
.
Specifically, if
A\subset\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
and
B\subset\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]

are compact subsets, define

(10.3.11)

\frac{|A|}{|B|}\equiv\frac{|g^{ij}_{-\ell}(A)|}{|g^{ij}_{-\ell}(B)|},

where
\ell
is chosen large enough such that both

g^{ij}_{-\ell}(A)
and
g^{ij}_{-\ell}(B)
are contained in a
set of the form
\mathcal{B}_{0}[z]
, for some
z\in X_{0}
.
It is immediate to check that replacing
\ell
by a larger number, the right-hand-side of
Eqn. (10.3.11)
is not changed.

10.3.12.
Balls in
\mathcal{F}_{ij}

Define the “balls”
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q,\ell]\subset\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
by

(10.3.13)

\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q,\ell]=\{q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]\colon g^{ij}_{-\ell}q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[g^{ij}_{-\ell}q]\}.

These have the property that, for
q\in X_{0}
and
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
, and sufficiently large
\ell
:

\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q,\ell]=\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q^{\prime},\ell].

Indeed, for
\ell
large enough we have that
g^{ij}_{-\ell}q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[g^{ij}_{-\ell}q]
,
and then
\mathcal{B}_{0}[g^{ij}_{-\ell}q^{\prime}]=\mathcal{B}_{0}[g^{ij}_{-\ell}q]
.

10.3.14.
The flows
g^{\mathbf{v}}_{t}

Let
q\in X_{0}
and
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}(q)
.
Define now a time change
\tau_{\mathbf{v}}(q;t)
and reparametrized flow by:

g^{\mathbf{v}}_{t}q=g_{\tau_{\mathbf{v}}(q;t)}q\quad\text{ such that }\quad\|(g^{\mathbf{v}}_{t})_{*}{\mathbf{v}}\|_{g^{{\mathbf{v}}}_{t}q}=e^{t}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{q}.

(Note that we are not defining
g_{t}^{{\mathbf{v}}}q^{\prime}
for
q^{\prime}\neq q
).
The composition rule for the flow is then:

g_{t+s}^{{\mathbf{v}}}q=g_{s}^{(g_{t})_{*}{\mathbf{v}}}g_{t}^{{\mathbf{v}}}q.

For
q^{\prime}\in G[q]
and
\ell\in\mathbb{R}
, define

(10.3.15)

\tilde{g}^{{\mathbf{v}},q}_{-\ell}:=g_{-\ell}^{{\mathbf{w}}}q^{\prime},\quad\text{ where ${\mathbf{w}}=\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}$. }

(When there is no potential for confusion about the point
q
and the
vector
{\mathbf{v}}
used, we
denote
\tilde{g}^{{\mathbf{v}},q}_{-\ell}
by
\tilde{g}_{-\ell}
.)
Note that
Proposition 10.2.5
still holds if

g^{ij}_{-t}
is replaced by
\tilde{g}^{{\mathbf{v}},q}_{-t}
.

10.3.16.
The foliations
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{v}}

For
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}(q)
we
can define the foliations

\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbf{v}}}[q]

and the “balls”

\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbf{v}}}[q,\ell]
as in

Eqn. (10.3.7)
and

Eqn. (10.3.13)
, with
\tilde{g}^{{\mathbf{v}},q}_{-t}

playing the role of
g^{ij}_{-t}
.

For
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbf{v}}}[q]
, we have

\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbf{v}}}[q]=\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbf{w}}}[q^{\prime}],\qquad\text{ where }{\mathbf{w}}=\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}.

We can
define the measure (up to normalization)
|\cdot|
on

\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbf{v}}}[q,\ell]
as in
Eqn. (10.3.11)
.
Then
§10.3.12
holds for
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbf{v}}}[q]

without modifications.
The following follows immediately from the
construction:

10.3.17 Proposition
.

For
q\in X_{0}
and any
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}(q)
, for a.e.
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{v}}[q]
, we have that

\|\mathbf{R}(q,y){\mathbf{v}}\|_{q^{\prime}}=\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{q}.

10.4.
A maximal inequality

10.4.1 Lemma
.

Suppose
K\subset X
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta
. Then, for any

\theta^{\prime}>0
there exists a
subset
K^{*}\subset X
with
\nu(K^{*})>1-2\kappa^{2}\delta/\theta^{\prime}
such that
for any
x\in K^{*}
and any
\ell>0
,

(10.4.2)

|\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]\cap K|>(1-\theta^{\prime})|\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]|.

Furthermore, if
x\in K^{*}
then
\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]:=\left(U^{+}[x]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]\right)\subset K^{*}
.

Proof.

Note that if
x
satisfies
Eqn. (10.4.2)
and
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]
then
x^{\prime}
also satisfies the same equation, since
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]
consists of pieces of
U^{+}
-orbits as in
Eqn. (10.3.9)
, so the last asserted property of
K^{*}
can be ensured by saturating it accordingly.

For
t>0
let now

\mathcal{B}_{t}^{ij}[x]=g_{-t}^{ij}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}[g_{t}^{ij}x]\cap U^{+}[g_{t}^{ij}x])=\mathcal{B}_{\tau}[x],

where
\tau
is such that
g_{\tau}x=g_{t}^{ij}x
.
Let
s>0
be arbitrary. Let
K_{s}=g_{-s}^{ij}K
. Then

\nu(K_{s})>1-\kappa\delta
.
Then, by

Lemma A.6.6
, there exists a subset
K_{s}^{\prime}
with

\nu(K_{s}^{\prime})\geq(1-2\kappa\delta/\theta^{\prime})
such that
for
x\in K_{s}^{\prime}
and all
t>0
,

|K_{s}\cap\mathcal{B}_{t}^{ij}[x]|\geq(1-\theta^{\prime}/2)|\mathcal{B}_{t}^{ij}[x]|.

Let
K_{s}^{*}=g_{s}^{ij}K_{s}^{\prime}
, and
note that
g_{s}^{ij}\mathcal{B}_{t}^{ij}[x]=\mathcal{F}_{ij}[g_{s}^{ij}x,s-t]
. Then, for all

x\in K_{s}^{*}
and all
0<s-t<s
,

|\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,s-t]\cap K|\geq(1-\theta^{\prime}/2)|\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,s-t]|.

We have
\nu(K_{s}^{*})\geq(1-2\kappa^{2}\delta/\theta^{\prime})
. Now take a
sequence
s_{n}\to\infty
, and let
K^{*}

be the set of points which are in infinitely many
K_{s_{n}}^{*}
.
∎

10.5.
Bounded subspaces, Synchronized Exponents, and the Equivalence Relation

Outline of section

In this section, we recall from
[
EM18
]
Prop. 10.1, Prop. 10.2, and Prop. 10.3.
The rest of §10 of loc.cit. contains the proofs of these statements.

Recall from
§10.2.1
that
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
indexes the “fine Lyapunov spectrum” on
\mathbf{E}
.

10.5.1 Proposition

(Subbundles
\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}
)
.

There exists a family of subcocycles
\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}\subset\mathbf{E}
, indexed by equivalence classes
[ij]\subset\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
that partition
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
, such that their direct sum

\bigoplus_{[ij]}\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}\text{ maps injectively to }\mathbf{E}

and furthermore the bundles satisfy
Proposition 10.5.2
,
Proposition 10.5.3
, and
Proposition 10.5.4
.

In fact, the next three propositions characterize
\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}
.
Let
\widetilde{\Lambda}
denote the set of equivalence classes
[ij]\subset\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
as above.

10.5.2 Proposition
.

There exists
\theta>0
depending only on
\nu
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
depending only on the dimension of
Q
such that the
following holds:
for every
\delta>0
, every
T>0
and every
\eta>0
,
there exists a subset
K=K(\delta,T,\eta)
of measure at least

1-\delta
and
L_{0}=L_{0}(\delta,T,\eta)>0

such that the following holds:

Suppose
q\in X
,

{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}(q)
,
L\geq L_{0}
,
and

|g_{{[-T,T]}}K\cap\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{v}}[q,L]|\geq(1-(\theta/2)^{n+1})|\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{v}}[q,L]|.

Then, for at least
(\theta/2)^{n}
-fraction of
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{v}}[q,L]
,

d\left(\frac{\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}}{\|\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}\|},\bigcup_{[ij]\in\tilde{\Lambda}}\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(q^{\prime})\right)<\eta.

In

[
EM18
]

Prop. 10.1, it is assumed that
T=1
. However, the same proof also
shows this more general version (in fact, in the paragraph labeled
“Proof of Proposition 10.1”, one should replace
L_{n-1}+1
by

L_{n-1}+T
and
L_{1}+1
by
L_{1}+T
; no other changes are needed).

10.5.3 Proposition
.

There exists a measurable function
C_{3}\colon X\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
finite almost
everywhere such that for all
q\in Q
, for all
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q]
, for all
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(q)
, we have

C_{3}(q)^{-1}C_{3}(q^{\prime})^{-1}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|\leq\|\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}\|\leq C_{3}(q)C_{3}(q^{\prime})\|{\mathbf{v}}\|.

10.5.4 Proposition
.

There exists
\theta>0
(depending only on
\nu
) and a subset

X_{0}\subset X
with
\nu(X_{0})=1
such that the following holds:

Suppose
q\in X_{0}
,
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{H}(q)
,
and there exists
C>0
such that for all
\ell>0
, and at least

(1-\theta)
-fraction of
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q,\ell]
,

\|\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}\|\leq C\|{\mathbf{v}}\|.

Then,
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(q)
.

We refer to the discussion after Prop. 10.3 in
[
EM18
]
for the technical issues that the above propositions address.
All the proofs of the above propositions are contained in §10 of loc.cit. and, since they take place in the
g_{t}
-orbit of a single unstable, carry over to our setting with virtually no modifications.

10.6.
A variant of
Proposition 10.5.4
.

We will need the following:

10.6.1 Proposition
.

There exists
\theta>0
(depending only on
\nu
)
\epsilon>0

and a subset

X_{0}\subset X
with
\nu(X_{0})=1
such that the following holds:

Suppose
q\in X_{0}
,
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{H}(q)
,
and there exists
C>0
such that for all
L>0
, for at least

(1-\epsilon)
-fraction of
\ell\in[0,L]
, and at least

(1-\theta)
-fraction of
q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[q,\ell]
,

\|\mathbf{R}(q,q^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}\|\leq C\|{\mathbf{v}}\|.

Then,
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(q)
.

10.6.2 Definition
.

Suppose
x\in X
,
\epsilon>0
,
\theta>0
,
ij\in\Lambda^{\prime}
.
A vector
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{H}(x)
is called
(\epsilon,\theta,ij)
-bounded
if there
exists
C_{x}<\infty
such that for a set
\mathcal{D}_{x}\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
of
asymptotic density at least
(1-\epsilon)
,
for all
\ell\in\mathcal{D}_{x}
and for (
1-\theta
)-fraction
of
y\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]
,

(10.6.3)

\|\mathbf{R}(x,y){\mathbf{v}}\|\leq C_{x}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|.

10.6.4 Lemma
.

Let
n=\dim\mathbf{H}(x)
(for a.e
x
). If there exists no non-zero

(\epsilon/n^{2},\theta/n^{2},ij)
-bounded vectors in
\mathbf{H}(x)
,
we set
\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)=\{0\}
. Otherwise, we define

\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)\subset\mathbf{H}(x)
to
be the linear span of the
(\epsilon/n^{2},\theta/n^{2},ij)
-bounded vectors in
\mathbf{H}(x)
.
This is a subspace of
\mathbf{H}(x)
, and any vector in this
subspace is
(\epsilon,\theta,ij)
-bounded. Also,

(a)

\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
is
g_{t}
-equivariant, i.e.
g_{t}\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)=\mathbf{V}_{ij}(g_{t}x)
.

(b)

For almost all
u\in\mathcal{B}(x)
,
\mathbf{V}_{ij}(ux)=(u)_{*}\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
.

Proof.

This is virtually the same as the proof of

[
EM18
, Lemma 10.6]
.
The assertion (b) follows immediately from the definition since for
u\in\mathcal{B}(x)
,
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[ux,\ell]=\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]
.
∎

10.6.5 Remark
.

Formally, from its definition, the subspace

\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
depends on the choice of
\epsilon
and
\theta
.
It is clear that
as we decrease
\theta
or
\epsilon
,
the subspace
\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
decreases.
In view of
Lemma 10.6.4
, there
exist
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
\theta_{0}>0
and
m\geq 0

such that for all
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
, all
\theta<\theta_{0}
and almost
all
x\in X
, the dimension of
\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
is
m
. We will
always choose
\epsilon
and
\theta
so that
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}

and
\theta<\theta_{0}
.

10.6.6 Lemma
.

Suppose
\mathbf{V}
is a subbundle of

\mathbf{H}
, which is
g_{t}
and
(u)_{\ast}
-equivariant.
Suppose that
\theta>0
,
\delta>0
and
\ell>0
, and
suppose there exists a set
K_{\ell}\subset X

with
\nu(K_{\ell})>1-\delta
and a constant
C_{1}<\infty
(depending
on
\delta
and
\theta
but not on
\ell
, such that
such that for all
x\in K_{\ell}
,
and at least
(1-\theta)
-fraction of
y\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]
,

(10.6.7)

\|\mathbf{R}(x,y){\mathbf{v}}\|\leq C_{1}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|\quad\text{ for all ${\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{V}(x)$.}

Then there exists a subset
K^{\prime\prime}(\ell)\subset X

with
\nu(K^{\prime\prime}(\ell))>1-c(\delta)
where
c(\delta)\to 0
as

\delta\to 0
, and there exists
\theta^{\prime\prime}=\theta^{\prime\prime}(\theta,\delta)
with

\theta^{\prime\prime}\to 0
as
\theta\to 0
and
\delta\to 0

such that for all
x\in K^{\prime\prime}(\ell)
, for at least
(1-\theta^{\prime\prime})
-fraction of
y\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]
,

(10.6.8)

C_{1}^{-1}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|\leq\|\mathbf{R}(x,y){\mathbf{v}}\|\leq C_{1}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|\quad\text{
for all ${\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{V}(x)$}.

Proof.

This is the same as the proof of

[
EM18
, Lemma 10.16]

∎

Recall that any bundle is measurably trivial.
As in

[
EM18
, Lemma 10.13]
,
we make let
\tilde{\mu}_{\ell}
be the measure on
X\times\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{V})
defined by

(10.6.9)

\tilde{\mu}_{\ell}(f)=\int_{X}\int_{\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{V})}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]|}\int_{\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]}f(x,\mathbf{R}(y,x){\mathbf{v}})\,dy\,d\rho_{0}({\mathbf{v}})\,d\nu(x)

where
\rho_{0}
is the “round” measure on
\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{V})
.
(In fact,
\rho_{0}
can be any measure on
\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{V})
in the measure class of
Lebesgue measure, independent of x and fixed once and for all).

The following is analogous to
[
EM18
, Lemma 10.17]
:

10.6.10 Lemma
.

Suppose
\mathbf{V}(x)=\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
. Then
there exists a function
C:X\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
finite almost
everywhere such that for all
x\in X
,
all
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
,
and all
y\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x]
,

C(x)^{-1}C(y)^{-1}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|\leq\|\mathbf{R}(x,y){\mathbf{v}}\|\leq C(x)C(y)\|{\mathbf{v}}\|,

Proof.

Let
\epsilon_{0},\theta_{0}
be as in Remark
10.6.5
.
Choose
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}^{2}
,
\theta<\theta_{0}
.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{x}
be as in the definition of
\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
,
so that for almost all
x

By Fubini’s theorem and the definition of
\mathbf{V}_{ij}
there exists

\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
of asymptotic density at least
(1-\epsilon^{1/2})

such that for
\ell\in\mathcal{D}
, the set
K^{\prime}_{\ell}=\{x\in X\;\>:\;\>\ell\in\mathcal{D}_{x}\}

satisfies
\nu(K^{\prime}_{\ell})>1-\epsilon^{1/2}
.
There exists a constant
C_{1}>0
and a compact set
\hat{K}\subset X
with
\nu(\hat{K})<1-\epsilon^{1/2}
such that for
x\in K
,
C_{x}<C_{1}
, where
C_{x}
is
as in
Definition 10.6.2
. Let
\delta=2\epsilon^{1/2}
, and let
K_{\ell}=\hat{K}\cap K^{\prime}_{\ell}
. Then,

\nu(K_{\ell})>1-\delta
, and for
x\in K_{\ell}
and
\ell\in\mathcal{D}
,
(
10.6.7
). Then, we can apply

Lemma 10.6.6
to get
K^{\prime\prime}(\ell)
and
\theta^{\prime\prime}>0
such that for
\ell\in\mathcal{D}
,
x\in K^{\prime\prime}(\ell)
and at least

(1-\theta^{\prime\prime})
-fraction of
y\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]
,
(
10.6.8
) holds.

Choose a sequence
\ell_{k}\to\infty
such that
\ell_{k}\in\mathcal{D}
, and

\tilde{\mu}_{\ell_{k}}
converges to a measure
\tilde{\mu}_{\infty}
. The rest
of the proof of

[
EM18
, Lemma 10.17]

goes through without modifications.
∎

10.6.11 Lemma
.

We have for a.e.
x\in X
,
\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)=\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(x)
.

Proof.

Let
\theta_{0}
be as in

Proposition 10.5.4
. Choose

\theta<\theta_{0}
, and choose
0<\delta<2\kappa^{2}\theta^{2}
.
Let
C(\cdot)
be as in
Lemma 10.6.10
. Since

C(\cdot)
is finite a.e.
there exists
C_{1}>0
and a set
K\subset X
with

\nu(K)>1-\delta
such that for
x\in K
,
C(x)<C_{1}
. Let

K^{\ast}
be as in
Lemma 10.4.1
, so in
particular
\nu(K^{\ast})>0
. Let
\Psi=\{x\in X\;\>:\;\>g_{-t}x\in\Psi\text{ for some $t>0$ }\}
.

Suppose
x\in\Psi
and
{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x)
. Let
t>0
be such
that
x^{\prime}\equiv g_{-t}x\in K^{\ast}
. Then,
{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\equiv g_{-t}{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathbf{V}_{ij}(x^{\prime})
. By
Lemma 10.4.1
, for any

\ell>0
, and at least
(1-\theta)
-fraction of
y\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]
, we have
y\in K
, so
C(y)<C_{1}
. Thus, in view
of
Lemma 10.6.10
,

\|\mathbf{R}(x,y){\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|\leq C_{1}\|{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|.

Then, by
Proposition 10.5.4
, we get
{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(x^{\prime})
. Then,
{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(x)
.
∎

Proof of

Proposition 10.6.1
.

This follows immediately from
Definition 10.6.2
,

Lemma 10.6.4
and

.

11.
The equivalence relation on
\mathcal{W}^{u}

11.1.
The Equivalence Relation

Going forward, our space
\mathcal{C}
corresponds in our arguments to the space denoted
\operatorname{GSpc}
in
[
EM18
]
.

11.1.1.
On local invariance of measures

Recall that in
Definition 3.1.5
we defined what it means to have a (measurable) compatible family of subgroups
U^{+}(x)\subset\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(x)
.
This notion formalizes the situation where a leafwise measure on an unstable has Haar conditionals in some directions, but the directions themselves are allowed to vary, even for points within the same unstable manifold, see
Remark 3.1.6
.

With this in mind, starting from the compatible family
U^{+}(x)
we will construct a measurable partition with atoms denoted
\Psi_{ij}[x]
subordinated to the unstable (and even to
\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]
).
The conditional measures
f_{ij}[x]
(supported on

\Psi_{ij}[x]
) will lead to leafwise measures
\widetilde{f}_{ij}[x]
, and these measures will be invariant under
U^{+}(x)
.
Furthermore, the leafwise measures
\widetilde{f}_{ij}[x]
will be shown in
Section 12
, if the assumptions of
Theorem 4.1.2
hold, to be invariant under a group
U^{+}_{new}(x)
of dimension strictly larger than
U^{+}(x)
.

11.1.2.
The map
I(x,y)
.

We will use the notation from
Proposition D.2.3
, with
\wp^{+}(x,y)
for the strictly subresonant map induced by the measurable connection
P^{+}(x,y)
on the cocycle
L\mathcal{W}^{u}
linearizing the unstable manifold, for
y\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
.
We then have that
U^{+}[y]=\wp^{+}(x,y)U^{+}[x]
and
U^{+}(y)=\wp^{+}(x,y)U^{+}(x)\wp^{+}(x,y)^{-1}
, where
\wp^{+}(x,y)\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])
.
Also recall that
\mathcal{C}(x)=\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])/U^{+}(x)
parametrizes generalized subspaces in
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
, with
gU^{+}(x)\in\mathcal{C}(x)
corresponding to the generalized subspace
gU^{+}[x]\subset\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
.

We now define
I(x,y):\mathcal{C}(x)\to\mathcal{C}(y)
by

I(x,y)(gU^{+}(x))=g\wp^{+}(y,x)U^{+}(y).

We claim that
gU^{+}(x)\in\mathcal{C}(x)
and
I(x,y)(gU^{+}(x))\equiv g^{\prime}U^{+}(y)\in\mathcal{C}(y)
parametrize the same generalize subspace in
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
.
Indeed,

g^{\prime}U^{+}[y]=g\wp^{+}(y,x)U^{+}[y]=gU^{+}[x],

so the claim follows.

11.1.3.
The subset
\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x)
.

Recall that we constructed an equivalence relation on the fine Lyapunov spectrum in
Proposition 10.5.1
, and denoted by
\widetilde{\Lambda}
the corresponding set of equivalence classes.
Going forward, to ease the notation we will refer to the entire equivalence class by either
ij\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
or
[ij]
.

For
ij\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
, let

\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x)=\{gU^{+}(x)\in\mathcal{C}(x)\colon\mathbf{j}(gU^{+}(x))\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(x)\}.

Here,
\mathbf{j}\colon\mathcal{C}(x)\to\mathbf{H}(x)
is the composition of the linearization map
\mathcal{C}(x)\to L\mathcal{C}(x)
and the natural projection
L\mathcal{C}(x)\to\mathbf{H}(x)
, see
Eqn. (5.1.10)
.

11.1.4.
A partition of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
.

Recall that
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
denotes the measurable partition
described in
§A.5.5
.
The atom of
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
containing
x
is denoted by
\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]
.
In this section, the only properties of
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
we
will use is that it is subordinate to
\mathcal{W}^{u}
, and that the atoms

\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]
are relatively open in
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
.

11.1.5.
Equivalence relations.

Fix
x_{0}\in X
.
For
x
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x_{0}]
we say that

x^{\prime}\sim_{ij}x\text{ if $x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]$ and $I(x^{\prime},x)(U^{+}(x^{\prime}))\in\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x)$. }

In the above equation, we think of
U^{+}(x^{\prime})
as the identity coset of
\mathcal{C}(x^{\prime})=\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])/U^{+}(x^{\prime})
.

11.1.6 Proposition
.

The relation
\sim_{ij}
is a measurable equivalence relation.
In fact,
x\sim_{ij}x^{\prime}
if and only if
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]
and
\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x^{\prime})=I(x,x^{\prime})\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x)
.

The main part of the proof of

Proposition 11.1.6
is the following:

11.1.7 Lemma
.

There exists a subset
\Psi\subset X

with
\nu(\Psi)=1
such
that for any
ij\in\tilde{\Lambda}
,
if
x_{0}\in\Psi
,
x_{1}\in\Psi
and
x_{1}\sim_{ij}x_{0}
then

\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x_{1})=I(x_{0},x_{1})\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x_{0})
.

Warning.

In what follows
we will consider the condition
x^{\prime}\sim_{ij}x
to be undefined unless

x
and
x^{\prime}
both belong to the set
\Psi
of

Lemma 11.1.7
.

11.1.8.
Notation

As in §
5.1.7
,
let

\mathcal{C}[x]=\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])\cdot U^{+}[x]
; we view
\mathcal{C}[x]
as a collection of subsets of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
.
Then, for a.e.
y\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x]
, we have
U^{+}[y]=hU^{+}[x]
for
some
h\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x])
by
Proposition D.2.3
and therefore
\mathcal{C}[y]=\mathcal{C}[x]
.

We think of a point
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{C}[x_{0}]
as a subset of
\mathcal{W}^{u}[x_{0}]
, and call it a generalized subspace.
For
x\in\mathcal{W}^{u}[x_{0}]
let
\mathcal{U}_{x}:\mathcal{C}(x)\to\mathcal{C}[x]
denote the natural map. More precisely, for
gU^{+}(x)\in\mathcal{C}(x)
we have

\mathcal{U}_{x}(gU^{+}(x))=gU^{+}[x]\subset\mathcal{C}[x]=\mathcal{C}[x_{0}].

We then have the inverse map
\mathcal{U}_{x}^{-1}:\mathcal{C}[x_{0}]\to\mathcal{C}(x)
.
We also use the notation
\mathcal{E}_{ij}[x]=\mathcal{U}_{x}(\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x))
. Clearly,
\mathcal{E}_{ij}[x]\subset\mathcal{C}[x]
. Note also that
I(x,y)=\mathcal{U}_{y}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{U}_{x}
.

We will need the following lemma:

11.1.9 Lemma
.

For every
\delta>0
there exists constants
\kappa(\delta)>1
and

\epsilon(\delta)>0
and a
compact set
K
with
\nu(K)>1-\delta

Suppose

,

.

.

-fraction of

, (

) holds,

Proof.

Let
x_{k}^{\prime}=g^{ij}_{-\ell}x_{k}

Proof of
Lemma 11.1.7
.

This proof follows
[
EM18
, §11.1*]
.

Let
\delta>0
be a small constant to be chosen later.
Let
K\subset X
and
C>0
be such
that
Lemma 11.1.9
holds with
\epsilon(\delta)<C^{-1}
,
for
x\in K

Proposition 5.1.16
holds with
c_{1}(x)>C^{-1}
, and for all
x\in K
, all
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(x)
and all

\ell>0
, for all
y\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]\cap K
, we have

(11.1.16)

\|\mathbf{R}(x,y){\mathbf{v}}\|<C\|{\mathbf{v}}\|.

By
Lemma 10.4.1
there exists a subset
K^{*}\subset K
with
\nu(K^{*})\geq(1-4\kappa^{2}\delta^{1/2})
such that for

x\in K^{*}
,
|\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]\cap K|\geq 0.99|\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]|
,

Eqn. (10.4.2)
holds with
\theta^{\prime}=\delta^{1/2}
, and
x\in K^{*}\implies\mathcal{B}_{0}[x]\subset K^{*}
.
Furthermore, we may ensure that for
x\in K^{*}
,
K^{*}\cap\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x]
is relatively open in
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x]
.
(Indeed, suppose
z\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x]\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x]
. Then,
for all
\ell
,
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x,\ell]=\mathcal{F}_{ij}[z,\ell]
,
and thus
Eqn. (10.4.2)
holds for
z
.)

Let

\Psi=\left\{x\in X\colon\lim_{T\to\infty}\tfrac{1}{T}|\{t\in[0,T]\colon g_{-t}x\in K^{*}\}|\geq(1-8\kappa^{2}\delta^{1/2})\right\}

Then
\nu(\Psi)=1
. From its definition,
\Psi
is invariant
under
g_{t}
. Since
K^{*}\cap\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x]
is
relatively open in
\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x]
,
\Psi
is saturated by the leaves
of
\mathcal{F}_{ij}
. This implies that
\Psi
is (locally) invariant under

U^{+}
. We also assume that for
x\in\Psi
,
\dim\mathcal{E}_{ij}(x)
is
constant. This condition is also locally invariant under
U^{+}
.

Suppose
x_{0}\in\Psi
,
x_{1}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x_{0}]\cap\Psi
.
We will use notation for Hausdorff distances from

§5.1.15
.
By

Proposition D.2.3
,

U^{+}[x_{1}]\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x_{0}])U^{+}[x_{0}]
. Therefore,

U^{+}[x_{1}]\in\mathcal{C}[x_{0}]
, and thus,
\mathcal{U}_{x_{0}}^{-1}(U^{+}[x_{1}])

is defined. Let

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}_{x_{1}}^{-1}(U^{+}[x_{1}]))\in\mathbf{H}(x_{0}).

Suppose
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{E}_{ij}[x_{1}]
. Let

{\mathbf{v}}=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}_{x_{1}}^{-1}(\mathcal{Q})).

Assume

(11.1.17)

\|{\mathbf{v}}\|\leq 2\|\mathbf{u}\|.

Since, for
x_{k}
fixed, the map
\mathbf{j}\circ\mathcal{U}_{x_{k}}^{-1}:\mathcal{C}[x_{k}]\to\mathbf{H}(x_{k})
is analytic, (
11.1.17
) will hold assuming

\mathcal{Q}
is sufficiently close to
U^{+}[x_{1}]
.

Note that

\mathcal{Q}\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x_{1}])U^{+}[x_{1}]
. Since

\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x_{0}])=\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x_{1}])
is a group, we get

\mathcal{Q}\in\mathbf{G}^{ssr}(\mathcal{W}^{u}[x_{0}])U^{+}[x_{0}].

Therefore
\mathcal{U}_{x_{0}}^{-1}(\mathcal{Q})
is defined.
Let

{\mathbf{w}}=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}_{x_{0}}^{-1}(\mathcal{Q})).

For any
C_{1}>0
sufficiently large (depending on
x_{0}
,
x_{1}
), we can
find
t\in\mathbb{R}
with

C_{1}<t<2C_{1}
such that

x_{0}^{\prime}\equiv g_{-t}^{ij}x_{0}\in K^{*}
,

x_{1}^{\prime}\equiv g^{ij}_{-t}x_{1}\in K^{*}
. By

Proposition 10.2.5
,

x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{0}[x_{0}^{\prime}]
.
Let
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=g_{-t}^{ij}\mathbf{u}
,

{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}=g_{-t}^{ij}{\mathbf{v}}
,

{\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}=g_{-t}^{ij}{\mathbf{w}}
,

\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}=g_{-t}^{ij}\mathcal{Q}
.
By choosing
C_{1}
sufficiently large (depending on
x_{0},x_{1}
), we can
ensure that

(11.1.18)

\|\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|\leq C^{-3},\qquad\|\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\|\leq C^{-3},

where
C
is defined in
Eqn. (11.1.16)
.

Suppose
\ell^{\prime}>0
. Then, for
k=0,1
, since
x_{k}^{\prime}\in K^{*}
,

|\{y_{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x_{k}^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}]\colon y_{k}^{\prime}\in K\}|\geq(1-\delta^{1/2})|\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}]|,

Since
U^{+}[x_{1}]\in\mathcal{E}_{ij}[x_{0}]
, we have
U^{+}[x_{1}^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{E}_{ij}[x_{0}^{\prime}]
, and thus

\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(x_{0}^{\prime}).

Since
x_{0}^{\prime}\in K^{*}\subset K
, we have by
(
Eqn. (11.1.16)
),
for all
y_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x_{0}^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}]\cap K
,

(11.1.19)

\|\mathbf{R}(x_{0}^{\prime},y_{0}^{\prime})\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|\leq C\|\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|\leq C^{-2},

where we have used (
Eqn. (11.1.18)
)
for the last estimate. Note that for a suitable
y_{1}^{\prime}
,

\mathbf{R}(x_{0}^{\prime},y_{0}^{\prime})\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}_{y_{0}^{\prime}}^{-1}(U^{+}[y_{1}^{\prime}])).

Therefore, by
Proposition 5.1.16
,
for all
y_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x_{0}^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}]\cap K
, for a suitable
y_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x_{1}^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}]
,

(11.1.20)

d^{y_{0}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[y_{0}^{\prime}],U^{+}[y_{1}^{\prime}])\leq C^{-1}.

Let

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\{\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\;\>:\;\>g^{ij}_{-\ell^{\prime}}x_{0}^{\prime}\in K^{\ast}\text{
and }g^{ij}_{-\ell^{\prime}}x_{1}^{\prime}\in K^{\ast}\}.

By the definition of
\Psi
, the density of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is at least
1-4\kappa^{2}\delta^{1/2}
. Now suppose
\ell^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}
. Then,
in view of
Lemma 11.1.9
, for at least

(1-6\delta^{1/2})
-fraction of
y_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x_{0}^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}]
,

(11.1.21)

\text{ $y_{0}^{\prime}\in K$ and there exists }\\
\text{$y_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{ij}[x_{1}^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}]\cap K\cap\mathfrak{B}_{0}[y_{1}^{\prime}]$ such that
(\ref{eq:hd:U:y0:prime:U:y1:prime:bounded}) holds. }

Now suppose
y_{0}^{\prime}
,
y_{1}^{\prime}
are such that
(
11.1.21
) holds.
Since
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{E}_{ij}[x_{1}]
,
{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(x_{1})
, and thus,
{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{E}_{[ij],bdd}(x_{1}^{\prime})
.
Hence, by (
Eqn. (11.1.16)
), since
y_{1}^{\prime}\in K
,

(11.1.22)

\|\mathbf{R}(x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}\|\leq C\|{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}\|\leq C^{-2}.

where we used (
Eqn. (11.1.18)
) for the last
estimate. Note that

\mathbf{R}(x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime}){\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}_{y_{1}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathbf{R}(x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})).

Therefore, by

Proposition 5.1.16
,

d^{y_{1}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[y_{1}^{\prime}],\mathbf{R}(x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\leq C^{-1}.

Since

, this implies

d^{y_{0}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[y_{1}^{\prime}],\mathbf{R}(x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\leq C^{-1}.

Then, by the triangle inequality,

(11.1.23)

d^{y_{0}^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{H},loc}(U^{+}[y_{0}^{\prime}],\mathbf{R}(x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\leq 2C^{-1}.

Note that

\mathbf{R}(x_{0}^{\prime},y_{0}^{\prime}){\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}=\mathbf{j}(\mathcal{U}_{y_{0}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathbf{R}(x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})).

Then,
by
Proposition 5.1.16

(11.1.24)

\|\mathbf{R}(x_{0}^{\prime},y_{0}^{\prime}){\mathbf{w}}^{\prime}\|\leq 2.

Thus, for
\ell^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
and at least
(1-6\delta^{1/2})
-fraction of

, (
11.1.24
) holds. Let

\epsilon,\theta
be as in
